**Cause** — **premature epiphyseal (physeal) closure** of metatarsal → congenital hypoplasia of ≥1 metatarsal

**Associated** — **Down** · **Turner** · **Larsen** · Albright · diastrophic dwarfism

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — present since birth · **discomfort with shoe wear** (e.g. heels) · forefoot pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **weight-bearing AP + lateral**

**Findings** — short metatarsal(s) · **broken metatarsal parabola**

![Brachymetatarsia of 4th metatarsal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4082-bcm-ap.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Initial management – shoe modification

**First line** — **extra-depth or extra-wide shoes** → generally improves symptoms\
✗ Wrong options: immobilisation + NSAIDs · MTP fusion · ray resection · lengthening first

**Ineffective** — taping, manipulative reduction

```
Symptomatic brachymetatarsia
├─ First → extra-depth / extra-wide shoes
└─ Persistent symptoms in older child → metatarsal lengthening
       └─ Very rarely → ray amputation
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Metatarsal lengthening** | **Persistent symptoms in older child** after shoe modification |
| Amputation (ray resection) | **Very rarely**, persistent symptoms |
| Extensor tenotomy + capsulotomy | **None** — insufficient correction |
| Fusion (MTPJ) | **Not indicated** — complications, ineffective |

![Metatarsal lengthening](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4082-lengthening.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"18-year-old, congenital short 4th metatarsal, new forefoot pain in heels — next step?"** → **extra-depth shoe modification**\
✗ Wrong options: 4th MTPJ fusion · ray resection · lengthening

**"Most commonly affected metatarsal?"** → **4th**

**"Cause?"** → **premature physeal closure**

**"Failed shoe modification?"** → **metatarsal lengthening**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Calcaneovalgus Foot

*Pediatrics · 12 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — mild form up to **40% of newborns** · true deformity **1 in 1,000**

**Demographics** — **females**, **first-born**, young mothers

### Etiology

**Cause** — positional, **intrauterine "packaging"** → benign **soft-tissue contracture** · hindfoot **eversion + dorsiflexion**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — excessively dorsiflexed hindfoot, **passively correctable** · **dorsum of foot rests on anterior tibia**

**Associated** — **posteromedial tibial bowing** (almost always some calcaneovalgus)

![Calcaneovalgus foot — dorsum against tibia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4067-clinical-photo-showing-a-calcaneovalgus-foot.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical: calcaneovalgus foot **without congenital deformity or dislocation**

### Key radiology findings

**Not required** if foot flexible enough to exclude CVT

**AP + lateral tibia** — look for **posteromedial bowing**

**Plantarflexion lateral** — distinguishes from **CVT** · before navicular ossifies (**age 3**) use **1st metatarsal** as proxy · calcaneovalgus: **1st MT lines up with talus** · CVT: talar axis passes **plantar** to 1st MT on standard + plantarflexion laterals

**Apex of deformity** — calcaneovalgus: **ankle joint** · posteromedial bowing: **distal tibia**

### Diagnosis & posteromedial tibial bowing

**Posteromedial bowing** — intrauterine positioning · named for apex (posterior + medial) · **"PosteroMedial = Probably Mild"** · bowing improves; follow for **LLD**

![Posteromedial bowing — apex at distal tibia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4067-calcaneovalgus-foot.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Calcaneovalgus foot
├─ Plantarflexes beyond neutral → observation + parental passive stretching
└─ Cannot plantarflex beyond neutral → serial casting
       (resolves spontaneously by 3–6 months)
```

**Outcome** — **resolves by 3–6 months** · severity of initial deformity **does not predict** outcome

## 6 Complications

**LLD** — when associated with **posteromedial bowing** · LLD correction = **most common surgery** for posteromedial bowing

**Flexible flatfoot** — years after resolution

## X vs Y

| | **Calcaneovalgus** | **Congenital vertical talus** |
|---|---|---|
| Flexibility | **Flexible**, passively correctable | **Rigid** |
| Hindfoot | **Calcaneus (dorsiflexion)** | **Equinus** + valgus |
| Midfoot | Normal | **Dorsal TN dislocation**, rocker-bottom, medial talar head |
| Plantarflexion lateral | **1st MT lines up with talus** | Talus plantar to 1st MT |
| Treatment | Stretching ± casting | Reverse Ponseti / surgery |

| Paralytic calcaneovalgus (L5 spina bifida) | |
|---|---|
| Overactive / spared | **Dorsiflexors (L4, L5)**, **evertors (S1)** |
| Weak | **Plantarflexors (S1, S2)**, **invertors (L5)** |

## How it's asked

**"Newborn, dorsum of foot resting on tibia — associated X-ray?"** → **posteromedial bowing of tibia**\
✗ Wrong options: fibular hemimelia · anterolateral bowing · clubfoot · tibial deficiency

**"Apex posteromedial tibia, dorsiflexed foot correctable to neutral?"** → **calcaneovalgus**\
✗ Wrong options: cavovarus · clubfoot · skewfoot · CVT (not correctable)

**"Foot cannot plantarflex past neutral?"** → **serial casting**

**"Natural history?"** → **resolves by 3–6 months**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cavovarus Foot in Pediatrics & Adults

*Pediatrics · 13 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Deformity components

**Components** — **cavus** (high arch) · **plantarflexed 1st ray + forefoot pronation** · **hindfoot varus** · forefoot adduction

### Etiology

**Neurologic** — **~67% (two-thirds)** · identifying the neuro cause is critical

**Unilateral** → rule out **tethered cord / spinal cord tumour** · **Bilateral** → most commonly **Charcot-Marie-Tooth (CMT)**, often hereditary/congenital

**Idiopathic** — subtle, bilateral (**subtle cavus**, no weakness)

**Traumatic** — talus fracture malunion · compartment syndrome · crush injury

**Conditions with cavovarus** — **CMT** · **cerebral palsy** · **Friedreich ataxia** · spinal cord lesions · polio · amniotic band syndrome

### Pathomechanics (PL vs TA, PB vs PTT) & Charcot-Marie-Tooth

| Pair | Strong (spared) | Weak | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PL vs TA** | **Peroneus longus** (hypertrophic) | **Tibialis anterior** | **Plantarflexed 1st ray** (cavus) — primary deformity |
| **PTT vs PB** | **Tibialis posterior** | **Peroneus brevis** | **Hindfoot varus** + forefoot pronation |
| Extrinsics vs intrinsics | **Long flexors + extensors** | **Lumbricals, interossei** (first affected) | **Claw toes** (intrinsic minus) |
| EHL recruited for weak TA | **EHL** | TA | **1st MTP hyperextension**, windlass drives 1st MT down |

**Sequence (forefoot-driven)** — plantarflexed 1st MT + pronated forefoot → medial forefoot strikes first → **subtalar joint supinates** for tripod contact → **compensatory hindfoot varus** → flexible at first, **rigid with time**

**Forefoot position** — **pronation**\
✗ Wrong options: supination (the EXCEPT answer)

**CMT genetics** — HMSN · **PMP22 duplication, chromosome 17** · weak TA + PB, **normal PL + PT**

**Overactive muscle with cavus** — **posterior tibialis**\
✗ Wrong options: TA (causes DF + inversion) · toe flexors/extensors

**Gait effect** — transverse tarsal joint **locks in stance** → lateral column overload

## 3 Presentation

**History** — recurrent **ankle sprains**, lateral ankle pain (peroneal pathology) · **lateral foot pain** (5th MT stress fracture) · **calluses under 1st + 5th MT heads** · **plantar fasciitis**

**Exam** — **peek-a-boo heel** (varus heel seen from front) · prominent 1st MT fat pad · **Silfverskiöld** (gastrocnemius tightness common) · unstable gait, ↑ double-limb stance

**Neuro clues** — **1st dorsal interosseous wasting** of hand → **CMT** · **scoliosis** → CMT · **spinal dysraphism** · high-steppage gait

### Coleman block test – hindfoot flexibility & forefoot-driven deformity

**Used for** — **cavovarus foot** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: CVT · planovalgus · metatarsus adductus

**Tests** — **hindfoot flexibility**\
✗ Wrong options: forefoot flexibility · ankle instability · Achilles tightness (that's Silfverskiöld)

**Technique** — **1–1.5 inch block under lateral foot + heel** → 1st ray drops off medially → removes forefoot contribution

**Flexible** — heel corrects to **neutral or valgus** → forefoot-driven → **forefoot procedures** only

**Rigid** — heel **stays in varus** → **lateralising calcaneal osteotomy** + forefoot procedures

![Coleman block test — flexible hindfoot corrects](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4063-coleman-block.jpg)

**Other signs (distractors)** — "too many toes" = forefoot abduction in flatfoot · inability to single-heel-rise = PTT insufficiency

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — standing AP + lateral ankle · standing AP, lateral, oblique foot

| View | Measurement | Cavovarus value |
|---|---|---|
| **AP foot** | **Talocalcaneal (Kite) angle** | **<20°** (nl 20–45°) → hindfoot varus |
| AP foot | Talonavicular coverage | **>7°** overcoverage → forefoot adduction |
| AP foot | Metatarsal overlap | Forefoot pronation |
| **Lateral foot** | **Meary's (talo–1st MT) angle** | **>4° apex dorsal** (1st MT line passes dorsal to talus) |
| Lateral foot | **Calcaneal pitch** | **>30°** |
| Lateral foot | **Sinus tarsi see-through, double talar dome** | Hindfoot ER relative to cassette |
| Lateral foot | Bell-shaped cuboid · ↑ 5th MT base–medial cuneiform distance | — |
| Oblique | MT stress fractures, CN coalition | — |

![Meary's angle — apex dorsal break](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4063-meary.jpg)

### Work-up – electrodiagnostic studies

**CMT diagnostic algorithm** — **neurologic exam → EMG/NCS → genetic testing** (genetic first if family member has known CMT)

**Bilateral cavovarus, lateral pain, hand intrinsic wasting, family history** → **electrodiagnostic studies** next\
✗ Wrong options: muscle biopsy (dystrophies) · nerve biopsy (only if inconclusive) · CK/aldolase · echo

## 5 Management

```
Cavovarus foot
├─ Mild, flexible → orthotic (lateral posting + recessed 1st ray)
├─ Failed nonop, flexible hindfoot (Coleman corrects) → plantar release + 1st MT DF osteotomy ± PL→PB transfer
└─ Rigid hindfoot (Coleman stays varus) → add lateralising calcaneal osteotomy
       └─ Painful rigid arthritic → triple arthrodesis (rarely; poor long-term)
```

### Orthoses – lateral hindfoot posting & recessed first ray

**Supple adult cavovarus** — **full-length semi-rigid orthotic with 1st-ray recess + lateral hindfoot posting/wedge** · not indicated in children (Orthobullets)\
✗ Wrong options: medial posting (flatfoot) · walker boot · accommodative · lace-up

**Other orthotic matches** — hallux rigidus → **Morton's extension** · midfoot arthritis → rocker-bottom shoe · flexible planovalgus → **medial posting** · rigid cavovarus → orthoses don't work

**More severe** — **SMO** · **AFO** if dynamic equinus (equinocavovarus) · lace-up brace / high-top if rigid bracing not tolerated · accommodative shoes rarely enough

### Surgery – first metatarsal dorsiflexion osteotomy, plantar release, calcaneal osteotomy, EHL transfer

| Problem | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Cavus** | **Plantar fascia release** · Steindler stripping (short flexors off calcaneus) |
| **Plantarflexed 1st ray** | **PL → PB transfer** (↓ 1st-ray PF without losing eversion) · **1st MT dorsiflexion osteotomy** |
| **Flexible hindfoot varus** (Coleman corrects) | **1st MT DF osteotomy** ± plantar release — calcaneus spared |
| **Rigid hindfoot varus** (Coleman fails) | **Lateralising (lateral slide / Dwyer closing-wedge) calcaneal osteotomy** + 1st MT DF osteotomy |
| PTT overpull / TA weakness | **PTT transfer to dorsum** |
| Equinus | **Gastrocnemius recession** (less weakness, with plantar release) · **TAL staged weeks after** plantar release |
| Chronic lateral ankle instability | **Broström** (fails unless deformity corrected) |
| **Clawed hallux** | **Modified Jones: EHL → 1st MT neck + IP fusion** |
| Lesser toe clawing | Extensors → 2nd–5th MT necks |
| Painful rigid arthritic hindfoot | Triple arthrodesis — **almost never** (poor long-term) |

**Clawed hallux distractors** — ✗ Wrong options: EHL to proximal phalanx / FHB tenotomy (worsen MTP hyperextension) · original Jones (EHL to neck only — inadequate) · FHL tenotomy alone · MTP + IP fusion (stiff ray) · Keller · metatarsal head resection

**Flatfoot procedures (distractors)** — ✗ Wrong options: medial calcaneal slide · lateral column lengthening · FDL transfer · 1st TMT fusion in plantarflexion

### Equinus – gastrocnemius recession (Silfverskiöld)

**Silfverskiöld** — DF ↑ with **knee flexion** → **isolated gastrocnemius** tightness → **gastrocnemius recession** · tight in both positions → **Achilles/soleus** → TAL

**Diabetic equinus after casting** — toe-drag in swing + DF improves with knee flexion → **gastrocnemius recession**\
✗ Wrong options: Thompson positive · too-many-toes · fixed dorsiflexion

## 6 Complications

**Ankle instability** — lateral ligament reconstruction **fails** if cavovarus not corrected · → **varus ankle arthritis**

**Stress fractures** — **5th MT base (Jones)** · 4th MT · **navicular** · **medial malleolus** (↑ medial compression; ↑ nonunion → cast NWB)

**Others** — **hallux sesamoiditis** (plantarflexed 1st MT) · **peroneal tendon pathology** (**PB** most common) · **plantar fasciitis** · metatarsalgia, calluses

**NOT caused by cavovarus** — **hallux valgus** (→ planovalgus)

## X vs Y

| | **Flexible hindfoot** | **Rigid hindfoot** |
|---|---|---|
| Coleman block | Heel → **neutral / valgus** | Heel **stays varus** |
| Driver | **Forefoot** (plantarflexed 1st ray) | Fixed hindfoot |
| Orthotic | **Lateral posting + recessed 1st ray** | Not effective |
| Surgery | **1st MT DF osteotomy + plantar release** ± PL→PB | **+ lateralising calcaneal osteotomy** |

## How it's asked

**"Coleman block test assesses?"** → **cavovarus foot** · **hindfoot flexibility** (TO)

**"Had lateralising calcaneal + 1st MT DF osteotomy — preop finding?"** → **heel stays in varus with block under lateral foot**

**"Calluses under 1st + 5th MT heads; heel corrects on block?"** → **flexible hindfoot varus**

**"Adult cavovarus not correcting on Coleman?"** → **1st MT DF osteotomy + lateral displacement calcaneal osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: LCL · medial slide

**"First-ray plantarflexion: weak X, strong Y, transfer Y to Z?"** → **TA weak, PL strong, PL → PB**

**"Meary's line dorsal to talus, neuromuscular disorder — overactive muscle?"** → **posterior tibialis**

**"PMP22 duplication — EXCEPT?"** → **forefoot supination** (it's pronation)

**"Potential result of cavovarus EXCEPT?"** → **hallux valgus** (✓ instability, 5th MT fracture, peroneal subluxation, plantar fasciitis)

**"PMP duplication chr 17 — relative strengths?"** → **weak PB, normal PT**

**"CMT child warned of higher risk of?"** → **medial malleolus stress fracture**

**"Bilateral cavovarus, hand wasting, family history — next diagnostic step?"** → **electrodiagnostic studies**

**"Orthotic for flexible adult cavovarus?"** → **lateral hindfoot posting with recessed 1st ray**

**"Pictured orthotic suits?"** → **supple adult pes cavus**

**"Coleman → 3° valgus, failed orthotic — part of surgery?"** → **1st ray DF osteotomy + plantar fascia release**\
✗ Wrong options: triple · subtalar fusion · medial slide

**"Fixed clawed hallux?"** → **EHL to MT neck + IP fusion (modified Jones)**

**"HMSN 12-year-old, block test corrects heel?"** → **plantar release + 1st MT osteotomy ± tendon transfers**\
✗ Wrong options: calcaneal osteotomy · triple

**"Clawed hallux in cavovarus, painless MTP/IP motion?"** → **1st IP fusion + EHL transfer**

**"Hindfoot corrects on Coleman, no arthritis — include?"** → **1st ray DF osteotomy**

**"PL→PB, Broström, lateralising calcaneal osteotomy — supporting exam?"** → **↑ inversion vs other side + no change on Coleman**

**"Equinus — when is gastrocnemius recession right?"** → **DF improves with knee flexion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cerebral Palsy - Foot Conditions

*Pediatrics · 14 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Foot deformity** — **most common deformity in CP** · forms: **equinus**, **hallux valgus**, **equinoplanovalgus**, **equinocavovarus**

| Deformity | Typical CP type | Muscle imbalance |
|---|---|---|
| **Equinus** | All; **isolated in hemiplegia** | Spastic/contracted **gastrocsoleus** vs dorsiflexors |
| **Equinoplanovalgus** | **Spastic diplegia + quadriplegia**, bilateral | **Spastic peroneals + heel cord**, **weak PTT**, lax ligaments |
| **Equinocavovarus** | **Spastic hemiplegia** | **Invertors (PTT ± TA) overpower peroneals** |
| **Hallux valgus** | **Diplegics** with equinus + planovalgus | **Adductor hallucis overactivity** + external forces |

**Lever-arm dysfunction** — planovalgus and cavovarus both disrupt push-off / second rocker

## 3 Presentation

### Equinus

**Symptoms** — shoe wear, **tripping** (poor clearance), instability

**Exam** — forefoot calluses · **toe walking, no heel strike** · compensatory **knee hyperextension** · late equinoplanovalgus

**True equinus gait** — toe walking with **lack of heel strike** at initial contact · **plantarflexion moment** in stance\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ DF in stance/swing · DF moment

**Silfverskiöld** — DF ↑ with **knee flexed** → **gastrocnemius** tight · equal → **Achilles** tight · DF is **worse with knee extended** when gastroc spastic (crosses knee)

**Planovalgus trap** — uncontrolled subtalar joint **overestimates** ankle DF → **correct heel valgus first** before testing for Achilles contracture

### Equinoplanovalgus

**Findings** — bilateral · **valgus heel** · **talar head callus** in arch · **midfoot break** · **hallux valgus develops over time**

### Equinocavovarus

**Findings** — lateral-border / **5th MT calluses** (ulcers) · in-toeing · foot drop if TA weak · supination on TA firing = TA is main source

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Deformity | Views | Findings |
|---|---|---|
| Equinus | Not required unless hindfoot malalignment | — |
| **Equinoplanovalgus** | **WB AP ankle** (rule out **ankle valgus**) + foot | **↓ calcaneal pitch** · **negative talo–1st MT angle** · talar head uncovering |
| Equinocavovarus | WB foot + ankle | **Metatarsal overlap** · **↑ calcaneal pitch** |
| Hallux valgus | WB series | ↑ HVA, IMA, DMAA, HVI |

![Equinoplanovalgus in CP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12120-equinovalgusxr.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Equinus – botulinum toxin, serial casting & gastrocnemius recession

| Treatment | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Serial casting** | Mild, dynamic, young · **unbraceable foot** (can't reach neutral) after botox → cast, then brace |
| **Botulinum toxin A** (gastrocnemius) | Mild spasticity, delays surgery · **prevents presynaptic release of acetylcholine (ACh)** · not more often than **every 3 months** |
| **Hinged / articulated AFO** | Mild, passively correctable, no myostatic contracture · not if excessive midstance DF |
| **Solid AFO** | Mild–moderate, partly correctable, mild contracture · not if heavy, ≥12 yr, rigid malalignment |
| **Gastrocnemius recession** | **Silfverskiöld positive** (isolated gastroc) · less immobilisation, **higher recurrence** |
| **TAL** | Gastrocsoleus (Achilles) tight · rarely isolated **except in hemiplegia** · walking cast 4–6 wk |

**Botox mechanism distractors** — ✗ Wrong options: binds nicotinic receptor (curare, succinylcholine) · destroys receptor (myasthenia) · sarcolemma instability (dystrophy)

**Surgery timing** — delay equinus surgery until **≥4 yr** (OB) · **≥6 yr** to prevent recurrence (OB) — read the options

**Hemiplegic 3-year-old, toe-walking, DF short of neutral after botox** → **serial casting** of affected side\
✗ Wrong options: bilateral AFOs (unaffected side toe-walks in compensation) · heel-cord lengthening · repeat botox at 1 month · baclofen pump (diplegia)

**Gastrocnemius recession technique** — posterior/posteromedial incision · protect **sural nerve** · incise aponeurosis transversely or U-shape · cast in neutral

### Equinovarus – split tibialis posterior transfer

```
Flexible equinocavovarus (hemiplegia)
├─ PTT EMG continuous → SPOTT (split PTT to peroneus brevis), age 4–7
├─ PTT EMG out-of-phase → whole PTT transfer to dorsum (lateral cuneiform)
├─ Spastic TA (supination on TA firing) → SPLATT ± TAL + PTT lengthening
└─ Fixed deformity → add calcaneal osteotomy / arthrodesis
```

**SPOTT** — **passively correctable** · **age 4–7** · **spastic hemiplegia** · **continuous PTT EMG** · posterior half rerouted behind tibia (anterior to NV bundle) → woven into **PB** · more consistent than whole transfer\
✗ Wrong options: athetosis (unpredictable) · rigid deformity · quadriplegic/diplegic valgus

**SPLATT** — passively correctable + **spastic TA** · lateral half → **cuboid** tunnel · not for **weak TA / foot drop**

**Bony** — **lateral slide or lateral closing-wedge calcaneal osteotomy** (fixed varus) · **triple arthrodesis** (severe fixed; recurs if soft tissues not balanced)

**Bracing** — supple only · rarely successful, worsens calluses

### Equinoplanovalgus

**Flexible** → **bracing**

**Pain / pressure sores despite bracing** → soft tissue + bone

| Soft tissue | Bone |
|---|---|
| TAL · **peroneus brevis lengthening** · **PTT advancement** | **Medial slide calcaneal osteotomy** · **lateral column lengthening (Evans)** (trapezoidal graft anterior to middle facet) · **Grice** (extra-articular subtalar, **does not affect tarsal growth**) · **subtalar arthroereisis** (plug; breakdown) |

![Grice extra-articular subtalar arthrodesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12120-grice-moved.jpg)

### Hallux valgus – first MTP arthrodesis

**Painless, shoes OK** → observe

**Painful (failed accommodative shoes)** → **1st MTP arthrodesis** — **highest success** in ambulatory + nonambulatory CP\
✗ Wrong options: double/crescentic MT osteotomy · modified McBride · night orthotics (recurrence unacceptably high)

**Valgus interphalangeus** → **Akin** proximal phalanx osteotomy

## 6 Complications

**Planovalgus surgery** — **overcorrection into varus (most common)** · **sural nerve injury** · **overlengthened lateral column → lateral forefoot overload** · CC joint destabilisation

**Cavovarus** — **recurrence** if all components not addressed

## X vs Y

| | **Equinoplanovalgus** | **Equinocavovarus** |
|---|---|---|
| CP type | **Diplegia / quadriplegia** | **Hemiplegia** |
| Imbalance | Spastic peroneals, weak PTT | **PTT ± TA > peroneals** |
| Callus | **Talar head** | **Lateral border / 5th MT** |
| Calcaneal pitch | **↓** | **↑** |
| Surgery | LCL, medial slide, Grice, arthroereisis | **SPOTT / SPLATT**, lateral calcaneal osteotomy |

## How it's asked

**"5-year-old hemiplegic, flexible equinocavovarus, lateral ulcers, continuous PTT EMG?"** → **split posterior tibial tendon transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT to lateral cuneiform (out-of-phase) · triple · calcaneal slide (fixed)

**"Best candidate for SPOTT?"** → **6-year-old spastic hemiplegic, flexible equinovarus**\
✗ Wrong options: athetosis · rigid

**"Spastic diplegic with true equinus gait — expected?"** → **toe walking, no heel strike**

**"Botulinum toxin mechanism?"** → **prevents presynaptic ACh release**

**"3-year-old hemiplegic, DF −5° after botox 1 month ago?"** → **serial casting** of affected side

**"Isolated gastrocnemius recession best when?"** → **DF 15° knee flexed vs neutral knee extended**\
✗ Wrong options: too-many-toes · Thompson · equal DF · no single heel rise

**"15-year-old CP community ambulator, painful hallux valgus?"** → **1st MTP arthrodesis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cerebral Palsy - Gait Disorders

*Pediatrics · 15 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Gait disorder** — **primary reason for orthopaedic consultation** in CP

### Ambulatory prognosis & milestones

**Independent walking** — expected **12–18 months**\
✗ Wrong options: towers of 4 blocks = 2–3 yr · transfers toys = 6–9 mo · jumping = 2–3 yr · overhand throw = 18–24 mo

**Good prognostic sign for walking** — **positive parachute reaction** (reaches toward floor when lowered prone)

**Poor signs** — **Moro beyond 6 months** · **asymmetric tonic neck reflex**, **extensor thrust**, **absent foot placement** (abnormal at any age)

### Pathophysiology – deviations

| Level | Definition | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Primary** | Direct CNS insult | **Spasticity**, weakness, ↓ proprioception |
| **Secondary** | Growth-related from abnormal loading | **Myostatic contractures**, **femoral anteversion**, hip subluxation, equinoplanovalgus |
| **Tertiary** | **Compensations** for secondary deviations | — |

**Modern approach** — define deviation, **plane** (sagittal, coronal, transverse) and **level** (hip, knee, ankle)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Gait patterns – crouch, jump & stiff-knee (rectus femoris)

| Pattern | Hip | Knee | Ankle | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **True equinus** | Normal | Normal / hyperextended | **Equinus** (foot < plantigrade) | Isolated, **hemiplegics** · GRF anterior to knee |
| **Jump gait** | **Flexed** | **Flexed** | **Equinus** | + anterior pelvic tilt · diplegia |
| **Apparent equinus** | Flexed | Flexed | **Plantigrade-capable** but toe-walks | Proximal deviation (knee flexion) |
| **Crouch gait** | **Flexed** | **Flexed** | **Excess dorsiflexion (calcaneus)** | GRF **posterior to knee** · diplegia, quadriplegia at puberty |
| **Stiff-knee gait** | — | **↓ swing flexion** | — | **Rectus femoris** out of phase |
| Asymmetric | Each limb in a different group | | | — |

**Crouch causes** — **iatrogenic isolated TAL in jump gait** · pubertal growth spurt → ↓ strength-to-weight · breaks plantarflexion–knee-extension couple → very inefficient, tiring

**Stiff-knee gait** — **rectus femoris overactive in pre-swing + early swing** · EMG: quadriceps active from **terminal stance through swing** · compensation from hip deviation → surgery won't help\
✗ Wrong options: adductor magnus · gluteus medius · sartorius · TFL

**Descriptive classifications** — fail to classify **up to 40%** of patterns

![Sagittal gait patterns in spastic diplegia](../../MCQ/attachments/q4853-illa-illus-a-table-new.jpg)

### Key radiology findings & gait analysis

**Quantitative gait analysis** — **more accurate than observation** · physical exam (spasticity, contractures, torsion) · **kinetics** (forces, moments) · **kinematics** (3-plane motion) · **pedobarography** · **dynamic EMG** · video

**Planes** — sagittal: pelvic tilt, hip/knee/ankle flexion · coronal: pelvic obliquity, hip ab/adduction · **transverse = least reliable** (rotation, foot progression)

## 5 Management

### Equinus management – AFO, serial casting & gastrocnemius recession

| Orthosis | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Solid AFO** | Flexible equinus — ankle passively correctable to neutral (subtalar neutral) · stance + swing control · excessive midstance DF |
| **Posterior leaf-spring (PLSO) / hinged** | **Absent heel strike + excessive swing plantarflexion + some passive DF (~5°)** |
| **Floor-reaction AFO** | **Crouch** gait |
| **KAFO** | Knee instability in minimally ambulatory/standers |
| **SMO** | Flexible **coronal** hindfoot supination/pronation |

**3-year-old diplegic (PVL, walked at 2), mild contractures, gastroc tightness** → **ankle bracing + therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous/open TAL (surgery not indicated at this age) · steroids (dystrophy)

**Botulinum toxin A** — temporise / primary · **hamstring spasticity without fixed deformity** in ambulators

### Silfverskiöld + popliteal angle — choosing surgery

| DF knee extended | DF knee flexed | Popliteal angle | Procedure |
|---|---|---|---|
| **−5°** | **+20°** | **25° (normal)** | **Gastrocnemius recession only** ✓ |
| −5° | +20° | 55° | Gastroc recession **+ hamstring lengthening** |
| −10° | −10° | 25° | **TAL** (Achilles) |
| −20° | −20° | 55° | TAL + hamstring lengthening |
| +20° | +20° | 55° | Hamstring lengthening only |

![Silfverskiöld test](../../MCQ/attachments/q9027-illa-silfverskiold.jpg)

**Normal popliteal angle ~25°** · **>50°** = significant hamstring tightness

**Gastrocnemius recession** — **Silfverskiöld positive** · protect **sural nerve** (superficial to fascia) · divide tendon only · goal **10° DF**

**TAL** — rigid (not correctable to neutral), true equinus, Silfverskiöld negative · **contraindicated in spastic diplegia** → weakness, **calcaneus/crouch**

### Operative criteria — SEMLS

**SEMLS** — **gold standard** · correct all primary + secondary deviations at multiple levels in **one** operation (avoids iatrogenic worsening) · AFOs + aggressive PT · **~1 year recovery**

| Deviation | SEMLS procedure / criterion |
|---|---|
| ↑ Femoral anteversion (lever-arm) | **External rotation proximal femoral osteotomy** |
| Hip flexion contracture | **Intramuscular psoas lengthening** |
| Mild knee flexion (<5° fixed), young | **Medial hamstring fractional lengthening** (✗ Wrong options: lateral (excess weakness)) |
| Knee flexion **10–25°**, **≥2 yr growth** | **Guided growth** (anterior distal femur) |
| Knee flexion **10–30°** + quad lag, at/near maturity | **Distal femoral extension osteotomy** ± patellar tendon advancement |
| **Stiff-knee gait** | **Distal rectus femoris transfer** posterior to knee axis (better than release) |
| Equinus | TAL or gastrocnemius recession |
| Flatfoot | Reconstruction |

### Selective dorsal rhizotomy

**Permanent tone reduction** — **selective posterior (dorsal) rhizotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: tone-reducing AFOs · botox (wears off 3–6 mo via axonal sprouting) · single intrathecal baclofen injection (pump gives prolonged reduction) · fractional lengthening

**Rhizotomy drawbacks** — excess weakness · **hip dislocation** · **spinal deformity**

## 6 Complications

**Recurrent hamstring contracture** — esp. jump gait

**Worsening crouch** — isolated / over-lengthened **Achilles**

**Patella alta** — multiple releases **without gait analysis** · hard to treat

**Knee pain** — TAL without gait analysis

## X vs Y

| | **Jump gait** | **Crouch gait** |
|---|---|---|
| Ankle | **Equinus** | **Excess dorsiflexion / calcaneus** |
| Hip + knee | Flexed | Flexed |
| GRF at knee | Anterior (early) | **Posterior** |
| Cause | Spastic gastroc + hamstrings + psoas | **Isolated TAL**, growth spurt weakness |
| Orthosis | — | **Floor-reaction AFO** |

## How it's asked

**"Crouch gait combination?"** → **hip flexion, knee flexion, ankle dorsiflexion**

**"Stiff-knee gait in spastic diplegia — overactive muscle?"** → **rectus femoris**

**"Equinus, knee + hip flexion contractures, anterior pelvic tilt?"** → **jump gait**\
✗ Wrong options: crouch (calcaneus) · apparent equinus

**"Posterior leaf-spring AFO appropriate for?"** → **absent heel strike, swing plantarflexion, 5° passive DF**

**"3-year-old hemiplegic, AFO not fitting, DF −10° after botox?"** → **serial casting**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat botox <3 mo · baclofen pump · heel-cord lengthening

**"3-year-old diplegic toe-walker, mild contractures?"** → **ankle bracing and therapy**

**"Hemiplegic, ONLY gastrocnemius recession needed?"** → **DF −5° extended, +20° flexed, popliteal angle 25°**

**"Milestone at 12–18 months?"** → **walks without assistance**

**"Good prognostic sign for walking at 22 months?"** → **positive parachute reaction**

**"Permanent decrease in tone?"** → **selective posterior rhizotomy**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cerebral Palsy - General

*Pediatrics · 16 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Definition** — **non-progressive UMN disease** (static encephalopathy) from **injury to immature brain** · onset **before age 2** · musculoskeletal picture **worsens with growth** (bone grows faster than spastic muscle)

**Incidence** — **2–3 per 1000** · **most common cause of chronic childhood disability**

**Risk factors** — **prematurity (most common)** (Orthobullets) · **prenatal causes most common (70%)** (TO) — TORCH, toxins, Rh (kernicterus), malformations · perinatal: anoxia, traumatic delivery, chorioamnionitis · postnatal: meningitis, encephalitis, HIE, **NAT**

**Brain MRI** — **periventricular leukomalacia (PVL) most frequent (56%)** (preterm, spastic diplegia) · grey matter 18% · malformations 9%

**Manifestations** — primary: abnormal tone, **spasticity**, loss of motor control, impaired balance · secondary: **contractures** (dynamic → static), hip subluxation, spine deformity, foot deformity, gait disorders, **fractures**

### Classification & ambulation prognosis

| Physiologic | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Spastic** | **Most common (80%)** · velocity-dependent ↑ tone, hyperreflexia, clonus, Babinski · agonist-antagonist co-contraction · **most amenable to surgery** |
| Athetoid (dyskinetic) | Slow writhing involuntary movements · normal reflexes · contractures uncommon |
| Choreiform | Continuous purposeless distal movements |
| Rigid | Lead-pipe/cogwheel, no hyperreflexia · most hypertonic |
| Ataxic | Cerebellar · wide-based, drunken gait, tremor |
| Hypotonic | Precedes spastic/ataxic for 2–3 yr |
| Mixed | Spastic + athetoid, total body |

| Anatomic (geographic) | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Diplegia** | **Legs > arms** · usually **ambulatory** · near-normal IQ · **most common** (TO) |
| **Hemiplegia** | One side, **arm > leg** · ~50% mentally retarded (TO) |
| **Quadriplegia** | All four, **nonambulatory**, low IQ, ↑ mortality |
| Paraplegia | Legs only, arms spared |
| Double hemiplegia | All four, **arms > legs** |
| Monoplegia | One limb (rare) |
| Total body | + absent head control, drooling, dysphagia |

**Lower > upper limb** → **diplegia**\
✗ Wrong options: paraplegia (arms spared) · double hemiplegia (arms > legs)

### GMFCS (best motor-function score)

| Level | Ability (age 6–12) |
|---|---|
| **I** | Walks without limits · runs, jumps · stairs without rail |
| **II** | Walks with limits — difficulty uneven ground, inclines, long distances · stairs with rail · minimal run/jump |
| **III** | **Walks with handheld mobility device** (walker, crutches) · wheeled mobility for community/long distance |
| **IV** | Self-mobility limited · **powered mobility** / pushed wheelchair |
| **V** | **Transported in manual wheelchair** · dependent in all care |

**Best score for motor function** — **GMFCS** · EDACS = eating/drinking\
✗ Wrong options: MACS (upper limb/hands) · CFCS (communication) · PEDI

**Motor plateau** — levels I–II by ~5 yr · III by ~8 · IV–V by ~7, may decline in adolescence

### Ambulation prognosis

**Most reliable predictor** — **independent sitting by age 2** (OR 26:1)\
✗ Wrong options: creeping · rolling · CP pattern · motor type

**Poor prognosis** — no sitting by **4 yr** · not walking by **8 yr** (without severe contracture) → unlikely ever

**Bleck poor signs** — asymmetric tonic neck reflex · **persistent Moro** · **strong extensor thrust** on vertical suspension · **persistent neck-righting reflex** · **absent parachute reaction after 11 months** → "all EXCEPT" = **presence of parachute reaction** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — perinatal, development, function (sitting, standing, hands, communication, hearing, vision)

**Spine** — scoliosis flexibility, pelvic obliquity · **hamstring contracture → ↓ lordosis** · **hip flexion contracture → ↑ lordosis**

**Hip** — flexion + **adduction** contractures · dislocation may be missed as "contracture" → thigh length sitting, Galeazzi

**Foot** — equinovarus, planovalgus · toe walking, knee hyperextension for heel contact · Silfverskiöld

![Equinus feet in CP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4084-equinus-feet.jpg)

### Foot & knee deformity patterns (recurvatum, foot deformities)

**Common CP foot** — **equinocavovarus (hemiplegia)** · **equinoplanovalgus (diplegia in crouch)** · **hallux valgus** · **isolated cavus is NOT typical** → think **spinal dysraphism / CMT** (TO)

**Knee recurvatum causes (TO)** — quad–hamstring **co-spasticity** (quads stronger) · **weakened hamstrings** (over-lengthening/transfer) · gastrocsoleus weakness · **ankle equinus** · **quadriceps weakness** is NOT a cause

**Recurvatum work-up** — **prone rectus test** (quad spasticity) · SLC/AFO at neutral: still recurvatum → not from equinus · treat with long-leg braces locked in 20° flexion

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Baseline** — **AP + lateral hips** · **standing spine** films

### Hip surveillance (TO)

**Reimers migration percentage (MP)** — % of femoral head lateral to **Perkin line** (Hilgenreiner line through triradiates) · supine, hips together, patellae forward

| MP | Meaning |
|---|---|
| <30% | Stable — monitor |
| **>30%** (surveillance) / **>33%** (Reimers) | **Displacement / subluxation** |
| **>60%** | High risk of dislocation · **soft tissue alone fails** |
| **>100%** | **Dislocation** |
| Rise **>10%/yr** | Red flag — rapid progression |

| GMFCS | Hip displacement risk | X-ray frequency |
|---|---|---|
| I | ~0–5% (≈1%) | Age 2, then if gait changes |
| II | ~15% | Age 2, then 2-yearly to 10 |
| III | ~35% | Age 2, then yearly to 10 |
| IV | ~70% | Age 2, then 6–12-monthly |
| **V** | **~90%** | Age 2, then **6-monthly** |

**Clinical red flags** — passive **abduction <30°** (knee extended) · new pain with dressing/hygiene/sitting · new LLD

**Hip at risk** — flexion contracture **>20°** + abduction **<30°** · ↑ neck-shaft angle (**coxa valga**) · ↑ **femoral anteversion** · acetabular dysplasia (**posterosuperior** in CP vs anterolateral in DDH)

**Dislocation by type** — **70–90% occur in quadriplegia**

## 5 Management

### Spasticity management – botulinum toxin, baclofen (withdrawal) & SDR contraindications

| Agent | Mechanism | Use / note |
|---|---|---|
| **Botulinum toxin A** | Binds **motor nerve terminal** → enters → cleaves **SNARE** → **blocks presynaptic ACh release** (flaccid chemical denervation) | **Dynamic** contracture, too young for surgery · little benefit in **static** contracture · lasts **2–3 mo** (Orthobullets) / **3–4 mo** (OB) / 3–6 mo (OB) → reverses by axonal sprouting · inject belly near nerve entry |
| **Baclofen** | **GABA-B agonist** (spinal cord, central) | **Intrathecal** preferred (oral → sedation, poor bioavailability) · **nonambulatory GMFCS IV–V** |
| Lidocaine | Na⁺ channel block | — |
| Acetylcholinesterase | Degrades ACh | — |
| Pyridostigmine / neostigmine | AChE inhibitors → ↑ ACh | — |
| Trihexyphenidyl | Muscarinic antagonist | — |
| Gabapentin | Ca²⁺ channel | — |
| Methacholine | Direct cholinergic agonist | — |

**Botox distractors** — ✗ Wrong options: destroys ACh receptor (myasthenia gravis) · binds muscle receptor (curare, succinylcholine)

**Baclofen withdrawal (pump failure)** — rebound rigidity, myoclonus, **hyperthermia**, **rhabdomyolysis** → **↑ creatinine**, **↑ K⁺**, **↑ CPK**, multi-organ failure\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ K⁺ · ↓ CPK · hypothermia · atony

**Spasticity grading (TO)** — **R1** = angle of muscle catch · **R2** = passive ROM · **R2 − R1 >20°** = significant **dynamic** component → **botox**

**Phelps test** — hip abduction ↓ only with knee extended → **gracilis** spasticity

**5-yr GMFCS III diplegic, jump gait, DF R1 −10/R2 +20, popliteal R1 80/R2 40, abduction 45° flexed / 20° extended** → **botox to gastrocnemius + gracilis + semitendinosus + semimembranosus** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: surgery · adductor release (abduction already 45°)

### Selective dorsal rhizotomy

**Procedure** — section **L2–S1 dorsal rootlets** with abnormal response to stimulation

**Ideal candidate** — **spastic diplegia**, ambulatory, **age 4–8** (Orthobullets, OB) / **3–8** (TO), GMFCS II–III, voluntary + trunk control, pure spasticity, **no fixed contractures**, preterm, motivated family · improves **one GMFCS level** (TO)

**Contraindications** — **athetosis** (unpredictable) · **nonambulatory spastic quadriplegia** (spinal deformity) · hemiplegia not recommended (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: "hemiplegics benefit most before 5"

**Complications** — **hip subluxation/dislocation** · lumbar **hyperlordosis** (esp. >60° preop) · scoliosis · spondylolysis/listhesis · weakness

**Athetoid 5-year-old — contraindicated?** → **SDR**\
✗ Wrong options: botox · UE tendon transfer · SEMLS · intrathecal baclofen

### Operative principles

| Muscle behaviour | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Continuously active** | **Tenotomy** (hip adductor) or **lengthening** (Achilles, hamstring) |
| **Out of phase** | **Tendon transfer** (rectus femoris, tibialis posterior) |

**Soft-tissue surgery** — age **3–5** with spasticity + voluntary control

**Bony surgery** — later childhood/adolescence

**SEMLS** — one event, gait-lab guided · simple lengthenings "uncover" other contractures and worsen gait

### Upper extremity surgery – voluntary control predicts outcome

**Voluntary motor control** — best predicts improvement after **wrist/hand tendon transfers**\
✗ Wrong options: botox · SDR · femoral/tibial derotation osteotomy

### Hip subluxation in GMFCS V – adductor release

```
CP hip by migration percentage
├─ MP 30–40%, dynamic, abduction <30° → adductor ± psoas release (preventive)
├─ MP >40–60%, coxa valga, anteversion → VDRO (bilateral) ± femoral shortening
│      └─ Acetabular index >25–30° → add Dega acetabuloplasty (posterosuperior)
└─ Painful, deformed head, GMFCS IV–V → salvage: McHale / proximal femoral resection
```

**Adductor release** — goal **>60° abduction hips flexed, 45° extended** · bilateral (avoid **windswept** pelvis) · then abduction splinting 6 mo · avoid excessive release in IV/V → abduction contracture

**Psoas** — flexion contracture **15–30° → intramuscular recession over pelvic brim** · **>30°** → add rectus, sartorius, TFL, anterior glutei release · ambulators: fractional at brim (complete lesser-trochanter release → flexor void, crouch)

**VDRO** — neck-shaft angle **110–115°** · correct anteversion · **shorten 1–3 cm** if tight (↓ AVN, sciatic tension)\
✗ Wrong options: 105° → abductor weakness, Trendelenburg

**Acetabuloplasty** — **Dega / San Diego** (incomplete trans-iliac, hinge on triradiate) → **deeper posterior graft** for **posterosuperior** coverage · Pemberton = volume-reducing, open triradiate\
✗ Wrong options: Salter (anterolateral redirection, leaves posterior uncovered) · extra varus · isolated psoas tenotomy

**GMFCS V, painful sitting, hygiene problems, coxa valga + unilateral dislocation, failed therapy** → **bilateral adductor release + bilateral VDRO ± pelvic osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · soft tissue alone (MP >60% fails) · shortening alone · unilateral VDRO (asymmetry)

**5-yr GMFCS IV, MP 18% → 31% in 1 yr, abduction 25°** → **6-monthly X-rays + refer for preventive soft-tissue surgery** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: annual surveillance · VDRO now · discharge at 5

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Choice |
|---|---|
| **Open reduction of dislocated hip** — all 4 | Moderately mature intellect · at least **sitting potential** · minimal/corrected **pelvic obliquity** · ideally **unilateral** |
| **GMFCS IV, MP 100%, deformed head** | **Femoral head resection** (McHale: resection + subtrochanteric valgus osteotomy + interposition) over reconstruction (TO) |
| Schanz osteotomy | Pelvic-support for sitting platform |
| THA | Selected higher-function painful arthritic hips |
| Hamstring lengthening | Popliteal angle **>45–50°** · **medial** preferred (lateral → ↑ ext. tibial torsion, lordosis) |
| **DFEO — "rule of 15"** | Fixed knee flexion **>15°** (TO) · guided growth/soft tissue below |
| Guided growth (anterior 8-plate) | FKFD with growth left (<11–12 yr) |

### Crouch gait & knee surgery (DFEO, patellar advancement)

**PFKE couple** — **plantarflexion–knee extension couple**: competent gastrocsoleus stops tibia falling forward · failure = **iatrogenic over-lengthened Achilles / triceps surae weakness** → crouch (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: rectus spasticity · psoas contracture · hamstring shortening

**Apparent equinus** (toe-walking, ankle neutral, knee 35° + hip 20° flexion, **patella alta** Insall-Salvati 1.4) → **DFEO + patellar tendon advancement** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: TAL (causes severe crouch) · isolated hamstring lengthening · gastroc recession + AFO

**Patellar tendon advancement/plication** — **mandatory with DFEO** in chronic crouch to restore quad lever arm (TO)

**Post-DFEO anterior knee pain / extensor shift** — **unaddressed patella alta** with shortened femur (TO)

**GRAFO (floor-reaction AFO)** — shifts GRF **anterior to knee** → **knee extension moment** (TO)

**Hamstring activity throughout stance on EMG** → **hamstring lengthening likely to fail** (TO)

**Post-op** — early weight bearing · night knee immobilisers for years

### Stiff-knee gait – rectus femoris transfer

**Rectus femoris transfer** — distal rectus → gracilis/semitendinosus · **removes swing-phase restraint → ↑ peak knee flexion** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ extension moment · pure hip extensor

**Stiff knee + internal rotation in swing + anteversion (hemiplegia Winters IV, double-bump rectus EMG)** → **femoral derotation osteotomy + rectus femoris transfer** (TO)

### Equinus – gastrocnemius recession & lengthening zones

| Zone | Procedure | Structures |
|---|---|---|
| **Zone 1** | **Strayer** (transverse, above junction) | **Isolated gastrocnemius** — safest vs crouch |
| Zone 1 | Baker (tongue-in-groove) | Gastroc + partial soleus |
| Zone 2 | **Vulpius** (inverted V at MTJ) | Gastroc + often soleus fascia |
| **Zone 3** | **Hoke triple hemisection / TAL** | **Tendo-Achilles** (TO) |
| Intramuscular | Baumann | Multiple deep gastroc recessions |

![Gastrosoleus lengthening zones](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17138290-1.jpg)

**Diplegia** → usually **isolated gastroc** contracture · **hemiplegia** → **gastrosoleus** tight (TO)

**8-yr diplegic, −10° knee extended, +15° knee flexed** → **Strayer** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous TAL (crouch) · Vulpius (weakens soleus) · SPLATT (varus)

### Neuromuscular scoliosis surgery – evaluation, outcomes & complications

![Neuromuscular scoliosis in CP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4084-cp-spine.jpg)

**Pre-op work-up** — nutrition (weight) · pulmonary (PFT/sleep study/clinical) · swallow + **GERD** (aspiration) · **valproate** may impair coagulation (↓ vWF) · **MRI NOT routine** (only if neuro change or rapid progression)

**Most predictable outcome** — **↑ caregiver satisfaction** (perceived comfort)\
✗ Wrong options: cognition · nutrition · pain · mobility

**Respiratory complication risk factor** — **gastrostomy tube** · resp. complications **15–28%** · NMS has highest complication rate (congenital 10.6%, idiopathic 6.3%)\
✗ Wrong options: lymphocytes <1000 · albumin <3.5 · reactive airway · Hb <8

**Wound drainage 3 weeks after T2–pelvis fusion** → **culture + I&D + IV antibiotics + retain instrumentation**\
✗ Wrong options: oral abx · aspiration · hardware removal → rapid progression

### Bone health – bisphosphonates for fragility fractures

**Fragility fractures** — **non-ambulators**, low BMD (immobility, nutrition, **anticonvulsants**, contractures) · prevalence 6–50% · annual 4% → **7% after a fracture**

**IV pamidronate** — **>3 fractures + DEXA Z-score <−2 SD** (Orthobullets) · prevents **refracture**\
✗ Wrong options: LLD · nonunion · malunion · contracture

**Mechanism** — **nitrogen-containing** (pamidronate, alendronate) inhibit **farnesyl pyrophosphate synthase** (mevalonate pathway) → osteoclast apoptosis · non-N (clodronate, etidronate) → toxic ATP analogues

**CROW walker** — **Charcot foot** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: CP equinus · polio · spina bifida

## X vs Y

| | **Botulinum toxin A** | **Baclofen** | **SDR** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Peripheral NMJ, presynaptic | Central spinal GABA-B | Dorsal rootlets L2–S1 |
| Duration | Temporary (months) | Continuous via pump | **Permanent** |
| Best for | Dynamic, focal, young | **Nonambulatory GMFCS IV–V** | **Ambulatory spastic diplegia 4–8** |
| Avoid | Static contracture | Abrupt withdrawal | **Athetosis**, quadriplegia |

| | **CP hip** | **DDH** |
|---|---|---|
| Deficiency | **Posterosuperior** | Anterolateral |
| Pelvic osteotomy | **Dega / San Diego** | Salter, Pemberton |
| Driver | Spastic adductors + psoas | Capsular laxity |

## How it's asked

**"Poor prognostic signs for walking — all EXCEPT?"** → **presence of parachute reaction after 11 months** (TO)

**"GMFCS IV at 6–12 years?"** → **powered mobility** (TO)

**"Lower limbs more affected than upper?"** → **diplegia** (TO)

**"Best score for motor function in CP?"** → **GMFCS** (TO)

**"7-year-old: walks level surfaces, rail on stairs, device for uneven/community, can't run?"** → **GMFCS III** (TO)

**"6-year-old, mild 4-limb spasticity, legs > arms, hip dysplasia?"** → **diplegia**

**"Most prognostic for walking?"** → **independent sitting by age 2**

**"SDR — all true EXCEPT?"** → **"spastic hemiplegics benefit most before 5"** (TO)

**"Botulinum toxin A action?"** → **binds motor terminal receptors, prevents ACh release** (TO)

**"Jump gait GMFCS III, R2−R1 >20°, Phelps positive?"** → **botox gastrocnemius + gracilis + medial hamstrings** (TO)

**"Athetoid diplegic — contraindicated?"** → **selective dorsal rhizotomy**

**"Decreases synaptic ACh by blocking presynaptic release?"** → **botulinum toxin A**\
✗ Wrong options: lidocaine · AChE · baclofen · pyridostigmine

**"Inhibits presynaptic vesicle docking (SNARE)?"** → **botulinum toxin A**\
✗ Wrong options: trihexyphenidyl · gabapentin · neostigmine

**"Baclofen pump failure sign?"** → **elevated serum creatinine**

**"Serial casting + drug blocking presynaptic ACh release?"** → **botulinum toxin A**\
✗ Wrong options: methacholine

**"Voluntary control predicts improvement after?"** → **wrist/hand tendon transfer**

**"Femoral head resection over reconstruction when?"** → **GMFCS IV, MP 100%, deformed head** (TO)

**"GMFCS IV, MP 18% → 31% in a year?"** → **6-monthly films + refer for soft-tissue surgery** (TO)

**"After VDRO head still uncovered posterosuperiorly?"** → **Dega acetabuloplasty, deeper posteriorly** (TO)

**"GMFCS V, painful sitting, dislocated hip?"** → **bilateral adductor release + bilateral VDRO ± pelvic osteotomy**

**"Pre-op CP scoliosis — EXCEPT?"** → **routine spinal MRI**

**"Infection 3 weeks after fusion?"** → **I&D, cultures, IV antibiotics, retain hardware**

**"Most predictable outcome of CP scoliosis fusion?"** → **improved caregiver satisfaction**

**"Post-op pneumonia risk factor?"** → **gastrostomy tube**

**"Bisphosphonate after CP fragility fracture prevents?"** → **refracture**

**"Apparent equinus, knee 35° flexion, patella alta?"** → **DFEO + patellar tendon advancement** (TO)

**"With supracondylar extension osteotomy, mandatory add-on?"** → **patellar tendon distalisation/plication** (TO)

**"Crouch with failed PFKE couple — cause?"** → **over-lengthened Achilles / triceps surae weakness** (TO)

**"Restore knee extension moment arm in crouch with FKFD?"** → **DFEO + patellar advancement** (TO)

**"Anterior knee pain after DFEO?"** → **patella alta not addressed** (TO)

**"GRAFO function?"** → **knee extension moment via floor reaction** (TO)

**"Hamstring EMG active through stance?"** → **hamstring lengthening likely to fail** (TO)

**"Rule of 15?"** → **DFEO when FKFD >15°** (TO)

**"Stiff knee + IR in swing + anteversion?"** → **FDRO + rectus femoris transfer** (TO)

**"Rectus femoris transfer objective?"** → **↑ peak knee flexion in swing** (TO)

**"Hoke lengthening — zone?"** → **zone 3** (TO)

**"Diplegic dynamic equinus, Silfverskiöld positive, avoid crouch?"** → **Strayer** (TO)

**"Foot deformity NOT common in CP?"** → **cavus** (TO)

**"Knee recurvatum NOT caused by?"** → **quadriceps weakness** (TO)

**"CROW walker suits?"** → **Charcot foot** (TO)

# Cerebral Palsy - Hip Conditions

*Pediatrics · 17 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Hip surveillance – risk by GMFCS level & pathogenesis

**Incidence** — progressive subluxation in up to **50% of spastic quadriparesis** · rare in ambulators

| GMFCS | Risk of subluxation **by age 7** (Orthobullets, OB) | Hip displacement risk (TO surveillance table) |
|---|---|---|
| I | **<10%** | ~0–5% |
| II | **10–15%** | ~15% |
| **III** (walks with assistive devices) | **30–40%** | ~35% |
| IV | **50–60%** | ~70% |
| **V** | **70–80%** | **~90%** |

**Pathogenesis** — hips **normal at birth** · spastic **adductors (longus, brevis, magnus, gracilis) + iliopsoas (psoas major, iliacus)** → scissoring → coxa valga, anteversion, acetabular dysplasia → subluxation → dislocation\
✗ Wrong options: sartorius · gluteus minimus · obturator externus · piriformis · rectus femoris

**Direction** — dislocation **posterior + superior (>95%)** · acetabular deficiency **posterosuperior**

**Degeneration** — femoral head cartilage erosion → **pain** · **50–75%** of dislocated hips become painful (OB) · pain by **2nd–3rd decade**

**Windswept hips** — one hip **abducted**, other **adducted** → pelvic obliquity · **adducted hip on high side** at risk of dislocation · infrapelvic obliquity → compensatory **scoliosis**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — hip/groin pain · **difficulty sitting** · **perineal care / hygiene / diaper** difficulty

**Exam** — unreliable alone · ↓ ROM, pain with motion · lever-arm gait dysfunction · thigh-length discrepancy ("**pseudo-Galeazzi**")

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP pelvis** + frog lateral if possible

**Reimers migration index** — % of femoral head **uncovered** lateral to Perkin line · **most accurate method** to identify and monitor stability · **<33% at risk · >33% subluxated · >100% dislocated**

**Natural history** — hips **will dislocate** untreated if MI **>60–70%**

**Acetabular index** — **>25°** (OB) / >27° (OB) → acetabular dysplasia → **add acetabuloplasty**

**CT** — **operative planning** (3D acetabular correction) · CT scanogram for femoral version before derotation

![Reimers migration index](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4130-reimers.jpg)

### Stages of hip deformity

| Stage | Characteristics | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Hip at risk** | Abduction **<45°** + partial uncovering · **MI <33%** | **Botox (<3 yr)** to delay · **adductor ± psoas release, hamstring lengthening (3–4 yr)** · **avoid obturator neurectomy** |
| **Subluxation** | **MI >33%** | Adductor tenotomy if abduction restricted · persistent → **VDRO (5–6 yr)** · pelvic osteotomy if acetabular insufficiency |
| **Spastic dislocation** | **MI >100%** | **Open reduction + VDRO + femoral shortening + pelvic osteotomy** |
| **Windswept** | One abducted, one adducted | Brace adducted hip ± tenotomy · release abduction contracture of abducted hip |

![Spastic hip dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4130-dislocation.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Nonop** — observation (mild) · **PT never shown to prevent subluxation** · **abduction bracing alone** does not ↓ dislocation, may cause **windswept** deformity

```
CP hip
├─ Young (<4–6 yr), MI 25/33–60%, limited abduction → adductor ± psoas release
├─ >4 yr or MI >60% (or >40% failing soft tissue) → VDRO + soft-tissue release
│      └─ Acetabular index >25° → + pelvic (Dega / San Diego) osteotomy, single stage
└─ Painful, chronically dislocated, deformed head (GMFCS V) → salvage
       └─ McHale valgus-support osteotomy · Castle resection-interposition
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Adductor + psoas release** | Age **<6** with MI **>25/33–60%** + limited abduction (Orthobullets) · **<4** with MI **>40%** (OB) · progressive subluxation **<8 yr** · adjunct to bony surgery |
| **Psoas lengthening** | Only with **hip flexion contracture (positive Thomas)** |
| **VDRO ± Dega + soft tissue** | Age **>4** or MI **>60%** · treat all at **single stage** |
| **Add acetabuloplasty** | **Acetabular index >25°** (✗ Wrong options: age <6 · GMFCS V · neck-shaft angle (VDRO fixes that) · prior botox) |
| **Proximal femoral resection / McHale** | Painful, chronically dislocated, **deformed head**, nonambulatory (GMFCS V) |
| **Castle** | Chronic dislocation, **adults**, can't stand to transfer (GMFCS V) |
| **THA** | Ambulatory or can **stand to transfer** · 85% 10-yr survival |
| Hip arthrodesis | Young patients |
| Girdlestone | **No longer done** — uniformly painful (proximal migration, no interposition) |

### Soft-tissue releases – adductor tenotomy indications & failure risk, obturator neurectomy

**3-yr diplegic, abduction 20°, MI 20%, Thomas negative** → **bilateral adductor releases only** · one of few surgeries in CP <4 yr\
✗ Wrong options: psoas (no flexion contracture) · VDRO · pelvic osteotomy

**Technique** — adductor longus tenotomy → sequentially **gracilis**, **adductor brevis** · psoas at **lesser trochanter (nonambulatory)** or **pelvic brim MTJ (ambulatory)** · goal **>45° abduction**

**Failure risk** — **preop MI >40%** · acetabular index **>27°**\
✗ Wrong options: lack of bracing · no neurectomy · age

**Obturator nerve** — anterior branch lies on **adductor brevis/magnus** after longus release (Ludloff) · supplies **adductor longus, brevis, gracilis** · **pinch neurectomy → weak hip adduction** · neurectomy **does not improve** results and risks **abduction contracture** — avoid (Orthobullets, OB) · sometimes used in nonambulatory spastic adduction (OB)

**6-yr nonambulatory quadriplegic, subluxation >50%, abduction 20°** → **open adductor tenotomy + iliopsoas release + bilateral VDRO** (too old for soft tissue alone)\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous tenotomy · oral baclofen · phenol · neurectomy

### Reconstruction – VDRO with pelvic (Dega) osteotomy

**VDRO** — shortening varus derotation → corrects **coxa valga + anteversion**

**Dega** — acetabular reshaping, incomplete trans-iliac · **improves posterior coverage** (neuromuscular hip needs posterior) · hinges on triradiate → **triradiate must be open** · bone graft placed · no fixation needed · **San Diego** = alternative\
✗ Wrong options: salvage (shelf/Chiari are salvage)

**Bilateral MI 70%/60%, AI 30°** → **combined bilateral VDRO + pelvic osteotomies + soft-tissue releases** — lowest resubluxation\
✗ Wrong options: soft tissue alone · VDRO alone · pelvic alone · closed reduction

**8-yr wheelchair-bound, hygiene problems, abduction 5°/15°, bilateral dislocation + dysplasia** → **bilateral adductor release + varus femoral osteotomies + acetabuloplasties**\
✗ Wrong options: botox + PT · Pavlik · abductor release + valgus osteotomy

### Salvage – proximal femoral resection

**GMFCS V** — no antigravity head/trunk control, transported

**15-yr GMFCS V, painful sitting, flattened head on CT** → **proximal femoral resection**\
✗ Wrong options: open reduction (degenerate head stays painful)

**Resection over reconstruction** → **GMFCS V, MI 100% + head deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: GMFCS IV MI 50% (reconstruct) · MI 25% (analgesia) · MI 100% normal head (osteotomy)

**McHale (valgus support)** — resect head/neck (ligamentum teres left, sutured to psoas/capsule) + **subtrochanteric valgus closing-wedge osteotomy** → **lesser trochanter articulates with acetabulum** · relieves pain, ↑ abduction

**Castle** — resect at **lesser trochanter** level · **vastus oversewn** over femur, abductors/psoas/capsule over acetabulum → large soft-tissue interposition (differs from Girdlestone)

![McHale procedure](../../MCQ/attachments/q4561-illb-cp-mchale-procedure.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Osteonecrosis** — **1–11%**

**Heterotopic ossification** — **radiation on POD 2–3** better than NSAIDs

**Insufficiency fractures** — **4–29%** · **distal femur after spica**

**Abduction contracture** — **anterior obturator neurectomy**

## X vs Y

| | **Spastic (CP) hip** | **DDH** |
|---|---|---|
| At birth | **Normal** | Abnormal |
| Recognised | **After age 2** | 1st year |
| Detection | **Radiographs** | Exam |
| Cause | Spastic muscles, pelvic obliquity | Breech, laxity, acetabular growth |
| Progression | **Common** | Rare |
| Pain | **2nd–3rd decade** | 4th–5th decade |
| Deficiency | **Posterosuperior** | Anterior |
| Early Rx | **Muscle lengthening** | Pavlik / closed reduction |
| Late Rx | **Osteotomies, often without open reduction** | Reduction, often without osteotomy (<18 mo) |
| Salvage | **Castle resection-interposition** | THA |

## How it's asked

**"GMFCS level with 30–40% risk of subluxation by age 7?"** → **level III (walks with assistive devices)**

**"GMFCS V dislocated hip — responsible muscles?"** → **gracilis and psoas major**

**"After adductor tenotomy, predictor of failure?"** → **preop MI >40%**\
✗ Wrong options: AI <27° · no bracing · no neurectomy · age

**"8-yr wheelchair-bound, hygiene problems, bilateral dysplastic dislocated hips?"** → **bilateral adductor release + varus osteotomies + acetabuloplasties**

**"6-yr quadriplegic, subluxation >50%, abduction 20°?"** → **open adductor tenotomy + iliopsoas release + bilateral VDRO**

**"3-yr diplegic, abduction 20°, MI 20%, negative Thomas?"** → **bilateral adductor releases**

**"Pinch neurectomy during medial approach weakens?"** → **hip adduction**

**"Primary benefit of Dega in CP?"** → **posterior coverage**

**"MI 70%/60%, AI 30° — least resubluxation?"** → **bilateral VDRO + pelvic osteotomies + soft-tissue release**

**"Why add acetabuloplasty to VDRO?"** → **acetabular index >25°**

**"15-yr nonambulatory, painful sitting, flattened head?"** → **GMFCS V — proximal femoral resection**

**"Femoral head resection rather than reconstruction?"** → **17-yr GMFCS V, MI 100%, head deformity**

# Cerebral Palsy - Upper Extremity Disorders

*Pediatrics · 18 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Who** — **hemiplegia** and **quadriplegia**

**Typical posture** — **shoulder IR · elbow flexion · forearm pronation · wrist flexion + ulnar deviation · thumb-in-palm** · finger flexion

**Wrist flexion cause** — weak wrist extensors · spastic wrist flexors · volar capsule contracture → weak grip, hygiene problems

### Hygienic vs functional procedures

| | **Hygienic** | **Functional** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | ↓ mental + physical function | **Voluntary control**, **IQ ≥50–70**, **good sensibility** (position sense without vision) |
| Examples | Releases, **wrist arthrodesis + PRC** | **Tendon transfers** (FCU → ECRB), PT rerouting |

**Voluntary control** — best predictor of improvement after **hand/wrist tendon transfer**

## 3 Presentation

### Volkmann angle

**Technique** — **fingers extended**, wrist from maximal flexion → extend wrist · angle short of full extension = **Volkmann angle**\
✗ Wrong options: fingers flexed (must stretch extrinsic flexors)

**Meaning** — high Volkmann angle → **extrinsic finger flexor contracture** → correcting wrist alone gives **clenched fist** unless finger flexors also lengthened

![Measuring Volkmann angle](../../MCQ/attachments/q219551-illc-illustration-c.jpg)

| Volkmann angle | Severity / treatment |
|---|---|
| **~15°** | Mild → **FCU or FCR lengthening** |
| **~25°**, correctable wrist | **FCU + FDS/FDP tenotomy + ECU → ECRB transfer** |
| **Cannot reach within 45° of neutral** | Severe → **wrist arthrodesis + PRC** |
| **90° + palmar wounds** (fingers-in-palm) | **PRC + wrist fusion + FDS → FDP** |

**Distractors** — locked IR shoulder + extended elbow = **arthrogryposis** · unstable thumb CMC = **Blauth IIIB hypoplasia** (pollicise) · constricting bands = constriction band syndrome

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### House classification (thumb-in-palm)

| Type | Deformity | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **1st MC adduction** contracture | Adductor release ± 1st dorsal interosseous release · **Z-plasty** 1st web |
| **II** | + **MP flexion** contracture | Adductor release |
| **III** | + **unstable / hyperextensible MP** | Adductor release + **MP fusion or capsulodesis** |
| **IV** | + **MP + IP flexion** contractures | Adductor release + **FPB + FPL release/lengthening** |

![Thumb-in-palm deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4129-thumb-in-palm.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Deformity | Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|---|
| **Shoulder IR** | Derotational humeral osteotomy and/or subscapularis + pectoralis lengthening | **>30°** contracture interfering with hand function |
| **Elbow flexion** | **Lacertus fibrosus release**, biceps + brachialis lengthening, brachioradialis origin release | Elbow flexion contracture |
| **Forearm pronation** | **Pronator teres release / rerouting** to anterolateral (makes it a **supinator**) · or FCU → ECRB | Pronation deformity · risk: **supination deformity** (worse than pronation) |
| **Wrist flexion, mild** | **FCU or FCR lengthening** | Good finger extension, little wrist-flexor spasticity |
| **Wrist flexion, functional** | **FCU → ECRB (Green)** · FCU → EDC | Supple wrist, voluntary control, IQ ≥50–70, sensibility · **better long-term than botox/therapy** |
| Wrist flexion, spastic FCU | **ECU → ECRB** | Avoids hyperextension from transferring spastic FCU |
| Wrist flexor weakening | **Flexor-pronator slide** (from medial epicondyle) | Severe fixed wrist + finger flexion (also post-compartment syndrome) |
| **Fixed, non-supple** | **Wrist arthrodesis ± PRC** | No volitional control · hygiene + function |
| Swan neck | Correct wrist flexion first | Intrinsic tightness + extensor overpull |

```
CP wrist flexion deformity
├─ Supple, voluntary control, functioning FCU
│      ├─ Good grasp → FCU to EDC
│      └─ Poor grasp / weak extensors → FCU to ECRB (Green)
├─ Supple but finger-flexor tightness (Volkmann 25°) → FCU + FDS/FDP tenotomy + ECU to ECRB
└─ Rigid, no volitional control, palmar wounds → PRC + wrist arthrodesis (dorsal plate)
```

### Wrist flexion deformity – tendon transfers vs arthrodesis (Volkmann angle, FCU to ECRB, pronator teres rerouting)

**Green transfer (flexor carpi ulnaris → extensor carpi radialis brevis, FCU → ECRB)** — supple wrist, **weak/absent wrist extensors**, resting ulnar deviation · improves **extension + supination** · **most effective for restoring supination** · not if no functioning **FCR** or **rigid wrist**

**Pronator teres rerouting** — converts PT to **supinator** · very functional patient with **no active but full passive supination** + good sensibility → **PT transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: anconeus, brachialis, brachioradialis, supinator

**PT to ECRB** — powers wrist extension when wrist flexors can't be used

**Distractors** — ✗ Wrong options: PT → FCU (↑ pronation + ulnar deviation) · botox (inferior to transfer) · PT/brachioradialis rerouting (less effective than Green for supination)

**Wrist arthrodesis** — best for deformity **not passively correctable** · **with PRC** (↓ soft-tissue tension, provides graft) · dorsal plate · **union >95%**, high satisfaction\
✗ Wrong options: <75% union · higher failure in spastic CP · only after failed Green

## X vs Y

| | **FCU → ECRB (Green)** | **Wrist arthrodesis + PRC** |
|---|---|---|
| Wrist | **Supple** | **Rigid** |
| Control | **Voluntary**, good sensibility | Lacks volitional control |
| Goal | Function — extension + supination | Hygiene, position, fingers out of palm |
| Contraindication | Rigid wrist, no FCR | — |

## How it's asked

**"Exam missed before wrist fusion + PRC?"** → **fingers extended, wrist can't extend to within 45° of neutral (Volkmann)**

**"Functioning flexors, flexible wrist, absent extensors — best result?"** → **FCU to ECRB**

**"No active, full passive supination, good sensibility — transfer?"** → **pronator teres**

**"Best restoration of supination?"** → **FCU to ECRB (Green)**\
✗ Wrong options: PT → FCU · PT/BR rerouting · fusion

**"True of wrist arthrodesis?"** → **best when deformity not passively correctable**

**"Correctable wrist, Volkmann 25°, lasting correction?"** → **FCU + FDS/FDP tenotomy with ECU transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · botox · FCU tenotomy alone · fusion

**"Rigid wrist fused in neutral with dorsal scar — indication?"** → **Volkmann 90° with palmar wounds**\
✗ Wrong options: GMFCS V alone · supple ulnar deviation

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Charcot-Marie-Tooth Disease

*Pediatrics · 19 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1 in 2500** · no ethnic predisposition · **most common inherited neuromuscular disease** seen by orthopaedists

**Sex** — more common in **males**; females often worse (OB)

**Name** — **HMSN** (hereditary motor sensory neuropathy) · peroneal muscular atrophy · most common HMSN (others: Dejerine-Sottas)

### Genetics & pathophysiology (PMP22 duplication, myelin, autosomal dominant)

**Most common** — **autosomal dominant duplication on chromosome 17p12** → **PMP22** (peripheral myelin protein 22) expressed in **Schwann cells** · PMP duplication in **~70%** (OB)

**Structure affected** — **myelin sheath** (CMT1)\
✗ Wrong options: perineural vessels · cell body · dendrite · NMJ

**PMP22 deletion** (same region) → **HNPP** (hereditary neuropathy with liability to pressure palsies)

| Type | Inheritance | Pathology | NCV | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CMT1** | **AD** | **Demyelinating** | **Slowed** (<38 m/s) | **50%** (Orthobullets, OB) · 80% (OB) · 1A = PMP22 duplication · onset 1st–2nd decade · **cavus** foot · motor > sensory · normal lifespan |
| **CMT2** | AD (AR-CMT2 subtype) | **Axonal — Wallerian degeneration**, intact myelin | **Normal** (>45 m/s), ↓ CMAP/SNAP amplitudes | ~20% · onset 2nd decade+ · **less disabled** · flaccid foot |
| CMT4 | **AR** | Demyelinating | Slowed | — |
| CMTX | **X-linked** | — | — | — |

### Genes & lab markers

| Disorder | Defect (distractors) |
|---|---|
| **CMT** | **PMP22**, chr 17 |
| Friedreich ataxia | **GAA repeat**, chr 9 (frataxin) |
| Duchenne / Becker | **Dystrophin** (X-linked recessive) |
| Limb-girdle | Sarcoglycan / dystroglycan |
| Myotonic dystrophy | Myotonin |
| SMA | Survival motor neuron protein |
| Marfan | **FBN1**, chr 15 (planovalgus) |
| OI | Type I collagen |
| Hurler (MPS I) | α-L-iduronidase |
| MED | COMP |

**HMSN look-alikes** — ALS (UMN + LMN death) · Refsum (branched-chain fatty acid oxidation) · **Dejerine-Sottas** (severe, rapidly progressive, early) · Parkinson (midbrain)

### Deformity mechanism – plantar-flexed first ray (PL over TA) & PTT

| Strong (preserved) | Weak | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **Peroneus longus** | **Tibialis anterior** | **Plantarflexed 1st ray = initial deformity** (cavus) |
| **Tibialis posterior** | **Peroneus brevis** | **Hindfoot varus**, foot adduction |
| Long toe extensors (recruited for DF) | **Intrinsics** | **Claw toes** |

**Predominant deforming muscles** — **posterior tibialis + peroneus longus**

**Weakest first** — **peroneals** first + most profound (Orthobullets) · initial presentation = **weak tibialis anterior** (OB) → foot drop, later fixed equinus

**Sequence** — plantarflexed 1st MT + pronated forefoot → **secondary hindfoot varus** → later fixed + equinus

**Bilateral progressive cavovarus** → **HMSN/CMT** (78% of bilateral cavovarus)\
✗ Wrong options: DMD (equinovarus) · CP (equinus/equinovalgus) · myelomeningocele (clubfoot/CVT/calcaneus) · Werdnig-Hoffmann

## 3 Presentation

**Motor** — distal weakness + atrophy · **high-steppage gait** (foot drop) · tripping, falls · **chronic ankle instability / sprains** · **"inverted champagne bottle"** legs · lateral foot pain · lateral shoe wear

**Sensory** — stocking-glove loss · ↓ proprioception → imbalance · burning/numbness

**Foot** — **bilateral symmetric cavovarus** + claw/hammer toes · flexible → rigid

**Reflexes** — **hyporeflexia/areflexia** (absent ankle + knee jerks)

**Hand** — **intrinsic wasting** (1st dorsal interosseous) · **weak pinch** (grasp, dexterity spared)

**Spine** — scoliosis on Adams test

![Pes cavus in CMT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4086-pes-cavus.jpg)

**Coleman block** — corrects → **forefoot-driven** · doesn't → **hindfoot-driven/rigid**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis & imaging

**NCS/EMG** — classifies demyelinating vs axonal · **demyelinating cut-off 38 m/s** · axonal **>45 m/s** with ↓ CMAP/SNAP · **prolonged distal latencies** in peroneal, ulnar, median nerves

**Genetic testing** — **PCR for PMP22** · chr 17 duplication

**Radiograph** — **high-arched cavus foot** (↑ calcaneal pitch)\
✗ Wrong options: accessory navicular · CN coalition · ball-and-socket ankle (fibular hemimelia)

**Pathology** — simultaneous demyelination + remyelination ("onion bulb")

![CMT1 demyelination](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4086-image-showing-demyelination.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Orthoses – lateral forefoot & heel posting

**Flexible cavovarus** → **lateral forefoot + lateral heel posting** (forefoot post lets 1st MT flex; heel post corrects varus) · (✗ Wrong options: medial heel wedge (worsens) · medial flare · SACH) · metatarsal pad = pain relief only

**Lateral hindfoot posting** suits **flexible cavovarus**\
✗ Wrong options: accessory navicular · middle-facet coalition · flexible flatfoot · metatarsus adductus

**Other** — accommodative shoes (mild) · semi-rigid insole with 1st-ray recess + lateral wedge (flexible adult) · SMO · **AFO for foot drop** (best if equinus dynamic) · lace-up brace

**24-yr HMSN, corrects with Coleman, no arthritis** → **custom orthotic with lateral forefoot + heel posting** first\
✗ Wrong options: medial posting · osteotomies

### Surgery – flexible (PL to PB, first MT osteotomy, PTT transfer, calcaneal osteotomy) vs rigid (triple arthrodesis)

```
CMT cavovarus — failed orthoses
├─ Flexible (Coleman corrects) → plantar release + PL→PB + 1st MT DF osteotomy ± PTT to dorsum
├─ Partial / no correction → + lateralising calcaneal osteotomy (proximal translation)
└─ Rigid + arthritic (TC, CC) → triple arthrodesis
       (poor long-term in CMT — last resort)
```

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Plantar release** (fascia ± Steindler) | Cavus |
| **PL → PB transfer** | Plantarflexed 1st ray · ↓ 1st-ray PF, restores eversion |
| **1st MT dorsiflexion osteotomy** (or 1st MC dorsal closing wedge) | **Flexible hindfoot** (Coleman normal) |
| **PTT transfer through IOM to dorsum** (lateral foot) | **Flexible deformity, TA weak, PTT strong** → ↓ varus + gives DF · **improved outcomes** |
| Gastrocnemius recession / TAL | Equinus (gastroc recession less weakness, with plantar release) |
| **Lateralising calcaneal osteotomy** (± proximal translation) | **Hindfoot not correcting on Coleman** · proximal shift improves triceps lever arm |
| **Triple arthrodesis** | **Rigid + arthritic** hindfoot · **poor outcomes** in CMT (Wetmore & Drennan) |
| **Jones (EHL → 1st MT neck + IP fusion)** | Symptomatic claw hallux failing nonop |

**Distractors** — ✗ Wrong options: PB → PL (wrong direction) · SPLATT (TA weak) · TA → PB (TA weak) · PTT → TA tendon (out-of-phase; go **lateral**) · FDL transfer (PTT is the deforming force) · medializing calcaneal osteotomy (↑ varus) · LCL (flatfoot) · botox (weakens push-off) · ankle/subtalar fusion in supple young foot

**25-yr chr-17 duplication, progressive foot drop, supple joints, equinus, strong PF + inversion** → **PTT transfer to dorsum + gastrocnemius lengthening**

**23-yr, partial Coleman correction, 1st MT callus, weak TA + PB** → **PL → PB + 1st MC dorsal closing wedge + lateralising calcaneal osteotomy with proximal translation**

### Claw toes

**Mechanism** — weak DF → **toe extensor recruitment** + weak intrinsics → claw · flexible → rigid

**Treatment** — **Jones procedure** (EHL to 1st MT neck + IP arthrodesis) for symptomatic claw hallux

### Associated hip dysplasia

**Prevalence** — **<10%** (Orthobullets) · **6–8%** (OB) · often **asymptomatic** → **screen** · presents in adolescence in ambulators

**Cavus foot + hip dysplasia in a teen** → **CMT**\
✗ Wrong options: OI · Down (planovalgus) · achondroplasia · cleidocranial

**Treatment** — **pelvic osteotomy** · ↑ **sciatic nerve palsy** risk · outcomes comparable to DDH

### Scoliosis

**Prevalence** — **10–20%**

**Distinguishing** — **left thoracic** + **kyphotic** curve (vs idiopathic)

**Bracing** — rarely effective · **fusion if >50°** and progressive

## 6 Complications

### Complication – transfer lesion after bunionette resection

**5th MT head resection in CMT** → **transfer lesion to 4th MT head** (lateral overload of cavovarus)\
✗ Wrong options: fixed 5th claw (toe flail) · ulcer (no bone) · peroneal atrophy · Charcot

**Rigid deformity** — untreated progression → arthritis

## X vs Y

| | **CMT1** | **CMT2** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | **Demyelination** | **Axonal (Wallerian)** |
| NCV | **↓ (<38 m/s)** | **Normal (>45)**, ↓ amplitude |
| Onset | 1st–2nd decade | 2nd decade+ |
| Disability | More | **Less** |
| Foot | **Cavus** | Flaccid |

| | **CMT** | **Friedreich ataxia** |
|---|---|---|
| Gene | PMP22, chr 17 | GAA repeat, chr 9 |
| Foot | Cavovarus | Cavovarus |
| Extra | Hand intrinsic wasting, areflexia | Ataxia, cardiomyopathy, progressive scoliosis |

## How it's asked

**"5-yr boy, bilateral cavus, PMP duplication chr 17?"** → **CMT**\
✗ Wrong options: myelodysplasia · CP · polio

**"Features of most common HMSN?"** → **myelin/axon mutation → leg atrophy, loss of sensation/proprioception in early adulthood**\
✗ Wrong options: ALS · Refsum · Dejerine-Sottas · Parkinson

**"PMP22 mutation disorder?"** → **CMT**\
✗ Wrong options: DMD · limb-girdle · myotonic · SMA

**"PMP22 chr 17p12 — initial presentation?"** → **weak tibialis anterior**

**"Tripping, hand weakness, high arches, areflexia, paternal family history — protein?"** → **PMP-22**\
✗ Wrong options: dystrophin · COMP

**"Cause of CMT cavovarus?"** → **autosomal-dominant myelin sheath disease**

**"CMT type I is a disorder of?"** → **myelin sheath**

**"Weak eversion, ↓ NCV in evertor nerve, chr 17 duplication?"** → **CMT**\
✗ Wrong options: Friedreich (chr 9)

**"Plantarflexed 1st ray is initial deformity in?"** → **CMT**\
✗ Wrong options: oblique talus · Köhler · calcaneovalgus

**"Cavus deformity imbalance?"** → **PL overpowering TA**

**"32-yr, lateral foot pain, awkward gait — initial deforming element?"** → **plantar flexion of the first ray**

**"Predominant functional muscles?"** → **posterior tibialis + peroneus longus**

**"Common CMT foot?"** → **plantarflexed 1st MT + hindfoot varus**

**"Progressive bilateral cavovarus most common in?"** → **HMSN**

**"17-yr with familial cavus — also evaluate for?"** → **acetabular dysplasia**

**"15-yr runner, hip dysplasia + cavus?"** → **CMT**

**"Lateral forefoot + heel posts suit?"** → **CMT**\
✗ Wrong options: PTTI stage II/III · Charcot · hallux rigidus

**"HMSN, flexible on Coleman — initial management?"** → **orthotic with lateral forefoot + heel posting**

**"Lateral hindfoot posting most useful for?"** → **flexible cavovarus foot**

**"Orthosis for flexible CMT deformity?"** → **lateral heel + forefoot posting**

**"42-yr CMT, rigid on Coleman, TC + CC arthritis?"** → **triple arthrodesis**

**"14-yr, correctable hindfoot — procedure with improved outcomes?"** → **PTT through IOM to dorsum**\
✗ Wrong options: PB → PL · SPLATT · triple · LCL

**"25-yr foot drop, supple, equinus, strong PF/inversion?"** → **PTT to dorsum + gastrocnemius lengthening**

**"Partial Coleman correction, 1st MT calluses?"** → **PL → PB + 1st MC dorsal closing wedge + lateralising calcaneal osteotomy**

**"Lateral calcaneal slide chosen — exam?"** → **cavovarus not correcting on Coleman**

**"After 5th MT head resection in CMT?"** → **transfer lesion to 4th MT head**

**"↑ peroneal latency + chr 17 duplication — radiograph?"** → **high-arched cavus foot**
