# Chronic Recurrent Multifocal Osteomyelitis (CRMO)

*Pediatrics · 20 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1 in 250,000** · prevalence 1 in 160,000

**Age** — children/adolescents · **peak 10 years**

**Sex** — **girls > boys**

**Site** — **tubular long bones** (metaphyses) + **clavicle**

### Definition & associations

**Also called** — chronic **non-bacterial** osteomyelitis · sterile, **rheumatologic** bone inflammation · multifocal, recurrent over years

**Associations** — **pustulosis palmoplantaris** (red patches + pustules on palms/soles → desquamation days later) · **SAPHO** (synovitis, acne, pustulosis, hyperostosis, osteitis) · **psoriasis** · **inflammatory bowel disease (Crohn's)**

**Syndromic forms** — **Majeed syndrome** · **DIRA** (deficiency of IL-1 receptor antagonist)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — localised pain + swelling, often **worse at night** · episodic fever, malaise · partial relief with NSAIDs

**Exam** — focal tenderness

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

| Criterion |
|---|
| **Multiple sites** of apparent osteomyelitis |
| **Pathology + cultures negative** |
| **No response to antibiotics** |

**Diagnosis of exclusion** — biopsy essential

### Genes & lab markers

**Labs** — **WBC normal** · **ESR / CRP mildly elevated**

**Biopsy** — inflammation, **no atypia** (✗ Wrong options: osteosarcoma), **no small round blue cells** (✗ Wrong options: Ewing) · cultures negative

![CRMO biopsy — bland inflammation](../../MCQ/attachments/q213915-c-crmo-biopsy.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **eccentric lytic metaphyseal lesions** abutting physis with **surrounding sclerosis + new bone**

**Bone scan** — finds other sites

**MRI** — if malignancy suspected (soft-tissue involvement)

![CRMO of distal tibiae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4036-crmo-distal-tibias.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Symptomatic during exacerbations** — **NSAIDs** · corticosteroids · **pamidronate** (symptoms, vertebral remodelling) \
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics (no response)

## 6 Complications

**Growth disturbance** — rare

**Natural history** — remissions + exacerbations · resolves in **3–5 years**

## X vs Y

| | **CRMO** | **Infectious osteomyelitis** | **Osteoid osteoma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sites | **Multiple** | — | **Solitary** |
| Cultures | **Negative** | Infection evident | — |
| Antibiotics | **No response** | Respond | — |
| X-ray | Metaphyseal lysis + sclerosis | — | **Nidus** |
| NSAIDs | Help | — | Help |

## How it's asked

**"9-yr girl with Crohn's, night pain in forearm + clavicle, normal WBC, ↑ ESR/CRP, bland biopsy?"** → **chronic recurrent multifocal osteomyelitis**\
✗ Wrong options: osteosarcoma · Ewing · osteomyelitis · osteoid osteoma

**"CRMO criteria?"** → **multifocal, culture-negative, no antibiotic response**

**"Skin association?"** → **palmoplantar pustulosis / SAPHO**

**"Treatment?"** → **NSAIDs ± pamidronate**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Clavicle Shaft Fracture - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 21 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **15% of pediatric upper-extremity injuries** · shaft = **80%** of pediatric clavicle injuries

**Birth fractures** — **0.5%** normal deliveries · **1.6%** breech · **90% of obstetric fractures**

**No trauma history** → think **congenital pseudarthrosis of clavicle** (usually **right**, except dextrocardia)

### Anatomy

**Deforming forces** — **medial fragment → posterosuperior** (**sternocleidomastoid**) · **lateral fragment → inferomedial** (**pectoralis** + arm weight)

**Open fractures** — buttonhole through **platysma**

**AC stability** — AC ligament (AP stability; **superior** strongest, then posterior) · CC ligaments trapezoid + **conoid (strongest)** (superior/inferior stability) · deltoid + trapezius dynamic

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **FOOSH** or **direct blow to lateral shoulder** · birth trauma

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, deformity · **skin tenting** (impending open fracture) · NV exam (rare NV / brachial plexus injury)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **upright (sitting/standing) AP bilateral shoulders** · **15° cephalic tilt (Zanca)** for superior/inferior displacement · ± 5–10 lb weight in hand

![Midshaft clavicle fracture — upright AP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322128-claviclefx1.jpg)

### Allman classification

| Type | Location |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Middle third (most common)** |
| II | Lateral third, distal to CC ligaments |
| III | Medial (proximal) third |

![Allman classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322128-allman-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Nonoperative care even when displaced

**Newborn birth fracture** — observation, careful lifting · union ~**1 week**

**<12 years** — **sling / shoulder immobiliser / figure-of-eight** (no difference sling vs figure-of-eight) · **no reduction attempted** · ROM at 2–4 wk, strengthening 6–10 wk · callus bump fades 6–12 mo

**Displacement / shortening in <12 yr** — **100% displaced + 12 mm short at 10 yr** → **nonoperative because of age**\
✗ Wrong options: plate for displacement · high nonunion risk

**Adolescent** — **14 yr, completely displaced, 24 mm short** → sling → **union without significant shortening** (remodelling)\
✗ Wrong options: malunion with shortening · nonunion

**Symptomatic malunion** — only **10–20%**\
✗ Wrong options: 50%

### Operative criteria

| Absolute | Controversial |
|---|---|
| **Open fracture** | **Shortening >2 cm** — not an indication <12 yr; debated in adolescents |
| **Skin at risk from tenting** | — |
| **Subclavian artery/vein injury** | — |

```
Pediatric clavicle shaft fracture
├─ Newborn → observe, careful handling
├─ <12 yr (even 100% displaced, shortened) → sling
└─ Open / tenting / vascular injury → ORIF (precontoured plate)
       (adolescent >2 cm shortening — controversial)
```

**ORIF** — precontoured plate (± recon / locking / IM rod) · sling 7–10 d · strengthening ~6 wk · sport ~3 mo

## 6 Complications

**Nonoperative** — **nonunion / malunion rare**

**Operative** — **hardware prominence (up to 59%)** → plate removal · **anterior chest wall numbness** (supraclavicular nerves) · refracture · infection/dehiscence ~5%

## How it's asked

**"10-yr, 100% displaced, 12 mm short, skin intact — true?"** → **nonoperative because of age**\
✗ Wrong options: plate · nonunion risk · 50% symptomatic malunion

**"14-yr, 24 mm shortened midshaft, sling — long-term?"** → **union without significant shortening**

**"Absolute indications for ORIF?"** → **open fracture, tenting skin, subclavian vessel injury**

**"Medial fragment displaced by?"** → **sternocleidomastoid (posterosuperior)**

**"Commonest complication of plating?"** → **hardware prominence**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cleidocranial Dysplasia (Dysostosis)

*Pediatrics · 22 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **~1 in 1,000,000**

### RUNX2 mutation, inheritance & features

**Defect** — **intramembranous ossification** → failure of **midline structures** (clavicles, skull, pubic symphysis, face)

**Hallmark** — **hypoplastic or absent clavicles**

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant** · ~**two-thirds familial**

**Gene** — **RUNX2 = CBFA1 (core-binding factor α1)** · **heterozygous loss of function** · transcription factor for **osteoblast differentiation**

**No commercial test** — many mutations → diagnosis = family history + clinical/radiographic features

| Gene (distractors) | Disorder |
|---|---|
| **RUNX2 / CBFA1** | **Cleidocranial dysplasia** ✓ |
| **SOX9** | **Campomelic dysplasia** |
| cAMP / GNAS → c-fos | **Fibrous dysplasia** |
| BMP-2 | — (not CCD) |
| COL1A1 / COL1A2 | OI |
| DTDST | Diastrophic dysplasia |
| FGFR2 | Apert (complex syndactyly) |
| FGFR3 | Achondroplasia |
| Fibrillin-1 | Marfan |
| Dystrophin | Duchenne (calf pseudohypertrophy) |

## 3 Presentation

**Body** — **proportionate dwarfism** · short stature

**Shoulders** — **hypermobile, droopy** · can **approximate shoulders in front of chest** · narrow chest\
✗ Wrong options: ectodermal dysplasia · MED · glenoid dysplasia

**Skull/face** — **frontal bossing**, large head · **delayed closure of fontanelles + sutures** · **wormian bones** · hypertelorism, midface hypoplasia

**Teeth** — **delayed eruption of permanent teeth** (Orthobullets) · delayed primary teeth (OB) · **supernumerary teeth** → crowding (TO)

**Hands** — **short middle phalanges of 3rd–5th fingers** (Orthobullets) · short terminal phalanges (TO)

**Hips/pelvis** — **coxa vara** (± coxa valga) · **delayed ossification of pubis** (wide symphysis) · lateral notching of capital femoral epiphysis

**Other** — scoliosis, kyphosis, **genu valgum**, pes planus, syringomyelia · **normal intellect**

![Cleidocranial dysplasia — shoulders approximated](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4100-clinical-photo-colorado.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Look for |
|---|---|
| **AP chest** | **Absent / hypoplastic clavicles** |
| Lateral skull | **Delayed suture closure**, wormian bones |
| **AP pelvis** | **Coxa vara**, **unossified pubis** |
| AP hands | Short middle phalanges |

![Absent clavicles](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4100-105-cleidocranial-dysplasia-moved.jpg)

![Coxa vara in cleidocranial dysplasia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4100-coxa-vara.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Clavicles** — **observe** · most manifestations need no intervention

### Operative criteria

**Intertrochanteric (reorienting valgus) osteotomy** — **coxa vara with neck-shaft angle <100°**

### Craniosynostosis – trigonocephaly

**Trigonocephaly** — **metopic suture** fusion (triangular "keel" forehead) · metopic is **first suture to close** (3–8 months) (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: unilateral/bilateral coronal · sagittal

| Shape | Suture |
|---|---|
| **Scaphocephaly / dolichocephaly** (boat) | **Sagittal — most common (~50%)** |
| Brachycephaly | Bicoronal ± bilambdoid |
| Frontal plagiocephaly | Unilateral coronal (harlequin eye) |
| Occipital plagiocephaly | Unilateral lambdoid |
| **Trigonocephaly** | **Metopic** |
| Oxycephaly / turricephaly | Sagittal + coronal + lambdoid |
| Cloverleaf | Intrauterine multi-suture (most severe) |

**Craniosynostosis** — M:F **3:1** · 1 in 2000–2500 · coronal ~20%, lambdoid ~5%, metopic ~5% · syndromic ~8–10%: **Apert, Crouzon, Carpenter** · anterior fontanelle closes **18–24 mo**

## X vs Y

| | **Cleidocranial dysplasia** | **SED tarda** | **SCFE / hypothyroid** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Clavicles | **Absent/hypoplastic** | Normal | Normal |
| Hip | **Coxa vara**, unossified pubis | Coxa vara, poorly ossified epiphyses | Slipped epiphysis |
| Ossification defect | **Intramembranous** | Endochondral (COL2A1) | — |

## How it's asked

**"Shoulder approximation in front of chest?"** → **cleidocranial dysplasia** (TO)

**"Heterozygous loss-of-function gene causing CCD?"** → **RUNX2**\
✗ Wrong options: BMP-2 · cAMP · c-fos · SOX-9

**"8-yr, extreme shoulder motion, frontal bossing, abnormal clavicles + coxa vara?"** → **cleidocranial dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: SED tarda · hypothyroidism · SCFE · renal osteodystrophy

**"Droopy shoulders, absent clavicles, delayed pubic ossification — inheritance?"** → **autosomal dominant**

**"Mutated core-binding factor A1 — clinical photo?"** → **shoulders touching (CCD)**\
✗ Wrong options: marfanoid · rhizomelia · calf pseudohypertrophy · cavovarus

**"Defective intramembranous ossification — gene?"** → **RUNX2/CBFA1**\
✗ Wrong options: COL1A1 · DTDST · FGFR2 · SOX9

**"Coxa vara in CCD — when operate?"** → **neck-shaft angle <100°**

**"Trigonocephaly results from?"** → **metopic suture fusion** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Clubfoot (congenital talipes equinovarus)

*Pediatrics · 23 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Most common musculoskeletal birth defect** — **1:1000** (1:250 in some; highest in **Hawaiians, Maoris**) · TO: 1.2/1000

**Sex** — **M:F 2:1**\
✗ Wrong options: equal

**Side** — **half bilateral**\
✗ Wrong options: majority unilateral

**Isolated** — **80%** idiopathic · 20% with syndromes / neuromuscular conditions

### Associated findings, genetics & prognosis (limb hypoplasia, neurological cause, prenatal diagnosis)

**Familial** — **25%** · unaffected parents with one child → **2.5–6.5%** recurrence · monozygotic > dizygotic concordance

**Gene** — **PITX1–TBX4** transcription pathway (hindfoot/limb development) · shared pathway with CVT\
✗ Wrong options: PI3K-AKT achondroplasia · COL2A1 Kniest · FGFR2 Apert · RUNX2 CCD

**Associations** — **arthrogryposis** · **diastrophic dysplasia** · **myelodysplasia** · **tibial hemimelia** · **amniotic band** (Streeter; hand anomalies) · Pierre Robin · Opitz · **Larsen** · prune-belly

**Vascular** — **anterior tibial artery hypoplasia/absence** common · **posterior tibial artery** predominant supply

**Limb** — **small foot + calf, short tibia** = **intrinsic hypoplasia** of the limb · calf girth difference in unilateral cases\
✗ Wrong options: tethered cord · scoliosis · contralateral hemihypertrophy

**Walking** — clubfoot does **not** delay walking · not walking by **18–24 months** → **neurologic disorder**\
✗ Wrong options: hip dysplasia · in-toeing · LLD · foot

**Prognosis** — with treatment → **walks and runs normally**\
✗ Wrong options: prosthesis

### Pathoanatomy — CAVE

| Component | Tight structures |
|---|---|
| **C**avus (midfoot) | Intrinsics, **FHL, FDL** · plantarflexed 1st ray |
| **A**dductus (forefoot) | **Tibialis posterior** |
| **V**arus (hindfoot) | **Tendoachilles, tibialis posterior, tibialis anterior** |
| **E**quinus | **Tendoachilles** |

**Bony** — medial spin of mid/forefoot around hindfoot · **talar neck medially + plantarly deviated** · calcaneus varus, rotated medially under talus · **navicular + cuboid medially displaced** · forefoot **pronated relative to hindfoot** (whole foot supinated)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — small foot/calf · **medial + posterior skin creases** · hindfoot **equinus + varus**, midfoot **cavus**, forefoot **adduction** · **rigid** equinus resisting passive correction (vs positional deformity)

![Clubfoot — CAVE](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4062-screen-shot-2014-05-25-at-9-56-26-pm.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**

### Key radiology findings

**Usually not needed** · simulate weight-bearing

| View | Clubfoot finding |
|---|---|
| **Dorsiflexion lateral (Turco)** | **Hindfoot parallelism** · **talocalcaneal angle <25°** · flat talar head (older) |
| **AP** | **Kite (talocalcaneal) angle <20°** (normal 20–40°) · **negative talus–1st MT angle** (normal 0–20°) · parallelism |

**Corrected TN joint after PMR (AP)** — **talar axis passes through centre of navicular and 1st MT shaft** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: points at 1st MT base only (residual adduction) · Kite <15° (that's deformity) · lateral parallelism

**Prenatal US** — from **12 weeks** · **1st trimester** dx → associated anomalies very common · 2nd trimester → true clubfoot · **3rd trimester → ↑ false positives** (crowding)

![Hindfoot parallelism on lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4062-hindfoot-paralellism-courtesy-miller-moved.png)

**Clubfoot vs CVT on X-ray** — clubfoot: **parallel** talus + calcaneus · CVT: no parallelism, navicular (unossified) doesn't align with talus on plantarflexion

## 5 Management

**Gold standard** — **Ponseti** (**>90%** avoid comprehensive release) · start **as early as possible** (first 1–2 weeks); older infants (e.g. 9 months) still treated with Ponseti

**French method** — daily PT manipulation + taping/splints · 1–3 mo · family-dependent · rarely used in US

### Ponseti technique – correction sequence, supination/abduction around talus & casting errors

**Principle** — **abduct the foot, rotating it laterally around a fixed talus** · **counterpressure on lateral talar head** (fulcrum) \
✗ Wrong options: push against calcaneocuboid joint (Kite → rocker bottom) · manipulate heel directly

**Order** — **C → A → V → E**

| Step | Manoeuvre |
|---|---|
| 1 **Cavus** (first cast) | **Forefoot SUPINATION** — elevate 1st MT, align forefoot with hindfoot (✗ Wrong options: pronation → ↑ cavus, midfoot break) |
| 2 Adductus + varus (together) | **Abduct** foot around talar head; supination gradually ↓ to neutral |
| 3 Equinus (last) | Only when heel neutral/valgus + foot **≥60° abducted** → **percutaneous Achilles tenotomy** → cast **3 weeks** in max DF (15°) |

**Casts** — **long-leg (toe-to-groin), knee 90°**, changed **weekly** · 5–6 casts usually · plaster\
✗ Wrong options: short-leg (slips, doesn't control tibial torsion)

**Final cast** — max abduction **~70°** (hypercorrection) + DF 15° · end-point before tenotomy: TN reduced + **60° passive abduction** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: **30° abduction** (the false statement)

**Manipulated in** — **supination + abduction** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: pronation · adduction · dorsiflexion first

```
Idiopathic clubfoot (newborn or infant)
├─ Weekly long-leg Ponseti casts: cavus → adductus/varus (~4–6 casts)
├─ Residual equinus (>80–90%) → percutaneous TA tenotomy + 3-wk cast
└─ FAO 23 h/day × 3 mo → nights/naps to age 3–4
       ├─ Relapse <2 yr → recast (± repeat tenotomy)
       └─ Dynamic supination ≥2–4 yr → whole TA transfer to lateral cuneiform
```

### Percutaneous Achilles tenotomy for residual equinus

**Rate** — **80–90%** (Orthobullets) · **70–75%** / 70% (OB) · **>90%** (OB) — "most/almost all"

**When** — residual rigid equinus after cavus/adductus/varus corrected (~6–8 wk) · **under local anaesthetic** in clinic\
✗ Wrong options: more casting · fiberglass · FAO first · TA transfer

**Why** — bracing a foot in equinus fails (slips, blisters) · prevents rocker bottom

**Technique** — **medial → lateral** (protect **posterior tibial artery**) · bleeding from **peroneal artery + lesser saphenous vein** (TO)

**Open Z-lengthening of TA in varus foot** — **distal horizontal limb medial** (corrects residual varus) (TO)

### Ponseti casting & bracing protocol

**Foot abduction orthosis (FAO / Denis Browne / Mitchell / Dobbs / Iowa / Steenbeek)** — **critical**; noncompliance = biggest recurrence risk

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| Wear | **23 h/day × 3 months** → **nights + naps until age 3–4** (✗ Wrong options: 23 h for 12 months (TO false statement)) |
| Affected foot ER/abduction | **60–70°** (Orthobullets 60° · OB 70° · TO 70°) |
| Normal foot (unilateral) | **30–40°** |
| Dorsiflexion | **10–15°** (TO answer: **70° abduction/ER + 15–20° DF**) |
| Bar width | Heels at **shoulder width** (~1 inch wider) |
| Shoes | Straight medial border, low posterior cut, heel cup |

**After walking age** — "daytime AFO + night bar" stated as **FALSE** (TO)

### Recurrence – bracing compliance & risk factors

**Most dependent on** — **brace (FAO) compliance**\
✗ Wrong options: maternal age · family history · walking age · BMI

**Risk factors** — **noncompliance** + **parental education ≤ high school**\
✗ Wrong options: race · sex

**Relapse <2 yr** → repeat manipulation + casting · **>2 yr** → casting, then **TA transfer** ± repeat TAL / gastroc recession

### Dynamic supination – tibialis anterior transfer

**Cause** — incomplete navicular reduction → **TA becomes a supinator** (medial overpull vs weak peroneals) · ~**one-third** of patients · begins **3–5 yr** (older than 12–18 mo)

**Gait** — **supination in swing**, lands on **lateral border** · forefoot inverts on active DF · foot passively plantigrade

**Treatment** — **whole TA transfer to lateral (3rd) cuneiform** (Garceau principle) · or split TA to cuboid / PB · Point = lateral cuneiform (more common) or cuboid · TA harvested from medial cuneiform + 1st MT base

**Distractors** — ✗ Wrong options: more casting (to 60° abduction) · PTT through IOM (poor in clubfoot; for paralysed TA) · TA lengthening · TA to calcaneus (myelodysplasia calcaneus) · midtarsal osteotomy (fixed) · floor-reaction AFO · cuboid wedge

| TA transfer criteria | |
|---|---|
| Rate | **10–50%** (Orthobullets) · **10–20% / 15–20%** (OB) |
| Age | **2–5 yr** (Orthobullets) · **~4 yr** (OB) · lateral cuneiform **ossified** — ≥2, usually ≥3 (TO) (✗ Wrong options: <1 yr · 12–15 yr) |
| TA strength | **≥ grade 4** |
| Subtalar joint | **Supple** — **rigidity = contraindication** (✗ Wrong options: "rigidity supplements") |
| Fixed hindfoot varus | Not a TA transfer case → osteotomy |

### Surgical release – outcomes & late deformities (dorsal bunion, residual cavus, rocker bottom)

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Posteromedial release + tendon lengthening** | Resistant/recurrent after Ponseti · **rocker bottom from failed casting** · syndromic failures · at **9–10 mo** (non-syndromic) · <6 mo surgery → poorer results |
| Medial column lengthening / lateral column shortening / **cuboid decancellation** | >2–3 yr with release · **3–10 yr** recurrent "bean-shaped" foot |
| **Talectomy** | Severe rigid recurrent clubfoot in **arthrogryposis**, 6–10 yr |
| Multiplanar supramalleolar osteotomy | Salvage, older (8–10 yr), complex rigid or **insensate** foot |
| Ring fixator (TSF / Ilizarov / JESS) | Complex resistant/neglected · **high recurrence after removal** |
| Triple arthrodesis | **Almost never** · not in insensate feet (ulcers) |

**Outcome** — **extent of soft-tissue release correlates inversely** with long-term function · stiffness + pain common\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar/ankle function · cast duration

**Master knot of Henry release** — frees **FDL/FHL tether** to spring ligament + sustentaculum → allows **TN reduction** (TO)

**Neglected clubfoot >3 yr** — Ilizarov distraction histogenesis → **better joint mobility, ↓ recurrence** vs open PMR (TO)

**JESS** — distract **CC + TN joints (correct midfoot) before equinus** → avoids rocker bottom (TO)

**PMR post-op** — TN K-wire 6 wk · cast change at 2 wk · then FAO

## 6 Complications

**Rocker bottom** — **correcting equinus before heel varus** → DF through **midfoot** · failed casting with rocker bottom → **PMR + TAL**\
✗ Wrong options: under-corrected pronation → residual cavus · over-corrected adductus → abduction · below-knee casts

**Residual cavus (after surgery)** — **navicular pinned dorsally subluxated** (22%) · insufficient plantar release\
✗ Wrong options: inadequate TAL → equinus · LCL → worsens adductus

**Pes planus** — overcorrection (extensive subtalar release)

**Intoeing** — **internal tibial torsion** / talar IR in mortise

**Talar osteonecrosis** — vascular insult → collapse

**Dorsal bunion** — **strong TA overpowering weak peroneus longus** (PL = **superficial peroneal nerve**) → elevated 1st ray · weak gastrocsoleus after release → **FHB/FHL** compensate → plantarflexed hallux · inadvertent PL lengthening · Rx: **TA lengthening/transfer + FHL transfer to 1st MT head (reverse Jones)** ± plantarflexion osteotomy

**NOT a complication** — **tarsal tunnel syndrome** (TO)

![Dorsal bunion after clubfoot surgery](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4062-dorsal-bunion-moved.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Clubfoot** | **CVT** | **Metatarsus adductus** | **Calcaneovalgus** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hindfoot | **Equinus + varus** | Equinus + valgus | **Normal** | Calcaneus (DF) + valgus |
| Midfoot | Cavus, adductus | **Dorsal TN dislocation**, rocker | Adducted forefoot only | Normal |
| Flexibility | Rigid | Rigid | Flexible / stiff | Flexible |
| Lateral X-ray | **Talocalcaneal parallelism** | Vertical talus | Normal | — |
| Treatment | **Ponseti** | Reverse Ponseti | Observe / stretch | Stretch |

| | **Ponseti** | **Kite** |
|---|---|---|
| Fulcrum | **Lateral talar head** | Calcaneocuboid joint |
| Result | Correction | **Rocker bottom** |

## How it's asked

**"Ponseti — all true EXCEPT?"** → **pronation in initial cast** (✓ weekly long-leg casts, ✓ tenotomy before final cast, ✓ abduction with talar counterpressure, ✓ adduction before equinus)

**"First component addressed?"** → **cavus**

**"9-month-old with talocalcaneal parallelism — first line?"** → **serial casting**

**"3-month-old with rocker bottom during Ponseti — error?"** → **corrected equinus before heel varus**

**"Key Ponseti concepts — EXCEPT?"** → **last cast at 30° abduction** (it's ~70°)

**"True of Ponseti?"** → **metatarsal heads turned into supination with first cast**\
✗ Wrong options: tenotomy in 20% · short-leg cast

**"Foot manipulated in?"** → **supination and abduction**

**"Healthy newborn with clubfoot — best treatment?"** → **Ponseti casting**\
✗ Wrong options: parental stretching · AFO · TA transfer · short-leg cast

**"Initial phase described to parents?"** → **weekly long-leg casts for cavus/adductus/varus, then tenotomy + final cast**

**"9-month-old untreated bilateral clubfoot — true?"** → **abduct foot rotating laterally around fixed talus**\
✗ Wrong options: 50% success

**"After 6 weeks Ponseti, rigid 10° plantarflexion?"** → **percutaneous Achilles release under local**

**"Recurrence most dependent on?"** → **brace compliance**

**"Recurrence risk factors?"** → **noncompliance + parental education ≤ high school**

**"3-yr, lands on lateral border, supinates in swing, DF 20°?"** → **tibialis anterior transfer** (TO)

**"Muscle causing swing-phase dynamic deformity?"** → **tibialis anterior**

**"Destination of transferred tendon?"** → **lateral cuneiform**

**"TA transfer appropriate for?"** → **3-yr whose foot supinates on dorsiflexion**

**"TA transfer to lateral cuneiform — EXCEPT?"** → **subtalar rigidity supplements the transfer**

**"Age for TA transfer?"** → **~4 years**

**"Walks on lateral border, forefoot inverts on DF, plantigrade passively?"** → **lateral transfer of anterior tibialis**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT through IOM

**"Supination in swing, 20° DF?"** → **TA transfer to dorsolateral foot**

**"Z-lengthening TA in CTEV — distal limb?"** → **medial** (TO)

**"Late deformity after clubfoot surgery — EXCEPT?"** → **tarsal tunnel syndrome** (TO)

**"Master knot of Henry release purpose?"** → **free long flexors to reduce TN joint** (TO)

**"JESS — prevent rocker bottom by?"** → **distract CC + TN before equinus** (TO)

**"Ilizarov vs PMR in neglected clubfoot >3 yr?"** → **better mobility, less recurrence with frame** (TO)

**"AP sign of reduced TN after PMR?"** → **talar axis through navicular centre and 1st MT shaft** (TO)

**"Residual cavus after release — technical error?"** → **navicular pinned dorsally subluxated**

**"Dorsal bunion — weak muscle's nerve?"** → **superficial peroneal**

**"Failed casting at 4 months with which deformity → PMR?"** → **rocker-bottom**

**"Long-term impairment after release correlates with?"** → **extent of soft-tissue release**

**"Release complications — EXCEPT?"** → **tarsal tunnel syndrome** (✓ dorsal bunion, talar AVN, rigid planus, intoeing)

**"Dorsal bunion + TA/PL imbalance — original condition?"** → **congenital talipes equinovarus**

**"16-yr dorsal bunion after childhood foot surgery — original deformity?"** → **talipes equinovarus**

**"Photograph consistent with clubfoot?"** → **equinovarus with cavus + adductus**\
✗ Wrong options: skewfoot · MTA · calcaneovalgus · CVT

**"24-month-old with clubfoot not walking?"** → **neurologic disorder**

**"10-yr after clubfoot surgery, smaller limb — why?"** → **intrinsic hypoplasia**

**"Prenatal clubfoot — true?"** → **will walk and run with treatment**

**"Limb-development transcription factor + clubfoot?"** → **PITX1–TBX4**

**"Ponseti — all EXCEPT?"** → **brace 23 h/day for 12 months** (it's 3 months) (TO)

**"Latest Ponseti casting?"** → **weekly long-leg cast → tenotomy → 3-week cast** (TO)

**"False statement?"** → **daytime AFO + night bar after walking age** (TO)

**"Ideal FAO position?"** → **70° abduction/ER + 15–20° DF** (TO)

# Congenital Curly Toe

*Pediatrics · 24 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Deformity** — **flexion + varus of the interphalangeal joints** · toe curls under its medial neighbour

**Location** — **lateral three toes** (3rd + 4th most often) · usually **bilateral**

**Cause** — contracture of **FDL** or **FDB** (most common)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — usually **asymptomatic** · if symptomatic: pain, shoe-wear difficulty, **nail problems**

**Natural history** — ~**25% improve spontaneously** (Sweetnam) · more likely to **persist in older child (e.g. 6 yr)**

![Congenital curly toes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4081-clinical-photo-copy-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **not indicated**

## 5 Management

```
Congenital curly toe
├─ Infant / asymptomatic → observation
└─ Symptomatic (pain, shoes, nail) after age 3 → flexor tenotomy
```

**Observation** — first line (same success as strapping, less painful) · **toe strapping/taping not beneficial** (no change or temporary)

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Severe toe or **nail-bed** deformity, symptomatic, **>3 yr** | **Flexor tenotomy** (FDL ± FDB) — **85–90%** effective |
| — | Tenotomy **= FDL transfer to extensor hood** in efficacy |
| Rarely | PIP arthrodesis |

**Techniques** — open tenotomy of both FDB slips + FDL · tenotomy of one FDB slip · FDL → extensor hood transfer

## How it's asked

**"8-month-old with curly toes, first surgeon offered FDB tenotomy + volar plate release?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: FDL transfer · FDB tenotomy · taping

**"Symptomatic curly toe in a 6-year-old?"** → **flexor tenotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · tape 8 wk · transfer (no better) · PIP fusion

**"Cause?"** → **FDL / FDB contracture**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Dislocation of Patella

*Pediatrics · 25 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — rare, **irreducible lateral (posterolateral) patellar dislocation** · present at birth (often missed) or reducible at birth → subluxation → **fixed dislocation in childhood**

### Pathoanatomy

**Bone** — **small or absent patella** · **hypoplastic trochlea** · **external tibial torsion**

**Soft tissue** — **thick, tight lateral structures (ITB, retinaculum)** · **tight quadriceps** (superior subluxation, patella alta)

**Associated** — **Larsen** · arthrogryposis · diastrophic dysplasia · **nail-patella** · **Down** · Ellis–van Creveld

### Pathomechanics

**Quadriceps action** — laterally displaced extensor mechanism → **quadriceps becomes a knee flexor**\
✗ Wrong options: extension · internal rotation (it's external) · varus (it's valgus) · posterior translation

**Patella anatomy** — largest sesamoid · ossifies **3 yr (♀) / 4–5 yr (♂)** · blood supply **distal → proximal** (geniculates, supreme geniculate, anterior tibial recurrent)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — associated syndromes · **delayed walking** (mimics **cerebral palsy**)

**Exam** — **genu valgum** · **knee flexion contracture** · external tibial torsion · **"smiley face" kneecaps** · prominent condyles · small, laterally placed patella hard to palpate · limited active extension

**Pain** — all patients **≥7 yr** had pain (Gordon & Schoenecker)

![Smiley-face knees in congenital patellar dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4047-clinical-photo-miller-colorado.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**<3 yr** — patella unossified → radiographs unhelpful → **ultrasound or MRI**

**>3 yr** — **AP, lateral, sunrise** · dislocated patella · hypoplastic trochlea

![Sunrise view — laterally dislocated patella](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4047-cdp-sunrise.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Observation** — **not recommended** (long-term functional deficit, malalignment, pain)

**Surgery early** — allows trochlear remodelling

```
Congenital patellar dislocation
└─ Early surgical reduction (Andrish)
       ├─ Soft tissue: lateral release + VL advancement + quad lengthening + MPFL (semitendinosus)
       └─ Distal realignment if needed → Roux-Goldthwait (open apophysis)
```

**Andrish soft-tissue steps** — lengthen lateral retinaculum · detach VL from septum, advance proximally · release distal patellomeniscal ligaments · **lengthen quadriceps tendon, shorten patellar tendon** (alta) · **MPFL reconstruction** (semitendinosus rerouted through MCL to patella)

**Bony** — distal realignment rarely needed · tubercle transfer limited by apophysis → **Roux-Goldthwait** (lateral half patellar tendon medialised)

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence**

## X vs Y

| | **Congenital dislocation** | **Recurrent patellar instability** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | Birth / early childhood | Older child, activity |
| Patella | **Fixed, irreducible** | Reducible; normal between episodes |
| Associated | Syndromes (Down, nail-patella, Larsen) | Ligamentous laxity |
| Treatment | **Surgical reduction** | Nonop → MPFL |

## How it's asked

**"10-day-old, laterally dislocated patella on US — quadriceps now does?"** → **flexes the knee**

**"Imaging under age 3?"** → **ultrasound / MRI**

**"Treatment?"** → **early surgical reduction (lateral release, quad lengthening, MPFL)**

**"Distal realignment with open apophysis?"** → **Roux-Goldthwait**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Dislocation of the Knee

*Pediatrics · 26 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Spectrum** — **positional contracture → rigid dislocation** · tibia anterior on femur

**Structural** — **quadriceps contracture** (main) · anterior subluxation of hamstrings · **absent suprapatellar pouch** · tight collaterals · ACL abnormalities

**Associated syndromes** — **myelomeningocele** · **arthrogryposis** · **Larsen** (clubfeet + hip, knee, elbow dislocations)

**Associated deformities** — **DDH** — **50%** (Orthobullets) · ipsilateral hip dislocation **70–100%** (OB) · **clubfoot** · metatarsus adductus

## 3 Presentation

**Hyperextended knee at birth** · anterior tibial translation on X-ray

![Congenital knee dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4046-clinical-photo-1-week-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Tarek classification

| Grade | Radiology | Passive flexion | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | Simple recurvatum | **>90°** | Manipulation + casting |
| **II** | Subluxation / dislocation | **30–90°** | Casting if <1 mo; **PQR** if seen later |
| **III** | Dislocation | **<30°** | **Surgery** (open V-Y quadricepsplasty) |

![Tarek grades](../../MCQ/attachments/q4749-illa-illus-a-tarek.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Congenital knee dislocation
├─ Newborn (Tarek I, early II) → weekly long-leg manipulation + flexion casting
│      (knee + hip dislocated → treat KNEE first, then Pavlik)
└─ Failed to gain 30° flexion by 3 mo / grade III / recurrent → surgical release
       ├─ Grade II (later) → percutaneous quadriceps recession
       └─ Older, failed casting, grade III → open V-Y quadricepsplasty / open reduction
```

### Serial casting vs quadricepsplasty/open reduction

**Knee + ipsilateral hip dislocated (2-day-old)** → **manipulate + cast knee first** — **can't fit Pavlik on a hyperextended knee**\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik first · spica · delay · quad lengthening

**Casting failure** — **90° not attained in 4 weeks** (OB) · **30° not gained after 3 months** (Orthobullets) → surgery

**6-month-old, failed 3 mo casting, flexion 15°, hyperextension 25° (recurrent grade III)** → **open V-Y quadricepsplasty + above-knee cast**\
✗ Wrong options: cast in extension · more flexion casts · ACL division alone · percutaneous recession (for grade II at presentation)

**7-month-old, can't flex >30° after casting, hips normal** → **open reduction** — ideally **~6 months, before age 1**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · more casting · closed reduction · wait for walking

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **<30° flexion after 3 months casting** | Soft-tissue release |
| Tarek II after 1 month, III, recurrent | Release |
| Goal | **90° flexion** |
| Options | **Percutaneous quadriceps recession (PQR)** · **V-Y quadricepsplasty / Z-lengthening** · anterior capsule release · posterior transposition of hamstrings · collateral mobilisation |
| Post-op | Cast **45–60° flexion × 3–4 weeks** |

**ACL division + quad release** — only fair results

## How it's asked

**"2-day-old hyperextended knee + subluxated ipsilateral hip — next?"** → **manipulation and casting of the knee**

**"6-month-old, failed casting, recurrent grade III?"** → **open V-Y quadricepsplasty + above-knee cast**

**"7-month-old can't flex past 30° after casting?"** → **open reduction**

**"Indication for surgery?"** → **failure to gain 30° flexion after 3 months casting**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Hallux Varus (Atavistic Great Toe)

*Pediatrics · 27 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — rare idiopathic **varus (medial) deviation of the great toe at the MTP joint** · noticed **after walking age** · few degrees → 90°

**Pathoanatomy** — **firm, fibrous, band-like abductor hallucis** · **short, thick 1st metatarsal** · **longitudinal epiphyseal bracket (LEB)** of 1st MT or proximal phalanx · skeletal dysplasia (**diastrophic dwarfism**)

**Associated** — **polydactyly** (often)

### Longitudinal epiphyseal bracket

**LEB** (delta phalanx / bracket epiphysis) — anomalous **C-shaped secondary ossification centre** along the **medial diaphysis** → blocks transverse/longitudinal growth → progressive shortening + angulation

**Frequency** — 2–14% of congenital hand/foot deformities · **bilateral ~75%** · hand → clinodactyly, foot → **hallux varus**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — cosmetic · **difficult shoe wear** · usually painless

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, oblique foot

**Findings** — **short, thick 1st metatarsal** · bracket (if unossified → **ultrasound** shows cartilaginous C-shaped structure)

![Congenital hallux varus — short thick 1st MT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4076-chv.jpg)

**Differentials** — **metatarsus adductus** · **metatarsus primus varus** (1st MT varus, **no MTP deformity**)

## 5 Management

**Observation** — rarely; deformity **worsens with age** → surgery often in infancy

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Mild, resistant | **Abductor hallucis release** |
| **Epiphyseal bracket** | **Excision of central bracket (physiolysis)** ± **PMMA / fat interposition** — **early, ideally <1 yr, before ossification** · growth often resumes · may need later realignment/lengthening |
| Moderate–severe | **Farmer** — **syndactylise hallux to 2nd toe** to hold correction |
| Older, ossified bracket, big deformity | Distraction / angular osteotomy |

![Farmer procedure — syndactyly to 2nd toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4076-f029-018-9780323374620.jpg)

### Longitudinal epiphyseal bracket – excision

**9-month-old, bilateral hallux varus, unossified C-shaped bracket on US** → **immediate excision of preossified bracket ± PMMA interposition**\
✗ Wrong options: observe until ossified then osteotomy · wait until 3–4 yr · ex-fix distraction · serial casting (soft-tissue deformities only)

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** · **incomplete correction**

## How it's asked

**"Infant hallux varus with preossified bracket?"** → **early bracket excision ± PMMA**

**"Muscle causing atavistic great toe?"** → **tight band-like abductor hallucis**

**"X-ray finding?"** → **short, thick 1st metatarsal**

**"Moderate–severe deformity — procedure that holds correction?"** → **Farmer (syndactyly to 2nd toe)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Popliteal Webbing

*Pediatrics · 28 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Also called** — **popliteal pterygium syndrome** · affects face, limbs, genitalia

**Incidence** — rare, **1:300,000** · **M = F**

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant** (or sporadic — **advanced paternal age**)

**Gene** — **IRF6** — transcription factor for head/face, skin, genital development

### Anatomy of the web

| Structure | Position |
|---|---|
| **Subcutaneous fibrous band** | Superficial, along **free edge** (ischium → calcaneus) |
| **Sciatic / peroneal nerve** | **Deep to band**, ~halfway between free edge and apex · often **shortened** |
| **Popliteal artery/vein** | Deep in popliteal space |

## 3 Presentation

**Web** — behind knee from **ischial tuberosity to heel** · usually bilateral · limits extension, abduction, rotation → **knee flexion contracture**

**Other** — **cleft lip/palate** · syndactyly · triangular skin fold over hallux nail · **ankyloblepharon** (eyelids), **syngnathia** (jaws) · hypoplastic labia / **cryptorchidism, bifid scrotum** · cognitive delay · spina bifida

![Popliteal pterygium](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422805-image.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP/lateral)** — knee flexion contracture

**MRI** — **pre-op planning** to locate neurovascular structures (wide variation, injury risk)

![MRI of popliteal pterygium](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422805-mri.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Femoral shortening with soft-tissue release

| Contracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<20°** | Nonoperative |
| **20–40°** | Hamstring lengthening + post-op splinting |
| **Up to 60°** | **Popliteal Z-plasties** + post-op serial casting (avoid NV tension) |
| **>60°** | **Femoral shortening osteotomy + soft-tissue releases** (Z-plasty, band excision, hamstring lengthening, posterior capsulotomy) · or gradual ex-fix — **recurs fast** without formal soft-tissue procedures + splinting |

**4-month-old, bilateral 100° fixed contractures** → **femoral shortening + soft-tissue releases**\
✗ Wrong options: serial casting · PT/splints · soft tissue alone · frame without release

**Technique** — resect/tenotomise fibrous band · **Z-plasty** skin · sciatic nerve grafting or tendon lengthening if needed

## How it's asked

**"100° popliteal pterygium contractures — most likely to succeed?"** → **femoral shortening osteotomy + soft-tissue releases**

**"Gene?"** → **IRF6, autosomal dominant**

**"Pre-op imaging?"** → **MRI to locate sciatic nerve and vessels**

**"Where is the sciatic nerve?"** → **deep to fibrous band, halfway between free edge and apex**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Pseudoarthrosis of Clavicle

*Pediatrics · 29 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **failure of fusion of medial and lateral ossification centres** of the clavicle

### Right-sided middle third

**Side** — **right ~90%** · **left only with dextrocardia / situs inversus** · **bilateral <10%** (Orthobullets) / ~10% (TO) — bilateral linked to **bilateral cervical ribs**

**Site** — **middle third**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral third · left · both equally

**Cause** — extrinsic compression / pulsation of **subclavian artery** (higher on right)

**Genetics** — no clear involvement

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **painless, nontender mass** on clavicle · present at birth, **no callus**, no trauma history · ± scapular winging

![Clavicular pseudarthrosis — painless bump](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4039-clavicular-pseudarthrosis-physical-exam.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**AP clavicle** — **rounded, sclerotic bone ends** at pseudarthrosis · no callus (vs fracture)

![Congenital pseudarthrosis of clavicle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4039-congenital-pseudoarthosis-of-clavicle.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Observation** — minimal symptoms / cosmetic only

### Operative criteria

**ORIF + iliac crest bone graft at 3–6 yr** — pain · functional impairment · cosmesis · union usually achieved · **avoid bone-graft substitutes** (↑ nonunion)

## X vs Y

| | **Congenital pseudarthrosis** | **Birth fracture** | **Cleidocranial dysplasia** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Side | **Right**, middle third | Either | Bilateral |
| Pain | **Painless** | Painful | Painless |
| X-ray | **Rounded sclerotic ends**, no callus | Callus by ~1 wk | Absent / hypoplastic clavicles |
| Other | — | — | Skull, pubis, teeth |

## How it's asked

**"Congenital pseudarthrosis of clavicle — side?"** → **right** (TO)

**"Almost always presents?"** → **right side, middle third**

**"Painless clavicle mass with rounded sclerotic ends — treatment?"** → **observe; ORIF + iliac crest graft at 3–6 yr if symptomatic**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Vertical Talus

*Pediatrics · 30 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — rare, **1:150,000** · **M:F 2:1** · **50% bilateral**

**Also called** — congenital convex pes valgus · "teratologic dorsolateral dislocation of the talocalcaneonavicular joint"

### Pathoanatomy

**Rigid** — **irreducible dorsolateral navicular dislocation** on talar neck · **vertical talus** · calcaneal **eversion** + equinus · **attenuated spring ligament**

**Contractures** — **Achilles** + **peroneals** (hindfoot equinovalgus) · **EDL, EHL, tibialis anterior** (forefoot DF + abduction)

**Tendon displacement** — **peroneus longus + tibialis posterior** shift to act as **dorsiflexors**

### Associations – neuromuscular/genetic conditions & neural axis work-up

**~50%** associated with **neuromuscular disease or chromosomal/syndromic** conditions (>50% / 60–85% in other OB explanations)

| Associated | Not associated |
|---|---|
| **Myelomeningocele** / spinal dysraphism, **diastematomyelia** | **Amniotic band syndrome** (TO, OB) |
| **Arthrogryposis** | Posteromedial tibial bowing (→ calcaneovalgus) |
| **Congenital hip dislocation** (43–50%) | Chromosome **22q11** deletion |
| Cerebral palsy · **spinal muscular atrophy** · NF | — |
| **Chromosomal deletions**, trisomy 18, Cri-du-Chat | — |
| Cleft lip/palate, CHD, chr 12 duplication (less often) | — |

**Highest association** → **neuromuscular disease**

### Genes & lab markers

**Familial** — up to **20%** · **HOXD10** (transcription factor) in familial/"idiopathic" cases · shared pathway with clubfoot

## 3 Presentation

**Rigid rocker-bottom foot** — **convex plantar surface**\
✗ Wrong options: "concave" (the false statement)

**Hindfoot** — **fixed equinus + valgus** (heel in **equinus**; ✗ Wrong options: calcaneus · equinovarus · supple)

**Midfoot** — rigid dorsiflexion (dislocated navicular) · **prominent talar head** in medial plantar arch

**Forefoot** — **abducted + dorsiflexed**

**Gait** — **"peg-leg" / calcaneal gait** (weak push-off, excess heel contact)

**Neuro exam** — always (frequent neuromuscular cause)

![Rigid rocker-bottom foot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4066-clinical-photo-courtesy-miller-moved.png)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – forced plantar-flexion lateral radiograph; oblique talus

**Diagnostic test** — **lateral X-ray in maximum (forced) plantar flexion** → **persistent dorsal TN dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · US in DF · max-DF lateral · CT

### Key radiology findings

| View | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Neutral lateral** | **Vertical talus**, dorsal navicular · talar axis passes **plantar to 1st MT–cuneiform axis** |
| **Forced plantarflexion lateral** | TN **stays dislocated** · **Meary's angle >20°** · **TAMBA >35°** (talar axis–metatarsal base angle, Hamanishi) |
| Forced dorsiflexion lateral | **Fixed equinus** |
| **AP** | **Talocalcaneal angle >40°** (normal 20–40°) |

**Before navicular ossifies (~age 3)** — use **1st metatarsal** as navicular proxy (✓ the true statement)

**MRI** — **neuraxial imaging** to exclude neurologic disorder

**Rigid flatfoot in infant (no plantarflexion, no subtalar motion, vertical talus not reducing)** → **neurologic work-up incl. neural-axis imaging** first\
✗ Wrong options: urgent surgery · foot MRI · reassurance

**4-month-old arthrogrypotic with suggestive X-rays** → **obtain forced plantarflexion radiograph** first\
✗ Wrong options: cast or operate before confirming

![Forced plantarflexion lateral — persistent dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4066-plantar-flexion-lateral-xray-courtesy-miller-moved.png)

![Meary and talocalcaneal angles](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4066-meary-and-talocal-labeled.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Congenital vertical talus (confirmed on forced PF lateral)
├─ Reverse-Ponseti serial casting (plantarflexion + inversion) — stretches dorsolateral tissues
└─ Then TN reduction + pinning (closed / mini-open) + percutaneous Achilles tenotomy, 6–12 mo
       ├─ Resistant / older → pantalar (extensive) release + tendon lengthening + TA to talar neck
       └─ Resistant / salvage → talectomy · triple arthrodesis (late)
```

### Treatment – reverse Ponseti casting then talonavicular pinning/Achilles tenotomy or open release

**Casting purpose** — **stretch dorsolateral soft tissues before surgery** · plantarflexion + supination/inversion until 1st MT aligns with talus\
✗ Wrong options: casting alone corrects (that's oblique talus)

**Initial management at 10 months** — **manipulation + casting**\
✗ Wrong options: shoes/scaphoid pad · immediate open reduction · LCL · observe

**After full casting course** → **TN reduction + pinning** (± Achilles tenotomy)\
✗ Wrong options: gastroc recession alone · observe · tenotomy alone · isolated tendon reconstruction

**Standard of care (Dobbs)** — **serial casting → mini-open/percutaneous TN reduction + K-wire + percutaneous Achilles tenotomy** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: casting alone · open extensive release first · subtalar fusion

**Toddler, irreducible CVT** → **casting then open reduction + Achilles lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: Ponseti alone · talectomy · subtalar fusion

### Operative criteria

| Item | Criterion |
|---|---|
| Timing | **6–12 months** (Orthobullets) · **before 2 years** (TO) |
| <2 yr | Release + tendon lengthening + TN reduction + fixation (neural tube defect: + tendon transfers) |
| >2 yr | Extensive release + tendon transfers · **bony procedures >4 yr** |
| Missed / >3 yr | Less predictable → **triple arthrodesis** salvage |
| Approach with least AVN | **Dorsal** (vs posteromedial) (TO) |
| Extensive release components | Lengthen **peroneals, Achilles, toe extensors** · reduce + pin TN · **spring ligament reconstruction** · **TA transfer to talar neck** |

## 6 Complications

**Missed CVT** — reconstruction unpredictable after **age 3** → triple arthrodesis

**Untreated** — painful rigid foot, midfoot calluses, significant disability

**Talar AVN** — less with dorsal approach

## X vs Y

| | **Congenital vertical talus** | **Congenital oblique talus** | **Calcaneovalgus** |
|---|---|---|---|
| TN joint | **Fixed dorsal dislocation** | Subluxation | Normal |
| Forced PF lateral | **Stays dislocated**, 1st MT dorsal to talar axis | **Reduces** — talus parallels 1st MT | Normal |
| Hindfoot | **Equinus**-valgus | — | **Calcaneus** (DF) |
| Rigidity | **Rigid** | Flexible | Flexible |
| Treatment | **Cast → TN pin + tenotomy** | **Stretching** / casting; rarely pin + TAL | Stretch |
| Associations | Neuromuscular / syndromic ~50% | — | Posteromedial bowing |

**Short-foot exercises** — strengthen intrinsics, raise medial arch → **flexible flatfoot** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: plantar fasciitis · tennis elbow · post-triple arthrodesis

## How it's asked

**"NOT true of CVT?"** → **rocker bottom with concave surface** (it's convex) (✓ TC angle >40°, ✓ calcaneal gait, ✓ surgery before 2 yr) (TO)

**"Heel position in CVT?"** → **equinus** (TO)

**"Diagnostic criterion?"** → **PF stress view TAMBA >35°** (TO)

**"13-month-old, TN reduces on plantarflexion lateral?"** → **congenital oblique talus**

**"NOT characteristic of CVT?"** → **posteromedial tibial bowing** association

**"9-month-old rocker-bottom feet — accurate statement?"** → **1st MT–talus relationship used before navicular ossifies**\
✗ Wrong options: reduces with PF · equinovarus · talectomy usually

**"Best study to confirm?"** → **lateral X-ray in maximum plantar flexion**

**"4-month-old arthrogrypotic, prominent talar head — next?"** → **forced plantarflexion radiograph**

**"NOT associated with CVT?"** → **amniotic band syndrome** (TO, OB)

**"Rocker-bottom feet — high association?"** → **neuromuscular disease**\
✗ Wrong options: 22q11 deletion

**"7-month-old rigid flatfeet, no subtalar motion — next?"** → **neurologic work-up with neural-axis imaging**

**"Approach with least AVN?"** → **dorsal** (TO)

**"Ideal management at 10 months?"** → **casting then mini-open reduction** (TO)

**"Why manipulate and cast first?"** → **stretch dorsolateral soft tissue before surgery**

**"Irreducible CVT in toddler?"** → **casting then open reduction + Achilles lengthening**

**"10-month-old CVT — initial management?"** → **manipulation and cast**

**"Which deformity gets serial casting then surgery?"** → **CVT**

**"After casting, next step?"** → **TN reduction and pinning**

**"7-month-old, talus–1st MT collinear on PF view — treatment?"** → **stretching** (oblique talus) (TO)

**"Short-foot exercises indicated for?"** → **flat foot** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Developmental Coxa Vara

*Pediatrics · 31 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1 in 25,000** (US) · **M = F** · presents **from walking age to 6 yr**

**Bilateral** — **1 in 3** (Orthobullets) · **30–50%** (OB)

**Genetics** — no clear inheritance

### Pathophysiology

**Defect** — **proximal femoral physis / ossification defect of inferior femoral neck** → ↓ neck-shaft angle, **vertical physis**

**Mechanics** — vertical physis → ↑ **shear** across physis + ↑ compression of inferomedial neck → progression

**Associated** — femoral neck **stress fractures** · LLD · **early OA**

### Etiologies of coxa vara

| Type | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Developmental** | This topic |
| **Congenital** | Congenital short femur, **PFFD** |
| **Acquired** | **SCFE**, infection, **Perthes**, trauma |
| **Dysplasia** | **OI**, Jansen, Schmid, **SED** |
| Other | **Cretinism** (hypothyroid) |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **painless** · **waddling / Trendelenburg gait** (abductor weakness from shortened lever)

**Exam** — **LLD**, limb shortening · **high-riding greater trochanter** · ↑ lumbar lordosis · non-tender ↓ hip ROM (esp. abduction, IR)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP hip in internal rotation** + lateral

| Finding | Value |
|---|---|
| **Neck-shaft angle** | **<120°** (varus) |
| Neck / physis | **Short neck, VERTICAL physis** (✗ Wrong options: horizontal) |
| **Hilgenreiner–epiphyseal (HE) angle** | **↑** (normal **<25°**) — Hilgenreiner line vs proximal femoral physis |
| **Inverted-Y lucency** | **Triangular metaphyseal fragment of INFERIOR neck** (✗ Wrong options: superior · upright Y) |
| Version | **↓ femoral anteversion** (✗ Wrong options: increased) |

**Southwick angle** (epiphyseal-diaphyseal on frog lateral) — ↑ in **SCFE**, not DCV

**CT (3D)** — surgical planning, bone stock, deformity orientation

![Inverted-Y ossification defect of inferior neck](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4041-xray-emedicine2-ossification-defect-moved.jpg)

![Hilgenreiner–epiphyseal angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4041-sa-hilgenreiners-physeal-angle-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Hilgenreiner-epiphyseal angle & valgus osteotomy

| HE angle | Action |
|---|---|
| **<45°** | **Unlikely to progress → observe** |
| **45–60°** | May progress → close follow-up · **operate if symptomatic** (limp, progression) (Orthobullets) or **neck-shaft <100°** (OB) |
| **>60°** | **Valgus (derotation) osteotomy** |
| Neck-shaft angle progressively **<110°** | Osteotomy (Orthobullets) |

```
Developmental coxa vara
├─ HE <45° → observe
├─ HE 45–60° → follow; osteotomy if symptomatic / progressive / NSA <100°
└─ HE >60° → valgus derotation osteotomy (overcorrect HE <38°)
       ± adductor tenotomy, GT epiphysiodesis / transfer
```

### Operative criteria

**Valgus (derotation) intertrochanteric osteotomy** — **overcorrect** → physis **horizontal**, **HE angle <38°** · correct LLD + version · restore abductor tension · blade plate · direct lateral approach · **adductor tenotomy** · spica / abduction pillow **4–6 wk**

**Bilateral disease** → **bilateral valgus osteotomies**\
✗ Wrong options: varus osteotomy · night abduction brace · fixing the metaphyseal fragment

**Greater trochanter** — **epiphysiodesis** (prevent GT overgrowth) · **distal-lateral transfer** (tip at level of head centre)

## 6 Complications

**Loss of correction / recurrence** (undercorrection) · **premature proximal femoral physeal closure** · proximal femoral overgrowth · **acetabular dysplasia**

## X vs Y

| | **Developmental coxa vara** | **SCFE** |
|---|---|---|
| Key angle | **Hilgenreiner–epiphyseal ↑** (AP) | **Southwick (epiphyseal-diaphyseal) ↑** (frog lateral) |
| Defect | Inferior-neck ossification defect, **vertical physis** | Through **hypertrophic zone** |
| Version | **↓ anteversion** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Radiographic feature of developmental coxa vara?"** → **increased Hilgenreiner–epiphyseal angle**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ anteversion · Southwick · horizontal physis · superior-neck Y

**"4-year-old waddling, HE >60° bilaterally?"** → **bilateral proximal femoral valgus osteotomies**

**"HE angle that can be observed?"** → **<45°**

**"Goal of osteotomy?"** → **overcorrect to HE <38° (horizontal physis)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Developmental Dysplasia of the Hip (DDH)

*Pediatrics · 32 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Most common orthopaedic disorder in newborns** — **dysplasia 1:100 · dislocation 1:1000** (TO answer: **1 in 1000**)

**Sex** — **female 6:1** (Orthobullets) · 8:1 (OB)

**Race** — ↑ **Native Americans, Laplanders** (swaddling hips extended + adducted) · **rare in African Americans**

**Side** — **left 60%** (left occiput anterior → left hip adducted against maternal sacrum) · **bilateral 20%**

### Risk factors & examination (Ortolani/Barlow, limited abduction)

**Risk factors** — **breech (highest relative risk, frank > footling)** · **female** (maternal relaxin/oestrogen laxity) · **firstborn** (tight uterus) · **family history** · **oligohydramnios** · macrosomia · limited abduction · talipes · swaddling\
✗ Wrong options: maternal diabetes · twins · metatarsus adductus (lesser)

**Packaging associations** — **torticollis (20%)** · **metatarsus adductus (10%)** · **congenital knee dislocation** · ↑ type III collagen conditions

### Spectrum & pathoanatomy

| Entity | Feature |
|---|---|
| Dysplasia | Shallow acetabulum (**anterior/anterolateral deficiency**; CP = posterosuperior) |
| Subluxation | Partial contact |
| Dislocation | No contact |
| **Teratologic** | Dislocated **in utero**, **irreducible** at birth, **pseudoacetabulum** · arthrogryposis, myelomeningocele, Larsen, Ehlers-Danlos |
| Late (adolescent) dysplasia | Reduced, stable, dysplastic |

**Chronic changes** — **limbus** (thickened rim cartilage) · ↑ femoral + acetabular **anteversion** · flat head · oblique roof · thick medial wall · secondary blocks (below)

### Blocks to reduction

| Extra-articular | Intra-articular |
|---|---|
| **Iliopsoas → hourglass capsule** | **Inverted labrum** (labrum deepens acetabulum 20–50%) |
| Adductor longus | **Inverted limbus** (fibrous response to superior migration) |
| — | **Transverse acetabular ligament** (contracted) |
| — | **Pulvinar** (fibrofatty; regresses after reduction) |
| — | **Hypertrophied ligamentum teres** |

## 3 Presentation

| Age | Key findings |
|---|---|
| **<3 months** | **Barlow** (adduct + depress flexed hip → **dislocates** a dislocatable hip, "clunk of exit") · **Ortolani** (abduct + elevate GT → **reduces** a dislocated hip, "clunk of entry") · Galeazzi · clicks nonspecific |
| **3–12 months** | **Limited abduction = most sensitive** · LLD · **Klisic** (GT–ASIS line points below umbilicus = dislocated; detects bilateral) |
| **>12 months / walking** | Limited abduction · **Galeazzi** · **Trendelenburg** gait · **toe-walking** (short side) · pelvic obliquity · ↑ lordosis (bilateral) · **Ortolani negative** |

**Barlow/Ortolani** — rarely positive after **~3 months** (8–12 weeks) as contractures develop

**Limited abduction** — present in ~70% of dysplastic infants but also **46% of normal** · **unilateral = specific (90%), sensitivity 70%** · **bilateral = not specific** · limited abduction with normal US → **observe**\
✗ Wrong options: adductor tenotomy · ↑ risk

![Galeazzi sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4118-galleazi-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Classification by phase** — subluxable (Barlow-suggestive) · dislocatable (**Barlow +**) · dislocated (**Ortolani +** reducible early; Ortolani − when irreducible)

**Screening** — **exam of all infants until walking** · **US at 4–6 weeks (AAP: 6 wk)** for **risk factors** (breech, family history) or abnormal exam · universal US **not cost-effective** in US · US before 4–6 wk → spurious

### Key radiology findings

**Ultrasound** — **birth to 4 months** · also to **monitor Pavlik** (in harness) · femoral head bisected by iliac line

**Radiograph** — primary after **4–6 months** (ossific nucleus visible by ~6 months in most)

| Radiographic line | Normal |
|---|---|
| **Hilgenreiner** | Horizontal through triradiates · head **inferior** |
| **Perkin** | ⟂ Hilgenreiner at lateral acetabular margin · head **medial** |
| **Shenton** | Inferior neck → superior obturator foramen · **continuous arc** |
| **Acetabular index** | **<25°** after 6 mo · mean 27.5°, **>35° dysplastic** (OB) |
| **CE angle (Wiberg)** | **<20° abnormal** · reliable only **>5 yr** |
| **Teardrop** | Present in all normal hips by **18 months** · absent before reduction of chronic dislocation · **appearance after reduction = good prognosis** · formed by quadrilateral surface + cotyloid fossa |

**Dislocated hip X-ray** — **delayed ossification** of affected head · head superolateral · broken Shenton · no teardrop\
✗ Wrong options: AI 20° · CEA 40° · alpha angle 60° (that's FAI cam)

**Klein's line** — SCFE (distractor)

**False-profile view** — best for **anterior acetabular deficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: inlet, Judet, AP, frog

**Post-reduction in spica** — **MRI (safest + accurate, no radiation)** · CT historically (selective cuts)\
✗ Wrong options: US · X-ray · arthrogram through cast

![Hilgenreiner, Perkin, Shenton lines](../../MCQ/attachments/q1585-illa-hip-ddh.jpg)

### Ultrasound – Graf classification, alpha angle & anatomy

**Alpha angle** — **lateral ilium vs bony acetabular roof** (acute angle) · **normal ≥60°** (by 6–8 wk) · defines Graf type

**Beta angle** — ilium vs **labrum** (echogenic triangle) · **normal <55°** · subtype

**US anatomy** — ilium · femoral head · triradiate · **labrum** · ischium · normal head coverage **≥50%**

| Graf | Alpha | Beta | Meaning | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **>60°** | <55° | Normal | None |
| **II** | **43–60°** | 55–77° | Immature / delayed ossification | Variable — **IIa (<3 mo): observe, repeat US**; high risk → repeat US 3 wk or Pavlik |
| **III** | **<43°** | >77° | Subluxated | **Pavlik** |
| **IV** | Unmeasurable | Unmeasurable | Dislocated | Pavlik / closed vs open reduction |

**4-week-old stable Graf IIa (α 56°, 48% coverage)** → **repeat US at 10–12 weeks** — >90% immature hips normalise\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik · night brace · MRI

![Hip ultrasound — alpha and beta angles](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4118-hip-ultrasound.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Age | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<6 months**, reducible | **Pavlik harness** (✗ Wrong options: teratologic, spina bifida, spasticity (needs normal muscle)) |
| Pavlik failed (3–4 wk) | **Semi-rigid abduction brace** 2–4 wk (weekly US) |
| **6–18 months** or brace failure | **Closed reduction + arthrogram ± adductor tenotomy + spica** |
| **>18 months** (18–24) / failed closed | **Open reduction + spica** |
| **>2 yr** | Open reduction **+ femoral shortening** ± VDRO (femoral side: anteversion, valga; best <4 yr) |
| **>2 yr, ↑ AI**, mostly >4 yr | **+ pelvic osteotomy** (AI >28° after 4 yr) |
| Bilateral **≥6 yr** / unilateral **>8 yr** | Often **leave unreduced** · epiphysiodesis for LLD |

```
DDH by age
├─ <6 mo reducible → Pavlik (US in harness at 1–2 wk)
│      └─ Not reduced by 3–4 wk → stop Pavlik → abduction brace 3–4 wk
├─ 6–18 mo or brace failure → closed reduction + arthrogram (± adductor tenotomy) + spica → MRI/CT
└─ >18 mo or failed closed → open reduction (anterior) ± femoral shortening (>2 yr) ± pelvic osteotomy
```

### Pavlik harness – indications, positioning & monitoring

**Position** — anterior straps: **hip flexion 90–100°** (prevent extension) · posterior straps: prevent adduction · **abduction 20–40°**, within **safe zone** · avoid flexion >120° or abduction >60–70°

**Wear** — **full-time (23 h/day)** ≥6 wk / until stable · wean 6–8 wk

**Monitoring** — **US in the harness at 1–2 weeks** (7–10 d) then every 4–6 wk\
✗ Wrong options: immediate US · night-time only · US out of harness · MRI/CT/X-ray

**Ortolani-positive newborn / 2-week-old** → **Pavlik** (✗ Wrong options: observe · closed reduction) · **6-week-old, α 40°, 10% coverage, Ortolani +** → **full-time Pavlik, check in 1 week** (✗ Wrong options: 3-month follow-up · double diapers)

**4-month-old, located but AI 35–40° bilaterally** → **Pavlik**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · triple diapers · reduction

**Well-positioned head at 2 wk** → **continue current position**\
✗ Wrong options: increase to 70° abduction

**Success** — **~90%** (depends on age at start, time in harness)

### Pavlik complications – femoral nerve palsy & osteonecrosis

| Position error | Complication |
|---|---|
| **Excessive flexion** (>120°) | **Femoral nerve palsy** — no active knee extension, ↓ quads/patellar reflex → **loosen anterior (flexion) strap** |
| **Excessive abduction** (>60°) | **AVN** — impinges **posterosuperior retinacular branch of MFCA** |
| Prolonged wear with unreduced hip | **Pavlik disease** — erosion of posterior/posterolateral acetabulum |

**Femoral nerve palsy risk factors** — **dislocated hip at start (independent)** · flexion >120° · **older / heavier** infants (>6 mo) · severe dysplasia → reduce flexion immediately; if persists → reduction + cast/rigid orthosis\
✗ Wrong options: younger/smaller

**Femoral nerve location** — lateral to femoral artery, anterior to iliopsoas

![Pavlik harness](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4118-prod-pavlik-harness-moved.jpg)

### Pavlik failure – abduction brace, stopping the harness & risk factors

**Stop Pavlik** if hip not reduced by **3–4 weeks** (Orthobullets) · **2–3 wk** / 2 wk (OB) → prevents **Pavlik disease**\
✗ Wrong options: increase abduction to 80° · continue and recheck · accept

**Next** — **semi-rigid / rigid abduction orthosis** 2–4 wk with weekly US (applied in office, no anaesthesia) · **93% success** protocol (Swaroop-Mubarak) · then closed ± open reduction\
✗ Wrong options: immediate arthrogram/open reduction · pelvic osteotomy

**Pavlik failure risk factors** — **Graf IV** (most) · **Ortolani-negative** · **start >7 weeks** · **right-sided**\
✗ Wrong options: sex · unilateral

### Closed reduction & spica – arthrogram, safe zone & post-reduction imaging

**4-month-old failed Pavlik 3 wk + brace 3 wk** → **closed reduction + arthrogram ± adductor tenotomy + spica**\
✗ Wrong options: harness at 75° · osteotomies

**Technique** — GA · Ortolani reduction (gentle; force → AVN) · **arthrogram** confirms concentric reduction

**Medial dye pool** — **<5 mm** good (5–6 mm acceptable) · **>6 mm** (OB) / **>7 mm** (Orthobullets) → failure + **AVN** · 17-month-old with **8 mm** pool → **inappropriate**

**Safe zone** — arc between max abduction and redislocation adduction · **≥25° arc** · **adductor tenotomy** if excessive abduction needed

**Spica** — **"human position"**: **100° flexion, 45° abduction (<55°)**, neutral rotation · 3 months · change at 6 wk

**Post-op** — **MRI or CT** in cast (✗ Wrong options: X-ray before discharge) · CT shows posterior subluxation → **urgent repeat closed reduction + recast** (✗ Wrong options: wait 6 wk · osteotomy at 8 mo)

**Arthrogram signs** — **hourglass capsule = iliopsoas constriction** · inverted limbus (rim) · pulvinar · TAL

![Hourglass capsule on arthrogram](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4118-hour-glass.jpg)

### Open reduction ± femoral shortening/pelvic osteotomy (older children)

**Indications** — **>18 months** · failed closed reduction · **reduction only in >60° abduction** (e.g. 70–80°) · unstable/persistent subluxation

**Approach** — **anterior (Smith-Petersen)**: most common, **>12 mo**, allows **capsulorrhaphy**, less MFCA risk · **medial**: <12 mo, addresses blocks directly, less blood loss, **no capsulorrhaphy, ↑ AVN**

| Medial approach | Interval |
|---|---|
| Ludloff | Pectineus ↔ adductor longus/brevis (Orthobullets) |
| Weinstein (anteromedial) | NV bundle ↔ pectineus |
| Ferguson (posteromedial) | Adductor longus ↔ gracilis; deep brevis ↔ magnus |

**Artery at risk with psoas tenotomy (medial)** — **MFCA** — usually from **profunda femoris** (sometimes femoral artery), passes between **psoas and pectineus** to posterior neck\
✗ Wrong options: superior gluteal · LFCA ascending branch · SFA · internal iliac branch

**Post-op position** — **30° flexion, 30° abduction, 30° IR**

**Femoral shortening** — **>2 yr** → ↓ tension, ↓ **AVN + redislocation** · VDRO corrects anteversion/valga

**26-month-old ambulatory, irreducible, severe dysplasia, ↑ IR/↓ ER (anteversion)** → **open reduction + iliac osteotomy + femoral shortening derotation + spica** · 22-mo, mild dysplasia, normal rotation → open reduction + spica

**2-yr, dislocated + valgus neck** → **open reduction + femoral shortening**\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik · brace · closed reduction · **abductor** tenotomy

**20-month-old, concentric only at 70–80° abduction** → **open capsulorrhaphy ± femoral ± pelvic osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: closed reduction (AVN) · hamstring/gastroc releases (spastic)

### Residual dysplasia – pelvic osteotomies (Salter, Chiari, shelf, PAO)

| Osteotomy | Requirement / indication | Key feature |
|---|---|---|
| **Salter** | Young, **open triradiate**, concentric | Single iliac cut to notch · **hinges on pubic symphysis** · anterolateral coverage · lengthens ≤1 cm |
| **Triple (Steele)** | Older child (stiff symphysis), open triradiate | Salter + superior + inferior rami |
| **PAO (Ganz)** | **Triradiate CLOSED** (adolescent/adult) | Pubis, ilium, ischium cuts · 3D correction · **posterior column intact** · early WB |
| **Pemberton** | **Open triradiate**, moderate–severe, most versatile | **Hinges at triradiate** (posteriorly) · **↓ acetabular volume** · no fixation · bilateral one sitting OK |
| **Dega** | **Neuromuscular (CP)**, posterior deficiency | Incomplete trans-iliac, hinge triradiate · ↓ volume · anterior/central/**posterior** coverage |
| Dial | Rare, difficult | Spherical, intra-articular, teardrop stays |
| **Shelf (Staheli)** | **Salvage, >8 yr** · **no concentric reduction needed** | Extra-articular buttress · **fibrocartilage** |
| **Chiari** | **Salvage — incongruous / non-concentric hip** (CEA ≥−10°) | Iliac osteotomy, medialise acetabulum · **fibrocartilage** |

**4-yr after closed reduction, AI 35°, normal femur** → **iliac (Pemberton/Dega) osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: PAO (open triradiate) · + femoral osteotomy

**15-yr soccer player, closed triradiate** → **PAO (Ganz)** · 20-yr → **PAO**

**Painful incongruous hip on abduction-IR view** → **Chiari**

**Reorientation osteotomies require concentric reduction** — all except **shelf**

**Pemberton true statement** — **hinges at triradiate** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: can't do bilateral · needs pins · volume unchanged

### Teratologic & neuromuscular hips

**Teratologic (amyoplasia newborn, stiff clubfeet, Ortolani −, pseudoacetabulum)** → **no hip treatment/imaging for months** · open reduction at **≥6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik · rigid brace · neonatal CT

**CP hip deficiency** — **posterosuperior** (adductor + iliopsoas pull)

## 6 Complications

**AVN** — all treatments · risks: **forceful/extreme abduction (>60°)** · **failed prior closed treatment** · repeat surgery · medial dye pool >7 mm · signs: no ossific nucleus growth 1 yr after reduction, broad neck, dense/fragmented head

**Recurrence** — ~**10%** → follow to maturity

**Transient femoral nerve palsy** — Pavlik hyperflexion

## X vs Y

| | **Barlow** | **Ortolani** |
|---|---|---|
| Manoeuvre | **Adduct + push posterior** | **Abduct + lift GT** |
| Result | **Dislocates** dislocatable hip | **Reduces** dislocated hip |
| Sound | Clunk of exit | Clunk of entry |

| | **Typical DDH** | **Teratologic** |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | Postnatal / perinatal | **In utero** |
| Reducible | Yes (Ortolani +) | **No** |
| Pavlik | Yes | **No** |
| Associations | Packaging | Arthrogryposis, myelomeningocele, Larsen |

## How it's asked

**"Incidence of DDH?"** → **1 in 1000** (TO)

**"DDH >12 months — finding NOT expected?"** → **positive Ortolani**

**"3-month-old, bilateral limited abduction, normal US — true?"** → **unilateral limited abduction is specific; bilateral is not**

**"Manoeuvre shown — reducing the hip?"** → **Ortolani**

**"Highest risk factor?"** → **frank breech**

**"18-month-old toe-walking, short left leg, limited abduction — X-ray?"** → **delayed ossification of left femoral head**

**"Alpha angle defined as?"** → **lateral ilium vs bony acetabular roof** (TO)

**"Follow-up imaging in Pavlik?"** → **ultrasound** (TO)

**"Labrum on US?"** → **echogenic triangle at acetabular rim**

**"Which angle is alpha?"** → **ilium + bony acetabulum**

**"Breech baby US at 6 weeks — left α <60°?"** → **right normal, left dysplastic**

**"28-day-old breech, α 48°, β 72°?"** → **Graf II — repeat US in 3 weeks or Pavlik**

**"Structure B on infant US?"** → **labrum**

**"4-week-old, α 56°, stable?"** → **repeat US at 10–12 weeks**

**"Which figure is Perkin's line?"** → **vertical line at lateral acetabular margin**

**"True of pediatric hips?"** → **ossific nucleus visible by 6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: anteversion 10–20° (it's 30–40°) · SCFE in proliferative zone · separate physes

**"Teardrop — true?"** → **present by 18 months in normal hips**

**"Line #1 along femoral neck to obturator?"** → **Shenton's line**

**"2-week-old Ortolani +?"** → **Pavlik harness**

**"Pavlik started — imaging at 2 weeks?"** → **ultrasound**

**"Pavlik placed — next?"** → **full-time, US in harness at 7–10 days**

**"Good position on 2-week US?"** → **continue harness**

**"6-week-old, α 40°, 10% coverage?"** → **full-time Pavlik, follow-up 1 week**

**"4-month-old located, AI 35–40°?"** → **Pavlik harness**

**"Can't kick, knee flexed, Pavlik flexion 125°?"** → **excessive hip flexion → femoral nerve palsy**

**"Diminished knee extension in Pavlik — fix?"** → **loosen anterior flexion strap**

**"No active knee extension at 120° flexion?"** → **improper harness positioning**

**"Structure responsible for no knee extension?"** → **femoral nerve**

**"Position–complication pairs?"** → **flexion → femoral nerve palsy; abduction → osteonecrosis**

**"Femoral nerve palsy risk factor?"** → **dislocated hip at diagnosis**

**"Pavlik 3 weeks, still dislocated at 4 weeks of age?"** → **semi-rigid abduction brace**

**"Semi-rigid brace after Pavlik failure?"** → **can achieve stable hip without surgery**

**"8-week-old, 4 weeks Pavlik, still dislocated?"** → **rigid abduction orthosis + weekly US**

**"Persistent dislocation at 3 weeks in harness?"** → **remove harness** (avoid acetabular deformity)

**"Dislocated after 3 weeks Pavlik (1-week-old start)?"** → **switch to abduction orthosis**

**"Graf IV — biggest Pavlik failure risk?"** → **Graf classification**

**"Failed Pavlik + brace at 4 months?"** → **closed reduction + arthrogram ± adductor tenotomy + spica**

**"Best imaging after closed reduction in spica?"** → **MRI**

**"Stable in 50° abduction, <5 mm pool?"** → **spica + post-reduction CT/MRI**

**"CT shows posterior subluxation in spica?"** → **urgent repeat closed reduction + recast**

**"Inappropriate age-based treatment?"** → **17 months, closed reduction with 8 mm dye pool**

**"Arrows on arthrogram (hourglass)?"** → **capsule constricted by iliopsoas**

**"Inverted limbus on arthrogram?"** → **rim structure pointed at acetabular edge**

**"20-month-old, reduced only at 70–80° abduction?"** → **open capsulorrhaphy ± femoral ± pelvic osteotomy**

**"24-month-old dislocated?"** → **open reduction ± pelvic/femoral osteotomy + spica**

**"26-month-old complete dislocation?"** → **open reduction + femoral shortening + capsulorrhaphy + spica**

**"2-yr dislocated, valgus neck?"** → **open reduction + femoral shortening**

**"Artery at risk with medial psoas tenotomy?"** → **MFCA (from profunda femoris)**

**"Open reduction + iliac osteotomy + femoral derotation shortening fits?"** → **26-month ambulatory, severe dysplasia, ↑ IR**

**"Pemberton — true?"** → **hinges at triradiate cartilage** (TO)

**"15-yr, closed triradiate?"** → **Ganz PAO**

**"4-yr residual dysplasia AI 35°?"** → **iliac (Pemberton/Dega) osteotomy**

**"Incongruous painful hip?"** → **Chiari**

**"No concentric reduction needed?"** → **Staheli shelf**

**"View for anterior deficiency?"** → **false profile**

**"4-yr residual left dysplasia after closed reduction?"** → **left pelvic osteotomy**

**"20-yr symptomatic dysplasia?"** → **PAO**

**"CP dislocated hip — deficient quadrant?"** → **posterior-superior**

**"Newborn amyoplasia, teratologic dislocation?"** → **no hip treatment/imaging for months**
