# Diastrophic Dysplasia

*Pediatrics · 33 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — rare · **Finland** (carrier **1 in 70** Finns)

### SLC26A2 sulfate transporter & phenotype

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal recessive** (both parents carriers)

**Gene** — **DTDST = SLC26A2**, **chromosome 5q** → defective **sulfate transporter** → **undersulfation of cartilage proteoglycan** → abnormal cartilage matrix (ear, trachea, ligaments)

**Ossification** — failure of secondary ossification centres (OB)

**No commercial test** — many mutations → clinical + radiographic + family history · prenatal US possible

| Gene (distractors) | Disorder |
|---|---|
| **SLC26A2 / DTDST** | **Diastrophic dysplasia** ✓ |
| FGFR3 | Achondroplasia, hypochondroplasia |
| FGFR2 | Apert (craniosynostosis + complex syndactyly) |
| CBFA-1 / RUNX2 | Cleidocranial dysplasia (osteocalcin) |
| Gs α (GNAS) | Fibrous dysplasia |
| COL1A1/2 | OI (blue sclera, bowing) |
| COL5A1/2 | Ehlers-Danlos (classic) |
| Lysosomal storage | Hunter, Hurler |
| Lipid storage | Gaucher |

## 3 Presentation

**"Twisted dwarf"** — **short-limbed** (rhizomelic) dwarfism, diagnosed at birth

| Feature | Note |
|---|---|
| **Cauliflower ears** | **80%** · cystic swelling of pinnae · responds to early **compressive bandages** |
| **Hitchhiker thumb** | **Short 1st MC + radial subluxation of MCP** (abducted) |
| **Cleft palate** | **60%** (59%) |
| **Cervical kyphosis** | **Severe**, early · often **resolves spontaneously** |
| Thoracolumbar kyphoscoliosis | Progressive |
| Feet | **Rigid clubfeet (equinocavovarus)** · **skewfoot** (TMT adductus + hindfoot valgus) · tarsal valgus 43%, MT adductus 37% |
| Lower limb | **Genu valgum** (± dislocated patellae) · hip + knee contractures · hip dislocation |
| Hand | Symphalangism (stiff fingers) |
| Other | **Tracheomalacia** (can be fatal) · atlantoaxial instability (rare) |

![Hitchhiker thumb](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4097-hitchhikers-thumb-colorado-moved.jpg)

![Cauliflower ear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4097-cauliflower-ear-colorado-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral **cervical** and **thoracolumbar** spine · standing 3-joint lower-limb films

![Cervical kyphosis in diastrophic dysplasia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4097-cervical-kyphosis-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Most** — observation + supportive

### Operative criteria

| Problem | Surgery |
|---|---|
| **Atlantoaxial instability + neuro symptoms** | **Occipitocervical fusion** (quadriplegia risk) |
| Cervical kyphosis **not resolving** | Posterior cervical fusion |
| Progressive TL kyphoscoliosis | ASF / PSF |
| Foot deformity after casting · severe hip/knee contractures | Soft-tissue release |
| Progressive valgus, resistant clubfeet, fixed knee flexion, early hip subluxation | Osteotomies (distal femoral extension, valgus intertrochanteric ± acetabuloplasty) |
| End-stage hip OA | THA |

## How it's asked

**"Newborn hitchhiker thumb, SLC26A2 mutation, carrier parents?"** → **diastrophic dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: achondroplasia · OI · hypochondroplasia · EDS

**"Pathoanatomy of hitchhiker thumb + cauliflower ear dwarfism?"** → **sulfate transporter mutation affecting cartilage matrix**\
✗ Wrong options: CBFA-1 · lysosomal · lipid storage · FGFR3

**"Genetic cause of hitchhiker thumb, cauliflower ears, severe clubfeet?"** → **sulfate transport protein**\
✗ Wrong options: FGFR2 · FGFR3 · CBFA-1 · Gs α

**"DTDST defect phenotype?"** → **cauliflower ears + hitchhiker thumb**\
✗ Wrong options: blue sclera · MR + cardiac = Down · craniosynostosis + syndactyly = Apert · rhizomelia + frontal bossing = achondroplasia

**"Spine region most deformed?"** → **cervical kyphosis** (often resolves)

**"Treatment of cauliflower ears?"** → **early compressive bandaging**

# Distal Clavicle Physeal Fractures

*Pediatrics · 34 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — rare · **5–10%** of pediatric clavicle fractures

**Equivalent** — childhood **"pseudodislocation"** = adult **AC separation**

### Pathoanatomy – intact periosteal sleeve

**Injury** — clavicle (metaphysis) displaces **superiorly** out of its **periosteal sleeve**, through the **physis** · **inferior periosteal sleeve + epiphysis stay attached to intact CC + AC ligaments** · **AC joint intact**

**Healing** — new clavicle forms **within the sleeve** → **"Y-shaped" clavicle**; displaced fragment remodels/resorbs

### Anatomy & ossification

**Clavicle** — **first bone to ossify** (5th week in utero, intramembranous) · **physes last to close**

**Medial physis** — **80% of growth** · ossifies **18–20 yr** · **last physis to close (20–25 yr)** → teenage "SC dislocations" are **physeal fracture-dislocations**

**Lateral epiphysis** — ossifies ~18 yr

**CC ligaments** — vertical stability · **trapezoid** 2 cm, **conoid** 4 cm from AC joint · **AC ligaments** — horizontal stability

## 2 Mechanism

**Fall on side of shoulder** / outstretched arm · direct blow · rarely **child abuse**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, distal clavicle tenderness, deformity, ecchymosis · rarely tenting

**Newborn** — **pseudoparalysis** with **intact reflexes** (vs brachial plexus palsy)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP ± Zanca** (intra-articular extension) · **axillary lateral** (type IV posterior displacement)

**Later** — new bone from intact sleeve **inferior** to the superiorly displaced fragment

![Distal clavicle physeal fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4122-distal-clavicle-physeal-xray.jpg)

### Rockwood (Dameron-Rockwood) classification

| Type | Injury |
|---|---|
| I | AC ligament sprain, **periosteal tube intact** |
| II | Partial periosteal tube disruption |
| III | Large split, **superior** displacement |
| IV | **Posterior** displacement through **trapezius** |
| V | Complete disruption, through deltoid + trapezius |
| VI | **Inferior**, below coracoid |

## 5 Management

### Pseudodislocation – intact periosteal sleeve & sling treatment

**Most (even displaced, elite athletes)** → **sling immobilization**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · plate · CC reconstruction/suture · stress views · MRI

![Remodelling within intact periosteal sleeve](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4122-periosteal-sleeve-healing-pediatric-clavicle-fracture.jpg)

### Operative criteria

| Absolute (rare) | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Open fracture** | Severely displaced, **near physeal closure** |
| **Skin compromise** | Fragment entrapped in **trapezius** |
| **Displaced intra-articular extension** | Floating shoulder |
| **NV injury needing surgery** | Some type III near maturity · **types IV–VI** (open reduction + sleeve repair) |

**Avoid pins** around clavicle (**migration**)

## 6 Complications

**Subclavian vessel laceration** — rare · **rapidly expanding hematoma** → vascular repair (thick periosteum protective)

**Nonunion** — rare, after open reduction → fixation + iliac crest graft

## How it's asked

**"6-yr distal clavicle fracture, lateral fragment superior?"** → **sling**

**"16-yr hockey player, likely injury pattern?"** → **physeal separation, intact inferior sleeve, intact CC + AC**

**"12-yr elite gymnast, distal clavicle physeal fracture?"** → **sling**\
✗ Wrong options: stress X-ray · MRI · fixation

**"MRI of 11-yr distal clavicle fracture shows?"** → **intact inferior periosteal sleeve**\
✗ Wrong options: CC disruption · AC dislocation

**"Last physis to close?"** → **medial clavicle (20–25 yr)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Distal Femoral Physeal Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 35 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Who** — open physes · often **misdiagnosed as collateral ligament sprain** · 2–5% of physeal injuries (<2% OB)

**Most common type** — **Salter-Harris II** (physis fails on **tension** side, metaphysis on **compression** side → **Thurston-Holland**)

### Anatomy

**Ossification** — single nucleus present **at birth** — **first epiphysis to ossify**

**Physis** — **undulating** (mammillary processes) → fracture crosses **multiple layers** → high arrest rate

**Muscles** — gastrocnemius + plantaris arise just proximal → **flex** distal fragment

**Ligaments** — collaterals + cruciates attach to **epiphysis** → stress transmitted to physis (ligament injury uncommon)

**Vessels** — **popliteal artery directly posterior**, trifurcates here, poor collaterals → limb-threatening

### Growth

| Physis | Growth / yr |
|---|---|
| Proximal femur | 3 mm |
| **Distal femur** | **9 mm** (Orthobullets) · **10 mm** (TO) · 10–12 mm at peak |
| Proximal tibia | 6 mm |
| Distal tibia | 5 mm |

**Distal femur** — **70% of femoral growth, 37% of limb** (TO) · most lower-limb growth at the **knee**

**Growth stops** — **14 yr girls, 16 yr boys** (Orthobullets) · fuses 14–16 ♀ / 16–18 ♂ (TO)

### Physeal histology – hypertrophic zone

**Fracture plane** — **hypertrophic zone** (chondrocytes **enlarge**, then **apoptosis** + matrix calcification in zone of provisional calcification)\
✗ Wrong options: proliferative (cells ↑ in number, max matrix) · reserve (precursors) · primary spongiosa (woven bone) · secondary spongiosa (lamellar)

**Distal femur SH I** — **propagates through multiple layers** (undulating physis) (OB)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — direct trauma + rotation · **valgus** or **hyperextension** force · sports, vehicle

**Displacement direction** — anterior = hyperextension · posterior = hyperflexion · medial = valgus · lateral = varus

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, can't bear weight · knee held **flexed** (hamstring spasm) · **tenderness over physis** (medial epicondyle region) · effusion · varus/valgus "instability" · popliteal swelling → vascular injury

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – occult Salter-Harris I, MRI & imaging

**Initial** — **AP, lateral, oblique** knee\
✗ Wrong options: MRI first · stress views · CT · notch view

**Physeal widening** — normal 3–5 mm · compare contralateral

**Stress radiographs** — **fallen out of favour** (pain, sedation, physeal damage) → **MRI** (or US) replaced them

**MRI** — **modality of choice to confirm** occult physeal fracture\
✗ Wrong options: CT · weight-bearing views · aspiration/NSAID trial

**CT** — intra-articular extension, fixation planning

**Angiography** — **ABI <0.9** · wide displacement, posterior spike

**Alternative** — suspected SH I → long-leg cast + repeat X-ray 2–3 wk

### Key radiology findings

**Peterson classification (TO)** — I metaphyseal transverse into physis · II SH II equivalent · III through physis · IV epiphyseal along physis · V traverses all · **VI part of physis missing**

![Distal femoral SH II fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4020-distal-femoral-physeal-fracture.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Fracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Nondisplaced** (incl. occult SH I) | **Long-leg cast 4–6 wk**, crutches, close follow-up (✗ Wrong options: hinged brace WBAT · immobiliser) |
| **Displaced SH I / II** (majority) | **Closed reduction + percutaneous fixation + cast** (unstable even if reduced) (✗ Wrong options: cast alone · traction · plate) |
| SH III / IV with anatomic closed reduction | CRPP |
| **SH III / IV** with articular displacement | **ORIF** (avoid physis) |
| **Irreducible SH I / II** | **Open reduction** — remove **periosteum on tension side** |

```
Distal femoral physeal fracture
├─ Nondisplaced → long-leg cast 4–6 wk, close follow-up
└─ Displaced → gentle reduction under GA (90% traction, 10% manipulation)
       ├─ SH I / SH II with small T-H fragment → smooth K-wires across physis (remove 3–6 wk)
       ├─ SH II with large T-H fragment → metaphyseal lag screws (avoid physis)
       └─ SH III / IV → epiphyseal lag screws (open if needed)
```

### Fixation – smooth pins vs screws (Thurston-Holland fragment)

**Pins vs screws** — decided by **size of Thurston-Holland fragment** · large → **metaphyseal lag screws** · small / SH I → **smooth K-wires across physis** + supplemental cast

**SH I** — very unstable → **anatomic reduction + smooth pins + cast**\
✗ Wrong options: screws (↑ physeal injury) · blade plate · flexible nails

**Avoid** — threaded wires, screws, plates across physis (rigid tether)

**Pin direction** — retrograde (through joint) theoretical ↑ septic arthritis (no proven difference)

**Screws** — fully vs partially threaded: no difference with lag technique

![Smooth-wire fixation of distal femoral physeal fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4020-anterograde-pinning-of-a-distal-femur-physeal-fracture-with-.jpg)

### Vascular injury

**Pulses only on Doppler / ABI 0.8** → **urgent reduction + (percutaneous) fixation, then reassess** vascular status\
✗ Wrong options: arteriogram/CT/MRI first · cast · fasciotomy first · vascular exploration before reduction · plate across physis

**Highest risk** — **anterior epiphyseal displacement (hyperextension)** / **posterior metaphyseal spike** → treat like **knee dislocation** · arterial injury up to 20% in some series · compartment syndrome <1–3%

## 6 Complications

### Growth arrest risk – LLD & angular deformity

**Most common complication** — **physeal arrest → LLD / angular deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion (rare) · ligament injury · stiffness (transient) · AVN

**Physeal arrest rate** — **30–50%** (Orthobullets, OB "~40%") · **~60%** (OB) · 50–55% / 50–80% (OB) — read the options

**LLD >2 cm** — **one-third** of cases

| SH type | Growth disturbance |
|---|---|
| I | 36% |
| II | 58% |
| III | 49% |
| **IV** | **64% — highest** (✗ Wrong options: "type III maximal" (TO false statement)) |

**Pattern** — **SH I + V → LLD (non-angular)** · **SH II → angular deformity**

**Predictors of complications** — SH type · **displacement** · **hardware crossing physis** · articular incongruity · open fracture · surgical treatment · **younger age / less skeletal maturity** (tibial tuberosity ossification stage) · **NOT direction of displacement**

**Rate of arrest ↑** with displacement; older age (OB)

### LLD calculation

**Complete arrest LLD** = **remaining years × ~9–10 mm** · e.g. 11-yr girl (stops 14) → **3 cm** · 13-yr boy with 2 cm LLD (stops 16) → 20 + 27 = **~4.7–5 cm**, affected side short

### Physeal bar – resection

```
Post-traumatic distal femoral bar / LLD
├─ Predicted LLD <2 cm, no angulation → observe
├─ Bar <50%, ≥2 yr (2–2.5 cm) growth left → bar resection + PMMA / fat
│      (angulation >20° → add osteotomy)
└─ LLD 2–6 cm → contralateral distal femur ± proximal tibia epiphysiodesis
       (>5 cm → ipsilateral lengthening)
```

**11-yr, 5° varus, 25% bar, rest open** → **bar resection + PMMA** · results 62–90% good\
✗ Wrong options: observe · osteotomy · guided growth · epiphysiodesis

**12-yr girl after fixation (~2 yr growth left)** → **physeal disturbance — close observation** (predicted LLD <2 cm)\
✗ Wrong options: contralateral epiphysiodesis · lengthening

**Septic arthritis** — **intra-articular pins**

## X vs Y

| | **Smooth K-wires** | **Lag screws** |
|---|---|---|
| Use | **SH I**, SH II with **small T-H** fragment | SH II **large T-H** (metaphyseal), SH III/IV (epiphyseal) |
| Physis | Cross it (smooth) | **Parallel, avoid physis** |
| Removal | 3–6 wk | — |

## How it's asked

**"NOT true about distal femoral physeal fracture?"** → **type 3 has maximum LLD risk** (it's type 4) (TO)

**"Risk factors for complications — EXCEPT?"** → **direction of displacement**

**"SH II treated with lag screws — least predictive of outcome?"** → **direction of displacement**

**"11-yr girl with complete arrest — LLD?"** → **3 cm**

**"Closed reduction + cast at rural hospital — main complication?"** → **limb angular deformity**

**"Prognosis?"** → **high risk of premature arrest that frequently causes deformity**

**"13-yr fell from truck, SH II — likely complication?"** → **growth arrest**

**"Physeal arrest risk after operative SH II?"** → **~60%** (OB) · **~40%** (other OB)

**"12-yr after fixation — expect and do?"** → **growth disturbance, observe**

**"13-yr with 2 cm LLD, 3 yr left — final LLD?"** → **~5 cm, affected side short**

**"10-yr 'knee sprain', MRI SH I — long-term sequel?"** → **leg length discrepancy**

**"More skeletally mature child (ossified tuberosity)?"** → **lower risk of angular deformity needing surgery**

**"25% bar, 5° varus, open physis?"** → **bar resection + PMMA**

**"13-yr displaced SH II?"** → **closed reduction + percutaneous fixation**

**"10-yr SH I?"** → **anatomic reduction + smooth pins + cast**

**"Which injury gets smooth K-wires?"** → **SH I**

**"True about SH II treatment?"** → **Thurston-Holland size decides pins vs screws**

**"Undisplaced SH I with physeal widening?"** → **cast + close follow-up**

**"Initial imaging for twisting knee injury in 9-yr?"** → **AP, lateral, oblique X-rays**

**"Effusion, physeal tenderness, normal X-ray?"** → **MRI**

**"Suspected SH I with normal films — confirm?"** → **MRI**

**"Nondisplaced medial distal femoral physeal injury?"** → **long-leg cast + crutches**

**"SH I of distal femur — fracture location?"** → **propagates through multiple layers**

**"MRI-confirmed physeal injury — zone?"** → **where chondrocytes grow in size (hypertrophic)**

**"Zone injured — what happens there?"** → **chondrocyte apoptosis**

**"Pulses only on Doppler?"** → **reduce + fix fracture, then reassess**

**"ABI 0.8 with displaced SH II?"** → **reduction + percutaneous fixation, then reassess**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Distal Humerus Physeal Separation - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 36 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Also called** — **transphyseal distal humerus fracture**

**Age** — **<3 years** (physis weaker than bone–ligament interface → physeal fracture, not dislocation)

**Mechanism** — **birth trauma** (vaginal delivery, shoulder dystocia; C-section traction) · **non-accidental trauma** (rotational/twisting shear — **up to 50% battered child**) · FOOSH, fall from bed/couch

**Displacement** — **posteromedial** (most) · rarely anterior

| Age | SH type |
|---|---|
| **<3 yr** | **SH I** (pure physeal) |
| >3 yr | SH II (metaphyseal fragment) |
| Rare | SH III/IV — mimics lateral condyle fracture |

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **pseudoparalysis** / not using arm · swelling, ecchymosis · NV compromise rare

**NAT red flags** — unwitnessed, inconsistent history, multiple injuries, burns, bites, bruises (**skin lesions most common NAT finding**)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis & non-accidental trauma association (skeletal survey)

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP + lateral centred on elbow** · stress views may help · **skeletal survey if abuse suspected**\
✗ Wrong options: "babygram" (misses it)

**Findings** — **radius + ulna displaced posteromedially relative to humerus** (may be the only sign in infants) · **forearm not aligned with humeral shaft** · **radiocapitellar alignment maintained** (capitellum stays with radius — definitive if ossified) · effusion, swelling

**Capitellum ossifies** — **1–2 yr** (first elbow centre)

**Ultrasound** — uncertain dx, **no sedation** · static (cartilage absent on metaphysis) / dynamic instability

**Arthrogram** — uncertain dx, in OR with CRPP · **direct posterior portal** (olecranon fossa) in young · shows pin start on capitellum + anterior humeral line

**MRI** — not routine (sedation)

**Missed** — up to **50%** by radiologists · delayed up to 1 wk

![Posteromedial displacement of forearm on humerus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4006-img-2100.jpg)

![Transphyseal distal humerus fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4006-xray-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Transphyseal distal humerus fracture (<3 yr)
├─ Suspect NAT → skeletal survey + child protective services
├─ Displaced (most) → arthrogram + closed reduction + percutaneous pinning
└─ Nondisplaced / late presentation → long-arm splint/cast 2–3 wk
       (late: deformity → osteotomy later)
```

**After immobilisation, 8-month-old** → **contact child protective services**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · CT elbow/head · emergent fixation (not emergent without open/NV injury)

**NAT team consult** — most likely for **distal humerus physeal separation in 16-month-old**\
✗ Wrong options: BBFF 5-yr · type II supracondylar 4-yr · femur 10-yr · Tillaux 13-yr

### Treatment – closed reduction & pinning

**Displaced (even if NAT ruled out)** → **CRPP** under GA with **arthrogram**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · sling · rigid immobilisation · compartment monitoring · ORIF / plate · hinged ex-fix

**Reduction** — gentle traction (little force) → correct translation/rotation → flex

**Acceptable** — **no cubitus varus** · **anterior humeral line bisects capitellum** · no malrotation

**Pins** — **2–3 × 0.062" lateral, divergent**, bicortical, wide spread · test under live fluoro

**Post-op** — splint · X-ray 1 wk · **remove pins at 3 wk** · follow **2–4 yr** (arrest, deformity, AVN)

**Nonop** — limited: **nondisplaced** · **late-presenting** (→ later osteotomy)

## 6 Complications

**Cubitus varus** — **up to 70%** — **more common than supracondylar** · from **medial condyle AVN**, malunion (missed/lost reduction), growth arrest → **lateral closing-wedge osteotomy**

**Medial/lateral condyle AVN** — fishtail deformity

**Growth disturbance** — observe, operate **>5 yr** (bigger limb, cooperative, one-stage)

**Loss of motion** — rarely functional

**NOT associated** — **tardy ulnar nerve palsy** (→ lateral condyle nonunion + cubitus valgus)

## X vs Y

| | **Transphyseal separation** | **Elbow dislocation** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **<3 yr** | Older (almost never <3) |
| Displacement | **Posteromedial** | **Posterolateral** |
| Radiocapitellar line | **Intact** | Disrupted |
| NAT | **Strong association** | — |

## How it's asked

**"10-month-old, posteromedial displacement — all EXCEPT?"** → **tardy ulnar nerve palsy** (✓ abuse, cubitus varus, medial condyle AVN)

**"Injury most commonly from child abuse?"** → **distal humerus physeal separation**\
✗ Wrong options: Monteggia · proximal femur · toddler's · supracondylar

**"8-month-old after immobilisation — next?"** → **contact child protective services**

**"2-year-old, no injury history, transphyseal separation — true?"** → **skeletal survey**\
✗ Wrong options: posterolateral · nonop reliable · 6–8 yr · incarcerated fragment (medial epicondyle)

**"NAT team present — your consult?"** → **distal humerus physeal separation in 16-month-old**

**"7-month-old swollen elbow, forearm displaced on humerus?"** → **closed reduction + percutaneous pinning**

**"12-month-old, NAT ruled out?"** → **operative percutaneous pinning**

**"9-month-old fell down steps?"** → **CRPP in OR, immobilise 3–4 wk**

**"8-month-old displaced transphyseal fracture?"** → **closed reduction with pinning**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Distal Radius Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 37 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology – most common paediatric fracture

**Most common fracture <16 yr** — **distal radius (22.7%)** > hand phalanges (18.9%) > carpal/MC (8.3%) > clavicle (8.1%) > supracondylar (3.3%) > distal femur (1.6%)

**Forearm** — ~40% of pediatric long-bone fractures (OB: distal radius = 40% of all pediatric fractures) · **M 2–3×** · peak **10–12 yr ♀, 12–14 yr ♂** (metaphyseal growth spurt)

**Lifetime fracture risk <16 yr** — boys 42%, girls 27%

### Anatomy & remodelling

**Distal radial physis** — **75% of radial growth**, **40% of upper limb** · ~**5.25 mm/yr**

**Location** — **metaphysis 62%** > diaphysis 20% > physis

**Remodelling** — greatest **near physis** + in **plane of joint motion (sagittal — flexion/extension)** · ~**1°/month** · **least for rotation** (rotation doesn't remodel)

## 2 Mechanism

**FOOSH**, wrist extended · rule out **abuse** (inconsistent history, multiple-age injuries, grip marks)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

| Type | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Torus / buckle** | **Unicortical**, nondisplaced, compression side |
| **Greenstick** | Fails on **tension (convex)** side; plastic deformation on compression side |
| Plastic deformation | Many microfractures, lower force over time |
| Complete metaphyseal | Apex volar (Colles-type) / apex dorsal (Smith-type) |
| Physeal | SH I–V (SH II most common) |
| **Galeazzi** | Distal-third radius + **DRUJ** injury |
| **Monteggia** | Ulna + radiocapitellar dislocation |

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral **wrist** and **forearm** · elbow if tender or diaphyseal · check SL interval, **DRUJ**, ulnar styloid

**CT** — intra-articular only (radiation)

![Torus (buckle) fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4014-torus-fx.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Remodelling potential & acceptable angulation (buckle fractures need no follow-up)

| Age | Bayonet | Shaft angulation | Malrotation | **Distal radius dorsal angulation** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **<10 yr** | **<1 cm** | 15–20° | 45° | **30°** |
| **>10 yr** | **None** | 10° | 30° | **20°** |

**Other cut-offs** — young child remodels sagittal deformity up to **~20°**, older **~10°** (OB) · operate <9 yr if post-reduction **dorsal angulation >30°** or malrotation · >10 yr: angulation >20°, rotation >30°

**Diaphyseal 10° angulation** → blocks 20–30° rotation

**4-yr, 12° apex volar at 2 weeks** → **observe — remodels within ~1 year**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoclasis · re-reduction · 2 years

**9-yr, 18° unreduced metaphyseal deformity** → **no long-term deformity or deficit**

**Buckle (torus) fracture** → **removable splint, no cast, no follow-up** · same pain + refracture as cast\
✗ Wrong options: physeal-arrest monitoring · close follow-up

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Unicortical/bicortical, **<10°**, buckle | **SAC 2–3 wk** / removable splint |
| **>10–20° angulation**, SH I/II unacceptable | **Closed reduction + cast** |
| Unstable / **lost reduction** in cast / reduced only in OR / SH I–II with **NV compromise** | **CRPP** |
| **Displaced SH III/IV** · **irreducible** (periosteum, **pronator quadratus**) | **ORIF** |
| Near-mature, redisplaced shaft / bayonet (15-yr) | **ORIF** (✗ Wrong options: accept · remanipulate) |

```
Pediatric distal radius fracture
├─ Buckle → removable splint, no follow-up
├─ Angulated beyond limits → closed reduction + well-moulded cast (index ≤0.7–0.8)
│      └─ Redisplaced (unacceptable) → re-reduce + percutaneous pins
└─ Irreducible / SH III–IV displaced → open reduction
```

### Cast index & loss of reduction – management of redisplacement

**Cast index** — **sagittal (lateral) ÷ coronal (AP) inner width** · **best predictor** of failure · good ≈ **0.7** · loss of reduction **>0.8** (mean 0.79 in failures) · OB answer **>0.85**\
✗ Wrong options: padding, gap, three-point, 2nd MC–radius indices

![Cast index = lateral ÷ AP inner width](../../MCQ/attachments/q3289-illa-cast-index.jpg)

**Short vs long arm cast** — well-moulded SAC **= LAC** (no ↑ displacement, fewer missed school days)\
✗ Wrong options: LAC/SAC/plaster/sedation as risks

**Redisplacement** — **20–25%** early · risks: **poor cast index**, ↑ **initial displacement**, incomplete reduction

**11-yr, redisplaced to 22° at 1 week despite good mould** → **closed reduction + percutaneous fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: accept · recast · flexible nail (too distal) · plate

**Physeal reduction** — gentle, **one attempt**, **not >1 week** after injury (late reduction → arrest)

**Technique** — metaphyseal: recreate deformity, use periosteal hinge (traction counterproductive) · intact ulna: ulnar-deviate wrist

### Reduction manoeuvre – greenstick fractures

**Rotate the palm toward the apex** — **apex-volar (supination injury) → pronate** · apex-dorsal (pronation injury) → supinate

### CRPP technique

**Radial styloid pins** — entry just proximal to physis (smooth if transphyseal) · avoid **dorsal sensory radial nerve** · dorsal pin for volar tilt · DRUJ pin → long-arm cast

## 6 Complications

### Cast thermal injury

| Risk factor | |
|---|---|
| **Dip water >24 °C (75 °F)** | Yes |
| **>8 plaster layers** / thick cast | Yes |
| **Resting on pillow while curing** (most significant) | Yes |
| **Fibreglass over plaster** | Yes |
| Excess moulding pressure | Yes |
| **Fracture pattern** | **not a risk** |
| 22 °C water, thin fibreglass, single padding layer | No |

### Physeal growth arrest (radius/ulna) – CT & epiphysiodesis

**Distal ulnar physis** — **most likely to arrest** (**30–40%**) vs distal radius **<5%**

**Radial arrest** → ulnar-positive variance → **ulnocarpal impaction**, TFCC + DRUJ injury, radial deviation · variance changes visible **3–4 months**

**Risk factors** — repeated injury, **late presentation/late reduction**, multiple attempts, SH III/IV

```
Distal radial physeal arrest
├─ First → CT (physeal map) to size the bar
├─ Bar <50% + growth left → bar resection
├─ Bar >50% / little growth → complete radial + ulnar epiphysiodesis
│      (± radial lengthening for established discrepancy)
└─ Skeletally mature, ulnar-positive → ulnar shortening osteotomy
```

**10-yr, painful radial deviation after SH II** → **CT to assess bar** first\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy · bilateral epiphysiodesis

**14-yr asymptomatic, radial arrest, small discrepancy** → **ulnar epiphysiodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: bar resection · lengthening · styloid repair

**>50% radial bar, ulnar-positive, immature** → **distal ulna epiphysiodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: mature (ulnar shortening) · Kienböck · Madelung (Vickers ligament release)

**Other** — **malunion (most common complication)** · median neuropathy / acute carpal tunnel · cast-saw burns (cool blade)

## How it's asked

**"4-yr, 12° apex volar at 2 weeks?"** → **observe — remodels within 1 year**

**"9-yr, 18° unreduced?"** → **no long-term deformity or deficit**

**"2-yr buckle fracture — true?"** → **no cast or follow-up needed**

**"Risk of displacement after reduction?"** → **cast index >0.85**\
✗ Wrong options: long/short arm cast · plaster · sedation

**"11-yr redisplaced 22° at 1 week?"** → **closed reduction + percutaneous fixation**

**"15-yr redisplaced distal radial shaft, physis closing?"** → **ORIF**

**"Best predictor of failure of closed treatment?"** → **cast index**

**"Isolated pronation reduces?"** → **apex-volar greenstick (supination injury)**

**"Thermal injury risk — EXCEPT?"** → **fracture pattern**

**"Most significant cast-burn risk?"** → **resting wet cast on pillow**

**"9-yr radius + ulna physeal fracture — likely complication?"** → **distal ulna growth arrest**

**"14-yr, radial arrest, asymptomatic?"** → **ulnar epiphysiodesis**

**"10-yr painful deformity after SH II?"** → **CT to evaluate arrest**

**"Best treated by distal ulna epiphysiodesis?"** → **immature >50% radial bar with ulnar-positive variance**

**"Most common fracture <16 yr?"** → **distal radius**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Down Syndrome

*Pediatrics · 38 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Most common chromosomal abnormality** (US) · **1:700** live births

**Risk** — **advanced maternal age**: 1 in 250 if >35 yr · 1 in 5000 if <30 yr

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — **trisomy 21** (maternal duplication)

**Collagen** — chr 21 codes **type VI collagen (COL6A1, COL6A2)** → generalised laxity

**Labs / medical** — **hypothyroidism** (check with SCFE) · cardiac disease **50%** (ASD, PDA) · duodenal atresia · premature aging · Alzheimer's · intellectual disability

### Orthopaedic manifestations

**Ligamentous laxity + hypotonia** · short stature · delayed walking (**2–3 yr**) · **C1–2 instability** · **occipitocervical instability** · **hip instability** · **patellofemoral instability** · scoliosis, **spondylolisthesis (6%)** · **pes planus (50%)** · metatarsus primus varus · **hallux valgus (25%)** · **SCFE**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — flattened facies · **upward-slanting eyes** · **epicanthal folds** · **single palmar (simian) crease** · laxity · hearing loss

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **chromosomal testing** (trisomy 21)

### Key radiology findings

**C-spine** — **flexion-extension lateral** — **ADI** normal **<5 mm** (Orthobullets) / up to 4 mm (OB) · 5–10 mm tolerated in Down · **space available for cord (SAC)** threshold **14 mm**

**Occipitocervical** — **Powers ratio**

**Hip** — **acetabular retroversion** (crossover + ischial spine signs) · coxa valga

**Knee** — long-leg film (genu valgum) · sunrise/Merchant (patellar subluxation)

![Powers ratio](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4104-power-s-ratio.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Atlantoaxial instability – ADI & sports clearance

![Atlantoaxial instability in Down syndrome](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4104-aa-instability-downs.jpg)

**Prevalence** — **17.5%** (Orthobullets) · ~15% (OB)

**Presentation** — subtle: change in gait, bowel/bladder

**Flexion-extension films** — **before intubation** · **Special Olympics requires** C-spine imaging before gymnastics, swimming, high jump etc.\
✗ Wrong options: AP pelvis · knee/elbow films · neurology referral · clear without work-up

```
Atlantoaxial instability in Down syndrome
├─ ADI 5–10 mm, asymptomatic, SAC >14 mm → surveillance (neuro exam + repeat imaging); avoid contact sports
└─ ADI >5 mm + myelopathy, or ADI >10 mm, or SAC <14 mm → C1–2 posterior fusion
       (complications up to 50%)
```

**Asymptomatic, ADI 6 mm** → **avoid contact sports, high jump, diving, gymnastics, butterfly**\
✗ Wrong options: clear all · collar · halo · fusion

**Asymptomatic, ADI 5 mm** → **surveillance: symptoms, neuro exam, repeat imaging**\
✗ Wrong options: O–C2 fusion · anterior fusion · soft collar · traction

### Operative criteria

| Problem | Surgery when |
|---|---|
| **C1–2 instability** | ADI >5 mm + **symptoms/myelopathy** · **ADI >10 mm** · **SAC ≤14 mm** · cord compression on MRI/CT |
| **Occipitocervical** | Progressive neuro deficit / myelopathy → occipitocervical fusion (most observed) |
| **Scoliosis** | Brace **25–30°** · fuse **>50°** (more complications than idiopathic) |
| **Hip instability** | Older symptomatic → capsulorrhaphy + pelvic (**triple / PAO** for retroversion) + femoral varus osteotomy (high complications) |
| Patellar instability | Symptomatic → lateral release, medial reefing, semitendinosus tenodesis, **TTO if mature** |
| SCFE | **Pin both hips** |
| Pes planus | Orthotics if symptomatic; surgery if refractory |

### Lower-limb problems – hip instability, patellar instability & genu valgum

**Hip instability** — **5%**, age **2–10 yr** · laxity + **acetabular retroversion**, coxa valga · young, mild subluxation, no bony change → **abduction brace**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · NSAIDs · shoe lift · osteotomy

**Patellofemoral instability** — mature, painless, knock-knees → **observation** · symptomatic → **patellar stabilising brace** → surgery (TTO in mature)\
✗ Wrong options: lateral release alone · trochleoplasty first

**10-yr with Down facies, laxity, new limp** → associated image = **acetabular retroversion**\
✗ Wrong options: foramen magnum stenosis (achondroplasia) · absent cruciates (fibular/femoral deficiency) · iliac horns (nail-patella) · absent clavicles (CCD)

## How it's asked

**"10-yr Down, high jump, ADI 6 mm, asymptomatic?"** → **avoid contact sports, high jump, diving**

**"7-yr Down, ADI 5 mm, asymptomatic?"** → **surveillance with neuro exam + repeat imaging**

**"15-yr gymnast with trisomy 21 — before participation?"** → **cervical spine imaging**

**"17-yr Down, painless knock-knees, dislocated patellae?"** → **observation**

**"3-yr Down, mild hip subluxation, no bony change?"** → **abduction brace**

**"Hip image associated with Down syndrome?"** → **acetabular retroversion**

**"Indication for C1–2 fusion?"** → **myelopathy with ADI >5 mm, ADI >10 mm, or SAC <14 mm**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy

*Pediatrics · 39 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Most common hereditary neuromuscular disease** (most common MD) · prevalence **2–3/10,000** (Orthobullets) · ~**1 in 3500 male births** (OB)

**Who** — **boys only** · onset **2–6 yr**

### Genetics – dystrophin, X-linked recessive, carrier mother & offspring risk

**Inheritance** — **X-linked recessive**\
✗ Wrong options: AD · AR · X-linked dominant · mitochondrial · Y-linked

**Gene** — **dystrophin, Xp21(.2)** · point deletion / nonsense (out-of-frame) → **absent dystrophin** (<5%)

**Spontaneous** — **one-third** new mutations

**Carriers** — mother carries · **sons 50% affected**, daughters 50% carriers · **affected male → 0% sons affected** (gives Y), all daughters carriers · **2.5–20% of carrier females symptomatic** (Lyon hypothesis) · **CK ↑ in 80% of carriers** (peak 8–12 yr)

**Confirm DMD via family** — **test mother's CK**\
✗ Wrong options: father (always normal) · unaffected brother

**Pathophysiology** — dystrophin–glycoprotein complex lost → membrane fragility, Ca²⁺ dysregulation, oxidative damage → necrosis/regeneration cycles → **fibrofatty replacement**

### Genes & lab markers

| Disorder | Gene / inheritance |
|---|---|
| **Duchenne** | **Dystrophin, Xp21** (XLR) |
| Becker | Dystrophin (in-frame, ↓ amount) (XLR) |
| Emery-Dreifuss | **Emerin** (X-linked) |
| **FSH dystrophy** | **D4Z4 deletion, 4q35** · **AD** |
| **Myotonic dystrophy** | **DMPK / CNBP**, **chromosome 19** |
| **Limb-girdle** | **AR and AD** (LGMD2 / LGMD1) · chr 2p (TO) |
| Marfan | FBN1 |
| CMT | PMP22 (AD) |
| Turner | 45,XO |

**CK** — **markedly ↑** — **10–200×** (Orthobullets) · 50–100× (TO) · often **>25,000 IU/L** (e.g. 43,000) · **peaks by age 2**, falls with fibrofatty replacement

**EMG** — myopathic (low amplitude, short, polyphasic), **normal NCV**

**Biopsy** — necrosis, fibrofatty infiltration, **absent dystrophin stain** · DNA confirms ~two-thirds · **dystrophin testing of biopsy NOT 100% confirmatory** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — delayed walking (e.g. 30 mo) · toe walking, clumsy, **waddling** · difficulty climbing stairs, hopping · falls

**Weakness** — **proximal first, starting with gluteals** · lumbar **lordosis** (gluteal weakness) · wide-based stance

**Gowers sign** — rises by **walking hands up thighs** (gluteus maximus + quadriceps weakness)

**Meryon sign** — child slips through examiner's hands when lifted under arms (shoulder girdle weakness)

**Calf pseudohypertrophy** — fat + fibrous infiltration

**Reflexes** — **present**\
✗ Wrong options: SMA · GBS

**Trendelenburg** · contractures (elbow, hip, knee, ankle) · **equinovarus** (tibialis posterior retains strength longest) · scoliosis · fractures (falls)

**Other** — cardiomyopathy · mild intellectual impairment · pharyngeal weakness · malignant hyperthermia risk

![Calf pseudohypertrophy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4092-calf-pseudohypertrophy.jpg)

![Gowers sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4092-gowers-sign-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – Gowers sign, CK & Becker variant

**First test** — **serum CK** (✗ Wrong options: leg X-rays · NCS · brain/spine MRI) · screen asymptomatic at-risk boy with **CK** (✗ Wrong options: DNA, biopsy, Western blot first)

**Confirm** — **DNA testing** (± biopsy)

### Key radiology findings

**MRI muscle pattern** — **gastrocnemius early** → gluteus max/med, adductor magnus → quads etc. · **spared: sartorius, gracilis, semitendinosus, semimembranosus, tibialis posterior** · upper limb: triceps, biceps, rotator cuff; **deltoid spared**

**Echo** — **dilated cardiomyopathy in all by late teens/20s**

**X-ray** — lucent fatty soft tissue, scoliosis, cardiomegaly

### Differential

| | **Duchenne** | **Becker** | **SMA** | **Emery-Dreifuss** | **Limb-girdle** | **GBS** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dystrophin | **Absent** | **Decreased/abnormal** | Normal | Normal (**emerin** absent) | Normal | — |
| CK | **Very high** | Very high | **Normal** | Near normal | Mildly ↑ | Normal |
| Pseudohypertrophy | Yes | Yes | No | No | No | No |
| Reflexes | Present | Present | **Absent + fasciculations** | — | — | **Absent** |
| Onset | 2–6 yr | **~8 yr, slower**, lives to 40s | Earlier | Teens; **early elbow + ankle contractures**, **heart block** (pacemaker) | 1st–4th decade | Acute, post-infectious |
| Key test | DNA | DNA | — | ECG | — | **CSF** (lumbar puncture) |

### Differential – Guillain-Barré syndrome

**GBS** — acute ascending flaccid weakness 1–6 wk after (GI) infection · areflexia · autonomic + diaphragm involvement · **lumbar puncture (CSF)** best test · IVIG / plasma exchange · 80% walk at 6 mo\
✗ Wrong options: C-spine MRI/CT · M-spike · stool

## 5 Management

**Corticosteroids (prednisone / deflazacort)** — age **5–7 yr** with progression · prolong ambulation (3–4 yr), ↓ **scoliosis needing fusion 92% → 20%**, delay pulmonary + cardiac decline, ↓ mortality 76% · side effects: **osteonecrosis, osteoporosis**, weight gain, cataracts, short stature · **deflazacort** — fewer glucose/bone effects, far costlier

**Exon-skipping (eteplirsen, golodirsen, viltolarsen, casimersen)** — convert out-of-frame to in-frame (**DMD → BMD-like**) · hot-spot exons 45–53 (~50%) · costly

**Cardiac** — ACE inhibitor + β-blocker · echo/ECG every 2 yr to age 10, then yearly (6-monthly with cardiomyopathy)

**Other** — nocturnal ventilation · PT, power chair · nutrition

**Soft-tissue releases (ambulatory)** — ITB (abduction contracture) · sartorius, rectus, TFL (flexion) · hamstrings · **TAL + tibialis posterior** lengthening/transfer · mobilise early

**Equinovarus** — stretching, night AFO → **TAL + PTT transfer + toe flexor tenotomies**

### Scoliosis – early fusion (20–30°, FVC), T2-pelvis & preoperative cardiopulmonary work-up

**Natural history** — **90–95%** develop scoliosis · starts when **full-time sitting (11–13 yr)** · **progresses rapidly** (1–2°/month), **small curves always progress** · **kyphoscoliosis** + pelvic obliquity · **restrictive lung disease**\
✗ Wrong options: 50%

**Bracing** — **ineffective / contraindicated** (impairs respiration)\
✗ Wrong options: TLSO · PT · observe a 25° curve

### Operative criteria

| Criterion | Value |
|---|---|
| **Cobb angle** | **20–30°** in non-ambulator (up to ~40° if responding to steroids) |
| **FVC** | Operate **before FVC ≤35%** (≤35% = indication / threshold) · **>50% = good outcomes** · <10% contraindication (some) |
| Other | **Non-ambulatory** · **rapid progression** · poor steroid response (✗ Wrong options: decreased muscle mass) |
| **Fuse to pelvis** | Curve **≥40°** · **pelvic obliquity ≥10°** · lumbar apex **below L1** |
| Construct | **T2–pelvis posterior instrumented fusion** (pedicle screws) (✗ Wrong options: T10–pelvis · growing rods · T2–L5 with obliquity · delay for steroids) |
| Anterior approach | **Avoid** (lung deflation, ↑ pulmonary complications) |

**Timing** — driven by **cardiopulmonary status, not Cobb angle** · 12-yr, 38°, FVC 50% → **as soon as possible**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for FVC 35% · cardiac decline · 50°

**Pre-op** — **echocardiogram + PFTs**, **cardiology consult** · stop ACE-I 2–3 d, taper β-blocker over weeks · TXA · beware anaesthesia-induced **rhabdomyolysis**, hypotensive anaesthesia may not be tolerated\
✗ Wrong options: hematology · neurology · rheumatology/biopsy · MRI for ectasia · baclofen trial · bracing trial

```
DMD scoliosis
├─ Ambulatory → steroids, keep walking (no brace)
└─ Non-ambulatory, curve ≥20–30° or FVC falling toward 35% → early PSF (echo + PFT first)
       ├─ Curve <40°, obliquity <10°, apex ≥L1 → T2–L5 possible
       └─ Curve ≥40° / obliquity ≥10° / apex below L1 → T2–pelvis
```

### Other muscular dystrophies (FSH, myotonic)

**FSH dystrophy** — **AD**, 4q35 · facial weakness (**can't whistle, purse lips**, wrinkle brow) · **scapular winging** — scapular stabilisers weak while **deltoid (abductors) stay strong** → can't flex/abduct arm · **scapulothoracic arthrodesis** · foot drop (AFO) · open-eyed sleeping\
✗ Wrong options: "due to weak abductors"

**Myotonic dystrophy** — **chromosome 19** (DMPK) · can't relax muscle · most common adult-onset MD · baldness, gonadal atrophy, cardiac, clubfoot, hip dysplasia, scoliosis

**Limb-girdle** — **AD (LGMD1) and AR (LGMD2)** · pelvic/shoulder girdle · CK mildly ↑

**Oculopharyngeal** — 40–70 yr, eyelids + throat

## 6 Complications & prognosis

**Untreated** — walking lost by **~10 yr** (10–12) · wheelchair by **15** · death from respiratory/cardiac failure ~**20** (Orthobullets) / ~25 (OB) / early 30s (TO) · 15-yr survival 27.9% → **78.6% with steroids**

## How it's asked

**"5-yr, Trendelenburg + calf pseudohypertrophy — genetic cause?"** → **deletion in Xp21**\
✗ Wrong options: FBN1 · D4Z4 4q35 = FSH · DMPK/CNBP = myotonic

**"Inheritance of DMD?"** → **X-linked recessive**

**"Most commonly X-linked recessive?"** → **Duchenne**\
✗ Wrong options: CMT · Marfan · Larsen · Turner

**"Affected boy's chance of passing to sons?"** → **0%**

**"3-yr large calves, Gowers, CK 43,000?"** → **X-linked recessive**

**"Gowers sign — pedigree?"** → **X-linked recessive pedigree**

**"DMD transmitted by?"** → **X-linked**

**"Gowers + scoliosis + ↑ CPK — protein?"** → **dystrophin**\
✗ Wrong options: type I collagen · neurofibromin · Runx2

**"Confirm DMD — test CK in?"** → **mother**

**"8-yr weak, big calves, equinovarus, scoliosis — pedigree?"** → **X-linked recessive**

**"Mother asks about future children — which pedigree?"** → **X-linked recessive**

**"DMD — all EXCEPT?"** → **dystrophin biopsy testing is 100% confirmatory** (TO)

**"Becker characteristics?"** → **pseudohypertrophy, very high CK, abnormal (present) dystrophin**

**"2-yr toe walker, big calves, Gowers — most useful test?"** → **creatine kinase**

**"Asymptomatic boy, uncle with DMD — screen?"** → **serum CK**

**"9-yr wheelchair, FVC 35%, lumbar 35° — indications?"** → **FVC 35%, non-ambulatory, curve >30° (all)**

**"12-yr non-ambulator with cardiomyopathy and scoliosis — true?"** → **surgery for Cobb >30°**\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · steroids eliminate surgery · A/P approach · FVC <35% better

**"9-yr DMD, 20° curve?"** → **PSF before curve passes 30°**

**"16-yr for T2–pelvis fusion — pre-op work-up?"** → **echocardiogram + PFTs**

**"Untreated DMD scoliosis — true?"** → **fusion offered at 20–30°**

**"Supports early PSF EXCEPT?"** → **decreased muscle mass**

**"12-yr, 40° curve — before surgery?"** → **cardiology consult**

**"12-yr, 38°, FVC 50% — when?"** → **as soon as possible**

**"85° curve, 20° pelvic tilt, FVC 40%?"** → **T2–pelvis posterior instrumented fusion**

**"14-yr wheelchair, 25° curve, FVC 55%?"** → **posterior spinal fusion to pelvis**

**"Ascending weakness after diarrhoea — test?"** → **lumbar puncture CSF analysis**

**"FSH dystrophy inheritance?"** → **AD** (TO)

**"Can't abduct arms or whistle — FALSE statement?"** → **"weak shoulder abductors cause it"** (TO)

**"Myotonic dystrophy chromosome?"** → **19** (TO)

**"Limb-girdle inheritance?"** → **both AD and AR** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Dysplasia Epiphysealis Hemimelica (Trevor's Disease)

*Pediatrics · 40 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **osteochondroma arising from the epiphysis** → **asymmetric (hemimelic) epiphyseal overgrowth** of cartilage

**Incidence** — extremely rare, **~1:1,000,000** · **male** predilection · young children

**Location** — **lower extremity**: **knee or ankle** (one side of the epiphysis, usually medial) · usually **one joint**\
✗ Wrong options: upper-limb exostoses

**Cause** — defect in the **groove of Ranvier**

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — **non-hereditary**\
✗ Wrong options: AD · AR / non-viable births

**Malignancy** — **no reported transformation**

**Histology** — identical to **osteochondroma**; diagnosis rests on **epiphyseal location**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — asymmetric limb deformity · joint symptoms · ↓ motion · joint-line tenderness

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — irregular calcified mass at joint line / epiphysis

**CT** — **cortical continuity with the epiphysis**

![Trevor's disease of the knee](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4115-xray-knee-colorado-moved.jpg)

![CT — continuity with epiphysis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4115-trevors-ct.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Stage | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Asymptomatic, small | Observe |
| **Symptomatic early** | **Excision of overgrowth** |
| **Symptomatic late** | **Osteotomies** |

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** after excision (common) · articular irregularity → **early OA**

## How it's asked

**"Woman with history of DEH, genetic counselling — consistent feature?"** → **asymmetric epiphyseal overgrowth**\
✗ Wrong options: malignant risk · upper-limb exostoses · AD inheritance

**"Most common sites?"** → **knee and ankle**

**"Symptomatic early lesion?"** → **excision**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ehlers-Danlos Syndrome

*Pediatrics · 41 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Genes & lab markers

**Classic EDS** — **COL5A1 / COL5A2** (40–50%) → **type V collagen** (co-expressed with type I; skin fibril + basement membrane assembly) · ↓ type V for fibrillogenesis

**Diagnosis** — **collagen typing of skin biopsy**

### Clinical features & Beighton score

**Features** — **hyperelastic, fragile skin** · easy bruising, **poor wound healing**, widened atrophic scars · **joint hypermobility + dislocation** (shoulder, patella, ankle) · generalised laxity · early OA · chronic MSK pain (50%)

**Other** — **mitral valve prolapse** · **aortic root dilatation (up to 1/3)** · DDH · clubfoot · pes planus · **scoliosis** · high palate · gastroparesis · soft-tissue calcification

**NOT EDS** — **superior lens dislocation → Marfan** (inferior → **homocystinuria**)

### Villefranche classification

| Type | Inheritance | Former name / feature |
|---|---|---|
| **Classical** | **AD** | I (gravis), II (mitis) · COL5A1/2 · skin + scars + hypermobility |
| **Hypermobile** | **AD** | III |
| **Vascular** | **AD** | IV |
| **Kyphoscoliosis** | **AR** | VI (ocular-scoliotic) |
| Arthrochalasis | AD | VIIA, VIIB |
| Dermatosparaxis | AR | VIIC — severe fragility, sagging skin |

**Berlin** — types I–XI · **II + III most common, least disabling**

## 3 Presentation

### Beighton-Horan score (/9)

| Manoeuvre | Points |
|---|---|
| Passive **5th MCP hyperextension >90°** | 1 each side |
| Passive **thumb apposition to forearm** | 1 each side |
| **Elbow hyperextension >10°** | 1 each side |
| **Knee hyperextension >10°** | 1 each side |
| **Palms flat on floor**, knees straight | 1 |

**Hypermobility** — **≥5/9** (threshold varies: >3, 5, 6/9) · used for **EDS / joint laxity**\
✗ Wrong options: CP · myelodysplasia · arthrogryposis · Kniest

| Thumb apposition to forearm | 5th finger hyperextension |
|---|---|
| ![Thumb apposition to forearm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4111-ehlers-danlos-thumb-apposition.jpg) | ![5th finger hyperextension](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4111-eds-small-finger-dorsiflexion.jpg) |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — subluxations/dislocations · kyphoscoliosis

**Echocardiogram** — **mandatory** (aortic root)

## 5 Management

**Mainstay** — **PT, orthotics, pain support**

### Operative criteria

| Problem | Surgery |
|---|---|
| Recalcitrant unstable/painful joint | **Arthrodesis** (soft-tissue stabilisation unlikely to hold) |
| **Progressive scoliosis** (esp. kyphoscoliosis type) | **PSF — longer fusion** (junctional problems) |

## How it's asked

**"NOT associated with EDS?"** → **superior lens dislocation** (Marfan)

**"Beighton-Horan scale used for?"** → **Ehlers-Danlos**

**"Image of thumb-to-forearm / 5th finger hyperextension — condition?"** → **Ehlers-Danlos** (TO; answer not provided by source)

**"Gene in classic EDS?"** → **COL5A1/COL5A2 (type V collagen)**

**"Mandatory investigation?"** → **echocardiogram**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Elbow Dislocation - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 42 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **3–6% of pediatric elbow injuries** · **M:F 3:1** · peak **10–15 yr** · **very rare <3 yr** (→ think transphyseal fracture + NAT)

**Mechanism** — **FOOSH** · posterior = hyperextension + valgus + supination · anterior = posterior-to-anterior blow on flexed elbow

**Small coronoid** in children → can't resist posterior ulnar displacement

**Associated** — **medial epicondyle avulsion = most common** (may **incarcerate** in joint) · radial head/neck, olecranon, coronoid fractures

**Nerves** — **ulnar** (with medial epicondyle) = most common neuropraxia · **median** + **brachial artery** stretched / entrapped

### Classification

**By proximal radioulnar joint position** — **posterolateral (most common)** · posteromedial · anterior (rare) · divergent

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — swollen, painful · **held flexed** · forearm looks short · **distal humerus fullness in antecubital fossa** · NV exam (brachial artery, median, ulnar)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral (± contralateral) · loss of radiocapitellar + ulnohumeral relationship, **radius–ulna relationship maintained**

**Post-reduction** — **locate the medial epicondyle** — if not seen in normal position, assume **intra-articular**

![Elbow dislocation with medial epicondyle fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4013-x-ray-and-lateral-of-a-pediatric-elbow-dislocation-with-asso.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Reduce promptly** — by the most available provider (ED) under sedation · don't delay for orthopaedics

**Technique** — supine: elbow flexed, forearm **supinated**, gradual traction · prone: forearm hanging, anterior push on olecranon · anterior dislocation: inline traction, posterior push on forearm

### Management & surgical indications

**Stable after reduction (most)** → **brief immobilisation 1–2 wk** (posterior splint ~8–12 days) → early active ROM\
✗ Wrong options: cast 6 wk · hinged ex-fix · MRI

### Operative criteria

| Absolute | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Open dislocation** | Significant instability (rare) |
| **Incarcerated medial epicondyle** (or coronoid) | — |
| **Failure to obtain/maintain closed reduction** | — |

**Medial epicondyle fracture — when to fix** — absolute: **incarcerated fragment**, open, articular extension · relative: **elbow dislocation / instability**, ulnar nerve palsy, **throwing/upper-limb athlete** · nonunion common nonoperatively (often asymptomatic) · fuses **16–18 yr** (last)\
✗ Wrong options: buckle radius fracture · <2 mm displacement · radial nerve symptoms · video games

### Entrapped medial epicondyle fragment

![Incarcerated medial epicondyle after reduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4013-combo.jpg)

**Incongruous joint after reduction / fragment in joint** → **open reduction (medial approach) + ORIF of medial epicondyle**\
✗ Wrong options: re-reduction attempt · lateral epicondyle · coronoid

## 6 Complications

### Loss of motion after immobilisation

**Stiffness — loss of terminal extension** — from **immobilisation >2 weeks**\
✗ Wrong options: sex · age >9 · ED reduction · no ORIF

**Others** — **heterotopic ossification** (vigorous reduction) · transient nerve injury (**median entrapment**, **ulnar with medial epicondyle**) · brachial artery (rare) · compartment syndrome (hyperflexion splint) · recurrent instability (coronoid / radial head fractures)

## X vs Y

| | **Elbow dislocation** | **Transphyseal distal humerus fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **10–15 yr** | **<3 yr** |
| Displacement | **Posterolateral** | Posteromedial |
| Radiocapitellar | Disrupted | Maintained |
| NAT | Rare | **Strong association** |

## How it's asked

**"11-yr — treated with posterior splint 8–12 days then ROM?"** → **acutely reduced elbow dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral condyle · type I supracondylar · olecranon (3–5 wk)

**"15-yr, post-reduction joint incongruous with medial epicondyle fracture?"** → **open reduction**

**"10-yr medial epicondyle fracture — indication for surgery?"** → **elbow dislocated during fall**

**"9-yr, fragment in joint after reduction?"** → **medial epicondyle — ORIF**

**"Loss of extension at 4 months?"** → **splint immobilisation for 4 weeks**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Epiphyseal bracket

*Pediatrics · 43 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Also called** — **longitudinal epiphyseal bracket (LEB)**, delta phalanx / delta bone

**Incidence** — **2–14%** of congenital hand + foot deformities · **11% in great toe**

**Pathology** — incomplete primary ossification · **aberrant physeal cartilage runs longitudinally along the diaphysis ("C-shaped")** → tethers growth → short, wide, **triangular / trapezoidal** bone

**Bones** — short tubular bones (phalanges, metatarsals — esp. **1st MT**)

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — **AD when in hand**, otherwise sporadic

**Associated** — **hallux varus** (feet) · **clinodactyly**, polydactyly (hands) · Rubinstein-Taybi · fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva · Apert

## 3 Presentation

**Deformity** — angulated, short digit / ray (hallux varus, medial great-toe deviation)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **trapezoidal / triangular** bone · **"D-shaped" metatarsal** · wedge-shaped middle phalanx

**Ultrasound** — shows **pre-ossified** bracket

**MRI** — **diagnoses** LEB

![Longitudinal epiphyseal bracket — C-shaped physis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12781-f132-024ac-9780323081764.jpg)

### Radiographic staging

| Stage | Feature |
|---|---|
| **1** | Delta-shaped diaphysis, radiolucent cartilaginous epiphysis |
| **2** | Secondary ossification at proximal + distal ends |
| **3** | Complete ossification of bracket along one side |
| **4** | Physeal closure |

## 5 Management

```
Longitudinal epiphyseal bracket
├─ Young child, open bracket → early excision (physiolysis) + fat / PMMA interposition
└─ Older, bracket closed → opening/closing wedge osteotomy ± lengthening
```

### Treatment - bracket excision

**Early open bracket excision + fat graft (or PMMA) interposition** — best; restores near-normal longitudinal growth, ↓ deformity\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous epiphysiolysis (open needed) · Syme · ex-fix lengthening as index · fusion (macrodactyly) · observation · ray resection

**Osteotomy** — **after bracket epiphysis closes** (older) · secondary realignment/lengthening if growth insufficient

![Physiolysis of bracket](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12781-11832-2013-544-fig4-html.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Untreated** — forefoot deformity, short bone, clinodactyly

**Operative** — recurrence, incomplete correction

## How it's asked

**"4-yr with metatarsal LEB — next step?"** → **bracket excision + fat graft interposition**

**"3-yr, first-ray bracket on X-ray?"** → **excision of the abnormal structure**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# External Tibial Torsion

*Pediatrics · 44 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — abnormal **external rotation of the tibia** → **out-toeing** · usually **right-sided** if unilateral

**Normal development** — tibia externally rotates ~15° in childhood · femoral anteversion falls ~25°

**Natural history** — **worsens** in late childhood/adolescence (tibia rotates externally with growth) · **more often needs surgery than internal tibial torsion**

**Associated** — **miserable malalignment** (ETT + femoral anteversion) · **Osgood-Schlatter** · **OCD** · early knee DJD · neuromuscular (myelodysplasia, polio)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — out-toeing, tripping · **anterior knee pain**, **patellofemoral instability** (positive apprehension)

![External tibial torsion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4121-external-tibial-torsion-example.jpg)

**Out-toeing by age** — infancy/toddler → **hip external rotation contracture** · late childhood/adolescence → **external tibial torsion**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Thigh–foot axis (TFA)** — **best clinical measure** · prone, knee 90° · infancy ~5° internal · **age 8: ~10° external (−5 to +30°)** · **>20° external** = ETT

**Transmalleolar axis** — supine · infancy 4–5° internal · adult **23° external** (0–40°)

**Foot progression angle** — ~5° external in late childhood

**CT torsional study** — tibial version = distal minus proximal axis; femoral version neck minus condyles

**Imaging** — usually none

![Thigh–foot axis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4121-schematic-of-the-thigh-foot-axis.jpg)

## 5 Management

**First line** — rest, rehab, activity modification (bracing ineffective)

### Operative criteria

| Source | Indication |
|---|---|
| Orthobullets | **>8 yr** + ETT **>3 SD (>40° external)** |
| OB | **≥9 yr** + **TFA >30°** (or < −15°) · normal TFA −5° to 20° |

**Procedure** — **supramalleolar (distal tibial) derotation osteotomy** (most common) ± oblique fibular osteotomy · pins or plate · IM nail only if mature

**Distal vs proximal** — distal: easier, safer (↓ NV injury, ↓ compartment syndrome) · proximal: more contact/soft tissue but **peroneal nerve risk** → anterior fasciotomy

**12-yr, right ETT 45° (left 32°), normal femoral version** → **right supramalleolar osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · KAFO · distal tibial guided growth (causes coronal deformity) · femoral osteotomy

## X vs Y

| Cause of out-toeing | Age |
|---|---|
| **Hip ER contracture** | Infant / toddler |
| **External tibial torsion** | Late childhood / adolescent |

## How it's asked

**"12-yr, out-toeing, TFA +30°, patellar apprehension, symmetric hip rotation?"** → **excessive external tibial torsion**\
✗ Wrong options: femoral anteversion · ER contracture · internal torsion

**"12-yr right ETT 45° on CT?"** → **supramalleolar osteotomy**

**"Best exam measure?"** → **thigh–foot axis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Femoral Anteversion

*Pediatrics · 45 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & measurement

**Definition** — transverse-plane angle between **femoral neck axis** and the **posterior femoral condylar plane** (TO) · femoral anteversion = functional anteversion + condylar twist\
✗ Wrong options: lesser trochanter · tibial plateau · distal condylar plane

**Pathology** — ↑ neck anteversion → **compensatory internal rotation of femur** → **in-toeing** · **packaging** disorder

**Demographics** — **3–6 yr** · **girls 2:1** · hereditary · usually **bilateral** (be wary of asymmetry)

**Associated packaging** — **DDH** · **metatarsus adductus** · **torticollis** · also ↑ in clubfoot, Blount, flatfoot, CP

### Normal anteversion

| Age | Anteversion |
|---|---|
| Birth / infancy | **30–40°** (≈39–40°) |
| 2nd year | ~31° |
| To age 10 | ↓ 1–2°/yr → ~24° |
| **Adult** | **~15–16°** |
| After age 8 | Minimal change |

### Three causes of in-toeing

| Cause | Age | Resolves by |
|---|---|---|
| **Metatarsus adductus** | **Infant** | ~4 yr |
| **Internal tibial torsion** | **Toddler** | 6–7 yr |
| **Femoral anteversion** | **Early childhood (3–6)** | **~10 yr** (8–13) |

**Order of resolution** — **MA → ITT → femoral anteversion**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — in-toeing gait, tripping, awkward running · **sits in "W" position** · knee pain if with tibial torsion · more symptomatic in neuromuscular / brace-dependent walkers

### Examination findings & diagnosis

| Test (prone) | Femoral anteversion | Normal |
|---|---|---|
| **Hip IR** | **>70°** | 20–60° |
| **Hip ER** | **<20°** | 30–60° |
| **Thigh–foot angle** | **Normal** (−5° to +20°) | Infant 5° internal · age 8: 10° external |
| Heel bisector | Normal (2nd–3rd web) | MA: 3rd–4th web |
| Patella in gait | **Internally rotated** | — |
| **Trochanteric prominence test** | Hip IR at max GT prominence ≈ anteversion | — |

**Internal tibial torsion** — **TFA < −10°** (e.g. −20° on right) with normal hip rotation → ITT on that side

![W-sitting](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4059-w-sitter-moved.jpg)

![Increased hip internal rotation in prone](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4059-screen-shot-2012-07-22-at-8-58-42-am.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**None** usually · **CT or MRI** to measure actual anteversion (pre-op)

## 5 Management

### Natural history & observation

**Observation + reassurance** — remodels in ~99% by 8–10 yr · **bracing, shoe inserts, PT, sitting restrictions do NOT change natural history**\
✗ Wrong options: night ER brace · inserts · osteotomy in a young child

### Operative criteria

| Source | Indication for derotation femoral osteotomy |
|---|---|
| Orthobullets | **ER <10°** in child **>10 yr** (rare) |
| **Staheli (TO)** | **>8 yr** · significant cosmetic + functional disability · **anteversion >50°** · **IR >85° + ER <10°** · family aware of risks |

**NOT a Staheli indication** — **medial (internal) hip rotation >35°** (TO)

**Osteotomy** — **intertrochanteric** (blade plate / screw-side plate) · subtrochanteric or supracondylar alternatives · correction ≈ **(IR − ER) / 2**

**Prognosis** — persistent anteversion **not linked to hip/knee OA** (Orthobullets) · TO lists hip OA, patellar malalignment, chondromalacia as associations · **miserable (torsional) malalignment** = anteversion + external tibial torsion

## X vs Y

| | **Femoral anteversion** | **Internal tibial torsion** | **Metatarsus adductus** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | 3–6 yr | Toddler | Infant |
| Key exam | **Hip IR >70°, ER <20°** | **TFA internal (>10°)** | **Convex lateral border**, heel bisector lateral |
| Patella | **Points in** | Forward | Forward |
| Resolves | ~10 yr | 6–7 yr | ~4 yr |

## How it's asked

**"6-yr in-toeing — finding favouring femoral anteversion?"** → **hip IR 80°, ER 15°**\
✗ Wrong options: TFA internal (ITT) · convex lateral border (MA)

**"5-yr, in-toeing with knees in line with feet — finding?"** → **increased hip internal rotation**

**"6-yr, hips IR 60/ER 40, TFA −20° right?"** → **internal tibial torsion, right**

**"Staheli — NOT an indication?"** → **medial hip rotation greater than 35 degrees** (TO)

**"3-yr, IR 85°/ER 15°, neutral TFA?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy · inserts · brace

**"7-yr, IR >70°, ER <20°?"** → **observation**

**"Order of resolution of in-toeing causes?"** → **metatarsus adductus → internal tibial torsion → femoral anteversion**

**"Anteversion measured relative to?"** → **posterior femoral condylar plane** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Femoral Shaft Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 46 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1.6–2%** of pediatric fractures · **most common orthopaedic cause of pediatric hospitalisation** · **bimodal: toddlers 2–4 yr + adolescents** · **M 2.6:1**

**Mechanism by age** — 0–2 yr: **NAT** · <10 yr: **falls** · >10 yr: **MVA** (cortex thickens with age)

**Associated** — OI · neuromuscular osteopenia · tumours

### Deforming forces

**Proximal fragment** — **iliopsoas** (flexion) + **abductors** (gluteus medius/minimus → abduction) + **short external rotators** (piriformis → ER)\
✗ Wrong options: adductors · hamstrings · rectus (insert distally)

**Distal fragment** — **adductors** → shortening + **varus**

**Typical malunion** — **varus + flexion (procurvatum)** of distal fragment

### Stability

**Length-stable** — transverse, short oblique

**Length-unstable** — **long spiral (length >2× diameter)**, comminuted

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — thigh pain, can't walk, deformity, shortening, swelling

**Hemodynamic instability** — **isolated pediatric femur rarely causes shock** → **re-evaluate for other injuries** (fluids if Hb >7, transfuse if <7)\
✗ Wrong options: pressors · transfusion first if Hb >7 · fasciotomy · emergent fixation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP + lateral femur** + ipsilateral **hip and knee**

### Non-accidental trauma

**Suspect** — **<3 yr** (AAOS: evaluate <36 months), **especially non-walkers**

**Patterns most predictive** — **transverse** femoral shaft · **metaphyseal corner fracture (pathognomonic)** · **posterior rib fractures**, scapula, sternum, spinous process · injury inconsistent with history (9-month-old "running")\
✗ Wrong options: spiral, long oblique, toddler's

**Infant with metaphyseal corner fracture** → **admit + social services**\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik and discharge · spondylometaphyseal dysplasia/syphilis work-up first · infection work-up

**Distractors** — lytic lesion = pathologic · **blue sclera = OI** · low vitamin D / metaphyseal fraying = **rickets**

## 5 Management

### Treatment selection by age & weight

| Age | Pattern / factor | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **<6 months** | Any | **Pavlik harness** (or spica) |
| **6 mo – 5 yr** | Stable, **shortening <2 cm** | **Immediate spica** |
| 6 mo – 5 yr | Unstable, **>2–3 cm short**, polytrauma, open | Traction → delayed spica · ex-fix |
| **5–11 yr** | **Length-stable + <49 kg (100 lb)** | **Flexible (titanium elastic) nails** |
| 5–11 yr | **Length-unstable** (comminuted, long spiral), very proximal/distal, any weight | **Submuscular bridge plate** · stainless Ender nails · ex-fix |
| **>11 yr / >49 kg / near maturity** | Diaphyseal | **Antegrade rigid nail — trochanteric or lateral entry** (✗ Wrong options: piriformis) |
| >11 yr | Proximal/distal, severe comminution | Submuscular plate |
| Polytrauma, open, vascular repair, damage control | — | **External fixation** |

**Age ranges vary** — spica 6 mo–5 yr (Orthobullets) · 6 mo–6 yr / 1–6 yr (OB) · Pavlik <6 mo (Orthobullets) / <1 yr (OB) · trochanteric rigid nail from **~9 yr** (trochanteric apophysis no longer key; earlier → **coxa valga**) (OB)

```
Pediatric femoral shaft fracture
├─ <6 mo → Pavlik harness
├─ 6 mo–5 yr, <2 cm short → immediate spica
├─ 5–11 yr
│      ├─ Length-stable, <49 kg → flexible nails
│      └─ Length-unstable / heavy / proximal-distal → submuscular plate
└─ >11 yr or >49 kg → trochanteric/lateral-entry rigid IM nail
```

### Operative criteria

| Device | Key criterion |
|---|---|
| **Flexible nails** | 5–11 yr · **<49 kg / 100–108 lb** · length-stable · open or closed OK · not comminuted |
| **Contraindication to flexible nails** | **Weight** (e.g. 146 lb) — not age, height, multiple fractures, open fracture |
| **Submuscular plate** | Length-unstable · >49 kg · proximal/distal · comminuted (incl. **open comminuted in 4-yr**) |
| **Rigid IM nail** | >11 yr / near maturity · >49 kg · unstable · trochanteric/lateral entry |
| **Spica** | Shortening **<2 cm** (loss of reduction doubles per cm: 12% → 24% → 50%) · not polytrauma, open, >2–3 cm |
| **Ex-fix** | Polytrauma (damage control), open, vascular injury, segmental |

**Ipsilateral femur + tibia (floating knee) in 7-yr** → **flexible nails both**\
✗ Wrong options: spica · reamed nails (physeal/AVN risk)

### Infant fractures - Pavlik harness

**<6 months** → **Pavlik** (no anaesthesia, easy diapering, fewer skin problems than spica) · not in **neuromuscular spasticity** or **polytrauma** · 9-month-old → spica\
✗ Wrong options: flexible nails, ex-fix, growing rods, traction, plate

**Pavlik risk** — **femoral nerve compression** with excessive flexion + swollen thigh (↓ quads)

### Spica technique

**Position** — hips **60–90° flexion**, ~**30° abduction**, **external rotation** to correct rotation · mould into **recurvatum + valgus** · single-leg or 1½ spica (special car seat sometimes)

![Spica cast](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4019-screen-shot-2012-07-26-at-11-11-52-am.jpg)

**Acceptable** — **<10° coronal, <20° sagittal, ≤2 cm shortening, ≤10° rotation**

**Union** — 4–8 wk · check at 7–10 d

### Spica cast complications

**Most common needing revision** — **loss of reduction** (11%)\
✗ Wrong options: delayed/nonunion · cosmetic · LLD

**Compartment syndrome risk** — **"90/90" technique: short-leg portion first then traction** · **knee flexion >90°** (e.g. 105°) · **firm popliteal mould** · high energy · **NOT** immediate ED casting under sedation, single-leg spica, casting within 24 h → apply **body + thigh first**, lower leg last

### Traction

**Distal femoral pin** (proximal to physis) · **avoid proximal tibial pin → tibial tubercle damage → genu recurvatum** (also from SH V physeal injury) · 2–3 wk then spica · more complications than immediate spica

### Flexible nailing technique & complications

**Entry** — retrograde, medial + lateral, **~2–2.5 cm proximal to distal physis**

**Nail size** — each = **0.4 × isthmus**, **two equal nails**, **80% canal fill** (e.g. 8-yr, 35 kg → **two 4.0 mm**) · **largest equal nails** → best stability\
✗ Wrong options: asymmetric · impacted ends · open reduction routinely · hinged brace

**Most common complication** — **pain/irritation at insertion site near knee (up to 40%)** → keep protrusion **<25 mm**\
✗ Wrong options: shortening · AVN · malunion · rotation

**↑ Complications** — **≥11 yr or >49–50 kg (>108 lb)** → ↑ nonunion, malunion, complications, reoperation · comminuted/proximal/distal → malunion · ~50% have 15° malalignment

**Union** 10–12 wk · remove ~1 yr

![Submuscular bridge plate](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4019-screen-shot-2012-07-26-at-11-21-46-am.jpg)

### Rigid nail entry point & femoral head AVN

**Piriformis entry** — **contraindicated with open physes** → injures **deep branch of MFCA** (lateral epiphyseal / superior retinacular vessels) → **femoral head AVN** (groin/hip pain months later) · AVN **1–2%** (Orthobullets) · **≥4%** (OB)

**MFCA** — main head supply **after age 4**

**Safest** — **lateral entry** (lateral GT) > GT tip · GT entry risks **GT apophyseal arrest → coxa valga**, neck narrowing, hip subluxation

**Rigid vs flexible** — rigid: **higher AVN** (with open physes), less angular malunion, better in >11 yr / >49 kg · flexible: more knee pain, more malunion in older/heavier

![Medial femoral circumflex artery near piriformis fossa](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4019-mfca-moved.jpg)

### Polytrauma, head injury & timing

**Polytrauma + emergent laparotomy (13-yr, GCS 6)** → **external fixation** (damage control)\
✗ Wrong options: traction · nailing · plate

**Early fixation with head injury** — **no ↑ pulmonary or CNS complications**, **↓ length of stay**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ ARDS/pneumonia · worse mental function

## 6 Complications

**Overgrowth** — **most common in young** · **0.7–2 cm <10 yr** (avg 0.9 cm 2–10 yr) · within **2 yr** → accept shortening ≤2–3 cm

**Shortening >2–3 cm** — symptomatic → traction/fixation

**Malunion** — varus + flexion · remodels best in **sagittal plane** · **rotation doesn't remodel**

**Refracture** — **after ex-fix removal**, esp. **varus** (1.5–21%) · ex-fix: pin-tract infection (~50% of its complications), delayed/nonunion

**Plate** — refracture after hardware removal

**Genu recurvatum** — tibial tubercle arrest (traction pin / SH V)

## X vs Y

| | **Flexible nails** | **Submuscular plate** | **Rigid trochanteric nail** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | 5–11 | 5–11+ | >11 (≥9 OB) |
| Weight | <49 kg | Any | >49 kg |
| Pattern | **Length-stable** | **Length-unstable**, comminuted, proximal/distal | Any |
| Main complication | Knee pain | Refracture on removal | AVN (piriformis), coxa valga (GT) |

## How it's asked

**"7-yr, 55 lb, isolated stable fracture?"** → **closed reduction + flexible nails**

**"11-yr, 146 lb, open femur — contraindication to elastic nails?"** → **her weight**

**"Best candidate for submuscular plate?"** → **10-yr, 120 lb, long spiral comminuted midshaft**

**"Trochanteric rigid nail appropriate for?"** → **15-yr with butterfly fragment**

**"Immediate spica for?"** → **26-month-old, <2 cm shortening**

**"Titanium elastic nails for?"** → **10-yr, 35 kg, transverse**\
✗ Wrong options: comminuted · 51 kg

**"Submuscular plate for?"** → **7-yr highly comminuted**

**"10-yr TEN — appropriate case?"** → **transverse open, 35 kg**

**"7-yr floating knee?"** → **flexible nails femur + tibia**

**"2-yr, 1 cm short?"** → **closed reduction + spica**

**"10-yr, 115 lb, transverse?"** → **solid nail via greater trochanter**

**"10-yr, 75 lb?"** → **retrograde elastic nails**

**"1-yr, 25 lb, no NAT?"** → **hip spica**

**"Best for immediate spica?"** → **3-yr long oblique, 2 cm short**

**"7-yr, 38 kg, length-stable?"** → **closed reduction + flexible nails**

**"4-yr GSW comminuted open femur, artery intact?"** → **submuscular plating**

**"9-yr, 35 kg, transverse, 1 cm short?"** → **flexible IM nails**

**"Pavlik appropriate for?"** → **4-month-old displaced spiral**\
✗ Wrong options: spastic · polytrauma · 9-month-old

**"3-month-old, minimally displaced?"** → **Pavlik harness**

**"5-month-old, father fell?"** → **Pavlik harness** (after excluding abuse)

**"2-month-old, NAT negative?"** → **Pavlik harness**

**"3-yr — most predictive of NAT?"** → **transverse femur fracture**

**"3-week-old, distal femoral metaphyseal corner fracture?"** → **admit + social services**

**"9-month-old 'running', spiral femur — strengthens CPS referral?"** → **posterior rib fractures**

**"13-yr polytrauma for laparotomy?"** → **external fixation**

**"Early fixation with head injury?"** → **no ↑ pulmonary complications**

**"10-yr with SDH + ↑ ICP — timing?"** → **no difference in pulmonary/CNS complications**

**"8-yr isolated femur, hypotensive, Hb 8.5?"** → **re-evaluate for other injuries**

**"Piriformis nail, groin pain at 6 months?"** → **femoral head osteonecrosis**

**"Greatest MFCA risk?"** → **piriformis entry nail**

**"Damage to deep MFCA branch?"** → **piriformis entry**

**"13-yr hip pain after piriformis nail?"** → **femoral head ischaemic necrosis**

**"Rigid vs flexible in 11-yr — true?"** → **rigid has higher femoral head AVN**

**"Concern with piriformis start in immature patient?"** → **femoral head AVN**

**"Implant NOT to use in adolescents?"** → **piriformis-entry nail**

**"Tibial traction pin + flexible nails — later deformity?"** → **genu recurvatum**

**"Most common problem after elastic nails?"** → **insertion-site pain**

**"100 lb, 3 cm short — optimise TEN stability?"** → **largest diameter nails**

**"8-yr, 70 lb, two flexible rods — most common complication?"** → **knee irritation**

**"8-yr, 35 kg — start point + nail size?"** → **2.5 cm above physis, two 4.0 mm nails**

**"115 lb vs 85 lb with flexible nails?"** → **heavier child: ↑ nonunion, malunion, complications, reoperation**

**"Spica in 4-yr — most common complication needing revision?"** → **loss of reduction**

**"Spica compartment syndrome — EXCEPT?"** → **immediate ED casting under sedation**

**"Spica technique raising compartment risk?"** → **short-leg portion first, then traction**

**"Subtrochanteric proximal fragment pulled by?"** → **iliopsoas, abductors, external rotators**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Fibular Deficiency (anteromedial bowing)

*Pediatrics · 47 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — shortening or absence of the fibula (formerly **fibular hemimelia**; postaxial hypoplasia) · involves the **whole limb**

**Most common congenital long-bone deficiency** · 7.4–20 per million (TO) · bilateral not uncommon

**Genetics** — no known inheritance (mostly sporadic) · linked to **sonic hedgehog** · AD/AR/X-linked reported (TO)

### Three types of tibial bowing

| Bowing | Association |
|---|---|
| Anterolateral | **NF1 / congenital pseudarthrosis** (✗ Wrong options: fibular deficiency (the EXCEPT)) |
| Posteromedial | Physiologic, calcaneovalgus |
| **Anteromedial** | **Fibular deficiency** (most common cause) |

### Associated anomalies

| Region | Association |
|---|---|
