| Tibia | **Anteromedial bowing** + **skin dimple** over apex (half) · **fibular anlage** (fibrocartilaginous band, 60% of complete CFD) tethers tibia |
| Foot | **Equinovalgus** (TO, Orthobullets) · talipes equinovarus less often · **absent lateral rays** · **tarsal coalition (50%)** · **ball-and-socket ankle** → instability |
| Knee | **ACL/PCL deficiency** · **lateral femoral condyle hypoplasia** → **genu valgum** · hypoplastic tibial spines, shallow notch · patella alta/hypoplasia |
| Femur / hip | **Femoral shortening (50%)** · **PFFD**, **coxa vara** · **DDH** |
| LLD | Significant (femur ± tibia) |

**NOT associated** — **quadriceps hypotonia** · **congenital vertical talus** · SCFE · scoliosis · polydactyly · MCL insufficiency · cleft hand / insufficient extensor mechanism (→ **tibial** hemimelia)

**ACL dysplasia correlates with** — **lateral femoral condyle hypoplasia** · lateral tibial spine dysplasia · absent fibula · absent lateral rays\
✗ Wrong options: medial condyle · anterolateral bowing · PCL · ↓ slope

**Foot conflict** — "pes planovalgus not associated; equinovarus is" (one OB explanation) vs **equinovalgus = classic** (Orthobullets, TO) — read the options

## 3 Presentation

**Classic** — **short limb** · **skin dimple over mid-anterior tibia** · **equinovalgus foot** · missing lateral toes · genu valgum

**Ankle sprains + ACL laxity + coalition** → look for **LLD** (fibular deficiency)

![Fibular deficiency — short limb, dimple, equinovalgus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4055-shows-fibular-deficiency.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — absent/short fibula · anteromedial bow · hypoplastic tibial spines · shallow notch · **ball-and-socket ankle** (from tarsal coalition)

### Achterman & Kalamchi classification

| Type | Fibula | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **IA** | Present but short — proximal epiphysis below tibial physis, distal above talus | Heel lift (LLD >2 cm) |
| **IB** | **Partial absence (30–50%)**, distal part can't support ankle | Contralateral epiphysiodesis |
| **II** | **Complete absence** | Multiple Ilizarov lengthenings **or Syme amputation** |

**Birch classification** — by LLD + foot function · **directs treatment**

![Achterman-Kalamchi types](../../MCQ/attachments/q218919-illa-achterman-kalamchi-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Treatment based on foot/ankle function + LLD** — **not** on amount of fibula

### LLD natural history

**Proportional growth inhibition** — LLD **increases slowly, limb stays in proportion** (same % at maturity) → **Paley multiplier** predicts final LLD early\
✗ Wrong options: stays 2 cm · equalises · 2 cm/yr · complete failure

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Mild LLD, functional foot | Shoe lift / orthosis |
| LLD **<5 cm or <10%**, stable plantigrade foot | **Contralateral epiphysiodesis** |
| Plantigrade stable foot, LLD **<10%** | Lengthening (resect **fibular anlage**) |
| **Moderate LLD 10–30%** | **Lengthening + contralateral epiphysiodesis** |
| **LLD >30%** / **>5 cm at birth** / **nonfunctional unstable foot** (≥3 absent rays) / can't cope with lengthenings | **Syme amputation (~1 yr)** + prosthesis |

**Syme > Boyd** — Boyd bulbous, only ~1 cm longer · satisfaction **88% amputation vs 55% lengthening**

```
Fibular deficiency
├─ Functional plantigrade foot
│      ├─ LLD <10% / <5 cm → lift or contralateral epiphysiodesis / lengthening
│      └─ LLD 10–30% → lengthening + contralateral epiphysiodesis
└─ Nonfunctional foot or LLD >30% → Syme amputation ~1 yr
```

### Fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva – palovarotene

**FOP** — **ACVR1 (R206H)** activating BMP type I receptor mutation → progressive heterotopic ossification

**Palovarotene** — oral selective **RAR-γ agonist** → inhibits BMP/SMAD 1/5/8 → ↓ HO (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: FSH dystrophy · fibromuscular dysplasia · OI

## How it's asked

**"Congenital fibular deficiency associated with all EXCEPT?"** → **anterolateral bowing** (TO)

**"Fibular hemimelia — EXCEPT?"** → **quadriceps hypotonia** (✓ absent lateral rays, ACL deficiency, ball-and-socket ankle, coxa vara)

**"Associated EXCEPT?"** → **congenital vertical talus**

**"Image associated with fibular hemimelia?"** → **ball-and-socket ankle**

**"7-yr congenital limb difference — commonly associated with?"** → **absent lateral rays**

**"Concurrent conditions?"** → **tarsal coalition + ball-and-socket ankle**

**"ACL dysplasia correlates with?"** → **lateral femoral condyle hypoplasia**

**"Recurrent sprains, TC coalition, ACL laxity — also present?"** → **leg length discrepancy**

**"12-month-old, 2 cm tibial shortening — natural history?"** → **increases slowly, staying proportional**

**"Palovarotene treats?"** → **fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Flexible Pes Planovalgus (Flexible Flatfoot)

*Pediatrics · 48 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — ↓ medial arch + **hindfoot valgus** + forefoot abduction **on weight-bearing**, from **ligamentous laxity**

**Prevalence** — **44%** of school-age children (↓ with age) · 20–25% adults · normal toddler variant

**Associated** — generalised laxity · **gastrocsoleus contracture (25%)** · rotational problems · accessory navicular (controversial)

### Types

| Type | Note |
|---|---|
| **Hypermobile flexible** | **Most common**, familial, bilateral |
| Flexible with **tight heel cord** | Stretch |
| **Rigid flatfoot / tarsal coalition** | Least common · hindfoot valgus **doesn't correct** on toe-standing |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — usually **asymptomatic** · arch or pretibial pain

**Exam** — **flat only on standing**; arch **reconstitutes on toe-standing, hallux dorsiflexion (Jack test), or hanging** · hindfoot valgus → **varus on toe-rise** · **normal painless subtalar motion** · check **Silfverskiöld** for tight heel cord

![Arch reconstitutes on toe standing](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4069-20171003-135410.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Indications** — **painful** flatfoot or **rigid** flatfoot only\
✗ Wrong options: painless flexible

**Views** — **WB AP** (talar head coverage, TC angle) · **WB lateral** (Meary's) · **WB oblique** (coalition) · ± plantarflexion lateral (exclude CVT) · ± ankle AP/lateral (ankle valgus — myelodysplasia)

**Meary's angle** — **apex plantar** · **>4°** = arch collapse

![Meary's angle — apex plantar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4069-pediatric-flex-flatfoot-normal-mearys-angle-moved.jpg)

**Differentials** — tarsal coalition · CVT · accessory navicular

## 5 Management

### Nonoperative management & observation

**Asymptomatic** — **observation + education** · arch develops with age\
✗ Wrong options: arch supports · X-rays · PT "to help feet develop"

**Tight heel cord, painless** — **gastrocnemius stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: spine MRI (that's cavus) · X-rays · night AFO · UCBL without pain

**Orthotics** — **do NOT change natural history** · use/stop by **comfort** (no harm, no benefit) · soft arch support for symptoms · **UCBL** for symptomatic advanced cases (rigid → poorly tolerated)

**Short-foot exercises** — intrinsics, medial arch (TO)

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Painful + tight heel cord refractory to stretching** | **TAL / gastrocnemius recession** |
| **Refractory pain** (talar head, sinus tarsi) after extensive nonop | **Calcaneal (lateral column) lengthening — Evans** ± cuneiform osteotomy, peroneal lengthening |
| Hindfoot valgus | Medial **sliding calcaneal osteotomy** |
| **Forefoot supination** | **Plantar-based closing-wedge osteotomy of medial cuneiform** |

### Surgical - calcaneal lengthening osteotomy

**Evans lateral calcaneal lengthening** → **flexible pes planovalgus**\
✗ Wrong options: clubfoot · cavus · hallux valgus · Köhler

**12-yr, 2 yr pain, failed UCBL + orthoses, supple** → **calcaneal neck lengthening osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral slide · PL→PB · 1st MT DF osteotomy · PTT to dorsum (all cavus procedures)

**Isolated medial calcaneal slide without cuneiform osteotomy** → **persistent forefoot supination**\
✗ Wrong options: persistent heel valgus

![Evans calcaneal lengthening](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4069-evans.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Flexible flatfoot** | **Rigid flatfoot (coalition)** | **CVT** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Toe-rise | Arch + heel varus **return** | **No correction** | Rigid |
| Subtalar motion | Normal | **↓** | — |
| Pain | Rare | Often | — |
| X-ray | Only if painful | Oblique / CT | Forced PF lateral |

## How it's asked

**"5-yr painless flexible flatfeet, DF neutral knee extended / 15° flexed?"** → **gastrocnemius stretching**

**"2-yr flatfoot, arches reconstitute, normal ROM?"** → **observation and education**

**"3-yr, mother asks about custom inserts?"** → **continue/discontinue by comfort; resolves with time**

**"Evans osteotomy for?"** → **flexible pes planovalgus**

**"12-yr refractory painful flexible flatfoot?"** → **calcaneal neck lengthening osteotomy**

**"Calcaneal slide without medial cuneiform osteotomy?"** → **persistent forefoot supination**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Friedreich's Ataxia

*Pediatrics · 49 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — inherited **spinocerebellar degeneration**

**Incidence** — **1 in 50,000** · onset **7–25 yr** (Orthobullets) / **7–15 yr** (OB) · earlier with more GAA repeats

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal recessive**

**Gene** — **GAA trinucleotide repeat** in **frataxin (FXN)**, **chromosome 9q13** · frataxin = mitochondrial protein (iron metabolism, oxidative stress)

**Pathology** — **dorsal root ganglia neuronopathy** + loss of peripheral sensory fibres + **posterior column** degeneration · not anterior horn α-motor neuron loss (= **SMA**)

**EMG** — motor + sensory defects, ↑ polyphasic potentials · ↓ NCV in upper limbs

## 3 Presentation

**Classic triad** — **ataxia** (wide-based staggering gait — presenting symptom) · **areflexia** · **positive Babinski (extensor plantar)**

**Other** — weakness · nystagmus · ↓ proprioception · **cardiomyopathy**

**Orthopaedic** — **cavovarus foot** (very high arch, **rigid**, progressive, claw toes; strong plantar reflex + PTT pull into equinovarus) · **scoliosis**

![Cavovarus foot — Coleman block assessment](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4087-colman-block-test-flexible-hindfoot-courtesy-miller-moved.png)

**Most likely condition** — **foot deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: hip dysplasia (CMT) · malignant hyperthermia (myelomeningocele) · tibia vara (Blount)

![Scoliosis in Friedreich's ataxia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4087-scoli-fre.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **genetic testing** (frataxin)

### Key radiology findings

**Standing scoliosis series** · AP + lateral foot if cavovarus

## 5 Management

**Cardiomyopathy** — **cardiology review before surgery** · coenzyme Q (antioxidant) slows cardiac decline, not ataxia

### Operative criteria

| Problem | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Cavovarus, **early, ambulatory** | **Plantar release + tendon transfers ± MT/calcaneal osteotomy** (tenotomies + bracing early) |
| Cavovarus, **rigid / late / nonambulatory** | **Triple arthrodesis** (bracing/stretching ineffective) |
| Scoliosis **<40°**, no progression predictors | Observe |
| Scoliosis **40–60°** | Grey zone |
| Scoliosis **>60°** or rapid progression with predictors | **PSF + segmental instrumentation**, upper thoracic → lower lumbar (**usually not to pelvis**) |

**Scoliosis progression predictors** — **disease onset <10 yr** · **scoliosis onset <15 yr**

```
Friedreich ataxia
├─ Cavovarus: ambulatory early → soft-tissue releases/transfers ± osteotomy
│      └─ Rigid / nonambulatory → triple arthrodesis
└─ Scoliosis: <40° observe · >60° or predictors → PSF (not to pelvis)
```

## 6 Complications & prognosis

**Wheelchair** — by ~**30** (Orthobullets) / 1st–2nd decade of disease (OB)

**Death** — **cardiomyopathy** (± pneumonia, aspiration), 4th–5th decade (~50)

## X vs Y

| | **Friedreich ataxia** | **CMT** | **SMA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gene | **Frataxin (AR)** | PMP22 (AD) | Survival motor neuron protein |
| Lesion | DRG + posterior columns + cerebellum | Peripheral myelin | **Anterior horn cells** |
| Reflexes | Areflexia + **Babinski +** | Areflexia | Areflexia, fasciculations |
| Foot | Rigid cavovarus | Cavovarus (flexible → rigid) | — |
| Heart | **Cardiomyopathy** | — | — |

## How it's asked

**"Friedreich's ataxia — all true EXCEPT?"** → **loss of anterior horn α-motor neurons** (SMA)

**"Patients most likely have?"** → **foot deformity (cavovarus)**

**"Scoliosis surgery threshold?"** → **>60° (observe <40°)**

**"Gene + inheritance?"** → **frataxin GAA repeat, autosomal recessive**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Gaucher Disease

*Pediatrics · 50 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Most common lysosomal storage disease** · ~**1 in 40,000** · **Ashkenazi Jewish**

### Enzyme deficiency

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal recessive** · chromosome **1q21** (TO)

**Enzyme** — **β-glucocerebrosidase** deficiency → **glucocerebroside (sphingolipid)** accumulates in macrophages of **liver, spleen, marrow**

| Disease | Deficient enzyme |
|---|---|
| **Gaucher** | **β-glucocerebrosidase** |
| Niemann-Pick | Sphingomyelinase |
| Fabry | α-galactosidase |
| Tay-Sachs | Hexosaminidase A |
| Krabbe | Galactocerebrosidase |

**Labs** — **anaemia + thrombocytopenia** · **↑ plasma glucocerebrosides** (diagnostic)

**Histology** — marrow: **giant (binucleate) Gaucher storage cells** filled with glucocerebroside

**Physis** — **reserve (resting) zone** (OB, achondroplasia chapter)

### Classification

| Type | Features | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|
| **1 (adult)** — **most common** | Bruising, anaemia, fractures, no CNS | **Enzyme replacement** works |
| 2 (infantile) | Brain + organs | **Lethal by ~3 yr**, untreatable |
| 3 (juvenile) | Teen onset, thrombocytopenia, hepatomegaly, fractures, gradual CNS | Non-CNS responds to ERT |

## 3 Presentation

**Systemic** — fatigue (anaemia), bleeding (thrombocytopenia), infection, seizures / delay (CNS) · **hepatosplenomegaly** · murmur

**Orthopaedic** — bone pain · **"bone crisis"** (acute infarct, like sickle crisis) · joint pain/contracture · **pathologic fractures** · osteomyelitis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Erlenmeyer flask** — **distal femur / proximal tibia** in **~80%** · **most common radiological finding**, **asymptomatic abnormal remodelling**, needs no treatment

![Bilateral distal femoral Erlenmeyer flask flaring](../../MCQ/attachments/q966-illa-untitled.jpg)

**Osteopenia** — **almost all** (worse after splenectomy)

**Osteonecrosis** — medullary (often asymptomatic) + corticocancellous: **proximal/distal femur, proximal tibia, proximal humerus** · **10× more likely after splenectomy**

**Osteomyelitis** — ↑ prevalence

**FALSE statement (TO)** — "**medullary osteonecrosis is the most common skeletal manifestation**" (✓ Erlenmeyer flask most common radiological; ✓ abnormal remodelling most common asymptomatic; ✓ post-splenectomy ↑ AVN)

![Erlenmeyer flask deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10132-erlynmeyer-flas.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Treatment | Indication |
|---|---|
| Observation / supportive | Unaffected (normal bloods, minimal organomegaly, no MRI bone lesions) |
| **Enzyme replacement** (imiglucerase, velaglucerase, taliglucerase) | **All children + symptomatic** · not type 2 |
| **Substrate reduction** (miglustat) | Milder, can't tolerate IV ERT |
| **Bone marrow transplant** | Early → may be curative |

### Fracture surgery

**Pre-op** — **optimise with ERT** · blood, clotting factors, platelets available (bleeding) · maintain **oxygenation** (avoid bone crisis) · ↑ infection risk

## How it's asked

**"Absent glucocerebrosidase causes?"** → **Gaucher disease**\
✗ Wrong options: Niemann-Pick · Fabry · Tay-Sachs · Krabbe

**"Gaucher — all correct EXCEPT?"** → **medullary osteonecrosis is the most common skeletal manifestation** (TO)

**"Erlenmeyer flask distal femur?"** → **Gaucher**

**"Most common type?"** → **type 1 (adult), treatable with ERT**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Genu Valgum (knocked knees)

*Pediatrics · 51 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Physiologic tibiofemoral angle by age

| Age | Alignment |
|---|---|
| Birth – <2 yr | **Varus** (10–15° at birth, TO) |
| **~2 yr** | **Neutral** |
| **3–4 yr** | **Peak valgus** — **15–20°** (Orthobullets) · **10° at age 3** (TO answer) |
| **≥7 yr** | Adult — valgus **≤12°**, intermalleolar distance **<8 cm** · adult **~7°** (TO) / 4–7° / 5–10° (OB) |

**Presentation age** — **3–5 yr** (2–8) · rarely worsens after **7**

**Pathologic site** — **distal femur** most common

**Risk factors** — prior infection/trauma · **rickets** · obesity · skeletal dysplasia · lysosomal storage

### Causes

| Bilateral | Unilateral |
|---|---|
| **Physiologic** | **Physeal injury** (trauma, infection, vascular) |
| **Renal osteodystrophy**, hypophosphatemic rickets | **Proximal tibial metaphyseal fracture (Cozen)** |
| **Morquio (MPS IV)** · **SED (tarda)** · **Ellis–van Creveld** · pseudoachondroplasia | **Fibrous dysplasia**, **osteochondroma/MHE**, **enchondroma** |
| — | Fibular hemimelia |

**Apparent valgus** — obese thighs · femoral anteversion · external tibial torsion

### Anatomy & mechanics

**Normal LDFA / MPTA** — **85–90°** each

**Mechanical axis** — hip centre → ankle centre should pass knee centre · valgus → **lateral** deviation, ↑ lateral compartment load

![Mechanical axis, LDFA and MPTA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4052-mechanical-axis-mechanical-lateral-distal-femur-angle-medial.jpg)

**Hueter-Volkmann** — compression ↓, distraction ↑ growth · growth change mostly from **hypertrophic zone (75%)** vs proliferative (25%)

**Patellar instability** — ↑ Q angle, hypoplastic lateral femoral condyle / shallow sulcus

## 3 Presentation

**Complaints** — cosmesis (most common) · medial knee pain · circumduction gait · patellar instability

**Exam** — knees touch, **intermalleolar distance >8 cm** · rotational profile · stigmata of rickets/dysplasia (Maffucci)

![Intermalleolar distance](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4052-intermalleolar-distance.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Indications** — asymmetry · beyond physiologic range for age · short stature · trauma/infection · LLD

**View** — **standing long-leg AP, patellae forward** · lateral MAD · physeal narrowing/closure · Park-Harris lines

**CT/MRI** — rarely (tumour, physeal bar)

### Genes & lab markers

**Rickets** — Ca, PO₄, **25-OH vitamin D**, PTH · **Morquio** — urinary **keratan sulfate** · syndromes — genetic tests

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<7 yr**, angle **<15°** | **Observe** (bracing ineffective long term) |
| **>15–20° valgus at 7–10 yr** · TF angle >15° · **IMD >10 cm after age 10** · rapid progression after 7 | **Medial hemiepiphysiodesis** |
| Age **>10**, mechanical axis in **lateral quadrant** (outer 25%) of plateau | Medial hemiepiphysiodesis |
| **Skeletally mature** / insufficient growth / physeal bar | **Osteotomy** at site of deformity |

**Hemiepiphysiodesis** — temporary (**8-plate**, staples, Metaizeau screw — most common) or permanent (Phemister) · extraperiosteal, mid-sagittal · one 8-plate or 2 staples per physis · correction ~**7°/yr femur, 5°/yr tibia** · >95% idiopathic / 80% pathologic correct · remove when axis at/just medial of centre

**8-yr, IMD 12 cm** → **bilateral medial proximal tibial tension-band plates**\
✗ Wrong options: 11-yr Drennan 18° (that's Blount → lateral plate) · 4-yr 20° (physiologic) · 7-yr 9° (normal) · 16-yr (near mature → osteotomy)

**12-yr bilateral valgus, open physes** → **temporary hemiepiphysiodesis of bilateral medial distal femurs**\
✗ Wrong options: HKAFO · osteotomy · unilateral · lateral (worsens)

### Treatment - hemiepiphysiodesis vs osteotomy

**Mature 18-yr, mLDFA 73° (femoral valgus), normal MPTA** → **medial closing-wedge distal femoral (varus) osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial osteotomy → oblique joint line

| | **Lateral opening-wedge DFO** | **Medial closing-wedge DFO** |
|---|---|---|
| Pros | Adjustable correction | **Stable**, shorter immobilisation, **no peroneal stretch** |
| Cons | Graft, less stable, **peroneal nerve stretch**, nonunion, lengthens | Technically demanding, shortens |

## 6 Complications

**Rebound** — **56%** (≥5° loss after plate removal) · risks: **femoral deformity, younger age, faster correction**, intentional overcorrection → slight overcorrection / operate nearer maturity

**Overcorrection** — lost to follow-up (12%) · **undercorrection** — little growth left

**Peroneal nerve** — opening-wedge / acute correction → **decompress** (lateral compartment fascia + anterior-lateral septum) or gradual frame

**Nonunion** — opening wedge, >20° · **physeal closure** <1% (extraperiosteal, remove within 2–3 yr)

**Cozen** — post-fracture valgus peaks **12–18 mo**, remodels in **2–4 yr** → **observe**

## How it's asked

**"18-yr, mLDFA 73°, MPTA 87°, TF angle 25°?"** → **medial closing-wedge femoral osteotomy**

**"12-yr skeletally immature bilateral severe valgus?"** → **temporary hemiepiphysiodesis of bilateral medial distal femoral physes**

**"Medial proximal tibial tension-band plates — best candidate?"** → **8-yr with intermalleolar distance 12 cm**

**"Normal tibiofemoral axis at age 3?"** → **valgus of 10°** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Septic Arthritis - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 52 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 4–5 / 100,000 children/yr · **50% <2 yr** · **M:F 2:1** · hip and knee each **35%** of septic joints

**Risk factors** — **prematurity** · **C-section** · NICU · umbilical/venous catheters, heel puncture (transient bacteremia)

### Mechanism & pathophysiology

**Routes** — haematogenous (URI precedes ~80%) · direct inoculation · **contiguous metaphyseal osteomyelitis** — **intra-articular metaphysis: hip, shoulder, elbow, ankle (NOT knee)** · neonates: **transphyseal vessels**

**Cartilage destruction** — **matrix metalloproteinases (MMPs)** from bacteria, synovium, PMNs, cartilage → damage within **8 hours** (6 h in animal studies) · TLR → NF-κB → MMP production\
✗ Wrong options: MMPs anabolic · TLRs inhibit · activated by chelators (chelators inactivate; stromelysin is an MMP)

**↑ Intra-articular pressure** → femoral head **osteonecrosis**

### Causative organisms by age (S aureus, GBS, Kingella, gonococcus)

| Age / setting | Organism | Gram stain |
|---|---|---|
| **Neonate, community** (vaginal birth) | **Group B streptococcus** | GP cocci in chains |
| Neonate, nosocomial | **S. aureus** | — |
| **<4 yr, subacute, mild labs, negative cultures** | **Kingella kingae** — grows in **blood-culture bottles** (BACTEC); **PCR (rtxA)** · resistant to **vancomycin + clindamycin** | **GN coccobacillus** (facultative anaerobe) |
| **>2 yr / overall** | **S. aureus** — **most common** (catalase + coagulase +) · also causes **impetigo** | **GP cocci in clusters** |
| **Adolescent, sexually active** | **N. gonorrhoeae** — **migratory polyarthralgia**, maculopapular rash, tenosynovitis · F:M 3:1 · cultures negative ~50% | **GN intracellular diplococci** |
| Post-varicella | **Group A β-haemolytic strep** | — |
| Unvaccinated | H. influenzae (↓ with vaccine) — LP if meningitis signs | GN coccobacillus |
| **MRSA osteomyelitis + DVT** | **Panton-Valentine leukocidin** · CRP very high | — |

**Kingella** — "most common in children of all ages" (Orthobullets) · **S. aureus** most common overall (OB) · no organism in **up to 55%**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **acute** pain (faster than osteomyelitis) · fever, **toxic** · limp / **refusal to bear weight** · antibiotics may mask

**Exam** — hip held in **flexion, abduction, external rotation** (max capsular volume) · pain with passive motion / log-roll · pseudoparalysis · check adjacent joints + spine

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria — Kocher

| Original Kocher criterion | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Fever** | **>38.5 °C (101.3 °F)** |
| **Non-weight-bearing** | ✓ |
| **ESR** | **>40 mm/h** |
| **Serum WBC** | **>12,000/mm³** |
| Modified (Caird) — **CRP** | **>2.0 mg/dL (20 mg/L)** |

| Criteria met | Probability of septic hip |
|---|---|
| 0 | **<0.2%** |
| 1 | **3%** |
| **2** | **40%** |
| **3** | **93%** |
| 4 | **99.6%** |

**Most predictive order** — **fever > CRP > ESR > refusal to bear weight > WBC** (CRP 2nd-best after fever)

**CRP >2 mg/dL + refusal to bear weight** → **74%** · **CRP <2 mg/dL (<20 mg/L)** → favours **transient synovitis** · both absent <1%

**Kocher criteria apply to the HIP only** — **not the knee** → knee: **aspirate**

**Labs** — WBC ↑ in only 30–60% (neonates may be leukopenic) · ESR may be normal early · **CRP rises in 6–8 h** · **blood cultures** if febrile (often positive when joint culture negative)

### Synovial fluid

| Diagnosis | Synovial WBC | PMN |
|---|---|---|
| Traumatic effusion | <5,000 | — |
| Transient (toxic) synovitis | 5,000–15,000 | <25% |
| Rheumatic fever | 10,000–15,000 | ~50% |
| JRA | 15,000–80,000 | ~75% (gradual onset, keeps walking) |
| **Septic arthritis** | **>50,000** | **>75%** |

**PMN % more sensitive than total WBC** (85% PMN → 88% sensitivity) · glucose ≥50 mg/dL below serum · Gram stain + culture (± synovial tissue)

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + frog)** — often normal early · **joint-space widening** (infant: **lateral displacement of proximal femur**) · subluxation/dislocation · bone lesions

**Ultrasound** — **effusion** (can't tell septic vs sterile) · guides aspiration · **neonate: scan both hips** if any septic joint

**MRI** — effusion + adjacent osteomyelitis · don't let it delay surgery

![Joint-space widening — lateral displacement of proximal femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4032-joint-space-widening.jpg)

![Hip effusion on ultrasound (right) vs normal (left)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4032-shows-hip-effusion-on-the-right-and-normal-hip-on-the-left.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Workup & next step (US, aspiration, arthrotomy)

```
Irritable hip in a child
├─ Non-toxic, equivocal → ultrasound for effusion
├─ Effusion / high suspicion (≥2–3 Kocher, CRP ↑) → aspiration (before antibiotics)
│      └─ WBC >50,000, PMN >75%, +Gram → urgent I&D + IV antibiotics
└─ 3–4 criteria + effusion on US → emergent arthrotomy
```

**6-week-old (preterm, C-section), afebrile, widened joint space, painful motion** → **aspiration**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI (delays) · CT · observe · Pavlik

**Non-toxic 3-yr, normal X-ray, fever, ESR 50, CRP 5** → **ultrasound** next\
✗ Wrong options: NSAID trial · CT · IV antibiotics first · discharge

**Fever + refusal + ↑ CRP/ESR** → **hip aspiration**\
✗ Wrong options: IV abx · oral abx · ibuprofen · MRI · Lyme Western blot · rheumatology

**3-yr, 3/4 criteria + US effusion** → **emergent arthrotomy + I&D**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · repeat US · MRI · antibiotics before cultures in a non-septic child

**Lyme-endemic hiker, fever 38.2, WBC 14, CRP 5.5** → **aspirate** (septic more likely than Lyme)

### Operative criteria

**Urgent I&D = standard of care** — **surgical emergency** (removes chondrolytic enzymes, ↓ pressure / epiphyseal ischaemia) · err toward drainage

**Antibiotics alone** — **adolescent gonococcal** arthritis (high-dose penicillin ± aspiration)

**Technique** — anterolateral or **anterior (Smith-Petersen)** arthrotomy · remove pus, irrigate · ± **1 × 1 cm capsular window** · drain · other joints open/arthroscopic

**Antibiotics** — aspirate first, then empiric IV · **2–7 d culture-specific IV → 2–3 wk oral** · stop when CRP/ESR + clinical picture normal

| Age | Empiric |
|---|---|
| <3 mo | 1st-gen cephalosporin (GBS, S. aureus, gram-neg) |
| 3 mo–5 yr | 2nd/3rd-gen cephalosporin (Kingella, S. aureus, pneumococcus, GAS, H. flu) |
| 5–12 yr | 1st-gen cephalosporin |
| 12–18 yr | 2nd/3rd-gen cephalosporin (gonococcus) |

## 6 Complications

### Sequelae, prognosis & cartilage destruction

**Poor prognosis** — **age <6 months** · **associated osteomyelitis** · **delay >4 days** (strongest) · **hip** (vs knee)

**Sequelae** — **complete femoral head/neck destruction** (neglected neonatal sepsis) · **osteonecrosis**, articular destruction · physeal arrest → **LLD**, angular deformity · chronic osteomyelitis · persistent limp · contracture · follow **1–2 yr**

**NOT a typical sequel** — **chronic posterior instability/dislocation** (esp. after anterior approach)

**Salvage** — varus/valgus proximal femoral osteotomy · trochanteric osteotomy · modified Albee arthroplasty

![Destroyed femoral head after untreated septic hip](../../MCQ/attachments/q566-a-xray-pelvis-ap-septic-hip.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Septic hip** | **Transient synovitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Fever | **>38.5 °C** | Low / none |
| Weight bearing | Refuses | Often limps |
| ESR / WBC | **>40 / >12,000** | Normal |
| CRP | **>2 mg/dL** | **<2 mg/dL** |
| Synovial WBC | **>50,000, >75% PMN** | 5,000–15,000 |
| Treatment | **Urgent I&D** | NSAIDs, observe |

## How it's asked

**"ESR >40, fever, refusal + which other Kocher finding?"** → **serum WBC >12,000**

**"3-yr, febrile, refuses to walk, WBC 8,000, ESR 20, CRP 1.9?"** → **arthrocentesis best confirms** (2/4 = 40%)\
✗ Wrong options: CRP is a Kocher criterion

**"8-month-old FABER hip — 2nd best predictor after fever?"** → **elevated CRP**

**"Fever + refusal, WBC 11, ESR 18 — probability?"** → **26–50%** (2/4 = 40%)

**"Afebrile 37.8, WBC 13, ESR 19, refuses — criteria / risk / organism?"** → **2/4, 40%, S. aureus**

**"Favours transient synovitis?"** → **CRP 15 mg/L** (<20)\
✗ Wrong options: ESR 43 · 38.7 °C · WBC 12,500 · leptin (SCFE)

**"Lab supporting septic hip?"** → **CRP 22 mg/L**\
✗ Wrong options: WBC 10,000 · ESR 38 · 38.2 °C · synovial 40,000

**"6-yr knee effusion, 1/4 Kocher?"** → **aspirate the knee — Kocher not valid for knees**

**"6-week-old preterm, widened joint space?"** → **aspiration**

**"18-month-old, 60,000 WBC, 95% PMN?"** → **septic arthritis**

**"3-yr, WBC 15, ESR 120, CRP 5, effusion?"** → **emergent arthrotomy + I&D**

**"Lyme-area hiker, fever, ↑ WBC/CRP, effusion?"** → **hip aspiration**

**"3-yr, 39 °C, WBC 19,000, ESR 45, effusion?"** → **hip aspiration**

**"5-yr, fever, ↓ hip ROM, normal WBC, ↑ CRP/ESR?"** → **aspiration of right hip**

**"Non-toxic 3-yr, fever, ESR 50, CRP 5, normal X-ray?"** → **ultrasound left hip**

**"Gram stain of main organism in pediatric osteomyelitis/septic arthritis?"** → **gram-positive cocci (clusters)**

**"8-day-old after vaginal birth?"** → **group B streptococcus**

**"No growth on blood agar, grew in blood-culture medium after 1 wk?"** → **Kingella kingae**

**"Osteomyelitis + DVT, positive blood culture?"** → **MRSA**

**"3-yr septic hip — most common organism's Gram stain?"** → **gram-positive cocci in clusters**

**"6-yr septic hip organism?"** → **catalase + coagulase positive GP cocci in clusters**

**"17-yr with macular rash?"** → **gram-negative diplococci**

**"19-month-old, mild labs, cultures negative at 14 days?"** → **gram-negative facultative anaerobic coccobacillus (Kingella)**

**"18-month-old, mild labs, effusion — micrograph?"** → **Kingella kingae**

**"16-yr gonococcal hip — true?"** → **usually polyarthralgia of multiple joints**

**"2-yr knee, PCR positive, culture negative?"** → **gram-negative coccobacilli (Kingella)**

**"Most common organism is also associated with?"** → **impetigo**

**"7-yr septic hip micrograph?"** → **staphylococcus (clusters)**

**"3-yr limp, prior neonatal sepsis, destroyed femoral head?"** → **residual untreated septic hip**

**"Strongest poor-prognosis predictor?"** → **delay >4 days**

**"2-yr limp, neonatal knee infection, absent femoral head?"** → **untreated neonatal hip infection**

**"MMPs — true?"** → **mediate cartilage destruction in septic arthritis**

**"LEAST likely complication of delayed treatment?"** → **chronic posterior hip instability**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Humeral Shaft Fracture - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 53 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **<10%** of pediatric humerus fractures

**Mechanism** — neonates: birth (hyperextension/rotation) · adolescents: direct trauma · low energy → **pathologic** lesion · **<3 yr + spiral → abuse** · any fracture in **non-ambulator → NAT** work-up

**Growth** — **proximal 80% : distal 20%** of humeral length (distal ~3 mm/yr)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — swelling, deformity · neonate **pseudoparalysis** (reflexes intact) · **radial nerve palsy** (no wrist/finger extension)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Full-length AP + lateral humerus** + shoulder and elbow views · transverse / oblique · look for pathologic lesion

![Pediatric humeral shaft fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4005-pediatric-humerus-fracture-2.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment & prognosis

**Almost all → immobilisation** (huge remodelling) · neonate: swathe / pin sleeve to shirt · young child: **sling and swathe**, collar-and-cuff · coaptation splint, hanging cast · **Sarmiento brace** (older) · ROM at 2–3 wk

| Acceptable alignment | Value |
|---|---|
| Younger child | **<35–45°** angulation (Orthobullets) · up to 40° (OB) |
| Older child | **<20°** varus/valgus · **<20°** procurvatum · **<15°** rotation · **<2 cm** shortening |

**Neonatal birth fracture** → **callus by 2 weeks, complete remodelling within 6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: malunion · nonunion · radial nerve risk

**8-month-old midshaft fracture, angulated** → **splint + sling for comfort** · consider NAT\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · CRPP · hanging cast (child doesn't sit/stand)

![Remodelling of a pediatric humeral shaft fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4005-figure-3-remodelling-humeral-shaft.jpg)

### Operative criteria

| Indication for fixation |
|---|
| **Open fracture** |
| Polytrauma (needs arms for mobilisation) |
| **Ipsilateral forearm fracture — "floating elbow"** (✓ the indication) |
| Associated shoulder injury |
| Unacceptable / unstable alignment |

**Not indications** — mechanism, age, open physes, **radial nerve palsy**

**Fixation** — **flexible IM nails** · K-wires · plate (anterior/anterolateral/posterior) 3.5/4.5 mm

## 6 Complications

**Radial nerve palsy** — **<5%**, middle + distal third · **neurapraxia** → **observe** · EMG ± explore if no return by **3–4 months**\
✗ Wrong options: immediate exploration

**Malunion** — rarely functional (shoulder ROM) · 20–30° fine · LLD common but harmless · delayed union rare (US stimulator)

## How it's asked

**"3-day-old birth humeral fracture — prognosis?"** → **callus by 2 weeks, remodelled by 6 months**

**"6-yr distal-third fracture, can't dorsiflex wrist?"** → **monitor radial nerve + sling and swathe**

**"8-month-old midshaft humerus?"** → **splint and sling for comfort**

**"10-yr humerus fracture — best indication for fixation?"** → **ipsilateral forearm fracture (floating elbow)**

**"Distal humeral physis contribution?"** → **20%**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Infantile Blount's Disease (tibia vara)

*Pediatrics · 54 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — **progressive pathologic genu varum centred at the proximal tibia** · age **2–5 yr** (Orthobullets) · 0–3 yr (OB) · infantile **<8 yr** vs adolescent >8 yr (TO) — read the options

**Risk factors** — **obesity** · **early walkers (<1 yr)** · **African American / Hispanic** · never in non-ambulatory children (TO)

**Sex / side** — **M > F** (Orthobullets) · OB lists female association · **bilateral in 50%**

### Pathophysiology

**Cause** — **mechanical overload** of medial proximal tibial physis in a **genetically susceptible** child → **osteochondrosis** of medial physis + epiphysis → may progress to **physeal bar**

**Deformity components** — **varus** of proximal medial metaphysis + **procurvatum (apex anterior)** + **internal rotation** (TO) · **external rotation** is NOT a component (TO) · not recurvatum (OB) · one TO explanation says "genu recurvatum" — read the options

**Associated** — **internal tibial torsion** (✗ Wrong options: metatarsus adductus) · **compensatory distal femoral VALGUS** (✗ Wrong options: distal femoral varus (adolescent))

### Physiologic alignment

| Age | Orthobullets | TO |
|---|---|---|
| Birth–1 yr | **Varus** (bow legs, normal <2 yr) | Varus up to 10–15° |
| Neutral | **~14 months** | **18–24 months** |
| Peak valgus | **~3 yr** | **3–4 yr** (up to 15–20°) |
| Adult valgus | **~7 yr** | 5–6 yr (5–6°) |

**Physiologic bowing** — resolves by **36 months** in almost all (OB) · symmetric, femur + tibia · Brooks & Gross: varus to 18 mo → valgus peak 4 yr → adult 5–6 yr (OB)

**Tibial torsion (TO)** — 5° internal at birth → neutral by 4–5 yr → **20–25° external by 8 yr**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **genu varum + flexion + internal rotation** deformity · **lateral thrust on walking** (✓ abnormal finding) · internal tibial torsion · LLD · **no** tenderness, effusion or ↓ ROM

**Not abnormal** — **internal thigh–foot angle 5°** in an infant (OB)

### Cover-up test

**Technique** — patella forward · examiner covers lower leg + foot · look at thigh–upper tibia axis (age 1–3 yr) (TO)

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Positive** (bowing in upper tibia) | X-ray · **high sensitivity** |
| Neutral (straight axis) | X-ray |
| **Negative** (slight valgus upper tibia) | Physiologic bowing · **high specificity + NPV** |

![Cover-up test](../../MCQ/attachments/q218582-illb-cover-up-test.jpg)

### When to X-ray

**Physiologic, <2 yr, symmetric** → **clinical observation** (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: X-rays · vitamin D · genetic screen

**Triggers for workup** — persists **>2 yr** (OB) · persists **>18 months** or asymmetric (Birch) · severe **>20°** · worsening · asymmetry · **varus thrust** · low height/weight percentile\
✗ Wrong options: family history alone

**Vitamin D workup** — if **<20th percentile** on growth curves (OB)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**View** — standing AP **with patellae facing forward** (ITT confounds)

**Findings** — varus focused at proximal tibia · sharp angular deformity · asymmetry · **medial + posterior sloping** of epiphysis · **metaphyseal beaking**\
✗ Wrong options: physiologic (symmetric flaring of femur + tibia)

**Pathognomonic** — **medial metaphyseal fragmentation** → progressive tibia vara (TO)

**MRI (TO)** — ↑ thickness of medial proximal tibial chondroepiphysis · ↑ height + width of **medial meniscus** · abnormal medial femoral epiphysis

**Neglected infantile Blount (14 yr)** — **severe depression of medial tibial epiphysis**\
✗ Wrong options: adolescent Blount (metaphyseal) · septic/JRA (both sides of joint) · hemophilia (widened intercondylar notch)

### Metaphyseal–diaphyseal (Drennan) angle

**Measurement** — line through metaphyseal beaks vs line **perpendicular to tibial long axis**

| MDA | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **<10°** | **95% resolve** naturally |
| **11–16°** | **Close observation** (Orthobullets) · **>11°** warrants observation (TO) · 9–16° → brace if no correction after 24 months (OB) |
| **>16°** | **Abnormal** · **95% progress** · ✓ most reliably separates Blount from physiologic |
| **>20°** | Operative indication |

**Tibiofemoral angle** — femoral vs tibial long axes

![Metaphyseal–diaphyseal angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4050-metaphyseal-diaphyseal-angle-moved.jpg)

### Langenskiöld classification

| Stage | Feature |
|---|---|
| **I–IV** | Increasing medial **metaphyseal beaking + sloping** |
| **V–VI** | **Epiphyseal–metaphyseal bony bridge** (physeal bar) |

**Use** — prognosis · stage II and IV can correct spontaneously in young children

![Langenskiöld stages](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4050-langen.jpg)

### CT for physeal bar (TO)

**Order CT pre-op if** — age **>5 yr** · medial physeal slope **50–70°** · **Langenskiöld IV** · weight **>95th percentile** · black girls meeting the above

### Differential (pathologic genu varum)

**List** — persistent physiologic varus · **rickets** · OI · MED · SED · metaphyseal dysostosis (Schmid, Jansen) · focal fibrocartilaginous defect · TAR · physeal injury (radiation, infection, trauma)

## 5 Management

```
Infantile Blount
├─ <3 yr, Langenskiöld I–II → KAFO
│      (improvement within 1 yr; wear ~2 yr)
├─ Brace failure / progression → proximal tibia-fibula valgus osteotomy
└─ ≥3–4 yr, stage III–VI or MDA >20° → osteotomy
       └─ physeal bar (V–VI) → add bar resection / hemiepiphysiodesis
```

### Bracing (knee-ankle-foot orthosis, KAFO)

**Indication** — **stage I–II in children <3 yr** (Orthobullets) · <3 yr **and unilateral** stage I–II (TO) · TO calls it "mermaid splints"

**Duration** — ~**2 yr** for bony resolution · improvement should show **within 1 yr** · fails after **12 months** in 2–3 yr old → osteotomy (OB)

**Poor results / failure risk** — **obesity (>90th percentile)** · **bilateral** · **varus thrust** · age **≥3 yr** at start · Langenskiöld **III** · progressive deformity · age >4 (TO)

**Evidence** — **no strong consensus** on bracing efficacy (OB) · night KAFO poorly tolerated · (✗ Wrong options: AFO (doesn't address knee) · serial casting)\
✗ Wrong options: "effective" · "ineffective" · needs PT

### Proximal tibial osteotomy

### Operative criteria

| Indication for proximal tibia/fibula valgus osteotomy |
|---|
| Stage I–II in child **>3 yr** |
| **Stage III, IV, V, VI** |
| **Age ≥4 yr** (all stages) |
| **Failed brace** / progressive deformity |
| **MDA >20°** |
| Morbidly obese (OB) |

**Timing** — **before age 4** → recurrence significantly ↓ · early osteotomy (**2–4 yr**) = best results, least recurrence (TO) · best results **<4 yr** (OB)

**Technique** — **below the tibial tubercle** · **acute correction** · distal fragment into **valgus + external rotation + lateral translation** · staged for IV–VI · epiphysiolysis in V–VI · medial plateau elevation if depressed · **prophylactic anterior compartment fasciotomy**

**Overcorrection** — **10–15° valgus** (Orthobullets, TO) · **~10° valgus** (OB) · at least **5° valgus** (OB60) · ✓ medial physeal abnormality persists\
✗ Wrong options: correction to neutral · undercorrection

**Site** — **tibia, not femur**\
✗ Wrong options: distal femur osteotomy

**Renal osteodystrophy** — **postpone** until renal status stable (osteotomy short-lived otherwise)

**Late-onset with distal femoral varus** — **LDFA >92°** → distal femoral osteotomy or guided growth (OB)

### Guided growth & Hueter-Volkmann

**Principle** — **↑ compression → slows longitudinal growth** · ↑ tension → stimulates growth

**Lateral proximal tibial hemiepiphysiodesis** — tension band plate / staples (extraperiosteal plates) · **prevents worsening, does NOT fully correct** (OB) · less morbid than osteotomy · not **medial** hemiepiphysiodesis (worsens varus)

**Caution** — young child, severe stage III → relies on injured medial physis → **severe shortening** → prefer osteotomy (OB) · lateral tibial epiphysiodesis after age **9** (TO)

**Unilateral LLD** — epiphysiodesis of uninvolved leg (TO)

### Physeal bar

**Bar resection** — needs **≥4 yr growth remaining** · with osteotomy · interposition **fat or PMMA** · **hemiepiphysiodesis if bar >50%**

**Hemi-plateau elevation** — severe uncorrected or **relapsed** disease

## 6 Complications

**Compartment syndrome** — after high tibial/fibular osteotomy → prophylactic anterior compartment release

**Recurrence** — **medial physeal bar** → progressive varus after well-done osteotomy → lateral hemiepiphysiodesis or bar resection\
✗ Wrong options: overcorrection · plate malposition · age 5 · vitamin D

## X vs Y

| | **Infantile Blount** | **Adolescent Blount** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **2–5 yr** | **>10 yr** |
| Side | **50% bilateral** | Usually unilateral |
| Risk | Early walking, large stature, obesity | Obesity |
| Classification | **Langenskiöld** | None radiographic |
| Physeal disturbance | More severe | Less severe |
| Femur | **Compensatory distal femoral valgus** | **Distal femoral varus** + distal tibial valgus |
| Natural history | Stage II/IV may resolve | Never resolves |
| Treatment | Bracing + surgery | Surgery only |

| | **Physiologic bowing** | **Infantile Blount** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **<2 yr** | **>2 yr** |
| Shape | Symmetric, femur + tibia | Asymmetric, **proximal tibia**, sharp |
| MDA | **<10–11°** | **>16°** |
| X-ray | Symmetric flaring | **Metaphyseal beaking**, medial sloping |
| Gait | No thrust | **Lateral thrust** |

## How it's asked

**"NOT a component of Blount's?"** → **external rotation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: varus · procurvatum · internal rotation

**"1-yr, bowing, normal MDA?"** → **within normal limits** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · surgery · vitamin D

**"17-month-old, symmetric 10° varus, no thrust?"** → **clinical observation**\
✗ Wrong options: X-rays · vitamin D · guided growth

**"Physiologic bowing corrects by?"** → **36 months**

**"12-month-old just standing with bowed legs?"** → **normal development**\
✗ Wrong options: rickets · NAT · bracing

**"Most reliable sign separating Blount from physiologic?"** → **MDA >16°**\
✗ Wrong options: walking at 15 months · normal ICD

**"3-yr progressive bowing — abnormal exam?"** → **lateral thrust**\
✗ Wrong options: TFA 5° internal · forefoot adduction · recurvatum · distal femoral varus

**"14-yr, severe medial epiphyseal depression?"** → **neglected infantile Blount**\
✗ Wrong options: adolescent Blount · hemophilia

**"1.5-yr, acute bowing below knee, MDA 14°/12°?"** → **Blount's / tibia vara — mermaid splints** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: physiologic · osteotomy · vitamin D

**"30-month-old, Langenskiöld II?"** → **KAFO**\
✗ Wrong options: AFO · osteotomy

**"2½-yr, Langenskiöld I?"** → **KAFO**

**"Counsel on bracing efficacy?"** → **no strong consensus**\
✗ Wrong options: effective · ineffective

**"4-yr, 90th percentile, bilateral, thrust?"** → **predisposed to failing nonoperative care**\
✗ Wrong options: undercorrect · medial hemiepiphysiodesis · KAFO

**"4-yr obese, MDA 18°, medial articular depression?"** → **osteotomy correcting to 10° valgus** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: neutral axis · bar resection

**"32-month-old, worsening despite 1 yr bracing?"** → **bilateral proximal tibial osteotomies**\
✗ Wrong options: femoral · medial hemiepiphysiodesis

**"Valgus osteotomy with 10° overcorrection — which patient?"** → **4-yr obese, Langenskiöld IV**\
✗ Wrong options: renal osteodystrophy · 18-month MDA 11°

**"4-yr, 12 months KAFO, MDA 25°, worsening?"** → **proximal tibia/fibula osteotomy**

**"3 yr 10 months, worsening?"** → **proximal tibia valgus osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: KAFO · distal femur

**"Obese 3-yr, 30° unilateral varus, stage 3?"** → **proximal tibiofibular osteotomy, acute correction**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral hemiepiphysiodesis (shortening)

**"3½-yr progressive bowing, medial sloping?"** → **tibia and fibula osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: hemi-plateau elevation · epiphysiodesis

**"4-yr, Langenskiöld IV, progressive?"** → **tibial osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: bar resection · bracing

**"3½-yr, MDA >20°, advanced changes?"** → **bilateral tibia and fibular osteotomies**\
✗ Wrong options: KAFO · Denis-Browne bar

**"4-yr, Langenskiöld II?"** → **proximal tibia/fibula valgus osteotomy** (age ≥4)\
✗ Wrong options: KAFO · distal femur

**"How does a lateral staple work?"** → **↑ compression slows growth** (Hueter-Volkmann)

**"5-yr leg bowing — next step?"** → **bilateral lateral proximal tibial epiphysiodesis, extraperiosteal plates**\
✗ Wrong options: medial · night KAFO · casting

**"4-yr, MDA 25°, expected outcome of lateral hemiepiphysiodesis?"** → **prevents worsening**\
✗ Wrong options: complete correction

**"Varus recurs 2 yr after osteotomy?"** → **medial tibial physeal bar**\
✗ Wrong options: overcorrection · plate malposition

# Internal Tibial Torsion

*Pediatrics · 55 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Most common cause of in-toeing in toddlers** (**1–3 yr**) · often bilateral · if unilateral → **left** more often (TO) · intrauterine moulding

**Age-wise in-toeing** — infant: **metatarsus adductus** · toddler: **internal tibial torsion** · early childhood: **femoral anteversion** · OB: MA ± ITT most common at 18 months

## 3 Presentation

**History** — noticed on walking · legs "turn in" · tripping

### Rotational profile evaluation

**Staheli rotational profile (5)** — **foot progression angle** · **hip IR/ER** · **thigh–foot axis** · **transmalleolar axis** · **heel bisector**\
✗ Wrong options: **Q angle** (not part)

| Measure | Normal | ITT |
|---|---|---|
| Foot progression angle | −5° to +20° | Negative (internal) |
| **Thigh–foot angle** (prone) | Infant 5° internal (−30 to +20) · age 8: 10° external (−5 to +30) · −10° to +20° (OB) | **>10° internal** |
| Transmalleolar axis | Infant 0 to −10° → 15–23° external | **>15° internal** |
| Hip IR / ER | IR ≤ 70° | Normal |
| Heel bisector | 2nd web space | Normal |

| Thigh–foot axis | Transmalleolar axis |
|---|---|
| ![Thigh–foot axis](../../MCQ/attachments/q2852-a-thigh-foot-axis-2.jpg) | ![Transmalleolar axis](../../MCQ/attachments/q2852-b-transmalleolar-axis.jpg) |

**Red flags for work-up (X-rays)** — **pain** · **LLD** · **progression** · family history of rickets / dysplasia / MPS · rotational profile **>2 SD**\
✗ Wrong options: **metatarsus adductus** (benign)

| In-toeing cause | Key finding |
|---|---|
| Metatarsus adductus | Abnormal heel bisector, normal hindfoot |
| **Internal tibial torsion** | **TFA >10° internal** |
| Femoral anteversion | **IR >70°, ER <20°** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-rays** — not needed unless red flags · CT/MRI for surgical planning

## 5 Management

### Natural history & observation

**Observation + parental education** — resolves by **age 3–4** (Orthobullets, OB) · 6–8 yr (one OB explanation) · **bracing, Denis Browne bar, twister cables, casting, shoes don't help**

**18-month-old triplets with bowing + ITT** → **observation** (packaging)\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin D · serial alignment films · genetics

### Operative criteria

**Supramalleolar derotation osteotomy** — **>6–8 yr** (adolescent) + **functional problems** + **TFA >15°** internal · fewer complications than proximal · plate/K-wires (IM nail if mature)

## How it's asked

**"2-yr, negative foot progression angle (L > R)?"** → **internal tibial torsion** (TO)

**"In-toeing — prompts further work-up EXCEPT?"** → **metatarsus adductus**

**"Part of rotational profile EXCEPT?"** → **Q angle**

**"4-yr, TFA −10° left, normal hips?"** → **observation + education**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial/femoral osteotomy · brace · casting

**"Toddler ITT — expected outcome?"** → **resolves by 3–4 yr without treatment**

**"18-month-old, TFA 30° internal, normal hips?"** → **no treatment — resolves**

**"18-month-old triplets, bowlegs + in-toeing?"** → **observation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Kohler's Disease

*Pediatrics · 56 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Natural history & etiology

**Definition** — idiopathic **avascular necrosis (osteochondrosis) of the tarsal navicular** in young children

**Demographics** — age **4–7 yr** (Orthobullets) · **2–9 yr** (OB) · **boys 4:1** (80%) · **bilateral up to 25%**

**Blood supply** — intraosseous flow **centripetal** → **central one-third = watershed** · primary supply = perforating branches of **dorsalis pedis** · revascularised from the **perichondral ring** → excellent prognosis

**Theory** — navicular is the **LAST tarsal bone to ossify** → **compressed between the ossified talus and cuneiforms** → central ischemia (✓ theorised etiology)\
✗ Wrong options: first

**Mimic** — often misdiagnosed as **infection**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **dorsomedial midfoot pain** + **limp** · may be asymptomatic · swelling, warmth, redness possible · point tenderness over navicular

**Exam** — antalgic gait, weight on **lateral border** of foot · no fever, no constitutional symptoms

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **sclerosis, fragmentation, flattening** of navicular · diagnosis on **plain films alone**\
✗ Wrong options: cysts · widening

**Bone scan** — **↓ uptake** · unnecessary

**MRI** — not needed initially · if pain **persists >6 months** or diagnosis unclear → exclude **(cartilaginous) tarsal coalition**

**Labs / biopsy** — unnecessary when no illness or constitutional symptoms\
✗ Wrong options: ESR/CRP/cultures · biopsy

**Classification** — none widely used

![Kohler's — sclerotic, flattened navicular](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4071-xray-combined-moved.jpg)

![Navicular sclerosis and fragmentation](../../MCQ/attachments/q211605-a-kohler-xr.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Kohler's disease
├─ Mild / no severe pain → observation · NSAIDs · arch support orthotic
└─ Pain with activity / failed orthotic → short-leg WALKING cast 4–6 wk
       (surgery never indicated)
```

### Treatment - short leg cast

**First line** — **NSAIDs** short course · activity limitation · **shoe modification / arch support**

**Cast** — **short-leg walking (weight-bearing) cast for 4–6 wk** → **shortens duration of symptoms** · **2 wk splint is insufficient** (OB)

**Weight bearing** — **safe**, does not affect outcome · OB6130 accepts "restriction of weight bearing or short leg cast" · NWB 6 wk also relieves symptoms (OB211605)\
✗ Wrong options: NWB cast (unnecessary, hard to enforce)

**Long vs short cast** — excellent function **regardless of casting technique**\
✗ Wrong options: long leg cast quicker

**Surgery** — **not indicated**\
✗ Wrong options: core decompression · triple / subtalar fusion · curettage + graft · medial column lengthening · ORIF · I&D

**PT** — does not aid recovery

### Prognosis

**Course** — **self-limiting** · intermittent activity symptoms for **1–3 yr** (✓ resolution in 1–3 yr; ✗ Wrong options: 6 months)

**X-ray** — improves **6–48 months** from onset · navicular **reconstitutes**\
✗ Wrong options: complete absorption

**Long term** — **no long-term disability** · no midfoot collapse · **no talonavicular arthritis** · residual deformity almost always asymptomatic

## X vs Y

| | **Kohler's disease** | **Infection (mimic)** |
|---|---|---|
| History | No illness, **no constitutional symptoms** | Recent illness, constitutional symptoms |
| X-ray | **Sclerosis, flattening, fragmentation** — diagnostic | — |
| Workup | **X-ray alone** (✗ Wrong options: ESR/CRP/WBC · biopsy) | Bloods, cultures |
| Treatment | **Walking cast / NSAIDs** (✗ Wrong options: antibiotics) | Antibiotics |

## How it's asked

**"5-yr, medial foot pain, failed orthotics?"** → **short-leg walking cast**\
✗ Wrong options: core decompression · fusion · I&D

**"6-yr, limp, navicular sclerosis — treatment?"** → **short leg walking cast**\
✗ Wrong options: long leg NWB cast · biopsy

**"Splinted 2 wk, still painful — next step?"** → **weight-bearing cast**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · bone scan · NWB cast

**"5-yr, navicular tenderness + swelling?"** → **symptomatic: restrict weight bearing or short leg cast**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics · medial column lengthening

**"5-yr, limp, normally active, mild pain?"** → **observation or orthotic arch support**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · aspiration

**"Redness, warmth over midfoot + flattened navicular?"** → **weight-bearing short leg cast**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics · excision

**"Kicked at soccer, 2 months dorsal pain?"** → **walking cast**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · bloods · MRI

**"Natural history untreated?"** → **resolution in 1–3 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: 6 months · collapse · TN arthritis

**"Most accurate statement about etiology?"** → **compression between ossified talus and cuneiforms**\
✗ Wrong options: first to ossify · cysts + widening

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Larsen's Syndrome

*Pediatrics · 57 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — ~**1 in 100,000** live births

### Genes & lab markers

| Inheritance | Gene |
|---|---|
| **Autosomal dominant** | **Filamin B** |
| **Autosomal recessive** | **Carbohydrate sulfotransferase 3** deficiency |

**Diagnosis** — family history + clinical/radiographic features · **no commercial genetic test** (many mutations)

### Orthopaedic manifestations

**Dislocations** — **hips** · **knees (bilateral)** · shoulders · **elbows (radial heads, bilateral)**

**Other** — **clubfeet** (equinovarus / equinovalgus) · scoliosis · **cervical kyphosis** (hypoplastic cervical vertebrae) · extra carpal ossification · hand deformities

**Non-ortho** — laryngeal / tracheal problems · heart valve or aortic lesions · hyperelasticity → hypotonia

## 3 Presentation

**Face** — **flattened (depressed) nasal bridge** · **prominent forehead** · hypertelorism / telecanthus

**Hands** — long cylindrical **non-tapering fingers** · wide distal phalanx of thumb

**Intelligence** — **normal**

**Hypotonia** — uncommon · may signal **cervical cord compression** · myelopathy → extremity weakness

![Larsen facies](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4090-larsen-syndrome.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Cervical kyphosis screening

**C-spine AP + lateral** — **in the first year of life** · and **before any intubation** or manipulation → avoid iatrogenic cord injury

**MRI** — cervical kyphosis or myelopathy

**Findings** — vertebral **hypoplasia** · **cervical kyphosis with subluxation** · hip dislocation

**Hips** — AP pelvis + lateral · **ultrasound if <3 months**

![Cervical kyphosis in Larsen syndrome](../../MCQ/attachments/q210092-illa-cervical-spine.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Priority** — **stabilise / image the cervical spine BEFORE any hip or knee procedure**\
✗ Wrong options: hip open reduction first · knee procedures first

### Operative criteria

| Problem | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Cervical kyphosis, **no deficit** | **Posterior cervical fusion** in **first 18 months** |
| Cervical kyphosis **with deficit** | **Anterior/posterior decompression + fusion** |
| Hip dislocation | Closed reduction rarely works → **open reduction** (failed closed, contractures, unilateral) · bilateral = controversial, do early and only once |
| Knee dislocation | Closed reduction + casting rarely works → **open reduction + femoral shortening + collateral ligament excision** |

## How it's asked

**"Bilateral radial head + knee dislocations, flat facies — must check?"** → **cervical kyphosis**\
✗ Wrong options: vertical talus · trigger thumb · tibial hemimelia

**"Larsen child, hip pain, knee dislocations — next step?"** → **cervical spine radiographs**\
✗ Wrong options: hip open reduction · VDRO · tibial osteotomy · ACL reconstruction

**"Cervical kyphosis without deficit — timing of fusion?"** → **posterior fusion in first 18 months**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lateral Condyle Fracture - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 58 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2nd most common pediatric elbow fracture** (after supracondylar) · 17% of distal humerus fractures · **11%** of elbow injuries · peak age **6 yr**

**Signature** — **highest nonunion rate** about the pediatric elbow · ↑ malunion, AVN

**Physeal pattern** — usually **Salter-Harris IV** (Orthobullets, OB) · TO: Milch II "is really SH II", Milch I = SH IV — read the options

### Pathoanatomy

**Mechanism** — **pull-off** (avulsion by common extensors) · **push-off** (FOOSH → radial head impacts lateral condyle)

**Fracture line** — starts **posterior** distal humeral metaphysis → distal + anterior across physis + epiphysis into joint · may extend into **trochlear groove** → unstable, prone to dislocation

**Posteromedial dislocation** — rare association · FOOSH, slight flexion + **adduction** · **LCL stays intact**, attached to condylar fragment + radial neck\
✗ Wrong options: LCL repair

### Blood supply - avoid posterior dissection

**Supply** — distal humerus mainly from **posterior** anastomotic vessels · brachial artery anterior

**ORIF rule** — **avoid posterior dissection** → **AVN of lateral condyle** (and trochlea)

### Ossification ages

| Centre | Appears (yr) | Fuses (yr) |
|---|---|---|
| **Capitellum** | **1** | 12–14 |
| **Radial head** | **3** | 14–16 |
| **Internal (medial) epicondyle** | **5** | 16–18 |
| **Trochlea** | **7** | 12–14 |
| **Olecranon** | **9** | 15–17 |
| **External (lateral) epicondyle** | **11** | 12–14 |

**OB ages** — capitellum first 6 months · radial head 3–6 · medial epicondyle 5–7 · trochlea 8 · olecranon 10 · **lateral epicondyle last, 12–14 yr in males** (OB answer) · **females ~1 yr earlier**

**Trap** — appearance and fusion are independent events

## 3 Presentation

**History** — FOOSH · lateral elbow pain + swelling · may be subtle

**Exam** — **no obvious deformity** · lateral tenderness · pain with resisted wrist flexion/extension · crepitus\
✗ Wrong options: supracondylar look

**Lateral ecchymosis** — tear of **brachioradialis aponeurosis** → **unstable** fracture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP, lateral + INTERNAL oblique** · internal oblique shows **maximum displacement** (fracture is **posterolateral**)\
✗ Wrong options: external oblique (TO EXCEPT trap)

**Findings** — capitellum displaced laterally vs radial head · **posterior Thurston-Holland** fragment on lateral

**Arthrogram** — minimally displaced fractures · assess cartilage when epiphysis unossified · dynamic · **preferred over MRI**

**MRI** — cartilaginous trochlear integrity · planning of delayed/nonunion · expensive, needs sedation

**CT** — rarely, only if fracture type unclear · stress views **not** recommended

![Internal oblique view shows true displacement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4009-internal-oblique-view-demonstrating-a-fracture-of-the-latera.jpg)

### Milch classification

| Type | Fracture line | Stability |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Lateral** to trochlear groove | Stable · less common |
| **II** | Into **trochlear groove** | **Unstable** · **more common** (95%, TO) |

![Milch classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4009-milch-classification.jpg)

### Weiss classification & treatment

| Type | Displacement | Cartilage | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **<2 mm** | Intact hinge | **Long arm cast** |
| **2** | **2–4 mm** (Orthobullets) · >2 mm (TO) | **Intact** on arthrogram | **CRPP** |
| **3** | **>4 mm** (Orthobullets) · >2 mm (TO) | **Disrupted** | **Open reduction + fixation** |

**Prognosis (TO)** — type 3 complications **>3×** type 2 · type 3 = **highest nonunion risk** (✓ even after K-wires)

### Pediatric elbow injury frequency

| Injury | % | Peak age | Needs OR |
|---|---|---|---|
| Supracondylar | 41% | 7 | Majority |
| Radial head subluxation | 28% | 3 | Rare |
| **Lateral condyle** | **11%** | **6** | **Majority** |
| Medial epicondyle | 8% | 11 | Minority |
| Radial head/neck | 5% | 10 | Minority |
| Elbow dislocation | 5% | 13 | Rare |
| Medial condyle | 1% | 10 | Rare |

## 5 Management

```
Lateral condyle fracture
├─ <2 mm all views, hinge intact → long arm cast 4–6 wk, weekly X-ray ×3 wk
├─ 2–4 mm, cartilage intact → CRPP
└─ >4 mm / incongruity → open reduction + fixation
       (anterolateral approach, no posterior dissection)
```

### Operative criteria

**Cast** — only if **<2 mm in ALL views** + **intact medial cartilaginous hinge** · elbow ~90° · **weekly X-rays (incl. internal oblique) for 3 wk** · occasionally >6 wk

**Surgery** — **>2 mm on any view** = unstable → fixation

### Treatment (ORIF displaced)

**Approach** — **anterolateral** (blood supply posterior) · dissection often done by injury · **see joint reduction directly** · metaphysis may not fit perfectly (plastic deformation)

**Fixation** — **2 smooth pins** (3 if comminuted), parallel or **divergent** (most stable) · **smooth pins** because fixation **crosses the physis** · single **cannulated screw** for large fragment / nonunion → compression + early motion · no plate · 3–6 wk above-elbow cast

**Closed reduction** — rarely succeeds for type 3 · CRPP can't inspect articular surface\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP in widely displaced

**With posteromedial dislocation** — **open reduction + percutaneous or internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: LCL repair · cast

## 6 Complications

### Nonunion, cubitus valgus & tardy ulnar palsy

**Stiffness** — **most common** · may be early sign of delayed/nonunion · 90% motion by 24 wk, full by 48 wk

**Delayed union** — not healed after **6 wk** · risk: seen **>2 wk** after injury

**Nonunion causes** — **pull of wrist extensors** (fragment rotates up to 180°, TO) · **intra-articular synovial fluid** · poor metaphyseal circulation / small metaphyseal fragment · instability in cast · risk ↑ **nonoperative care**

| Nonunion scenario | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Asymptomatic, isolated** | **Observation** |
| **Painful** / unstable / valgus + ulnar palsy | **ORIF of nonunion** (minimal posterior stripping) ± bone graft (✗ Wrong options: fragment excision → valgus) |
| **Painless** nonunion (OB3302) | **In situ screw + bone graft** |
| Untreated 4 months, painful | **ORIF** (✗ Wrong options: late percutaneous pins · cast · MRI) |
| **Well-established** (TO) | **Osteotomy** (varus) · avoid arthrotomy / articular realignment → stiffness, AVN |

**Nonunion goal** — union of **metaphyseal fragment**, not joint surface

**Cubitus valgus** — from **nonunion** (most common) or lateral physeal arrest · ~**10%** · less common than cubitus varus

**Tardy ulnar nerve palsy** — slow **stretch** palsy from cubitus valgus · **lateral condyle nonunion = most common cause** (TO) · counsel **ring + small finger numbness** · also after cubitus varus (nerve dislocates anterior to medial epicondyle, FCU band, TO) · treat: **supracondylar osteotomy after maturity + ulnar nerve transposition**

**AVN** — 1–3 yr after fracture · from **posterior dissection**, most often in treatment of nonunion/delayed union

**Fishtail deformity** — gap between medial + lateral ossification centres fails · usually no dysfunction · supracondylar osteotomy if needed

![Cubitus valgus after nonunion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4009-illustration-depicting-cubitus-valgus.jpg)

### Lateral spur/overgrowth

**Incidence** — up to **50%** regardless of treatment → **counsel families in advance**

**Facts** — size **correlates with initial displacement** · **independent of age** · **does NOT affect final ROM** · gives **pseudo-varus** look, not true varus · no trimming asymptomatic spur · lateral periosteal realignment prevents it\
✗ Wrong options: caused by surgery

**Growth arrest** — rare · young → bar resection or osteotomy · older → complete epiphysiodesis + osteotomy

**Prognosis** — worse than supracondylar (articular, missed diagnosis, nonunion) · with dislocation: slower ROM, same final ROM

## X vs Y

| | **Lateral condyle** | **Medial condyle (TO)** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | 11% | **1%** |
| Main complication | **Nonunion** → cubitus **valgus** | **Missed diagnosis** (mistaken for medial epicondyle) |
| Late deformity | Valgus + tardy ulnar palsy | Nonunion → cubitus **varus**, trochlear AVN |
| Pitfall | — | Trochlea unossified until ~8 yr · complication rate up to 33% |
| Displaced | ORIF | ORIF |

## How it's asked

**"View showing maximum displacement?"** → **internal oblique**\
✗ Wrong options: external oblique

**"6-yr, displaced intra-articular fragment?"** → **ORIF with K-wires**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · plate

**"6-yr, displaced — best treatment?"** → **ORIF with smooth pins or cannulated screw**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 3–8 wk · plate

**"2 mm on internal oblique, casted — injury with highest nonunion rate?"** → **lateral condyle**\
✗ Wrong options: medial condyle · transphyseal separation

**"3-yr, lateral condyle + posteromedial dislocation?"** → **open reduction + fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: LCL repair · CRPP

**"ORIF — avoid dissection where?"** → **posterior**

**"Tardy ulnar palsy seen after?"** → **nonunion of lateral condyle** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: malunion · gunstock

**"Treatment of well-established nonunion?"** → **varus osteotomy** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF + graft · observation

**"Weiss type most likely to go to nonunion?"** → **type 3** (TO)

**"Nonunion associated with?"** → **ulnar nerve palsy**\
✗ Wrong options: radial nerve · cubitus varus

**"36-yr claw hand, old untreated condyle fracture — elbow shows?"** → **cubitus valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: AVN · fishtail · normal carrying angle

**"7-yr, painful lateral condyle nonunion, normal alignment?"** → **internal fixation of nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: excision · ulnar decompression

**"Injured 4 months ago, untreated, daily pain?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous pins · MRI

**"Untreated displaced fracture — expected deformity?"** → **cubitus valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: HO · PIN palsy · synostosis

**"Painless nonunion with valgus, observed — counsel about?"** → **ring + small finger numbness**\
✗ Wrong options: medial spurring

**"True about lateral spur?"** → **final ROM not affected by spur size**\
✗ Wrong options: caused by surgery · age-dependent · trim it

**"Lateral epicondyle ossifies in males at?"** → **12–14 yr** (OB)

**"All EXCEPT (lateral condyle)?"** → **external oblique helps assess displacement** (TO)

**"Most common complication of medial condyle fracture?"** → **missed diagnosis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · trochlear AVN

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Leg Length Discrepancy (LLD)

*Pediatrics · 59 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **2 cm LLD in up to 2/3** of the population · most <2 cm, asymptomatic, no long-term consequence

### Hemihypertrophy & congenital causes

| Group | Causes |
|---|---|
| **Congenital** | Hemihypertrophy · dysplasias · **PFFD** · DDH · **unilateral clubfoot** |
| **Paralytic** | Spasticity (CP) · polio |
| **Physeal disruption** | **Infection** · **trauma** · tumor |

**Unilateral clubfoot** — **entire involved limb hypoplastic** (✓) → LLD, mostly **tibia** · (✗ Wrong options: hemihypertrophy of other limb) · **no bone-age difference** · **posterior tibial artery dominant** (anterior tibial hypoplastic) · casting first line

**Beckwith-Wiedemann** — hemihypertrophy + **macroglossia** → up to **10% tumor (Wilms)** → **abdominal + pelvic US and AFP 3–4×/yr until age 8**\
✗ Wrong options: thyroid · echo · spine MRI

**Associations** — **back pain** · **OA of long-leg hip** (↓ head coverage, 84%) · functional scoliosis · inefficient gait · ankle equinus · observation of small LLD not linked to scoliosis or back pain (Orthobullets)

### Classification

| Type | Example | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| **Static** | Malunion of femur / tibia (extra-physeal trauma) | Doesn't change |
| **Progressive** | **Physeal arrest** · congenital | Absolute LLD ↑ · **congenital: proportion stays the same** |

## 3 Presentation

### Measurement & evaluation

**Block test** — blocks under short leg until **pelvis level** · **best initial screening** · accurate, simple, cheap

**Tape** — **ASIS → medial malleolus** (inaccurate with contracture)

**Contractures** — hip, knee, ankle alter apparent length · **hip adduction contracture → apparent shortening of adducted side**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Method | Technique | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Teleoradiograph** | Single exposure, both legs, long film | Good for **angular deformity** · **magnification error** (beam parallax) |
| **Orthoradiograph** | 3 exposures (hip, knee, ankle) | Avoids magnification |
| **Scanogram** | 3 exposures, small cassette | Avoids magnification · less error if patient moves |
| **CT scanogram** | AP scout | **Most accurate with hip / knee / ankle contractures** |
| **Lateral CT scanogram** | Lateral scout | **Best with knee flexion contracture** (others are coronal) |

**Primary initial imaging (Sabharwal, OB)** — full-length standing AP, pelvis level, **magnification marker**

**Bone age** — hand X-ray

![CT scanogram](../../MCQ/attachments/q1045-illa-ct-scanogram-enlarged.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Predicting LLD from growth arrest (physeal growth rates)

**Arithmetic (Menelaus) method** — growth stops **boys 16 yr, girls 14 yr** · LLD added = **physeal rate × years left** · easiest, least accurate, best for **final years** of growth and exams

| Physis | Orthobullets / most OB | Other OB values |
|---|---|---|
| **Proximal femur** | **3 mm/yr** (1/8 in) | 4 mm/yr |
| **Distal femur** | **9 mm/yr** (3/8 in) | **10 mm/yr** · 9–11 |
| **Proximal tibia** | **6 mm/yr** (1/4 in) | — |
| **Distal tibia** | **5 mm/yr** (3/16 in) | 4 mm/yr |
| Whole leg | **23 mm/yr**, **knee 15 mm/yr** | — |

**Other physes (OB)** — **distal femur fastest** · proximal humerus 7 · distal radius 5–6 · distal tibia 4–5 mm/yr

**Dimeglio** — knee grows **2 cm/yr** (femur 1.1 + tibia 0.9) · growth stops at **bone age 15.5 (boys) / 13.5 (girls)**

| Method | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Green-Anderson tables** | Limb length for age |
| **Moseley straight-line graph** | Graph form · accounts for **skeletal vs chronologic age** · averages serial measurements |
| **Multiplier (Paley)** | Current LLD × age/sex factor · **most accurate for congenital LLD** · uses **chronological age** |
| **Half of final leg length** | **Girls at 3 yr · boys at 4 yr** |

**Best predictor in adolescence** — **skeletal age**\
✗ Wrong options: chronological age · Tanner · father's height · Paley

### Worked examples (OB)

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| 14-yr boy, distal femoral arrest | 2 yr × 10 = **2 cm** |
| 9-yr boy, distal femoral arrest | 7 yr × 9 = **~6 cm** |
| 12-yr boy, closed distal femur, LLD 2 cm now | 2 + 4 × 10 = **6 cm** |
| 8-yr girl, closed distal femur, LLD 1 cm | 6 yr × 9 + 1 ≈ **6–7 cm, uninjured (left) longer**, no angulation (central arrest) |
| 14-yr boy, DF + PT epiphysiodesis | (10 + 6) × 2 = **3.2 cm** |
| 14-yr boy, PT arrest, LLD 3 mm | 3 + 6 × 2 = **15 mm → observe** |

### Epiphysiodesis indications & timing

| Projected LLD at maturity | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<2 cm** | **Observation ± shoe lift** |
| **2–5 cm** (OB: 2–6) | **Epiphysiodesis** of long side (femur, tibia or both) · shortening if already mature |
| **>5 cm** (OB: >5–6) | **Lengthening** of short side ± contralateral epiphysiodesis/ostectomy |
| **>20 cm** / non-reconstructable | **Amputation + prosthesis** |
| Historical (OB) | <1.5 cm none · 1.5–5 cm epiphysiodesis · >5 cm lengthening |

**Decide on LLD at maturity**, not at presentation

**Physeal bar excision** — bar **<50%** of physis + **≥2 yr growth** left

**DF + PT epiphysiodesis** — removes **1.5 cm/yr** · 13-yr boy with 4.6 cm = most equal (3 yr left)

**Timing (13-yr boy, puberty 1 month ago, 4 cm)** — **femur AND tibia** at **13 yr 4–6 months** (Menelaus 13 y 4 m · Dimeglio 13 y 6 m)

**Closed physis on short side + LLD already 2.5 cm** — close **both** long-side DF + PT\
✗ Wrong options: DF only (no differential left) · PT only

**Persistent LLD after epiphysiodesis** — most common cause = **error in timing**\
✗ Wrong options: overgrowth · joint penetration

**Small asymptomatic LLD + acute injury** — treat the injury (NSAIDs, rest)\
✗ Wrong options: epiphysiodesis

### Lengthening & distraction osteogenesis

**Corticotomy** — **metaphyseal** · periosteal supply matters more than endosteal (lengthening over nails works)

| Step | Value |
|---|---|
| **Latency** | **5–7 days** (no lengthening in week 1) |
| **Rate** | **1 mm/day** (0.25 mm ×4) |
| Too fast (>1 mm/day) | Painful neuropathy, soft-tissue problems, poor regenerate |
| **Poor regenerate** | **Slow the rate** → then **accordionization** |
| **Consolidation** | Fixator stays **as many days as lengthened** |

**Which bone** — lengthen the **femur** when the discrepancy is femoral (✗ Wrong options: tibia (asymmetric joint lines)) · 15-yr with 5.5 cm → **femoral lengthening** (✗ Wrong options: shoe lift · late epiphysiodesis · 6.5 cm femoral shortening (weak muscles))

**Pre-op stability** — need stable joints above + below · **cruciate deficiency → knee subluxation** · **hip CE angle <20° → hip subluxation**\
✗ Wrong options: Achilles contracture · 8 cm LLD

### Mechanical axis deviation

| Technique | MAD |
|---|---|
| Lengthen along **mechanical axis** (ex-fix) | **None** |
| Lengthen along **anatomic femoral axis** (nail) | **Lateral** MAD |
| Shorten along anatomic axis | **Medial** MAD |

**Angle** — ~**7°** between limb mechanical axis and femoral anatomic axis

![Lengthening along anatomic vs mechanical axis](../../MCQ/attachments/q4742-illa-illus-a-lengthening.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Epiphysiodesis** — incomplete arrest / **angular deformity** (open or percutaneous) · **persistent LLD from timing error**

**Lengthening** — pin-site infection · fracture · delayed union · premature cessation · **joint subluxation/dislocation**

| Complication | Clue | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Incomplete corticotomy** | Turning gets harder in **first week** → sudden **pop** + pain | — |
| **Premature consolidation** | Harder to turn at **~6 wk** · **bowed wires**, mature regenerate · femur + fibula most | Keep distracting until bridge ruptures · osteoclasis · repeat corticotomy |
| Causes of premature consolidation | Long latency, slow rate, exuberant bone, compliance, device failure | — |

## X vs Y

| | **Incomplete corticotomy** | **Premature consolidation** |
|---|---|---|
| When | **First days** of distraction | **Weeks** in (~6 wk) |
| Event | Acute pop as osteotomy completes | Device seizes, wires bow |

## How it's asked

**"14-yr boy, complete distal femoral arrest — LLD?"** → **2 cm**

**"9-yr boy, distal femoral arrest?"** → **6 cm**

**"Distal femur stops growing in boys at?"** → **16 yr**

**"12-yr, 2 cm LLD, closed distal femur — projected?"** → **6 cm**

**"8-yr girl, central distal femoral arrest — consequence?"** → **7 cm LLD, left femur longer**\
✗ Wrong options: varus · valgus · recurvatum

**"Same girl — LLD at maturity?"** → **6 cm**

**"Fastest-growing physis?"** → **distal femur**\
✗ Wrong options: proximal humerus

**"14-yr boy, DF + PT epiphysiodesis — correction?"** → **3.2 cm**

**"14-yr boy, PT arrest, LLD 3 mm, bone age 14?"** → **observation**

**"17-yr girl, 10 mm block levels pelvis?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: CT scanogram · epiphysiodesis

**"Epiphysiodesis at 14 with 4.2 cm, still 3 cm at 18 — why?"** → **error in timing of surgery**

**"13-yr boy, puberty 1 month ago, projected 4 cm?"** → **epiphysiodesis of left femur and tibia in 6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: immediate lengthening

**"11-yr boy, femoral LLD 4 cm at maturity?"** → **timed epiphysiodesis of longer leg**

**"13-yr, left DF closed, LLD 2.5 cm?"** → **right DF + proximal tibia/fibula epiphysiodesis**

**"DF + PT percutaneous epiphysiodesis — most equal result?"** → **13-yr boy with 4.6 cm**

**"Best predictor of remaining growth in adolescents?"** → **skeletal age**\
✗ Wrong options: Paley · Tanner

**"Distraction rate?"** → **1 mm/day**

**"15-yr, 5.5 cm femoral LLD, closed physis?"** → **right femoral lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial lengthening · femoral shortening

**"Lengthening along mechanical axis vs anatomic axis nail?"** → **no MAD vs lateral MAD**

**"Turning harder, pop on day 9 of lengthening?"** → **incomplete corticotomy with spontaneous completion**

**"Harder to turn at 6 wk, bowed wires?"** → **premature consolidation**
