**"Lengthening in first week after osteotomy?"** → **none** (latency)

**"Congenital short femur — risk factor for complication?"** → **cruciate deficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: Achilles contracture · LCEA 25°

**"JRA knee flexion contracture — most accurate LLD method?"** → **CT scanogram**\
✗ Wrong options: teleoradiograph

**"Best LLD method with knee flexion contracture?"** → **lateral CT scanogram**\
✗ Wrong options: blocks · tape

**"Gymnast, 1.2 cm LLD, acute knee strain?"** → **NSAIDs, rest 1 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: epiphysiodesis · cast

**"Unilateral clubfoot — true?"** → **whole limb hypoplastic → LLD**\
✗ Wrong options: hemihypertrophy · bone-age difference · anterior tibial dominant

**"Hemihypertrophy + large tongue?"** → **abdominal/pelvic US + AFP** (Beckwith-Wiedemann)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Legg-Calve-Perthes Disease

*Pediatrics · 60 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology – age

**Definition** — **idiopathic AVN of the proximal femoral epiphysis** in children · self-limiting, heals + remodels\
✗ Wrong options: adult osteonecrosis

**Age** — **4–8 yr** (Orthobullets) · TO answer **5–10 yr** — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: 4–8 yr in the TO MCQ; text says 4–10

**Other hip ages (TO)** — SCFE boys **12–15**, girls **11–13** · transient synovitis **4–10** (mean 6)

**Demographics** — **1 in 10,000** · **M:F 5:1** · urban · low socioeconomic class · high latitude · Caucasian > East Asian, African American

**Bilateral** — **12%** (Orthobullets) · 11% (OB) · **asymmetric + asynchronous** · symmetric, same stage → think **MED**

**Risk factors** — family history · **low birth weight** · abnormal birth presentation · **second-hand smoke** · Asian, Inuit, Central European

### Pathophysiology

**Sequence** — infarction → revascularisation → **resorption → collapse** → creeping substitution → remodeling

**Theories** — **coagulopathy** (protein C/S deficiency; thrombophilia 50%, **some coagulopathy up to 75%**) · repeated subclinical trauma (multiple-infarction theory) · smoking aggravates

**Associated** — **ADHD 33%** · **delayed bone age 89%** · hypercoagulability (TO)

**Histology** — disorganised cartilage, hypercellularity, fibrillation in epiphysis + physis

### Femoral head blood supply in childhood

| Age (TO) | Supply |
|---|---|
| **Birth → 4–7 months** | **MFCA + LFCA + artery of ligamentum teres** (retinacular vessels cross freely) |
| **4–7 months → 14–18 yr** | **MFCA ALONE** (physis blocks LFCA) · **superior retinacular (lateral epiphyseal) artery = main** |
| **Adult** | Mainly MFCA · small LFCA (anterior retinacular) + ligamentum teres |

**18 months** → **MFCA alone**\
✗ Wrong options: MFCA + LFCA · + ligamentum teres

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — insidious **painless limp** · intermittent **hip, groin, thigh or knee** pain

**Exam** — loss of **internal rotation + abduction** · **Trendelenburg** (collapse → GT rises → slack gluteus medius) · **Catterall sign** = obligatory ER on hip flexion (TO) · LLD late, worsened by adduction contracture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **AP pelvis + frog lateral**

**Earliest X-ray sign** — **medial joint space widening** (teardrop → ossific nucleus)

**Other** — small, sclerotic ossific nucleus · **crescent sign** = subchondral fracture · X-ray may stay normal **3–6 months**

**Bone scan** — **cold** (↓ uptake) before X-ray changes · extent of involvement

**MRI** — early / occult disease · **more sensitive** than X-ray

**Perfusion studies** — predict max lateral pillar involvement · **dynamic arthrogram** → coverage, containment, hinge abduction

### Waldenström stages

| Stage | X-ray | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Initial** | Small sclerotic epiphysis, **medial joint space widening** | Occult 3–6 months |
| **Fragmentation** | **Crescent sign**, head fragments/dissolves | **Most symptomatic** · **lateral pillar graded here** · 6 months–2 yr |
| **Reossification** | New bone replaces necrotic bone | Up to 18 months |
| **Healing / remodeling** | Head remodels to maturity | Trabeculae return |

### Lateral pillar classification timing

**Lateral pillar (Herring)** — **best agreement, most predictive** · graded in **early fragmentation**, **~6 months after symptom onset**\
✗ Wrong options: at first visit

| Group | Lateral pillar | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Full height**, no density change | Consistently good |
| **B** | **>50%** height | Poor if **bone age >6** (Orthobullets) · >6 yr age (TO) |
| **B/C** | Narrow (2–3 mm) / poorly ossified, ~50% | Added for consistency |
| **C** | **<50%** height | **Poor in all**, regardless of treatment |

![Lateral pillar (Herring) classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4119-herring-classification-illustration-moved.jpg)

### Catterall classification & head-at-risk signs

| Group | Head involvement |
|---|---|
| I | Anterior epiphysis only |
| II | Anterior + **central sequestrum** |
| III | Only a small part spared |
| IV | **Total head** |

**Head-at-risk signs (poor)** — **Gage sign** (lateral V-shaped lucency) · **calcification lateral to epiphysis** · **metaphyseal cyst** · **lateral subluxation** · **horizontal physis**

![Gage sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4119-gage-sign.jpg)

**Salter-Thompson** — crescent sign **<½** head (A) vs **>½** (B)

**Stulberg** — **gold standard for residual head shape + congruence** at maturity · poor inter/intra-observer reliability

### Differential - bilateral disease workup

**Differential** — infection (septic hip, osteomyelitis, pyomyositis) · transient synovitis · **MED** · **SED** · sickle cell · Gaucher · hypothyroidism · **Meyers dysplasia**

**Symmetric bilateral changes** → **X-rays of knees (MED) and spine (SED)**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · brace · Salter

## 5 Management

**Goals** — symptom relief (NSAIDs, traction, crutches) · **restore ROM** (PT, muscle lengthening, Petrie casts) · **containment** (head seated in acetabulum)

```
Perthes disease
├─ <8 yr (bone age <6) or pillar A → observation, activity limits, ROM PT
├─ >8 yr (bone age >6), pillar B or B/C → femoral varus and/or pelvic osteotomy
├─ Pillar C → poor regardless of treatment
└─ Hinge abduction → valgus (abduction-extension) ± shelf osteotomy
```

### Treatment (osteotomy, containment, observation)

| Scenario | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<8 yr** (bone age <6) · **pillar A** | **Observation**, activity restriction / protected weight bearing, **ROM PT** (60% need no surgery) |
| **>8 yr** (bone age >6) · **pillar B, B/C** | **Proximal femoral varus osteotomy** and/or **pelvic osteotomy** (Salter, triple) |
| Lateral subluxation | **Shelf** → prevents lateral epiphyseal overgrowth |
| **Hinge abduction** | **Valgus (abduction-extension) osteotomy** ± shelf — moves hinge away from acetabular edge, fixes adduction shortening, improves abductor length |
| Head no longer containable | Shelf or **Chiari** |
| Healed LCPD with FAI | Hip arthroscopy (emerging) |
| Arthrodiastasis (ex-fix distraction) | Controversial |

**Bracing / casting for containment** — **no benefit** in large prospective study

**Surgery evidence** — pillar A + B <8 yr do well regardless · surgery improves **B and B/C >8 yr** · earlier surgery before deformity may be best

**Varus derotation osteotomy** — early extrusion → re-seats head

**Never** — core decompression · hip aspiration (no infection)

**TO false statement** — "**early containment surgery before age 6 prevents arthritis**" is false · ✓ true: loss of abduction + IR · ✓ age at presentation = most important prognostic factor · ✓ 7-yr with maintained pillar → activity modification

**Valgus femoral osteotomy is for** — **Perthes with painful hinge abduction** \
✗ Wrong options: CP MI 40% (VDRO) · DDH AI 35° at 4 yr (pelvic osteotomy; AI should be <25° by 6 months) · coxa vara HE 35° (observe) · post-SCFE FAI (osteochondroplasty)

## 6 Complications

**Head deformity** — **coxa magna** (wide) · **coxa plana** (flat) · Stulberg grades it

**Lateral subluxation (extrusion)** — poor prognosis → **hinge abduction**

**Premature physeal arrest** — **trochanteric overgrowth** · **coxa breva** · mild LLD

**Other** — acetabular dysplasia · labral tear / FAI · OCD (loose bodies)

### Prognostic factors

**Best good prognostic factor** — **bone age <6 yr at presentation** · <6 yr with pillar A/B → **80% good outcome** (spherical, congruent)

**Clinical predictors** — **age at presentation + hip ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: LLD · pain duration

**Poor** — **female** · adduction contracture / ↓ abduction · heavy · long course · stiffness · head-at-risk signs · **pillar C** · bilateral · extensive subchondral fracture · age >9 worst (TO)

**Arthritis predictor** — **asphericity** of head at maturity → OA after 40\
✗ Wrong options: lateral calcification

**Natural history** — most fine until **5th–6th decade** · **~½ get premature OA** (aspherical head) · Stulberg I + most II do well lifelong · 2–5 yr course

![Stulberg classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q2202-illc-stulberg-classification.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Perthes (bilateral)** | **MED / Meyers dysplasia** |
|---|---|---|
| Hips | **Asymmetric, asynchronous**, different stages | **Symmetric**, same stage |
| Next test | — | **Knee + spine X-rays** |

| | **Femoral varus osteotomy** | **Valgus osteotomy** |
|---|---|---|
| Purpose | **Containment** | Relieve **hinge abduction** |
| When | Early, containable head, >8 yr, B/BC | Late, deformed lateral head |

## How it's asked

**"Lateral pillar classification possible how long after onset?"** → **6 months**

**"All worsen outcome EXCEPT?"** → **presentation at 5 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: pillar <50% · Gage sign · lateral calcification

**"Prognostic factor most linked to OA in 5th–6th decade?"** → **femoral head asphericity**

**"Clinical factors predicting long-term outcome?"** → **age at presentation + hip ROM**

**"4-yr, pillar B — prognosis?"** → **80% chance of good outcome**

**"FALSE about Perthes?"** → **early containment surgery before 6 prevents arthritis** (TO)

**"9-yr, 6 months painless limp, pillar B?"** → **femoral or pelvic osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: core decompression · aspiration · bracing

**"5-yr Caucasian boy, painless limp?"** → **physical therapy and range-of-motion exercises**\
✗ Wrong options: Salter · VDRO · shelf · Chiari

**"10-yr, IR 5°, abduction 10°, Trendelenburg, pillar B?"** → **femoral varus and/or pelvic osteotomy; group B**

**"Valgus proximal femoral osteotomy indicated for?"** → **9-yr Perthes with painful hinge abduction**

**"5-yr, symmetric bilateral changes, symptoms resolved?"** → **knee and spine X-rays**

**"Femoral head blood supply at 18 months?"** → **MCFA (MFCA) alone** (TO)

**"Most common age group for Perthes?"** → **5 to 10 years** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 4–8 in that MCQ

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Local Gigantism

*Pediatrics · 61 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — overgrowth of some or all tissue elements of a digit / limb · rare · mostly **nonhereditary** · classically **one toe, often the 2nd**, with **normal nails**

| Type | Causes |
|---|---|
| **Congenital** | **Neurofibromatosis** · **Proteus syndrome** · **Klippel-Trenaunay-Weber** |
| **Acquired** | Amyloidosis · elephantiasis (filariasis) · AV malformation · tumor · acromegaly |

**Growth plate layers** — resting · proliferative · hypertrophic

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain · **can't fit shoes** · enlarged digit or limb

**Diagnosis** — clinical · X-rays only for **surgical planning** · **no biopsy** needed\
✗ Wrong options: antifungals (not fungal)

![Local gigantism of a toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4083-local-gigantism-moved.jpg)

![Second toe gigantism](../../MCQ/attachments/q2243-a-screen-shot-2020-01-30-at-7-42-42-pm.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment - ray amputation

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Initial / mild | **Observation**, monitor growth |
| **Open physes** | **Epiphysiodesis** ± bony + soft-tissue debulking |
| **Closed physes** | **Bony reduction and/or ray amputation** |
| Recurrence prevention | **Complete ray amputation ↓ recurrence** |

**Goals** — cosmesis + function (shoe wear) · individualised by etiology, site, growth left, severity

**Cosmesis** — after ray resection often **poor** · Syme amputation = excessive\
✗ Wrong options: "favorable"

## How it's asked

**"14-yr, closed physes, enlarged 2nd toe, shoes don't fit — true?"** → **complete 2nd ray amputation ↓ recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · antifungals · cosmesis favorable · epiphysiodesis

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lyme Disease

*Pediatrics · 62 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Organism - spirochete

**Organism** — ***Borrelia burgdorferi*** · **spirochete** · tick-borne (**Ixodes** deer tick) · zoonosis (mice, squirrels, shrews)

![Borrelia burgdorferi spirochete](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9052-borrelia.jpg)

**Transmission** — tick must stay attached **48–72 h** · bites **May–November**, most cases **June–August**

**Epidemiology** — **most common tick-borne illness in US** · bimodal: **children 5–9**, adults 55–59

**Geography** — **Northeast** (Maryland → Maine, **Connecticut**, Rhode Island, Catskills) · Great Lakes / Midwest (I. scapularis) · Pacific Northwest (I. pacificus) · deer areas

**Joint pathology** — synovial hypertrophy · vascular proliferation · mononuclear infiltrate · immune complexes · **MMPs from chondrocytes** → cartilage damage

### Stages

| Stage | Timing | Features |
|---|---|---|
| **1 Early localized** | **1–30 days** | **Erythema migrans** (hallmark) · flu-like illness |
| **2 Early disseminated** | **Weeks–months** · 15–20% untreated | **CN VII** (bilateral 50%, unlike Bell's) · **CN VI** · **lymphocytic meningitis** · migratory poly/monoarthritis · **carditis (heart block)** — rare |
| **3 Late** | **Months–years** · **60%** untreated | **Arthritis, usually knee** · intermittent / chronic monoarthritis · **acrodermatitis chronica atrophicans** |

## 3 Presentation

**Erythema migrans** — **60–80%** · expanding **>5 cm** bull's-eye · **1–3 wk** after bite (Orthobullets) · 1 wk–1 month (OB) · itch/burn · fades ~1 month · **axillary / gluteal folds**, hairline, elastic bands

**Lyme arthritis** — **knee** effusion, **large and relatively painless** · **swelling > tenderness** · near-full ROM · **can bear weight** · recurrent, migratory · rash often already resolved

**Acrodermatitis chronica atrophicans** — **"cigarette-paper" skin** · dorsum hands/feet/knees/elbows · older patients

![Acrodermatitis chronica atrophicans](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9052-atrophica.jpg)

**Neuro** — headache, neck stiffness, encephalitis · **polyradiculoneuropathy**

![Erythema migrans](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9052-erythema-migrans.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Presentation & diagnosis (Western blot)

**Serology (two-tier)** — **ELISA** (sensitive, not specific) → **Western blot** (specific, ✓ confirmatory)\
✗ Wrong options: Northern (RNA) · Southern (DNA) · blood/synovial culture

**Serology pitfalls** — negative early (weeks to seroconvert) · stays positive after old infection · **vaccination: ELISA +, Western blot −**

**Endemic area + EM** → **treat without blood tests**

**Blood** — WBC **normal or mildly ↑** · **ESR/CRP ↑**

**Synovial fluid** — **10,000–25,000 WBC/mm³** (lower than septic) · PMN predominance

**CSF** — ↑ protein + **lymphocytic pleocytosis**

**Other** — skin-edge punch biopsy of EM → culture on **Barbour-Stoenner-Kelly** medium · PCR

**MRI** — **large knee effusion with no other pathology**\
✗ Wrong options: hip AVN · HAGL with effusion · vertebral osteomyelitis · Kienböck

**Organism images (distractors)** — S. aureus (GPC clusters) · Pseudomonas · P. acnes · AFB (TB) · Corynebacterium (Chinese letters) · E. coli · gonococcus (GN diplococci) · Kingella (GN coccobacilli, <3 yr)

## 5 Management

### Treatment - doxycycline mechanism

| Group | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Adult / ≥8 yr, mild** | Oral **doxycycline** · amoxicillin · cefuroxime |
| **Child <8 yr** | **Amoxicillin** · cefuroxime (**no tetracyclines**) |
| **Neuro, arthritis**, failed oral therapy | **IV ceftriaxone** / cefotaxime / penicillin G |
| Chronic arthritis failing IV antibiotics | **Synovectomy** |

**Duration** — doxycycline **10 days** (Orthobullets) · **28 days** for arthritis (OB) · single **4-wk** oral course (OB) · 1–2 wk (OB) — read the options

**Doxycycline mechanism** — binds **30S** ribosomal subunit → blocks **tRNA binding** → ↓ protein synthesis (minor 50S)

| Mechanism | Drugs |
|---|---|
| **30S** | **Tetracyclines (doxycycline)** |
| **50S** | Macrolides, chloramphenicol, clindamycin, linezolid, streptogramins |
| **Cell wall** | Penicillins, cephalosporins, **vancomycin (D-ala-D-ala)**, carbapenems, aztreonam, bacitracin |
| **Folate** | Sulfonamides, trimethoprim (TMP-SMX) |
| **DNA gyrase** | Fluoroquinolones |
| **RNA synthesis** | Rifampin |

## 6 Complications

**Persistent joint pain / swelling** despite therapy · **chronic Lyme disease** (disabling MSK pain, neurocognitive symptoms, fatigue) · chronic arthritis rare

### Transfusion screening

**Not screened in donated blood** — **Lyme** · malaria · babesiosis · Chagas

**Screened** — HBV · HCV · syphilis · HIV-1/2 · **West Nile virus**

## X vs Y

| | **Lyme arthritis** | **Bacterial septic arthritis** |
|---|---|---|
| Weight bearing | **Can bear weight** | Usually can't |
| Serum WBC | **Normal** | ↑ |
| Fever / CRP | Low-grade, mild ↑ | High |
| Synovial WBC | **10–25k** | Higher |
| History | Rash + endemic travel 1–2 wk before | — |
| Treatment | **Antibiotics** | **I&D** |

## How it's asked

**"MRI best supporting B. burgdorferi?"** → **knee with large isolated effusion**

**"Bull's-eye rash, fever — organism?"** → **Borrelia burgdorferi**\
✗ Wrong options: Bartonella · Kingella · Brucella

**"10-yr, painless swollen knee, target rash in Connecticut?"** → **Lyme arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: septic · rheumatic fever · transient synovitis

**"Confirmatory test after positive ELISA?"** → **Western blot**\
✗ Wrong options: Northern · Southern · culture

**"Catskills camp, intermittent painless knee swelling — organism image?"** → **spirochete (Borrelia)**\
✗ Wrong options: S. aureus · P. acnes · AFB

**"Connecticut camping, WBC 25,000 in knee, walking?"** → **Borrelia burgdorferi**

**"Appalachian trip, rash, warm knee, bears weight — organism?"** → **spirochete**\
✗ Wrong options: GPC clusters · GN diplococci · GN coccobacilli

**"13-yr, Rhode Island, EM + tense knee — oral drug + mechanism?"** → **doxycycline, 30S ribosomal subunit**\
✗ Wrong options: 50S · cephalexin

**"Polyarthralgia + EM — mechanism of first-line drug?"** → **blocks tRNA binding at 30S**\
✗ Wrong options: 50S translocation · DNA gyrase · D-ala-D-ala

**"Not tested in routine blood screening?"** → **Lyme disease**\
✗ Wrong options: West Nile · HCV · syphilis

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Marfan Syndrome

*Pediatrics · 63 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **1 in 10,000** · no ethnic or sex predilection

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant** · **~30% sporadic**

**Gene** — **fibrillin-1 (FBN1)**, chromosome **15q21** · fibrillin = glycoprotein of **elastin-containing microfibrils**

**TGF-β** — misfolded fibrillin **sequesters TGF-β poorly** → ↑ tissue TGF-β (lungs, valves, aorta) → **aortic dilatation**

**Aneurysm mechanism** — **TGF-β + renin-angiotensin system** act synergistically (AngII → wall remodeling + TGF-β via Smad) → **losartan (ARB)** proposed (mixed results vs β-blockers)\
✗ Wrong options: CRP · interferon-γ

**Genetic testing** — FBN1 testing **neither sensitive nor specific** → diagnosis by **Ghent nosology** (clinical + family history; diagnoses/excludes 86%, caution in young children)

### Genetics & pathophysiology (fibrillin, TGF-beta)

| Defective protein | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Fibrillin-1** | **Marfan** |
| Elastin | Supravalvular aortic stenosis, AD cutis laxa |
| **FGFR3** | Achondroplasia |
| Type I collagen (COL1A1/2) | OI, Ehlers-Danlos |
| Type II collagen | SED, achondrogenesis, Stickler |
| **COMP** | Pseudoachondroplasia |
| Neurofibromin (NF1) | Neurofibromatosis |

| Other molecule (distractors) | Role |
|---|---|
| BMP-2 | Osteoblast differentiation, bone growth |
| Osteocalcin | ↑ insulin, glucose uptake |
| Indian hedgehog | Chondrocyte proliferation |

### Clinical features

| Orthopaedic | Non-orthopaedic |
|---|---|
| **Arachnodactyly** | **Aortic root dilatation / dissection** |
| **Scoliosis (50%)** — often first diagnosed | **Mitral valve prolapse** |
| **Protrusio acetabuli (15–25%)** | **SUPERIOR lens dislocation (60%)** (✗ Wrong options: inferior (homocystinuria)) |
| **Dural ectasia (>60%)** · meningocele | **Spontaneous pneumothorax** |
| Ligamentous laxity, recurrent dislocations (patella, shoulder, fingers), ankle sprains | Myopia |
| **Pes planovalgus** | Skin striae, recurrent hernias |
| Pectus excavatum / carinatum | — |

**Dural ectasia** — also in **NF1** · complicates instrumentation

## 3 Presentation

**Dolichostenomelia** — **arm span > height (ratio >1.05)**

**Steinberg thumb sign** — thumb tip extends beyond ulnar border of hand when clasped under fingers

**Walker-Murdoch wrist sign** — thumb + small finger distal phalanges **overlap** around opposite wrist

**Sudden death** — aortic dissection / MVP → **exertion or pregnancy** · cardiovascular causes nearly all early deaths

![Arm span to height ratio](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4089-arm-span-to-height-ratio.jpg)

![Positive wrist and thumb signs](../../MCQ/attachments/q4999-b-positive-wrist-thumb-signs.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — scoliosis series → scoliosis, kyphosis

**MRI spine before surgery** — **dural ectasia** (→ CSF leak risk)

**CT before surgery** — pedicle / bony adequacy for fixation

**Echo + cardiology** — **before ANY surgery** (dissection, anticoagulation for prosthetic valves)

**Protrusio acetabuli** — CE angle **>40°** + medial wall past **ilioischial line** · differential: Marfan, Paget, RA, psoriatic, AS, infection (TB), osteomalacia, fibrous dysplasia, idiopathic

![Dural ectasia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4089-dural-ectasia.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Cardiovascular evaluation

**Rule** — orthopaedist often first to suspect → refer **cardiology** (± genetics) · **cardiovascular evaluation before conception** and before surgery\
✗ Wrong options: genetic testing first · spine/brain MRI first

**Medical** — **β-blockers** ↓ aortic dilatation · laxity → observation + orthotics

### Scoliosis management

**Bracing** — early mild curves · **less effective than AIS**, usually refractory\
✗ Wrong options: "often effective if started early"

**Surgery** — **PSF ± ASF** · rapidly progressive curve in immature patient · large curve in mature · **extend construct** (avoid adding-on) · fuse to pelvis for obliquity / poor distal fixation · growing rods if young · **maximise fixation points**, slow distraction/compression · **avoid overcorrection** (cardiovascular complications) · treating kyphosis risks vertebral subluxation

**Early surgery routinely needed for** — **progressive scoliosis**\
✗ Wrong options: kyphosis · ankle instability · protrusio · tibial pseudarthrosis (NF1)

| Complication vs AIS | Marfan |
|---|---|
| **Fixation failure** (most common — thin laminae/pedicles, osteopenia) | **↑** |
| Infection | ↑ |
| **Pseudarthrosis** | ↑ |
| **Dural tear / CSF leak (8%)** | ↑ |
| Decompensation, reoperation | ↑ |
| Blood loss | Same |
| **Neurologic deficit** | **Same** (✗ Wrong options: increased) |
| Length of stay | Same |

### Acetabular protrusio

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Asymptomatic, stable | Observation |
| **Progressing or painful**, immature | **Triradiate cartilage closure** (controversial) |
| Mature, young, minimal OA | **Valgus osteotomy** |
| Older, symptomatic OA | **THA** |

## X vs Y

| | **Marfan** | **Homocystinuria** |
|---|---|---|
| Lens | **Superior** dislocation | **Inferior** dislocation |

## How it's asked

**"Marfan — defective gene encodes?"** → **fibrillin**\
✗ Wrong options: elastin · FGFR3 · collagen I/II

**"Finding most linked to FBN1 mutation?"** → **dural ectasia**\
✗ Wrong options: Sprengel · sacral agenesis · fibular hemimelia · Lisch nodules

**"Misfolded glycoprotein, TGF-β accumulation, dural ectasia — associated?"** → **positive thumb and wrist signs**\
✗ Wrong options: spinal stenosis · odontoid hypoplasia · enlarged calves

**"Fibrillin-1 defect → diagnosis?"** → **Marfan syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: pseudoachondroplasia (COMP)

**"Two factors synergistic in Marfan aortic aneurysm?"** → **TGF-β and renin-angiotensin system**

**"Elevated TGF-β associated with?"** → **aortic dilatation**\
✗ Wrong options: impaired chondrocyte proliferation

**"All associated with Marfan EXCEPT?"** → **inferior lens dislocation**

**"23-yr, bilateral protrusio, wants to conceive?"** → **cardiovascular evaluation**\
✗ Wrong options: genetic testing · pelvic CT

**"Tall basketball player, pectus, flatfeet — refer for?"** → **cardiovascular evaluation**\
✗ Wrong options: ophthalmology · genetics

**"Marfan scoliosis — all true EXCEPT?"** → **bracing often effective if started early**

**"Vs AIS surgery — all true EXCEPT?"** → **increased neurologic deficits**

**"Routinely needs early surgery in Marfan?"** → **progressive scoliosis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Medial Clavicle Physeal Fracture

*Pediatrics · 64 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **pseudodislocation of the SC joint** · childhood equivalent of adult **SC separation** · rare · usually **SH I or II**

**Mechanism** — FOOSH · direct blow (tackle) · abuse rare

**Pathoanatomy** — physis = **weakest link** · periosteal sleeve + costoclavicular / SC ligaments usually intact → remodels · Lee et al: of 40 operated posterior SC injuries, **50% physeal fractures, 50% true dislocations**

### Physeal fusion age

**Clavicle** — **first bone to ossify** (5th week in utero) · central intramembranous centre <5 yr · lateral epiphysis ossifies ~18 yr

**Medial physis** — **~80% of clavicular growth** · ossifies **18–20 yr** · fuses **20–25 yr** (Orthobullets, OB) · **~20–21 yr** (TO, Schmeling) · **last physis to close** (body — Orthobullets; upper extremity — OB) → "SC dislocation" in teens/young adults is often a physeal fracture

**TO** — medial clavicle unites last with humeral head, acromion, ischial tuberosity · males 1–2 yr later than females

| Schmeling stage (TO) | Male (yr) | Female (yr) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 11–14 | 12–15 |
| 2 | 15–20 | 14–18 |
| 3 | 16–36 | 15–26 |
| 4 | 21–40 | 21–37 |
| 5 | 26–40 | 26–39 |

## 3 Presentation

| | **Anterior displacement** | **Posterior displacement** |
|---|---|---|
| Exam | **Palpable bump**, sharp metaphysis under skin · SCM pulls fragment → head tilts to affected side | Swelling, depression of medial clavicle |
| Danger | Low | **Dyspnea, dysphagia, stridor**, ↓ distal pulses, venous congestion, **paresthesias** |
| At risk | — | **Innominate artery + vein, IJV, phrenic + vagus nerves, trachea, esophagus** |

**General** — pain, ↓ arm ROM · prominence ↑ with abduction / elevation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**AP X-ray** — unreliable

**Serendipity view** — beam **40° cephalic** · **anterior** = affected clavicle **above** opposite · **posterior** = **below**

**Axial CT** — **study of choice** · distinguishes physeal fracture from **SC dislocation** · shows mediastinal injury

![Serendipity view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4123-seren.jpg)

![Axial CT of medial clavicle physeal fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4123-22-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Management

| Scenario | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Asymptomatic · **anterior** displacement | **Immobilization for comfort / observation** (remodels) |
| Posterior, **no mediastinal injury** | Observation |
| **Acute posterior + airway, esophageal or NV compromise** | **Closed reduction under GA, thoracic surgeon available** |
| Failed closed reduction · **chronic symptomatic posterior** | **ORIF** |
| **Late-presenting posterior** | No closed reduction (clavicle stuck to mediastinal vessels) |

**Anterior reduction** — supine, bolster, traction both arms, posterior pressure ± towel clip · fails → sling

**Posterior reduction** — supine, bolster, traction with **shoulder adducted**, posterior force on shoulder, **towel clip pulls medial clavicle forward** · fails → open

**ORIF** — horizontal incision · **sutures** clavicle → sternum / epiphysis (allow future MRI) · **no pins (migration)** · no plate · CT to confirm stability

## 6 Complications

**Persistent instability** — rare (children remodel)

**Subclavian artery/vein laceration** — rare · **rapidly expanding hematoma** · thick periosteum protective → vessel repair

**Pin migration** — avoid pins around clavicle

## How it's asked

**"9-yr tackled, CT 2 cm anterior displacement, no distress?"** → **immobilization for comfort**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · closed reduction with thoracic backup · pinning

**"Medial clavicle physis — true?"** → **fusion around 20–21 yr** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: fusion 15–16 · appears 10–11

**"10-yr medial clavicle physeal injury — fuses when?"** → **20–25 yr**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Medial Epicondylar Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 65 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **3rd most common** pediatric elbow fracture · up to **20%** of elbow fractures (Orthobullets, OB) · 12% (OB5909) · **75% boys 9–14 yr** · rising with youth sport

**Mechanism** — **avulsion**: valgus stress + **flexor-pronator contraction** (throwers) · direct blow

**Elbow dislocation** — present in **50–60%** · **most common fracture with pediatric elbow dislocation** (33% of dislocations, Rasool) · fragment stays **incarcerated in ~15%**

### Anatomy & ulnar nerve risk

**Medial epicondyle** — **apophysis** (no longitudinal growth) · **last distal humeral centre to fuse** · origin of **flexor-pronator mass + UCL** · lies **posteromedial**, fragment displaces **ANTERIORLY** (flexor-pronator pull)

**Flexor-pronator mass** — **pronator teres** · FCR · palmaris longus · FDS · FCU\
✗ Wrong options: pronator quadratus · brachioradialis · ECRL · brachialis

**Blood supply** — anterior: inferior ulnar collateral a. · posterior: superior + inferior ulnar collateral aa.

**Ulnar nerve** — **C8–T1**, **medial cord** · splits two heads of FCU → runs **between FDP and FCU** · Guyon's canal, **superficial** to transverse carpal ligament · sensation **palmar + dorsal ulnar 1½ digits** · injury → ↓ wrist palmar flexion (FCU) + ring/small finger numbness

| Centre | Appears (Orthobullets) | Fuses |
|---|---|---|
| Capitellum | 1 | 12–14 |
| Radial head | 3 | 14–16 |
| **Medial epicondyle** | **5** | **16–18** |
| Trochlea | 7 | 12–14 |
| Olecranon | 9 | 15–17 |
| Lateral epicondyle | 11 | 12–14 |

**CRITOE (OB214100 variant)** — 1, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9 yr · same text: medial side not ossified before ~5, trochlea complete ~9

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — medial pain · valgus instability · ecchymosis · **document ulnar nerve motor + sensory** · generalised swelling → elbow probably dislocated

**Classification** — none routine · acute (nondisplaced, minimally displaced, displaced, **entrapped**, through apophysis) vs chronic (tension stress)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Displacement assessment & imaging

**Views** — AP + lateral + **internal oblique** · **distal humeral axial view** (beam **25° anterior** to humeral axis) = **most accurate X-ray** for displacement

**Pitfall** — AP, lateral, IR views **underestimate**; fragment invisible on lateral if <10 mm

**CT** — most accurate, more radiation

**Arthrogram** — before ossification · e.g. **displaced medial condyle fracture in a 4-yr-old**\
✗ Wrong options: nondisplaced medial epicondyle at 6 · radial head at 5

**Young child (<5 yr)** — unossified epicondyle → **irreducible dislocation = incarcerated epicondyle until proven otherwise**\
✗ Wrong options: capitellum / radial head fracture (would be visible)

![Distal humeral axial view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4008-picture-demonstrating-the-technique-to-obtain-a-distal-humer.jpg)

![Medial epicondyle incarcerated in joint after reduction](../../MCQ/attachments/q41-illa-elbow-dislocation-with-medial-epicondyle-ap.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Medial epicondyle fracture
├─ Incarcerated fragment / open / medial condyle extension → ORIF (absolute)
├─ Thrower or gymnast >2–5 mm, ulnar nerve dysfunction, dislocation → consider ORIF (relative)
└─ Others → long arm cast 1–3 wk at 90°, then early motion
```

### Incarcerated fragment & post-reduction incongruity

**Post-reduction incongruent ulnohumeral joint** → **interposed medial epicondyle**\
✗ Wrong options: annular ligament · brachialis · ulnar nerve

**Roberts maneuver** — **forearm supination + wrist/finger extension + valgus load** → may pull fragment out \
✗ Wrong options: Patterson (radial neck)

**Persistent incarceration** — **absolute indication for ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat closed reduction · CRPP · immobilization

**With ulnar paresthesia** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · nerve transposition (not usually needed) · UCL reconstruction

### Operative indications & union (displacement, throwers)

| Absolute | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Incarcerated fragment** | **Ulnar nerve dysfunction** |
| **Medial condyle / articular extension** | Displacement **>2–15 mm** (controversial) · 2–10 mm (OB) |
| **Open fracture** | **>2–5 mm in throwers / gymnasts** (valgus stability) |
| — | Associated dislocation · valgus instability |

**Non-op** — long arm cast **1–3 wk**, elbow **90°** (AROM within ~1 wk) · historically **<5 mm** · RCTs: mean ~7 mm with equal outcomes · grip + ROM equal by 3 months

**Non-op vs ORIF** — **functional outcome equivalent** (displacement <15 mm) · **↑ nonunion** without surgery · **union odds 9× with surgery** \
✗ Wrong options: cubitus varus · fishtail · ↑ ulnar neurapraxia

**Throwing athlete, 7 mm or large displaced fragment** → **ORIF** (highest union, valgus stability)\
✗ Wrong options: excision (destabilises) · CRPP (ulnar nerve risk)

**ORIF technique** — **medial approach**, supine, arm abducted 90° (or prone) · interval **brachialis (musculocutaneous) / triceps (radial)** · find + protect **ulnar nerve proximal → distal** · **cannulated screw** ± washer · K-wires for small fragments / young children · avoid comminution · bicortical screw risks radial nerve \
✗ Wrong options: triceps/brachioradialis (lateral condyle) · brachialis/brachioradialis

![Screw fixation of medial epicondyle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4008-ap.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Asymptomatic fibrous union

**Nonunion** — common after non-op · **majority asymptomatic** · poor correlation with function\
✗ Wrong options: Tinel · valgus pain · malalignment

**Ulnar nerve injury** — **10–16%** (~10% OB) · neurapraxia after dislocation usually resolves · delayed palsy from missed fracture recovers if treated promptly

**Missed incarceration** — esp. young child with unossified epicondyle

**Stiffness** — **most common**: loss of a few degrees of **extension** · ↑ with prolonged immobilization

**Prognosis** — good to excellent with either treatment

## X vs Y

| | **Medial epicondyle** | **Medial condyle** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | 12–20% | **1–2%** |
| Age | 9–14 yr | Younger (trochlea unossified) |
| Articular | **Extra-articular** apophysis | **Intra-articular**, often SH IV |
| Risk | Incarceration, ulnar nerve | **Nonunion**, growth arrest, **AVN** (posterior terminal supply), misdiagnosis |
| Treatment | Mostly non-op | **ORIF** (anatomic) · arthrogram if unossified |

## How it's asked

**"11-yr, elbow reduced but ulnohumeral joint incongruent?"** → **interposed medial epicondyle fragment**

**"11-yr monkey bars, fragment in joint after reduction?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: hinged brace · sling · pinning

**"ORIF indicated because?"** → **incarcerated fragment in ulnohumeral joint**\
✗ Wrong options: >5 mm · prevent cubitus valgus

**"8-yr gymnast, incarcerated after reduction — approach?"** → **ORIF through brachialis/triceps plane**\
✗ Wrong options: triceps/brachioradialis

**"12-yr, incarcerated + ring/little finger numbness?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: nerve transposition · splint and observe · UCL reconstruction

**"Roberts maneuver fails, fragment still in joint?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: Patterson · CRPP

**"Which X-ray most likely needs surgery?"** → **post-reduction entrapped medial epicondyle**

**"4-yr, irreducible posterolateral dislocation, no fracture seen?"** → **incarcerated medial epicondyle**\
✗ Wrong options: capitellum · radial head

**"5 mm displaced, cast — vs surgery?"** → **↑ nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: worse function · cubitus varus

**"15-yr pitcher, 7 mm displaced — highest union?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: brace · excision

**"10-yr, displaced rotated medial condyle fracture?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: casting

**"17-yr baseball player, significantly displaced?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: excision · CRPP

**"Direction of displacement + best view?"** → **anteromedial; distal humeral axial view**

**"Arthrogram most useful for?"** → **displaced medial condyle fracture in a 4-yr-old**

**"Muscle causing avulsion?"** → **pronator teres**

**"Structure at risk in repair?"** → **ulnar nerve**

**"Post-op wrist flexion weakness + ring/small numbness — nerve?"** → **medial cord, between FDP and FCU**

**"5-yr medial condyle ORIF — C8–T1 nerve injured, numbness where?"** → **palmar ulnar 1½ digits**

**"Fracture most associated with pediatric elbow dislocation?"** → **medial epicondyle**

**"Pitcher, casted 3 wk, radiographic nonunion?"** → **asymptomatic**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Metatarsus Adductus

*Pediatrics · 66 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — forefoot **adduction at the tarsometatarsal joint** with **normal hindfoot** · **packaging** disorder (intra-uterine position)

**Epidemiology** — **1 in 1,000** · M = F · **bilateral ~50%** · ↑ late pregnancy, **first pregnancy**, **twins**, **oligohydramnios**

**Associated** — **DDH (15–20%)** · torticollis

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis

**History** — parents notice **in-toeing in first year**

**Exam** — adducted forefoot · **convex lateral border** · **medial soft-tissue crease → more rigid** · **normal hindfoot + subtalar motion** · tickle foot to test **active** correction\
✗ Wrong options: straight

**TO case** — 5-month-old, **no medial crease**, gap between 1st/2nd toes, adduction correctable to 10° abduction · options: metatarsus adductus / constriction band / metatarsus primus varus / positional MA · **answer not provided by the source** · no crease + correctable = flexible deformity

### Causes of intoeing

| Condition | Key finding |
|---|---|
| **Metatarsus adductus** | Forefoot medially deviated, **hindfoot normal** · heel bisector lateral |
| **Internal tibial torsion** | **Thigh–foot angle >10° internal** (normal 0–20° external) |
| **Femoral anteversion** | Prone **IR >70°** (normal 30–60°), **ER <20°** · patella points in |

**Work up intoeing if** — pain · LLD · progression · family history of rickets / dysplasia / MPS · rotational profile **>2 SD** from normal

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Bleck classification (heel bisector)

| Grade | Heel bisector passes through |
|---|---|
| **Normal** | **2nd–3rd web space** |
| Mild | 3rd toe |
| Moderate | 3rd–4th web space |
| Severe | 4th–5th web space |

![Heel bisector line](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4061-heel-foot-bisector-2-moved.jpg)

### Berg classification

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| Simple MTA | MTA |
| Complex MTA | MTA + **lateral shift of midfoot** |
| **Skew foot** | MTA + **valgus hindfoot** |
| Complex skew foot (**serpentine**) | MTA + lateral shift + valgus hindfoot |

**Serpentine foot** — TMT adductus + **talonavicular lateral subluxation** + hindfoot valgus · **casting ineffective**\
✗ Wrong options: like simple MTA

**X-rays** — only in older children

**Other foot differentials** — clubfoot · **atavistic great toe** (congenital hallux varus) · skewfoot

## 5 Management

### Treatment - observation & stretching

**Natural history** — **90% resolve by age 4** · another 5% in early walking years · flexible: **90–95% spontaneous correction**

| Flexibility | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Actively** correctable to midline | **No treatment — reassurance + observation** |
| **Passively** correctable | **Serial stretching by parents** |
| **Rigid**, medial crease | **Serial casting** (goal straight lateral border) · **before 6 months** (OB) |

![Rigid metatarsus adductus with medial crease](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4061-rigid-metatarsus-adductus-courtesy-miller-moved.png)

**Shoes, braces, splints** — efficacy not established · (✗ Wrong options: Ponseti (clubfoot) · Achilles lengthening unless tight heel cord)\
✗ Wrong options: dynamic AFO

### Residual deformity – tarsometatarsal capsulotomy

| Indication | Surgery |
|---|---|
| **Age 2–4 yr**, failed non-op | **TMT capsulotomies** |
| **Age >5 yr**, resistant (medial crease), pain / shoe problems | **Lateral column shortening (cuboid closing wedge) + medial column opening (cuneiform) wedge** · multiple metatarsal osteotomies · abductor hallucis recession (atavistic toe) |
| Symptomatic **serpentine** foot | Calcaneal osteotomy + midfoot osteotomies + **multiple metatarsal osteotomies with pinning + TMT release (Hamen)** · often accepted / observed |

**Surgery timing (OB)** — >3 yr or rigid unresponsive to casting · medial release if symptomatic beyond 4 yr

**Indications (TO)** — pain · objectionable appearance · **difficulty fitting shoes** after the age for stretching/casting has passed

**Prognosis** — residual MTA **not linked to pain or ↓ function** · late **medial cuneiform obliquity**\
✗ Wrong options: hallux valgus

## X vs Y

| | **Metatarsus adductus** | **Clubfoot** | **Skewfoot** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Forefoot | Adducted | Adducted | Adducted |
| Hindfoot | **Normal** | **Varus + equinus** | **Valgus** |
| Ankle | Full DF/PF | Equinus | — |
| First line | Observe / stretch / cast | Ponseti | Often accept; osteotomies if symptomatic |

## How it's asked

**"Newborn MTA, corrects actively and passively?"** → **parental reassurance and observation**\
✗ Wrong options: stretching · casting · Ponseti

**"Newborn mild–moderate MTA — initial treatment?"** → **observation with possible stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: serial casting · medial release · dynamic AFO

**"3-yr, moderate MTA, failed stretching/casting, shoe problems?"** → **tarsometatarsal capsulotomies** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: observation · more casting · metatarsal osteotomies

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Monteggia Fracture - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 67 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **radial head dislocation** + proximal ulna fracture **or plastic deformation** of ulna

**Age** — peak **4–10 yr**

**Rule** — **isolated radial head dislocation almost never occurs in children** → look for ulnar plastic deformation

**Pattern** — Bado **I most common in children** · II more common in adults · children do better than adults

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, swelling, deformity · **palpate radial head with every ulna fracture** (radial head often relocates spontaneously)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Rule** — **elbow X-rays for every forearm / ulna fracture** · **forearm X-rays for every radial head dislocation** · orthogonal films of whole forearm, wrist + elbow

**Radiocapitellar line** — line along radial neck axis passes through **centre of capitellum on every view** · any deviation = Monteggia

**Ulnar shaft fracture after ED reduction** → **dedicated elbow radiographs** before anything else\
✗ Wrong options: cast · fixation

![Radiocapitellar line](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4015-rc-line.jpg)

### Bado classification

| Type | Ulna apex | Radial head | Cast position |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Anterior** | **Anterior** (most common in children) | **110° flexion + full supination** |
| **II** | Posterior | Posterior | Full extension |
| **III** | **Lateral** | **Lateral** | Full extension + **valgus mold** |
| **IV** | **Radius + ulna** fractured same level | **Anterior** (1–11%) | — operative |

![Bado type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4015-37-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Pediatric Monteggia
├─ Length-stable ulna (plastic / greenstick / buckle) + stable RC joint → closed reduction + long arm cast
├─ Length-unstable ulna or RC unstable → fix ulna (IM nail or plate) + closed RC reduction
├─ RC joint irreducible after anatomic ulna → open reduction (annular ligament block)
└─ Bado IV · open · ≥10 yr unstable · closed physes → operative
```

### Acute treatment - restore ulnar length

**First consideration** — **reduce + maintain ulnar length and alignment** → radial head usually reduces itself\
✗ Wrong options: open RC reduction first · annular ligament reconstruction · RC pin

**Closed reduction first** — traction · Type I: **flexion** is main maneuver (+ supination) · prompt reduction → delay makes it difficult/impossible\
✗ Wrong options: plate · pins · splint without reduction

**Why supination + 110° flexion** — **tightens interosseous membrane** + **relaxes biceps** · annular, quadrate, oblique + interosseous ligaments tighten in supination\
✗ Wrong options: tightens biceps

| Operative indication | |
|---|---|
| Types I–III with **unstable radial head** after reduction | Yes |
| **Ulnar length not maintained** (complete, comminuted, displaced) | Yes |
| **Acute Bado IV** (even if head reduces) | Yes |
| Open fracture · ≥10 yr unstable · closed physes (adult ORIF) | Yes |
| **Loss of RC reduction on follow-up** | Yes |
| Incomplete ulna fracture + reduced radial head | No (cast) |

**Ulna fixation choice** — **transverse / short oblique → IM flexible nail** · **long oblique / comminuted → open reduction + plate** (nails won't prevent shortening)

**After failed closed treatment (Bado I, lost reduction at 5 days)** — **fix ulna + closed RC reduction + cast in supination**\
✗ Wrong options: open RC · annular / LCL repair (rarely needed)

**Comminuted ulna, RC won't hold** — **closed RC reduction + ORIF plate ulna**\
✗ Wrong options: RC pin (breaks) · IM nail · repeat closed reduction

![Intramedullary nailing of ulna](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4015-show-stacked-intramedullary-nailing-of-a-monteggia-fracture.jpg)

### Irreducible radial head - annular ligament

**Block to RC reduction** — **annular ligament** (most common, trapped between capitellum and radial head) · rare: biceps, PIN, capsule

**Annular ligament function** — **stabilises the proximal radioulnar joint**\
✗ Wrong options: PIN · LUCL · MUCL · biceps

**Check** — after anatomic ulna, examine radial head in pronation **and** supination · still out → **open reduction**

### Chronic missed Monteggia

**Presentation** — pain, ↓ motion, progressive valgus after missed / delayed diagnosis

**Treatment** — **open reduction of radial head** (lateral approach, radial head still concave) ± **annular ligament repair/reconstruction**, **ulnar osteotomy**, radial osteotomy · cast in **supination + flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: closed reduction · bracing

**Acute** — annular ligament reconstruction almost never needed

## X vs Y

| | **Acute Monteggia** | **Chronic (>2–3 wk) missed** |
|---|---|---|
| Key step | **Restore ulnar length** → RC reduces closed | **Open RC reduction** |
| Annular ligament | Rarely addressed | Repair / reconstruct |
| Extra | IM nail or plate ulna | **Ulnar osteotomy** |

## How it's asked

**"6-yr, comminuted ulna + anterior RC dislocation, RC won't stay reduced?"** → **closed RC reduction + ORIF plate of ulna**\
✗ Wrong options: RC pin · IM nail

**"Surgical indication EXCEPT?"** → **incomplete ulna fracture with lateral dislocation successfully reduced**

**"Why cast at 110° flexion + full supination?"** → **tightens interosseous membrane**\
✗ Wrong options: tightens biceps

**"Lost ulnar + RC reduction at 5 days?"** → **fix ulna, closed RC reduction, cast in supination**\
✗ Wrong options: annular / LCL repair

**"5-yr monkey bars Monteggia — first consideration?"** → **reduce and maintain ulnar fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: open RC · annular reconstruction

**"5-yr trampoline Monteggia — next step?"** → **closed reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: plate · splint

**"Ulnar shaft fracture post-reduction — next?"** → **dedicated elbow radiographs**

**"Failed closed RC reduction — blocking structure?"** → **annular ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: biceps · PIN

**"Interposed structure's function?"** → **stabilises proximal radioulnar joint**

**"3-month-old radial head dislocation, closed reduction fails?"** → **open reduction + cast in supination and flexion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Mucopolysaccharidoses

*Pediatrics · 68 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **13 lysosomal storage disorders** · missing/faulty lysosomal enzymes → **glycosaminoglycans (GAGs)** accumulate in chondrocytes, cartilage ECM, synovium, organs

**Most common** — **Morquio + Sanfilippo** (Orthobullets) · Morquio (OB)

### Genes & lab markers

| Syndrome | Enzyme | Urine GAG | Inheritance | Intelligence | Cornea | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Morquio (MPS IV)** | A: galactosamine-6-sulfatase · B: β-galactosidase | **Keratan sulfate** | AR | **Normal** | **Cloudy** | Adulthood (A worse) |
| **Hurler (MPS I)** | **α-L-iduronidase** | **Dermatan** (+ heparan, TO) | AR | Progressive MR | **Cloudy** | **Death 1st decade** · most severe |
| **Sanfilippo (MPS III)** | Several | **Heparan sulfate** | AR | MR | **Clear** | Death 2nd decade |
| **Hunter (MPS II)** | Sulpho-iduronate sulfatase | Dermatan + heparan | **X-linked recessive** | MR | **Clear** | Death by 2nd decade |

**Tests** — urine **toluidine blue spot** test · **skin fibroblast** enzyme assay · **chorionic villous sampling**

### Clinical features (CTS, contractures)

**Orthopaedic** — **proportionate dwarfism** · **C1–C2 instability** · **carpal tunnel syndrome** (esp. MPS I Scheie; thick retinaculum) · trigger finger · **progressive joint contractures** (✓ most common early MSK sign — wrist, digits, hips, knees) · delayed hip dysplasia · abnormal epiphyses · **bullet-shaped phalanges/metacarpals** · **genu valgum** (lateral proximal tibial metaphyseal dysplasia) · thoracolumbar kyphosis (L1–L2 beaking, gibbus)

**Non-orthopaedic** — complex sugars in urine · visceromegaly · corneal clouding · cardiac disease · deafness · **MR (except Morquio)** · enlarged skull · coarse facies

**Not in MPS** — **dural ectasia** (Marfan, EDS, NF1, AS) · Klippel-Feil triad · sacralization of L5 · congenital ACL absence (NF1, fibular hemimelia) · block vertebrae (VACTERL) · congenital radial head dislocation

**Lysosomal CTS** — Hurler, Hunter, Scheie, pseudo-Hurler · Phalen/Tinel unreliable in children · **transverse carpal ligament release**

## 3 Presentation

### Morquio syndrome

**Onset** — **18–24 months** · ~1 in 40,000 · short-trunk dwarfism

**Features** — **normal intelligence** · waddling gait · **genu valgum** · thoracic kyphosis · corneal clouding · skin + joint laxity · hip dysplasia

**X-ray** — thick skull · wide ribs · **vertebral beaking** · **platyspondyly** · coxa vara with unossified head · bullet-shaped metacarpals · **odontoid hypoplasia → C1–C2 instability**

![Genu valgum in Morquio](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4101-xray-genu-valum-colorado-moved.jpg)

### Hurler syndrome (gargoylism)

**Features** — **gibbus (thoracolumbar kyphosis) — often first sign, ~6 months** (TO) · C1–C2 instability (25%) · severe global spinal stenosis · CTS + trigger fingers · severe hip dysplasia · genu valgum · coxa valga → subluxation · J-shaped sella, thick calvarium, platyspondyly (TO)

**Survival** — median **6.8 yr** untreated (TO)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Spine screening & atlantoaxial instability (Morquio, Hurler)

**Odontoid hypoplasia + AAI** — **Morquio** most classic · OB220568 + TO: Hurler also has odontoid hypoplasia — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: Hurler per OB2836 (Hurler instability "not from odontoid hypoplasia")

**Morquio pre-op** — **C-spine flexion–extension X-rays** (intubation risk) · GAG behind dens → stenosis

**Hurler pre-op** — **MRI of whole spine**\
✗ Wrong options: Kniest · Schmid · Turner

**Others with odontoid hypoplasia** — pseudoachondroplasia (normal urine) · diastrophic dysplasia (sulfate transporter, cervical kyphosis, cauliflower ears, hitchhiker thumb) · SED · Down (no hypoplastic dens, abnormal intelligence)

**Pre-op C-spine NOT needed** — Friedreich ataxia · Gaucher · MED · cleidocranial dysplasia

![Odontoid hypoplasia with atlantoaxial instability](../../MCQ/attachments/q4638-illa-aa-instab-odon-hypo.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Morquio** — **decompression + C1–C2 fusion** for myelopathy with instability · realignment osteotomies (malalignment recurs) · guided growth (little evidence)

**AAI surgery triggers** — unsteady gait, limb weakness, dysesthesias, urinary retention

### Treatment – bone marrow transplant

**HSCT / BMT** — ↑ **life expectancy** (Hurler if done in 1st year · Sanfilippo) · **does NOT change orthopaedic manifestations** (poor enzyme penetration of cartilage/bone)

**Age** — HSCT **<24 months** → better development · **<2 yr severe MPS I → HCT preferred** (TO)

**Graft failure** — **relatively HIGH (~30%)** + GVHD (TO) \
✗ Wrong options: "low incidence"

**Enzyme replacement (ERT)** — ↓ visceral + cardiac GAG, ↑ joint mobility, endurance, respiration · **can't cross BBB** → no help for cognition or **cord compression** · little effect on valves, bone, cornea · approved MPS I, II, IVA, VI, VII

## X vs Y

| | **MPS** | **MED** | **Achondroplasia** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dwarfism | **Proportionate** | Short-limbed, disproportionate | Rhizomelic |
| Urine | **GAGs** | Normal | Normal |
| Spine | AAI, kyphosis | **Normal** | Stenosis |

## How it's asked

**"Lysosomal storage disease — increased rate of?"** → **carpal tunnel syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: syringomyelia · diastematomyelia

**"Morquio — all EXCEPT?"** → **low hairline + webbed neck + ↓ C-spine ROM (Klippel-Feil)**

**"6-yr on enzyme replacement — all EXCEPT?"** → **dural ectasia**\
✗ Wrong options: kyphosis · genu valgum · CTS · AAI

**"MPS I — most common MSK abnormality?"** → **progressive joint contracture**

**"Odontoid hypoplasia AAI — which MPS?"** → **Morquio**\
✗ Wrong options: Hurler · Hunter

**"Normal intelligence, urinary keratan sulfate, unstable C1–C2?"** → **Morquio**\
✗ Wrong options: Sanfilippo · pseudoachondroplasia · Down

**"Needs C-spine X-rays pre-op?"** → **Morquio syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: MED · Gaucher · cleidocranial

**"Genu valgum growth modulation — whole-spine MRI first?"** → **Hurler syndrome**

**"FALSE about MPS treatment?"** → **low incidence of graft failure and GVHD after HSCT** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Multiple Epiphyseal Dysplasia (MED)

*Pediatrics · 69 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — failure of formation of **secondary ossification centres** → irregular, **delayed epiphyseal ossification** at multiple sites → secondary articular cartilage deformity

**Epidemiology** — ~**1 in 10,000** · presents **5–14 yr**

**Sites** — **proximal femur + proximal humerus** most · **hips** = most common joint

### Diagnosis & genetics (COMP)

| Inheritance | Gene | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Autosomal dominant** (most common) | **COMP**, chromosome **19** | **Most common + most severe** |
| AD | **Type IX collagen** (COL9A1/2/3) | Link protein for type II collagen → articular cartilage |
| AD | **Matrilin 3** | — |
| **Autosomal recessive** | **SLC26A2** (diastrophic dysplasia sulfate transporter) | ≥1 finding at birth: clubfoot, cleft palate, clinodactyly |

**Up to ½** of clinical MED have none of the typical mutations

## 3 Presentation

**Features** — **short-limbed, disproportionate dwarfism** (mild) · joint pain · **waddling gait** / bilateral gluteus medius lurch · valgus knees · early OA · contractures · **short stubby fingers + toes**

**Normal** — **spine** · neuro exam · **intelligence** · facies

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**General** — **irregular, delayed ossification at multiple epiphyses** · multiple **OCD** lesions

**Hip** — **bilateral, symmetric** proximal femoral epiphyseal flattening · **early acetabular changes** · **no metaphyseal cysts** · acetabular dysplasia · may have concurrent AVN

**Knee** — valgus · flattened condyles · **double-layer patella**

**Hand / foot** — short stunted metacarpals · hyperextensible fingers · short metatarsals

![MED — bilateral symmetric femoral head changes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4095-xray-pelvis-colorado-moved.jpg)

![Double-layer patella and bilateral hip involvement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4095-patella-can-have-a-double-layer-appearance-and-hip-involveme.jpg)

### Workup - skeletal survey

**Bilateral symmetric femoral head flattening** → **skeletal survey**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · MRI · CT · CBC/CRP

**Unilateral flattening** → Perthes (no survey needed)

**Symmetric bilateral + family history of early OA + myopia** → **skeletal survey + COL2A1 genetic screening** (type II collagen disorder mimicking LCPD)\
✗ Wrong options: MRI for AVN · steroids for JIA · Perthes care

**Skeletal survey not for** — CP windswept hips · SCFE · hamstring avulsion · unilateral LCPD

## 5 Management

| Problem | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Early OA | **NSAIDs + PT** |
| Childhood acetabular dysplasia | Often resolves by maturity |
| **Progressive genu varum / valgum** | **Realigning osteotomy or hemiepiphysiodesis** |
| Severe hip arthritis (**by age ~30**) | **THA** |

## X vs Y

| | **MED** | **Bilateral Perthes** | **SED** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hips | **Symmetric, same stage** | **Asymmetric, different stages** (bilateral 10–15%) | Involved |
| Acetabulum | Early changes | Late | — |
| Metaphyseal cysts | **No** | Yes (head-at-risk) | — |
| Spine | **Normal** | Normal | **Involved**, AAI, myelopathy, sharp curve |
| Gene | **COMP**, type IX collagen | — | **Type II collagen** |

## How it's asked

**"9-yr short, valgus knee, waddling, COMP mutation?"** → **multiple epiphyseal dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: achondroplasia · diastrophic · Perthes

**"Which X-ray needs a skeletal survey?"** → **symmetric bilateral femoral epiphyseal flattening (MED)**\
✗ Wrong options: unilateral Perthes · SCFE

**"5-yr, mild short stature, bilateral gluteus medius lurch, flat femoral heads?"** → **skeletal survey**

**"6-yr, symmetric bilateral head irregularity, family early OA + myopia?"** → **skeletal survey + COL2A1 sequencing**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Myelodysplasia (myelomeningocele, spinal bifida)

*Pediatrics · 70 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — failure of fetal neural tube closure → anatomic anomalies + neurologic deficit · incidence **0.1–0.2%**

### Prevention - folic acid

**Risk factors** — **folate deficiency** (supplement ↓ risk **70%**) · maternal hyperthermia · maternal diabetes · **valproic acid**

**Supplement** — **folic acid**\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin D · B12 · niacin · thiamine

### Genes & lab markers

**Chromosomal** — up to **10%** of fetuses: **trisomy 13, trisomy 18**, triploidy, single-gene mutations

**Maternal AFP** — **↑ in 75%** of open spina bifida · **2nd trimester** · fetal ultrasound diagnoses in utero

### Latex allergy

**Mechanism** — **IgE-mediated type 1 hypersensitivity** → anaphylaxis · **20–70%** of patients · from repeated latex exposure · **latex-free OR** · non-spina bifida latex allergy = type 4 contact dermatitis\
✗ Wrong options: IgM · IgA · complement · T cells

### Associations

**Neurosurgical** — **Chiari II** (most common associated anomaly) · **hydrocephalus 70%** · **tethered cord**

**Other** — neurogenic bladder · pathologic fractures · scoliosis / kyphosis · hip dislocation + contractures · tibial torsion · foot deformity

### Split cord malformation & dysraphism types

| Form | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Spina bifida occulta** | Arch defect, **cord + meninges confined** |
| **Meningocele** | Sac **without** neural elements |
| **Myelomeningocele** | Sac **with neural elements** · **most common dysraphism** (TO) |
| **Rachischisis** | Neural elements **exposed**, no cover |

**Open vs closed (TO)** — open = skin defect, neural tissue exposed · closed = skin-covered, ± subcutaneous mass (lipomyelocele, lipomyelomeningocele, meningocele, myelocystocele)

| Split cord (diastematomyelia, TO) | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Type 1** | **Two dural tubes**, **rigid bony / cartilaginous septum** · more symptoms |
| **Type 2** | **One dural tube**, ± non-rigid fibrous septum, **no bony septum** |
| Associated | Scoliosis, tethered cord, **hairy tuft** |

**Exam (TO)** — gaps between spinous processes / absent bony prominences in sacral triangle → **spina bifida** · step-off → spondylolisthesis · becs de perroquet → spinal TB

![Rachischisis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4085-rachischisis.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Functional prognosis - motor level

**Level** — named by **lowest functioning level** · **functional motor level = most important predictor of outcome**\
✗ Wrong options: sensory level · hip dysplasia · hydrocephalus

| Level | Ambulation | Key motion (muscle) | Foot | Orthosis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1–L2 | **Non-ambulatory** | — | Equinovarus | **HKAFO** |
| **L3** | Marginal household · **high hip dislocation risk** | **Hip flexion + adduction** (iliopsoas, adductors) | Equinovarus | **KAFO** |
| **L4** | Household + · **key level (quads work)** | **Knee extension** (quads) · ankle DF + inversion (TA) | **Cavovarus** | **AFO** |
| **L5** | **Community** | Toe DF (EHL/EDL) · **hip extension + abduction** (gluteus med/min) | **Calcaneovalgus** | AFO |
| S1 | Normal | Ankle PF (gastroc-soleus) | — | Shoes |
| S2 | Normal | Toe PF (FHL) | — | — |
| S3–4 | Normal | Bowel + bladder | — | — |

**OB descriptions** — L1: hips ER + flexed, equinovarus, HKAFO · L3: hips adducted + flexed, **knee recurvatum**, equinovarus, KAFO

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — monitor scoliosis / kyphosis, hips, fractures · standing AP ankle → **tibiotalar valgus / talar tilt**

**MRI** — **any change in neuro exam or rapid curve progression → urgent MRI for tethered cord**

## 5 Management

### Hip dislocation management & functional level

**Cause** — paralytic: unopposed flexors + adductors · most common at **L3** · can be teratologic

**Rule** — function depends on **neurologic level, NOT hip status** · open reduction → high redislocation + complications, no functional gain · reduced hips may even hurt more

| Scenario | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Thoracic / L3 and above** | **Leave dislocated** · observation + **ROM to prevent contractures** (✗ Wrong options: Pavlik · spica (promote contractures) · open reduction) |
| **L4** | Most controversial · may consider if **unilateral** (OB) · L4 household ambulator with full ROM → **no intervention** |
| Contractures | **Contracture release** (improves ROM → ambulation) |

**Hip abduction contracture** — pelvic obliquity, scoliosis → **Ober-Yount** (proximal fascia lata + distal ITB release) if limits sitting/bracing

**Hip flexion contracture** — high lumbar / thoracic → anterior release (iliopsoas, sartorius, rectus, TFL) if **>40°**

### Knee

| Problem | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Weak quads | **KAFO** |
| Flexion contracture **>20°** | **Hamstring lengthening ± posterior capsulotomy** · older / failed → **supracondylar extension osteotomy** |
| Extension contracture | **Serial casting** to 90° flexion |
| Tibial torsion | **<5 yr** observe + orthotics · **>5 yr distal tibial derotation osteotomy** |

### Foot & ankle deformity

**Incidence** — **60–90%** (low nerve roots)

**Clubfoot** — **30%**, most common · **rigid + insensate** · serial casting first (high complication rate) → **posteromedial-lateral release at 12–18 months** · rigid clubfoot, no muscles below knee → **soft-tissue releases** for plantigrade AFO foot · releases beat transfers\
✗ Wrong options: tendon transfers (nothing to transfer) · Botox · triple

**Calcaneus / dorsiflexion (L5, sacral)** — unopposed tibialis anterior · bulky heel pad · **posterior transfer of tibialis anterior** if bracing can't get neutral\
✗ Wrong options: triple arthrodesis

**Planovalgus** — distal tibial valgus (mature) → **supramalleolar osteotomy** · hindfoot valgus → **calcaneal lengthening + TAL** · also calcaneal slide, subtalar arthrodesis, Grice · **tibiotalar valgus missed pre-op → poor surgical result**\
✗ Wrong options: equinus · calcaneovalgus · forefoot abduction

**Arthrodesis** — avoid (skin sores, Charcot) · triple only for severe deformity with **sensate** feet

![Calcaneus foot in L5 myelomeningocele](../../MCQ/attachments/q3115-a-myelomeningocele-heel-deformity.jpg)

### Spinal deformity & kyphectomy

**Scoliosis** — neurogenic or congenital · **100% with thoracic level** · rapid progression → **tethered cord** · **bracing ineffective** → **ASF + PSF with pelvic fixation** (anterior needed — dysplastic posterior elements) · high **pseudarthrosis** · **infection 15–25%** (poor soft tissue)

**Congenital kyphosis** — **10–15%** · progressive · **gibbus → recurrent skin breakdown** sitting · **kyphectomy + fusion + posterior instrumentation** · **check shunt function first** (shunt failure intra-op can kill)

**Both curves progressive** → **ASF + PSF with pelvic fixation + kyphectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: kyphectomy alone · bracing

**Multidisciplinary team** — **↓ postoperative complications**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ deformity progression · ↓ revision

![Gibbus deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4085-mps-hc-gibbus-deformity-arrow.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Pathologic fracture & neuropathic joints

**Fractures** — **disuse osteopenia** · ↑ with higher level · hip + knee, age 3–7 · **fever, warmth, erythema, swelling → mimics infection / cellulitis / osteomyelitis** → **get X-rays** · not immunocompromised · treat with short **well-padded** splint/cast · **avoid long casting** (↑ osteopenia, refracture)

**Prognosis** — untreated infants 90–100% mortality · L3 and above mostly wheelchair · L5 good community ambulation

## How it's asked

**"8-yr mid-lumbar MMC, bilateral hip dislocation — true?"** → **long-term function not associated with hip status**\
✗ Wrong options: reduction improves ambulation

**"4-yr L3, unilateral dislocation, walks with walker?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: open reduction · pelvic osteotomy

**"Newborn thoracic MMC, unstable hips?"** → **observation + ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik · spica · open reduction

**"13-yr, knee extension but no hip extension/abduction, dislocated hip?"** → **L4; no intervention**

**"6-yr, walks, strong DF, bulky heel pad, calcaneus?"** → **L5; posterior transfer of tibialis anterior**\
✗ Wrong options: triple

**"18-yr, planovalgus, distal tibia 15° valgus?"** → **supramalleolar osteotomy**

**"18-month, rigid clubfeet, nothing below knee?"** → **soft-tissue releases**\
✗ Wrong options: transfers · Botox

**"Pre-op deformity predicting poor foot surgery result?"** → **tibiotalar valgus**

**"Thoracic MMC, progressive scoliosis + kyphosis, skin ulcers?"** → **ASF + PSF with pelvic fixation + kyphectomy**

**"Benefit of multidisciplinary team?"** → **reduced postoperative complications**

**"L1 MMC, fall, febrile warm thigh?"** → **pathologic femur fracture from disuse osteopenia**\
✗ Wrong options: cellulitis · immunocompromise

**"Non-ambulatory, painless red swollen knee after PT, osteopenic tibia?"** → **myelomeningocele image**

**"Latex allergy mechanism?"** → **IgE antibodies**

**"Most important predictor of function?"** → **functional motor level**

**"Supplement ↓ neural tube defects?"** → **folic acid**

**"Most common spinal dysraphism?"** → **myelomeningocele** (TO)

**"Gaps between sacral spinous processes?"** → **spina bifida** (TO)

**"Split cord, no bony septum?"** → **split cord malformation type 2** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neurofibromatosis

*Pediatrics · 71 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — NF1 **1 in 3,000** (Orthobullets) · 1 in 4,000 (OB) · **~50% new mutations** · **most common genetic disorder from a new single-gene mutation** · neural crest origin

### Genetics & inheritance (NF1, neurofibromin)

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant**

**Gene** — **NF1 on chromosome 17q11.2** → **neurofibromin**\
✗ Wrong options: 11q17.2

**Pathway** — neurofibromin **negatively regulates Ras** → deficiency → ↑ Ras → ↑ **Ras-dependent MAPK** (osteoclast function/survival) · ↓ osteoblast activity → osteopenia

**Selumetinib** — oral **MEK1/2 inhibitor** · FDA 2020 for **symptomatic inoperable plexiform neurofibromas in children with NF1**

| Gene / protein (distractors) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Neurofibromin (NF1)** | **NF1** |
| FGFR3 | Achondroplasia |
| GNAS / Gs-α | **McCune-Albright** (coast-of-Maine spots, fibrous dysplasia, precocious puberty) |
| **SPRED1** | **Legius** (café-au-lait + freckling, **no neurofibromas**, no MPNST) |
| COMP | MED |
| Fibrillin | Marfan |
| COL2A1 | SED |
| RUNX2/CBFA1 | Cleidocranial dysplasia |
| EWS | Ewing (t11;22) |
| EXT1 | Multiple hereditary exostoses |
| p53 / Rb | Li-Fraumeni / retinoblastoma |

| Type | Feature |
|---|---|
| **NF1** (von Recklinghausen) | Most common · all orthopaedic features |
| **NF2** | **Bilateral vestibular schwannomas** · meningiomas, ependymomas · **no scoliosis** · no pseudarthrosis |
| Segmental | NF1 features in one body segment |

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnostic criteria & clinical features

**NIH 1987 — ≥2 of**

| Criterion | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Café-au-lait** | **≥6**, **>5 mm** prepubertal / **>15 mm** postpubertal · smooth "**coast of California**" border |
| **Neurofibromas** | **≥2 any type** or **1 plexiform** |
| **Freckling** | **Axillary or inguinal** (✗ Wrong options: plantar) |
| **Optic glioma** | (✗ Wrong options: glaucoma) |
| **Lisch nodules** | **≥2** iris hamartomas |
| **Osseous lesion** | **Sphenoid dysplasia** · thin long-bone cortex ± pseudarthrosis |
| **1st-degree relative** | With NF1 |

**Orthopaedic** — **anterolateral tibial bowing / pseudarthrosis** (most common presentation, more than forearm) · **forearm (radius/ulna) bowing + pseudarthrosis** · **hemihypertrophy** / limb overgrowth · scoliosis, kyphosis · **atlantoaxial instability** · verrucous hyperplasia

**NOT NF1** — **talipes equinus** · **posteromedial tibial bowing** · **pectus excavatum** (Marfan) · radial head subluxation (MHE) · glenoid retroversion (brachial plexus palsy) · ACL absence (fibular hemimelia) · DDH · **hemivertebrae**

**Crawford (588 pts)** — spinal deformity 21% · plexiform neurofibroma 25% · LLD 7.1% · congenital tibial dysplasia 5% · pectus 4.3% · hemihypertrophy 1.4%

![Café-au-lait spots](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4054-cafe-au-lait-colorado-moved.jpg)

### Neoplasia

**Plexiform neurofibroma** — **pathognomonic** · 4% (Orthobullets) · 25% (Crawford) · limb overgrowth · → **neurofibrosarcoma / MPNST**

**MPNST** — **50% occur in NF1** · enlarging painful mass · resection · 5-yr survival ~30%

**Other** — Wilms tumor · **aortic stenosis ~2%** → new fatigue/dyspnea needs urgent cardiology · hypertension · life expectancy ↓ **8–10 yr**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Spinal deformity (dystrophic scoliosis, rib penciling, dural ectasia)

**Spine** — **most common skeletal site** in NF1 (10–60%) · not in NF2

| | **Nondystrophic** | **Dystrophic** |
|---|---|---|
| Curve | Long, gentle, like **AIS** | **Short (4–6 vertebrae), sharp, angular** **kyphoscoliosis** (thoracic; thoracolumbar per OB) |
| Recognised | Later | **Earlier** |
| Bracing | Treat like AIS | **Ineffective** |
| Surgery | Like AIS | **Early ASF + PSF** (<7 yr if dystrophic) |

**X-ray (dystrophic)** — **vertebral scalloping** (posterior) · **rib penciling** · **enlarged foramina** · rotation

**Rapid progression ("modulation")** — **penciling of ≥3 ribs** = single strongest predictor (87% progress)\
✗ Wrong options: scalloping · curve >50° · rotation

**MRI before surgery** — always · **dural ectasia** + **dumbbell lesion** (intraforaminal neurofibroma) · paraspinal masses separate from AIS\
✗ Wrong options: syrinx · hemivertebra · diastematomyelia

**Spinal manifestations** — dural ectasia, dumbbell lesions, scalloping\
✗ Wrong options: **hemivertebrae** (TO)

**Fusion** — **PSF alone 40% pseudarthrosis** · **ASF + PSF 10%** · ± repeat iliac crest graft at 6 months · anterior fusion also prevents crankshaft

**Cervical kyphosis** — **anterior + posterior fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: in situ posterior · halo + posterior · collar

![Dystrophic scoliosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4054-scoliosis-courtesy-of-miller-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Congenital pseudarthrosis of tibia

**Association** — **50% of anterolateral bowing have NF1** (55% OB) · **10% of NF1** have it · pseudarthrosis = NF1 (✗ Wrong options: NF2) · dysplastic **constricted diaphysis** (✗ Wrong options: abuse · OI II · osteopetrosis)

**Pathology** — hamartomatous fibrous tissue in pseudarthrosis · cortical thinning → fracture in first year of walking

| Tibial bowing | Association |
|---|---|
| **Anterolateral** | **NF1 / congenital pseudarthrosis** |
| **Anteromedial** | **Fibular hemimelia**, absent lateral rays |
| **Posteromedial** | Intrauterine packaging · **calcaneovalgus** · LLD |

```
Anterolateral tibial bowing (NF1)
├─ No fracture / pseudarthrosis → total contact (clamshell) orthosis
│      (no remodeling expected; osteotomy contraindicated)
├─ Fracture / pseudarthrosis → resect + bone graft + fixation (IM rod / free fibula / Ilizarov)
└─ 3 failed surgeries → Syme amputation (preferred over BKA)
```

**Infant with fractured pseudarthrosis** → **genetics referral** + brace\
✗ Wrong options: abuse workup · ORIF first · cast if not walking

**IM rod (Charnley-Williams)** — resect, antegrade through site then retrograde through heel · **<4 yr → into calcaneus** · **5–10 yr → into talus** · rod pushed proximal ~2 yr later to free ankle

**Free fibula** — usually **contralateral** (ipsilateral abnormal) · **Ilizarov** alternative

**Failed bracing/casting with pseudarthrosis** → **resection + bone graft + IM nailing**\
✗ Wrong options: IM nail alone (hamartoma left) · bone stimulator

**Amputation** — after **3 failed surgeries** · **Syme** (calf atrophic and scarred)

![Anterolateral bowing — congenital pseudarthrosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4054-congenital-pseudo.jpg)

### Forearm pseudarthrosis

**Features** — bowing, **medullary canal obliterated in distal third**, little trauma · **~50% have NF** · early excision of fibrous tissue + bone replacement (free vascularised fibula)

**Forearm X-ray distractors** — radial club hand (VACTERL, Fanconi, Holt-Oram) · congenital radial head dislocation · Madelung (Leri-Weill) · radioulnar synostosis (Apert, Carpenter, arthrogryposis, Klinefelter)

## X vs Y

| | **NF1 café-au-lait** | **McCune-Albright** |
|---|---|---|
| Border | **Smooth — coast of California** | **Rough — coast of Maine** |
| Gene | NF1 | GNAS |

## How it's asked

**"NF1 criteria — all EXCEPT?"** → **plantar freckling**

**"3-yr radial bowing + Lisch nodules?"** → **NF1**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrous dysplasia · OI · Ollier

**"NF1 features — EXCEPT?"** → **talipes equinus**

**"Associated with NF — EXCEPT?"** → **posteromedial tibial bowing**

**"4-yr anterolateral bowing — exam finding NOT expected?"** → **pectus excavatum**

**"Healed-but-dysplastic tibia, axillary freckles — also expect?"** → **multiple café-au-lait spots**\
✗ Wrong options: posteromedial bowing · glaucoma

**"Untreated refugee: tibial pseudarthrosis + dystrophic scoliosis — also?"** → **hemihypertrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: DDH · ACL absence

**"NF1 — radiograph most associated?"** → **congenital forearm pseudarthrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: radial club hand · Madelung

**"Inheritance + mutation?"** → **AD; NF1 gene → neurofibromin**\
✗ Wrong options: FGFR3 · Gs-α

**"Plexiform neurofibroma of hand — gene?"** → **neurofibromin**\
✗ Wrong options: COMP · RUNX2

**"Popliteal mass + café-au-lait — gene?"** → **NF1**\
✗ Wrong options: EXT1 · EWS

**"Freckling + iris hamartomas — inheritance + leg?"** → **AD; anterolateral bowing**

**"Infant, café-au-lait, scoliosis-operated mother — genetics?"** → **AD NF1 on 17q11.2**

**"Enlarging thigh mass, parent with neurofibromas, MPNST?"** → **NF1**\
✗ Wrong options: Legius · McCune-Albright · NF2

**"Pseudarthrosis seen in?"** → **NF1** (TO)

**"3-yr anterolateral bowing, no fracture?"** → **total contact orthosis**\
✗ Wrong options: IM nail · free fibula

**"9 months KAFO + cast, now pseudarthrosis?"** → **resection + bone graft + fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: stimulator · Syme

**"2-yr constricted tibial diaphysis fracture?"** → **neurofibromatosis**\
✗ Wrong options: abuse

**"6-month-old fractured tibial pseudarthrosis — with brace?"** → **genetics referral**\
✗ Wrong options: abuse workup

**"6-yr NF1, failed brace + cast?"** → **resection + bone graft + IM nailing**\
✗ Wrong options: IM nail alone

**"NF spinal manifestations — EXCEPT?"** → **hemivertebrae** (TO)

**"True about NF scoliosis?"** → **rib penciling predicts rapid progression**\
✗ Wrong options: NF2 · dystrophic like AIS

**"Pre-op MRI must assess?"** → **dural ectasia**\
✗ Wrong options: syrinx · diastematomyelia

**"15-yr, axillary freckling, short sharp curve?"** → **dystrophic scoliosis in NF1**\
✗ Wrong options: NF2 · Klippel-Feil

**"10-yr Risser 0, 50° short thoracic curve, progressing?"** → **combined anterior + posterior fusion with instrumentation**\
✗ Wrong options: TLSO · in situ PSF

**"Sign of impending modulation?"** → **penciling of ≥3 ribs**

**"7-yr NF, severe cervical kyphosis?"** → **anterior + posterior fusion**

**"13-yr, axillary freckles, AIS-like curve?"** → **nondystrophic scoliosis in NF1**

**"56-yr NF1, fatigue + dyspnea — urgent?"** → **aortic stenosis**\
✗ Wrong options: MPNST · SMA syndrome

**"Selumetinib indicated for?"** → **inoperable plexiform neurofibromas in NF1 children**\
✗ Wrong options: CML (imatinib) · PVNS (pexidartinib) · GCT (denosumab) · desmoid (tamoxifen)
