# Nursemaid's Elbow

*Pediatrics · 72 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & recurrence

**Definition** — radial head subluxation / **annular ligament interposed between radial head and capitellum**

**Age** — **1–4 yr**, average **28 months** (Orthobullets) · peak **2–3 yr** (TO) · TO text also says peaks in first 2 yr · **rare after 5**\
✗ Wrong options: "peak 3–5 yr" · "common in infants"

**Why rare after 5** — thicker, stronger distal annular ligament attachment (Orthobullets) · radial head ossified (TO)

**Sex / side** — **slightly more common in girls** (✓ TO true statement) · left arm (TO)

**Mechanism** — **sudden longitudinal traction on hand, elbow EXTENDED + forearm PRONATED** (✗ Wrong options: supinated) · lifting / swinging by the hand · occasionally a fall · annular ligament tears or slips over radial head (✗ Wrong options: LCL tear)

**Recurrence** — **up to 5%** (TO true statement) · 5–39% reported (TO) · esp. **<3 yr** · capsule stays stretched for months\
✗ Wrong options: "recurrence is rare"

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **click** felt by the person pulling · child cries, **refuses to use arm**

**Exam** — **elbow slightly flexed, forearm pronated** · lateral elbow tenderness · full flexion/extension · **pain / restricted supination**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-rays** — **not needed** with classic history + age ≤5 + typical exam · when taken: **normal** · **25%** radiocapitellar line slightly lateral to capitellar centre

**Ultrasound** — when mechanism unclear or exam inconclusive · ↑ echo-negative area between capitellum + radial head · **sensitivity 64.9%, specificity 100%** · no radiation

**Diagnosis of exclusion** — supracondylar, olecranon, radial neck, lateral condyle fractures · contusion · septic arthritis · congenital radial head dislocation · synostosis

![Annular ligament interposed between radial head and capitellum](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4012-nursemaid-elbow.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Reduction - supination & flexion

**Rule** — exclude fracture before manipulating

**Supination technique** — supinate forearm → **flex elbow** (maximally per Orthobullets · 60–90° OB · 90° TO) · thumb on radial head → **click**

**Hyperpronation technique** — hyperpronate forearm in flexion

**After reduction** — child uses arm within minutes · **no immobilization after first episode**\
✗ Wrong options: long arm cast (stiffness) · pinning · MRI · observation · longitudinal traction

**Recurrent** — **cast in flexion + neutral or supination** (lets annular ligament heal)

**Open reduction** — rare: chronic irreducible symptomatic subluxation

![Supination–flexion reduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4012-nursemaid-flexion-supination.jpg)

### Misfiled TO – Weil osteotomy

**Weil osteotomy** — distal oblique metatarsal head osteotomy (dorsal-distal → proximal-plantar, ~30°, screw) for **metatarsalgia**\
✗ Wrong options: Perthes · SCFE · genu valgum

**Indications** — long / plantarflexed metatarsal · transverse-plane MTP deformity · MTP subluxation/dislocation · recalcitrant metatarsalgia

**Complications** — **dorsiflexed (floating) toe** · painful/↓ MTP dorsiflexion · PIP contracture

## How it's asked

**"2-yr, elbow flexed + pronated, normal X-rays?"** → **supination and flexion reduction maneuver**\
✗ Wrong options: + long arm cast · CRPP · MRI

**"3-yr, arm pulled crossing street, guarding?"** → **closed reduction via supination and flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration · longitudinal traction · splint

**"Pulled elbow — true?"** → **recurrence in 5% of cases** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: peak 3–5 yr · supinated forearm · LCL tear

**"Nursemaid's elbow — true?"** → **more common in girls** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: infants · recurrence rare

**"Weil osteotomy used for?"** → **metatarsalgia** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Obstetric Brachial Plexopathy (Erb's, Klumpke's Palsy)

*Pediatrics · 73 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — birth stretch injury of brachial plexus, usually difficult vaginal delivery · rare after C-section

**Incidence** — **1–4 per 1,000** (Orthobullets) · 0.9/1,000 · 0.38–1.56/1,000 (OB) · 0.1–0.4% (TO) · falling with better obstetric care · often right-sided or bilateral

### Risk factors

**Greatest risk** — **shoulder dystocia**\
✗ Wrong options: macrosomia · gestational diabetes · race

**Others** — **macrosomia** (LGA) · multiparous · difficult presentation · **forceps** · **breech** (→ lower plexus) · **prolonged labor** · maternal diabetes · induced labor · prior affected child · **50% have no risk factor**

### Anatomy & injury patterns (Erb upper trunk)

| Narakas group | Roots | Deficit | Frequency (TO) | Spontaneous recovery (TO) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I — Erb-Duchenne** | **C5–C6** | **Deltoid + biceps** paralysed · wrist/digit flex/ext intact | **46%** (most common) | **>80%** |
| II — Intermediate / extended Erb | **C5–C7** | + **wrist and digit extension** (wrist drop) | 30% | ~60% |
| III — Total | C5–T1 | **Flail**, no Horner | 20% | Shoulder/elbow 30–50% |
| IV — Total + Horner | C5–T1 | Flail + **Horner** | — | **Worst** |
| Klumpke | C8–T1 | Claw hand | **<1%** (TO) · 2–3% (TO) | Poor |

**Erb frequency elsewhere** — 50–60% (TO) · >80% (OB) — read the options

**OB212897 variant** — most common injury = deltoid + biceps paralysis **+ weak wrist/digit extension** (C6) · isolated deltoid + biceps = C5 only

| Root | Muscles (OB618) |
|---|---|
| **C5** | **Deltoid**, **biceps/brachialis** (C5–6), ECRL/ECRB (C5–6) |
| C6–7 | Triceps |
| C7–8 | FDP |
| T1 | Interossei |
| CN XI | Trapezius |

**Elbow flexion loss** → **upper trunk** (musculocutaneous fibres from C6)\
✗ Wrong options: lateral cord · posterior cord · C4

![Waiter's tip (Erb palsy)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4117-erb2.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Erb** — **lateral flexion of head away + depression of ipsilateral shoulder** (shoulder dystocia)

**Klumpke** — **traction on abducted arm** / arm presentation (breech)

**Total** — stretch, rupture + avulsion

## 3 Presentation

**Erb (waiter's tip)** — shoulder **adducted + internally rotated**, elbow **extended**, forearm **pronated**, wrist flexed · C5: axillary (deltoid, teres minor), suprascapular (SS, IS), musculocutaneous (biceps, brachialis) · C6: radial (brachioradialis, supinator)

**Klumpke** — **claw hand**: wrist extended (unopposed extensors), MCP hyperextended, IP flexed · intrinsics (ulnar + median) lost · often **preganglionic + Horner**

**Total** — flaccid arm, motor + sensory loss · **CXR for hemidiaphragm (phrenic)**

**Exam tools** — Moro, asymmetric tonic neck, Vojta reflexes · pain on gentle shaking of flail arm → **pseudoparalysis** (infection / fracture)

**Associated** — clavicle + humerus fractures (no prognostic value) · torticollis

![Klumpke claw hand](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4117-klumpke.jpg)

### Assessment tools

| Tool | Use |
|---|---|
| **Toronto Test Score** | Surgical indications, post-reconstruction assessment |
| **Hospital for Sick Children Active Movement Scale** | Whole plexus, **15 movements** · must reach **4 (full ROM gravity eliminated)** before higher scores |
| **(Modified) Mallet** | **Most common outcome measure** · weighted to ER · Abzug added hand-to-navel (IR) |
| Wechsler (WISC-IV) | Not used — neuropsychological IQ test (TO) |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — clavicle/humerus fractures · little use in infant shoulder (unossified) · **axillary view** in older child for subluxation

**Ultrasound** — glenohumeral subluxation/dislocation in infants

**Myelography / CT myelography / MRI** — **root avulsion (preganglionic) vs extraforaminal rupture** · MRI may show C6 avulsion

**EMG/NCV** — poor reliability, **underestimates** severity · baseline at 4 wk, repeat at 3 months if no recovery (TO)

**Preganglionic (root avulsion) signs**

| Sign | Nerve |
|---|---|
| **Horner** (ptosis, miosis, anhidrosis) | Sympathetic chain / T1 |
| **Elevated hemidiaphragm** | Phrenic |
| **Winged scapula** | Long thoracic |
| **Absent rhomboids** | Dorsal scapular |
| Absent rotator cuff | Suprascapular |
| Absent latissimus | Thoracodorsal |

## 5 Management

```
Obstetric brachial plexus palsy
├─ All → observation + daily passive ROM by parents (first 3 months)
├─ Antigravity biceps by 3 months → continue therapy, expect full recovery
├─ No biceps by 3 months, postganglionic → neuroma excision + sural graft (3–9 months)
└─ Preganglionic (Horner, phrenic, winging, rhomboids) → nerve transfer at ~3 months
```

### Prognosis (biceps recovery by 3 months, Horner)

**Overall** — **90% recover** without surgery · recovery up to **2 yr** · most with elbow flexion by 3 months normal by 18 months

**Good** — **Erb palsy** (absent shoulder abduction + ER, intact wrist/hand = best) · **antigravity biceps + deltoid by 3 months** · **early biceps twitch** · elbow flexion by 3 months → recovery up to 100% · partial antigravity strength in first 2 months

**Key indicator** — **active elbow flexion (biceps, musculocutaneous) at 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: shoulder abduction · ER · elbow extension · axillary at 1 yr

**Poor** — **no biceps by 3 months** · **preganglionic injury (worst)** · **Horner → <10% recover** motor function · **C7 involvement** · **Klumpke** · total plexus\
✗ Wrong options: 30–40%

**No prognostic value** — clavicle fracture · age at diagnosis · head circumference

### Indications for microsurgery / nerve transfer

| Lesion | Surgery | Timing |
|---|---|---|
| **Postganglionic** (rupture, neuroma-in-continuity) | **Neuroma excision + sural nerve grafting** (shorter grafts <10 cm better · no direct repair) | **3–9 months** (Gilbert: biceps < MRC 3 at 3 months → surgery at 4–6 months, TO) |
| **Preganglionic** (avulsion) | **Nerve transfer / neurotization** (grafting impossible) | **~3 months** (or less) |
| Global palsy + Horner | Microsurgery | ~3 months (TO) · flail + Horner at 1 month (OB) |

**Nerve transfer** — fascicles from one nerve into another nerve's motor branch · **neurotization** = fascicles directly into neuromuscular junction

**Transfers** — **ulnar fascicle → biceps branch (Oberlin)** + **median fascicle → brachialis branch** · accessory → suprascapular (abduction) · intercostals → musculocutaneous (elbow flexion) · triceps (long head) / thoracodorsal → axillary · contralateral C7 in total avulsion

**Most important activity for timing surgery** — **return of elbow flexion** (TO)

**13-yr traumatic plexus injury, 5 months, no Horner, Tinel at clavicle** → **exploration + nerve grafting** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: neurotization (preganglionic only) · tendon transfer (later)

**Urgent sub-specialist referral at 7 weeks** → **Horner (miosis, anhidrosis)**\
✗ Wrong options: absent biceps (normal at this age) · Moro present

### Glenohumeral dysplasia & IR contracture

**Cause** — strong internal rotators vs weak external rotators → **IR contracture** (most common problem needing treatment) → **glenohumeral dysplasia in 70%** · by age 2

**Deformity** — **glenoid hypoplasia + RETROVERSION** · **posterior humeral head subluxation** · **humeral head flattening** · pseudoglenoid ·\
✗ Wrong options: anteversion · anterior-superior escape · anterior subluxation

**Zancolli (TO)** — most common: **IR-adduction contracture with joint deformity + posterior subluxation/dislocation** (>60%)

**Putti sign** — prominent scapula on adduction

**Before shoulder reconstruction** — **MRI of shoulder**\
✗ Wrong options: EMG · brain MRI

| Waters type | Feature | Treatment (OB5599) |
|---|---|---|
| I | <5° retroversion difference | — |
| II | >5° retroversion difference | **Latissimus + teres major transfer** |
| III | Posterior subluxation (<35% anterior to scapular spine axis) | Transfer ± open/arthroscopic reduction + anterior release |
| IV | **False glenoid** | Reduction + release, or **humeral derotation osteotomy** |
| V | Flattened head, progressive / complete dislocation | **Humeral derotation osteotomy** |
| VI | Infantile posterior dislocation | — |
| VII | Proximal humeral growth arrest | — |

![Waters classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4117-waters-classification.jpg)

### Secondary reconstruction (tendon transfer, osteotomy, elbow contracture)

**Hoffer (latissimus + teres major → posterior rotator cuff)** — IR contracture / ER weakness **without dysplasia** (congruent joint) · ± pectoralis major / subscapularis lengthening · arthroscopic release

**Hoffer contraindications** — **convex or absent glenoid** · **complete posterior dislocation** · **humeral head flattening** · latissimus/teres major <4/5 · deltoid <2/5 ·\
✗ Wrong options: IR contracture (that's the indication) · no subscapularis function · positive hornblower

**Wickstrom humeral derotation osteotomy** — IR contracture / ER weakness **with dysplasia** (Waters IV–V; grade V TO) \
✗ Wrong options: glenoid osteotomy

**Shoulder arthrodesis** — non-functional deltoid, good hand

| Elbow flexion contracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<40°** (Orthobullets) · <35° (OB) | **Serial nighttime extension splinting** (prevents progression, doesn't correct) |
| **>40°** | **Serial extension casting** |
| Severe persistent | Anterior capsular release + biceps/brachialis lengthening (recurs) |

**Cause of elbow contracture** — persistent **triceps (C7) weakness** vs biceps (C5–6)

**Forearm supination contracture** — unopposed biceps (Klumpke / mixed) · **biceps rerouting** (supinator → pronator) if passive pronation intact · + **forearm osteotomy** if passive pronation limited · ± interosseous membrane release (TO)

**Wrist / hand transfers** — wrist drop: **PT → ECRB** · finger extension: **FCR or FCU → EDC** · thumb abduction: **EIP → APB** · force ∝ cross-section, amplitude ∝ length

## 6 Complications

**Phrenic palsy** — persistent → **diaphragm plication**

**Shoulder tendon transfers** — radial + axillary nerve palsy

## X vs Y

| | **Preganglionic** | **Postganglionic** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | Root avulsed from cord (proximal to DRG) | Distal to DRG (rupture, neuroma) |
| Signs | Horner, phrenic, winging, rhomboids | — |
| Recovery | **None spontaneous** | Possible |
| Surgery | **Nerve transfer ~3 months** | **Graft 3–9 months** |

| | **Erb** | **Klumpke** |
|---|---|---|
| Roots | C5–C6 | C8–T1 |
| Posture | Waiter's tip | Claw hand |
| Mechanism | Head-shoulder distraction | Abducted arm traction |
| Prognosis | **Best** | Poor, Horner |

## How it's asked

**"Most common root combination?"** → **C5–C6** (TO)

**"Most common pattern in Erb palsy?"** → **C5–C6** (TO)

**"C5 plexopathy — muscle affected?"** → **biceps**\
✗ Wrong options: trapezius · triceps · interossei

**"Waiter's tip — true?"** → **most common pattern**\
✗ Wrong options: Horner · 75% need surgery · Klumpke

**"Can't flex elbow at 4 months — structure?"** → **upper trunk**

**"Most common injury — deficits?"** → **deltoid + biceps paralysis, weak wrist and digit extension**

**"Most common presentation of the limb?"** → **pronated forearm and flexed wrist**\
✗ Wrong options: claw · benediction

**"Greatest risk factor?"** → **shoulder dystocia**

**"Poor prognosis for spontaneous recovery?"** → **no biceps function by 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: clavicle fracture · postganglionic

**"Total palsy + Horner — recovery by 3 months?"** → **<10%**

**"Best functional outcome?"** → **absent shoulder abduction + ER, intact wrist/digits**

**"Flail at birth, elbow flexion at 10 wk — at 18 months?"** → **normal exam**

**"Passive stretching appropriate for?"** → **1-month-old with partial antigravity biceps**\
✗ Wrong options: winging + elevated diaphragm · post-transfer

**"Poorest prognosis at 2 months?"** → **persistent ptosis, miosis, anhidrosis**

**"Favorable sign in flail arm?"** → **biceps return by 3 months**

**"Finding at 3 months predicting full recovery?"** → **active elbow flexion**

**"Muscle + timeline for good prognosis?"** → **musculocutaneous; 3 months**

**"True about outcomes?"** → **full recovery possible if biceps + deltoid antigravity by 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: twitch unfavourable · 50% resolve

**"13-yr, RTA, 5 months, no Horner?"** → **exploration and nerve grafting** (TO)

**"Determines surgical timing?"** → **return of elbow flexion** (TO)

**"Preganglionic Erb's — procedure?"** → **nerve transfer at 3 months** (TO)

**"4-month-old, winging, rhomboid atrophy, elevated diaphragm, flail?"** → **median/ulnar fascicles → biceps + brachialis branches** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: intercostal graft after neuroma excision

**"3-month-old, winging, no rhomboids, cuff, latissimus?"** → **nerve transfer to musculocutaneous branches**\
✗ Wrong options: sural graft · latissimus transfer

**"3-month-old, MRI C6 avulsion, no elbow flexion?"** → **nerve transfer(s)**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · graft C6

**"7-week-old — needs early surgical consideration?"** → **miosis + anhidrosis**

**"Zancolli — most common shoulder sequela?"** → **IR-adduction contracture with posterior subluxation** (TO)

**"Most common long-term sequel?"** → **glenoid hypoplasia with retroversion** (TO)

**"Untreated IR contracture — LEAST likely?"** → **anterior-superior escape**

**"Most common long-term consequence — image?"** → **posterior subluxation on dysplastic retroverted glenoid**\
✗ Wrong options: cleidocranial · winging

**"Before shoulder reconstruction?"** → **MRI of shoulder**

**"Persistent imbalance — shoulder changes?"** → **retroversion, posterior subluxation, flat head, IR contracture**

**"Ptosis + IR contracture at 3 months — long term?"** → **glenohumeral dysplasia**

**"Untreated Erb — dysplasia pattern?"** → **glenoid retroversion, posterior subluxation**

**"Congruent joint — procedure?"** → **latissimus dorsi + teres major transfer to rotator cuff** (TO)

**"Contraindication to Hoffer?"** → **convex glenoid**

**"Relative contraindication EXCEPT?"** → **IR contracture**

**"6-yr, Putti sign, posterior subluxation on MRI?"** → **humeral osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: glenoid osteotomy · isolated transfer

**"6-yr, elbow 35–130°?"** → **serial nighttime extension splinting**\
✗ Wrong options: biceps tenotomy · capsular release

**"NOT an OBPP assessment tool?"** → **Wechsler Scale for Children-IV** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Olecranon Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 74 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **uncommon** · <5% of pediatric fractures (US) · peak **5–10 yr**

**Mechanism**

| Mechanism | Result |
|---|---|
| **FOOSH, elbow FLEXED** (most common) | Triceps + brachialis tension → **transverse** fracture |
| FOOSH, elbow extended | Varus/valgus → **longitudinal** lines · varus → **radial head dislocation** · valgus → **radial neck fracture** |
| Direct blow (least common) | Shear → anterior tension failure, distal fragment anterior, posterior periosteum intact |

**Location** — **metaphyseal** (most common) · physeal · apophyseal (intra- or extra-articular)

### Association with osteogenesis imperfecta

**OI** — **olecranon (avulsion) fracture after trivial trauma = highly suspicious** ("pathognomonic" per OB) for **OI**\
✗ Wrong options: NF · abuse · ABC

### Ossification

**Olecranon apophysis** — appears **9 yr** · fuses **15–17 yr** · fuses **anterior → posterior** · **partial closure mimics fracture**

**Age 5 girl** — capitellum, radial head, medial epicondyle ossifying · trochlea, olecranon, lateral epicondyle still cartilaginous → any olecranon disruption = fracture

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — posterior swelling · pain · **can't actively extend elbow**

**Exam** — palpable gap / crepitus · **gapping → posterior periosteum torn → unstable** · abrasion suggests direct blow

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral

**Physis vs fracture** — physis is **oblique**; fracture usually **transverse** · **patella cubiti** (secondary ossification centre) ≠ fracture

**Look for** — radial neck, lateral condyle, distal radius injuries

![Oblique apophysis vs transverse fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4010-lateral-x-ray-of-the-elbow-showing-an-oblique-orientation-to.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment - tension band

| Fracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Stress fracture / apophysitis (throwers) | **NSAIDs, rest, immobilise, avoid resisted extension** |
| **Minimally displaced**, extensor mechanism intact | Long arm splint/cast **3–4 wk** · repeat X-ray at **7 days** |
| **Displaced** · lost posterior periosteum · comminuted | **ORIF** |

**Fixation**

| Option | Note |
|---|---|
| **Tension band wiring** (AO, axial K-wires) | ✓ standard for displaced · **wires stronger** for high loads · **high hardware removal** rate |
| Tension band suture (PDS / FiberWire) ± oblique lag screw | Metaphyseal fractures |
| Plate + screws | **Comminuted** with partly fused centres / near maturity |
| Axial screw ± wire | Risk of physeal disruption with IM lag screw |

**Never in children** — ESIN · CRPP · casting a displaced fracture · **fragment excision + triceps advancement** (elderly, low-demand only)

![Transverse olecranon fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4010-olecranon-transverse-fx.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**List** — nonunion · delayed union · compartment syndrome · **ulnar neurapraxia** (pseudarthrosis, poor fixation) · loss of reduction · stiffness

## How it's asked

**"Olecranon fracture after minimal trauma — suspect?"** → **osteogenesis imperfecta**\
✗ Wrong options: abuse · NF

**"5-yr, displaced olecranon fracture?"** → **ORIF with tension band**\
✗ Wrong options: ESIN · cast · plate

**"10-yr skateboard FOOSH, displaced?"** → **tension band construct with wires**\
✗ Wrong options: IM lag screw (physis) · CRPP · excision + triceps advancement

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteogenesis Imperfecta

*Pediatrics · 75 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Genetics & type I collagen defect

**Type I collagen** — main structural protein of bone, skin, tendon, dentin, sclera · triple helix = **2 α1 chains (COL1A1) + 1 α2 chain (COL1A2)** · **glycine every 3rd residue** allows helix

**Mutation** — **COL1A1 / COL1A2** in **90%** (Orthobullets) · **85%** (OB) · **missense glycine substitution** in procollagen → abnormal cross-linking · **dominant-negative** effect → severe phenotype\
✗ Wrong options: nonsense

**Pathophysiology** — **↓ collagen secretion** + **abnormal collagen** → **insufficient osteoid** by physeal + **periosteal osteoblasts** → **bone can't remodel**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ secretion · "uninterrupted remodeling"

**Quantitative vs qualitative** — **quantitative (half normal amount, haploinsufficiency) → mild (type I)** · **qualitative (glycine substitution) → severe (II, III, IV)**

**Inheritance** — **AD most common** · AR forms (VI, VII, CRTAP / LEPRE1 — severe/lethal, no primary collagen defect) · **not X-linked recessive** · AR forms more severe

| Defect (distractors) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Glycine substitution in procollagen** | **OI** |
| Gsα | Fibrous dysplasia |
| Vitamin C (hydroxylation) | Scurvy |
| Sulfate transporter | Diastrophic dysplasia |
| Homogentisic acid oxidase | Alkaptonuria |
| Osteoclast (carbonic anhydrase / M-CSF) | Osteopetrosis |
| Activating FGFR3 | Achondroplasia |
| Type V collagen (basement membrane) | Ehlers-Danlos (OB) |
| N-Ac-Gal-6-sulfate sulfatase | Morquio |
| EXT (prehypertrophic chondrocytes) | MHE |
| COMP / type II collagen | Pseudoachondroplasia / MED, SED |

### Sillence classification

| Type | Inheritance | Collagen | Sclera | Features |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | AD | **Quantitative** | **Blue** | **Mildest, most common** · preschool (tarda) · **hearing loss 50%** · A (no DI) / B (DI) |
| **II** | AD (Orthobullets, TO) · AR (OB) | Qualitative | Blue | **Perinatal lethal** |
| **III** | AD (Orthobullets, TO) · AR (OB) | Qualitative | **Normal** | **Most severe survivable** · fractures at birth · progressive short stature |
| **IV** | AD | Qualitative | **Normal** | **Moderate** · bowing, vertebral fractures · hearing normal · A/B by teeth |
| **V** | AD | **No collagen defect** (IFITM5) | — | **Congenital anterolateral radial head dislocation** · **hypertrophic callus** · **IOM ossification** (forearm, leg) |
| **VI** | AR | No collagen defect (SERPINF1) | — | Like IV · ↑ ALP · fish-scale lamellae · responds poorly to bisphosphonates → denosumab (TO) |
| **VII** | AR | No collagen defect (CRTAP) | — | Rhizomelia, coxa vara · severe |

**90%** fall into types I + IV · TO adds VIII (LEPRE1), IX (PPIB), X (SERPINH1), XI (FKBP10, Bruck), XV (WNT1)

**Recurrent fractures + hearing loss in a child** → **type I** (TO) · hearing loss 50–92%, rare in type IV

## 3 Presentation

**Orthopaedic** — fragility fractures (heal normally, **don't remodel** → bowing, **saber shin**) · ligamentous laxity · short stature · scoliosis · **codfish vertebrae** · **basilar invagination** · **coxa vara (10%)** · congenital radial head dislocation

**Olecranon apophyseal avulsion** — **most common first presenting sign** · minor trauma (e.g. pushing a sibling) · **bilateral** → work up OI · high contralateral risk · ORIF if displaced\
✗ Wrong options: abuse · Noonan · vitamin D

**Non-orthopaedic** — **blue sclera** · triangular facies · **hearing loss** (50% adults; conductive, sensorineural, mixed) · **dentinogenesis imperfecta** (brown/blue opalescent teeth, primary > secondary) · **wormian bones** · **hypermetabolism → malignant hyperthermia risk** · thin skin, bruising · MVP, aortic regurgitation

**Fracture course** — fewer with age, usually stop after puberty

![Wormian bones](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4102-wormian.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Radiographic recognition

**X-ray** — **thin cortices** · generalized osteopenia · **saber shins** / bowing · **wormian bones** on skull · coxa vara

**Bisphosphonate zebra lines** — **dense parallel horizontal metaphyseal bands** (growth arrest lines) after **8–10 wk** of cyclical therapy · migrate to diaphysis, vanish after physeal closure\
✗ Wrong options: from growth hormone (TO false statement)

**Diagnosis** — **family history + clinical + radiographic** · no commercial test · labs normal (mildly ↑ ALP) · **skin biopsy / fibroblast culture** for collagen (diagnostic per TO; positive in 80% of type IV) · iliac crest biopsy · **prenatal PCR on chorionic villi at 8–12 wk** (TO)

**Histology** — ↑ Haversian canal + lacunar diameter · replicated cement lines · ↑ osteoblasts + osteoclasts · ↓ trabeculae · thin cortex · woven bone, crowded osteocytes (TO)

![Bisphosphonate growth arrest lines](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4102-growth-arrest-lines-associated-with-long-term-bisphosphonate.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Bisphosphonates

**Indication** — most OI · ↓ fracture rate, ↓ pain (1–6 wk), ↑ ambulation, ↑ lumbar BMD

**Mechanism** — **inhibit osteoclast resorption** (N-bisphosphonate → **FPP synthase** → osteoclast apoptosis) · endosteal resorption ↓ while periosteal formation continues → **cortex thickens**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ mineralization · better collagen · ↑ turnover · RANKL antibody

**Effect** — cortical diameter **↑88%** · cancellous volume **↑46%** · **no effect on scoliosis**

**Pamidronate** — 1 mg/kg/day × 3 days every 2–4 months · zoledronic acid every 6 months (TO)

**IM rodding** — **bisphosphonate-free period** around surgery (impairs **osteotomy** healing >> fracture healing)

**Others** — growth hormone (adjunct) · BMT (some success) · denosumab (type VI, refractory) · teriparatide **adults only** (osteosarcoma risk) · early bracing to limit deformity

### Fracture management - intramedullary rodding

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<2 yr** | Treat as non-OI child (closed) |
| **>2 yr** | **Telescoping rods** (Bailey-Dubow, Sheffield, Fassier-Duval) — grow with child · **preferred** (TO) |
| Deformity beyond tolerance after reduction | **Load-sharing IM device** |
| **Severe bowing** | **Sofield-Miller (shish-kebab / "seekh kebab") multiple osteotomies + IM rod** |

**IM rod over plate** — plates → **stress shielding + periprosthetic fracture** · OI **not** at higher nonunion risk

**Teen forearm (14–15 yr)** — >10° won't remodel → **open reduction + IM rod**\
✗ Wrong options: plate · cast · realignment osteotomy (that's for chronic deformity)

**Non-telescopic** — Rush, Williams rods

![Sofield-Miller shish-kebab osteotomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4102-photo-shishkebab-osteotomy-moved.jpg)

### Spine (basilar invagination, scoliosis)

**Scoliosis** — curves early + progress · VC falls to **40% at 60°**

| Curve | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<45°** | Observe |
| **>45° mild** OI | **PSF** |
| **>35° severe** OI (thin bones) | **PSF early** |
| Bracing | **Ineffective, not recommended** (rib fragility) |

**PSF** — allograft (little autograft) · large blood loss · **ASF only in very young** (crankshaft) · no anterior approach (pulmonary) · not telescoping rods (long bones)

**Basilar invagination** — odontoid into foramen magnum · **severe forms** · 3rd–4th decade (teens possible) · **apnea, altered consciousness, ataxia, myelopathy** (finger escape, Hoffmann) · → brainstem compression, hydrocephalus, syrinx · **transoral decompression + posterior fusion** · also Klippel-Feil, achondroplasia, Morquio, SED\
✗ Wrong options: AAI (Down)

## X vs Y

| | **OI** | **Non-accidental trauma** |
|---|---|---|
| Sclera / teeth | Blue / DI | Normal |
| Family history | Fractures | — |
| Signature fracture | **Olecranon apophysis** | Metaphyseal corner, posterior ribs |
| Bones | Osteopenic, wormian | Normal |

## How it's asked

**"Skin biopsy helps diagnose?"** → **osteogenesis imperfecta** (TO)

**"Recurrent fractures + hearing loss in a child?"** → **OI type I** (TO)

**"Severe COL1A1 phenotype vs type I — mechanism?"** → **glycine missense, dominant-negative** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: haploinsufficiency

**"COL1A1/2 mutation — EXCEPT?"** → **webbed neck**

**"Normal sclera, dentinogenesis imperfecta, fractures?"** → **qualitative type I collagen defect (type IV)**

**"Gene causing OI?"** → **COL1A1/COL1A2**\
✗ Wrong options: FGFR3 · COMP

**"Glycine substitution in procollagen?"** → **OI**\
✗ Wrong options: scurvy · diastrophic

**"COL1A1/2 mutations cause?"** → **OI**\
✗ Wrong options: osteopetrosis · MED

**"FALSE about OI?"** → **X-linked recessive inheritance**

**"11-yr olecranon apophysis fracture, blue sclera, normal teeth?"** → **quantitative type I collagen defect**

**"14-yr, low-energy fractures, blue sclera — protein?"** → **collagen**

**"Majority of OI caused by?"** → **type I collagen genes**

**"AD, no collagen mutation, hypertrophic callus, radial head dislocation?"** → **type V**

**"Glycine substitution, olecranon avulsion — pathophysiology?"** → **insufficient osteoid by periosteal osteoblasts**

**"COL1A1/2 + blue sclera — associated abnormality?"** → **glycine substitution in a precursor protein**

**"Decreased functional type I collagen — X-ray?"** → **bowed osteopenic long bones**\
✗ Wrong options: cleidocranial · rickets

**"Dense parallel metaphyseal bands at knees?"** → **OI on bisphosphonates**\
✗ Wrong options: Caffey · JIA

**"Bilateral olecranon apophysis fractures?"** → **OI**\
✗ Wrong options: XLH · osteopetrosis

**"Displaced olecranon apophyseal fracture associated with?"** → **OI**\
✗ Wrong options: abuse

**"Common presenting injury in OI?"** → **olecranon fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: corner fracture · rib

**"Sofield procedure used for?"** → **OI** (TO)

**"14-yr OI type I forearm, residual deformity after casting?"** → **IM rod fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: plate

**"15-yr OI, bayonet, 20° after reduction?"** → **open reduction + IM rod + cast**\
✗ Wrong options: plate · accept malunion

**"OI — incorrect statement?"** → **brace effective for scoliosis** (TO)

**"COL1A1 adolescent, unsteady gait, finger escape, Hoffmann?"** → **basilar invagination**\
✗ Wrong options: AAI · OPLL

**"Severe OI, 42° curve progressing?"** → **posterior spinal fusion with instrumentation**\
✗ Wrong options: brace · ASF

**"OI — NOT true?"** → **zebra stripes from growth hormone** (TO)

**"Pamidronate mechanism?"** → **FPP synthase inhibition → osteoclast apoptosis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL antibody · Wnt

**"Pamidronate thickens cortex because?"** → **inhibits osteoclast resorption**

# Osteomyelitis - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 76 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1 in 5,000** children <13 yr · mean age **6.6 yr** · **boys 2.5×** · common in **healthy** children · first decade (rich metaphyseal supply, immature immunity) · mortality 50% → **<1%** with antibiotics

**Route** — **hematogenous seeding of the METAPHYSIS**\
✗ Wrong options: epiphysis · direct inoculation

**Trauma history** — **30%** (Orthobullets) · **20–40%** (OB) · bacteremia source: skin, otitis, pharyngitis, tooth brushing / dental care

**Risk factors** — diabetes · hemoglobinopathy · JRA · chronic renal disease · immune compromise · **varicella**

### Pathophysiology

**Metaphysis** — capillaries make **sharp turns** into venous sinusoids → **sluggish, turbulent flow** · low pH + low O2 near physis · spread via Haversian + Volkmann canals

**Pus through metaphyseal cortex** → **subperiosteal abscess**

**Pus through INTRA-ARTICULAR metaphysis** → **septic arthritis** · **hip, shoulder, elbow, ankle ("SHEA")** · not **knee** (extra-articular metaphysis)

**Infants** — transphyseal vessels → epiphyseal osteomyelitis + septic arthritis · **<1 yr** (Orthobullets) · **<2 yr** (TO)

![Metaphyseal osteomyelitis spreading to joint and subperiosteal space](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4031-illustration-showing-the-progression-of-hematogenous-osteomy.jpg)

### Intra-articular metaphysis & septic arthritis spread

| Bone (intra-articular metaphysis/physis) | Joint |
|---|---|
| **Proximal femur** | Hip |
| **Proximal humerus** | Shoulder |
| **Proximal radius / radial neck** | Elbow |
| **Distal fibula / distal lateral tibia** | Ankle |
| ✗ Wrong option: distal femur, proximal tibia | **Knee — least likely** |
| ✗ Wrong option: distal radius | **Wrist — not** (TO) |

### Organisms & antibiotics (MRSA, PVL, GAS, Pseudomonas)

| Setting | Organism |
|---|---|
| **All children** | ***S. aureus*** (90% Song & Sloboda · 70% Blyth) |
| **Neonate** | **Group B strep** |
| Young child | ***Kingella kingae*** (↑) · most common SA organism 3 months–5 yr · PCR-negative cases |
| **Puncture wound of foot** (through sneaker) | ***Pseudomonas*** (100% in Puffinbarger) |
| **After varicella** | **Group A β-hemolytic strep** |
| **Sickle cell** | ***Salmonella*** |
| Unvaccinated | *H. influenzae* (now rare) |
| TB | Extrapulmonary in children · spine 50%, large joints 25%, long bones 11% |

**PVL (Panton-Valentine leukocidin)** — CA-MRSA (also some MSSA) cytotoxin · **<5% of S. aureus** · complex/multifocal infection, prolonged fever, **abscess, DVT, sepsis**, necrotizing pneumonia · lyses neutrophils (indirect)\
✗ Wrong options: Strep · S. epidermidis · Klebsiella

**Coagulase** — S. aureus factor that **activates prothrombin → thrombin → fibrin clot** → DVT (✓ direct cause; PVL indirect)\
✗ Wrong options: adhesins · eDNA (biofilm) · vWF binding protein

**Other virulence (distractors)** — superantigens (toxic shock) · biofilm · protein A (binds IgG Fc)

**MRSA predictors** — **T >38°C · WBC >12,000 · Hct <34% · CRP >13 mg/L** → MRSA probability **4/4 = 92%** · 3 = 45% · 2 = 10% · 1 = 1%

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **limp / refusal to bear weight** · **usually not toxic** · ± fever · prior antibiotics mask

**Exam** — warm, swollen, **point tenderness** (check pelvis, spine) · motion limited by pain · pelvic compression pain (Gaenslen) → pelvic osteomyelitis

**Neonate** — failure to thrive, **multifocal** (shoulder + hips) → **ultrasound** of hip + shoulder\
✗ Wrong options: MRI first · Pavlik

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Workup - aspiration negative, MRI & US

**Labs**

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| **CRP** | **↑ in 98%** · rises in **6 h** · **best to monitor response** · should fall by **48–72 h** · normalises ~1 wk (Orthobullets) / 1 month (OB) |
| **ESR** | ↑ in **90%** · peaks **3–5 days** · falls too slowly (normal ~3 wk) · unreliable in neonates + sickle cell |
| **WBC** | ↑ in only **~25%** (✓ "often normal") |
| Procalcitonin | ↑ in **58%** · falls with effective treatment |
| **Blood culture** | + in **30–50%** (✗ Wrong options: 75–80%) |
| **Bone aspirate culture** | + in **50–70%** (51–73%, 50–85% OB) |

**Most likely abnormal test** → **CRP**

**Imaging**

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | Early normal / soft-tissue edema · periosteal new bone **5–7 days** · osteolysis **10–14 days** · metaphyseal rarefaction 1–2 wk |
| **MRI** | **Gold standard** · **T1 ↓, T2 ↑, gadolinium ↑** · sensitivity **88–100%** · shows abscess · plan surgery |
| Bone scan | **92%** sensitive · non-focal toddler, **multifocal** · **cold scan = aggressive / osteonecrosis** |
| CT | Later bone changes (✗ Wrong options: study of choice) |
| US | Neonate effusion, subperiosteal collection |

**Fever + ↑ CRP + negative joint aspirate (e.g. 1,000–9,000 cells)** → **MRI** (osteomyelitis / subperiosteal abscess)\
✗ Wrong options: arthrotomy · observe · oral antibiotics

**Septic joint excluded** — synovial WBC **<50,000** essentially rules out (Kocher 2/4 ≈ 40%)

**Suspected SA of shoulder/hip/elbow/ankle** — **pre-op MRI ↓ repeat surgery** · don't delay unstable patient\
✗ Wrong options: MRSA low risk · negative PCR rules out

![MRI subperiosteal abscess](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4031-spa-osteo.jpg)

### Subacute osteomyelitis vs tumour - biopsy

**Subacute** — bone pain + X-ray changes, **no systemic signs** · ↑ host resistance / ↓ virulence / prior antibiotics · afebrile, mild pain, **normal WBC, ↑ ESR**

**Classification** — IA/IB lucency · II metaphyseal with cortical loss · III diaphyseal · IV onion skin · V epiphyseal · VI spinal

**Biopsy + culture** — when diagnosis unclear / rule out tumour (Ewing, osteosarcoma, EG, lymphoma) · send tissue for pathology at every debridement

### Brodie abscess – subacute osteomyelitis

**Type** — **subacute** (TO) · age **10–20 yr** · **proximal tibia** most common · metaphysis (tibia, femur) · S. aureus

**X-ray** — solitary well-circumscribed **lucency with sclerotic rim** at metaphyseal-epiphyseal junction

**MRI** — **penumbra sign**: rim of vascular granulation tissue, **higher T1 signal** than cavity (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: hydatid · hemangioma

**Labs** — normal or mildly ↑\
✗ Wrong options: "elevated blood counts" (TO false statement)

**Treatment** — **surgical debridement / curettage (± graft) + antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: wide resection + chemo · empiric antibiotics alone · cast

### TB osteomyelitis

**Features** — constitutional symptoms, night sweats · normal WBC, ↑ ESR · **PPD +** · osteopenia + subchondral erosions · polyostotic

**Diagnosis** — biopsy: **caseating granulomas, giant cells** · **Ziehl-Neelsen** ("red snappers") · **Löwenstein-Jensen** culture

**Treatment** — multi-agent antibiotics **≥1 yr** · rarely debridement (sinus risk)

## 5 Management

```
Pediatric acute hematogenous osteomyelitis
├─ No abscess → aspirate / biopsy for culture → empiric IV antibiotics
│      (improves within 48 h → no surgery; IV → oral)
├─ Subperiosteal / intraosseous abscess, pus aspirated → I&D + antibiotics
└─ No improvement 48–72 h or CRP not falling → repeat MRI → I&D
```

### Surgical indications & subperiosteal abscess

**Surgery** — **subperiosteal or deep abscess** · **pus on aspiration** · **failure of appropriate antibiotics** (48–72 h) · **chronic infection / sequestrum** · adjacent septic arthritis

**Not failure** — improving at 24 h · on wrong drug (e.g. gentamicin for S. aureus) · no trial yet

**Worsening at 2–3 days on antibiotics** → **subperiosteal abscess**\
✗ Wrong options: CRMO · atypical organism · Brodie

**Subperiosteal abscess sites** — femur 23–29%, tibia 19–26%

**Septic shock + MRSA + abscess** → **fluids + antibiotics + urgent I&D**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for cultures

**Septic hip with adjacent osteomyelitis** — straight to **OR debridement** (cultures intra-op) · complication rate 38% vs <1% isolated SA

**Technique** — evacuate pus, debride, drill intraosseous collections, culture + pathology · drains or pack, re-look in 2–3 days · remove sequestrum in chronic

**Antibiotics before cultures** — only if septic / unstable

### Antibiotic choice

| Situation | Drug |
|---|---|
| Low MRSA prevalence | **Nafcillin / oxacillin** (PBP binders) |
| High MRSA prevalence | **Clindamycin** (50S) or **vancomycin** |
| **Sepsis / severe / MRSA** | **Vancomycin** — binds **D-Ala-D-Ala** |
| Gram-negative rods | + 3rd-gen cephalosporin |
| <1 month (Copley) | Ampicillin/sulbactam + gentamicin |
| 1–3 months | Vancomycin + ceftriaxone |

**Duration** — IV **4–6 wk** (Orthobullets, controversial) · 3–4 wk (OB) · 20 days = 30 days for MSSA/strep · MRSA: 24–48 h IV → 4–6 wk oral (OB)

**Oral vs PICC IV** — **equal cure** · PICC → **more ED visits / rehospitalisation**, ↑ cost

### Monitoring response - CRP

**Best early indicator** — **serial CRP** · peaks ~day 2 post-op then falls\
✗ Wrong options: ESR · WBC · MRI · X-ray · procalcitonin rise

## 6 Complications

### DVT with MRSA osteomyelitis

**Risk factors** — **CRP >6 mg/dL** · **surgical treatment** · **age >8 yr** · **MRSA**\
✗ Wrong options: fever >38.5°C

**Organism** — **MRSA** · PVL endothelial injury + coagulase\
✗ Wrong options: MSSA · Kingella · TB

**Action** — calf pain / swelling → **venous duplex US** · therapeutic anticoagulation · MRSA often needs repeat I&D

### Chronic osteomyelitis (sequestrum, involucrum)

**Sequestrum** — **necrotic bone walled off from blood supply = nidus** of chronic infection

**Involucrum** — **new periosteal bone** around sequestrum

**Brodie abscess** — chronic abscess walled by sclerosis + fibrous tissue

![Sequestrum and involucrum](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4031-sequestrum-diagram-2-moved.jpg)

### Late growth disturbance

**Physeal damage** → **LLD + angular deformity** (∝ physeal destruction) · LLD apparent ~**9 yr** average · **follow to maturity** with **standing full-length X-rays**

**Others** — septic arthritis (I&D) · meningitis · pathologic fracture · AVN · chronic / multifocal infection

## X vs Y

| | **Acute osteomyelitis** | **Subacute (Brodie)** |
|---|---|---|
| Systemic | Fever, ↑ CRP | Afebrile, normal WBC, ↑ ESR |
| Age | Mean 6.6 | 10–20 |
| X-ray | Normal early | Lucency + sclerotic rim |
| Treatment | IV antibiotics ± I&D if abscess | Curettage + antibiotics |

## How it's asked

**"10-yr distal femoral tenderness, fever, knee aspirate 1,700 cells?"** → **MRI of knee**

**"7-yr febrile, CRP 11, knee aspirate 2,500 cells?"** → **MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: IV antibiotics first · arthrotomy

**"10-yr bike fall, knee aspirate 1,000 WBC?"** → **MRI of lower extremity**

**"Posterior hip pain, pelvic compression pain, hip aspirate 9,000?"** → **MRI of pelvis**

**"4-day-old, swollen shoulder, stiff hips, ↑ CRP?"** → **ultrasound of hip and shoulder**

**"6-yr, swollen patella region, mild ↑ CRP?"** → **MRI of knee**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy first · debridement

**"Suspected shoulder SA — true?"** → **pre-op MRI ↓ repeat surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: Kingella infrequent · negative PCR rules out

**"14-yr, 6 months after sprain, distal tibial lesion, ↑ ESR only?"** → **biopsy and culture**

**"7-yr, elbow + wrist swelling, night sweats, lymphadenopathy, caseating granulomas?"** → **polyostotic mycobacterial infection**\
✗ Wrong options: LCH · multicentric GCT

**"8-yr, worsening tibial pain 6 days after twist, ↑ CRP, MRI marrow signal?"** → **percutaneous biopsy with culture and antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: chest CT + oncology

**"13-yr, fever, ↑ ESR/CRP, distal tibial marrow edema?"** → **admit, aspirate for culture, IV antibiotics, serial ESR/CRP**

**"Vertebral osteomyelitis — true?"** → **WBC often normal**\
✗ Wrong options: GBS · ESR best monitor

**"Brodie abscess — all true EXCEPT?"** → **wide resection + chemotherapy**

✗ Wrong options: Ewing · EG · osteosarcoma

**"Distal fibula osteomyelitis + subperiosteal abscess?"** → **I&D**

**"MSSA bacteremia, humeral MRI — surgical feature?"** → **subperiosteal abscess**\
✗ Wrong options: metaphyseal osteomyelitis alone

**"Cold bone scan + shoulder MRI sepsis?"** → **drain GH joint + debride humerus**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · PICC only

**"2-yr, humeral abscess on MRI?"** → **I&D of humerus**

**"14-yr 1st metatarsal abscess, worse on oral antibiotics?"** → **I&D + IV antibiotics**

**"12-month-old shoulder aspirate 63,000, 90% PMN?"** → **I&D**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI first

**"16-yr afebrile, Brodie abscess?"** → **surgical debridement + IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: empiric IV alone

**"Septic shock, MRSA, subperiosteal abscess?"** → **fluids + antibiotics + urgent I&D**

**"Improves day 1 then worse, CRP static?"** → **subperiosteal abscess**\
✗ Wrong options: CRMO · atypical organism

**"Persistent fever on pip-tazo, GPC in blood — image?"** → **subperiosteal abscess MRI**

**"4-yr, all 4 Kocher, hip effusion, proximal femoral metaphyseal signal?"** → **surgical debridement with cultures**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics before cultures · drain only

**"Best early test of response after debridement?"** → **CRP**

**"Most likely abnormal test in AHO?"** → **CRP**\
✗ Wrong options: WBC · blood culture

**"Response indicator after decompression?"** → **decrease in CRP**\
✗ Wrong options: procalcitonin rise · ESR

**"Nail through sneaker → calcaneal osteomyelitis ↑ rate of?"** → **Pseudomonas**

**"Hypotensive 8-yr, tibial osteomyelitis — empiric drug mechanism?"** → **D-Ala-D-Ala binding (vancomycin)**\
✗ Wrong options: PBP (oxacillin) · 30S

**"Fever + shoulder after varicella — organism?"** → **group A β-hemolytic strep**

**"T 38.8, WBC 16k, Hct 30%, CRP 20, GPC clusters?"** → **vancomycin**\

**"Oral vs PICC IV for 6 wk — true?"** → **PICC → more rehospitalisation**

**"Osteomyelitis + popliteal DVT — virulence factor?"** → **Panton-Valentine leukocidin**
