# Osteopetrosis

*Pediatrics · 77 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — "marble bone disease" · **defective osteoclastic resorption** → dense but **brittle** bone, obliterated medullary canals

**Prevalence** — AD **1 in 20,000** · AR **1 in 200,000** · AD = **most common** · 75% penetrance, may skip generations

### Pathophysiology (carbonic anhydrase II, osteoclast acidification)

**Core defect** — osteoclast **cannot acidify Howship's lacuna** → no HCl → hydroxyapatite not dissolved → bone stays thick, disorganised\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ remodeling · ↓ osteoblasts · ↓ hydroxyapatite

**Carbonic anhydrase II** — CO2 + H2O → H+ + HCO3− · H+ pumped across **ruffled border**

**Causes** — **carbonic anhydrase II** loss-of-function or **chloride channel** dysfunction (Orthobullets) · CA II "most common" (OB4418, OB219284) · proton pump 60%, CLCN7 12%, CA II <5% (OB4814) — read the options

**Osteoblasts** — normal

| Defect (distractors) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **CA II / Cl channel / proton pump (osteoclast)** | **Osteopetrosis** |
| Gsα | Fibrous dysplasia |
| ↓ Type I collagen | OI |
| ↓ Osteoid mineralization | Rickets |
| Renal 1α-hydroxylase | Vitamin D-dependent rickets type I |
| Proximal tubule phosphate loss | Hypophosphatemic rickets |
| FGFR3 | Achondroplasia |
| FGFR2 | Apert |
| RUNX2 | Cleidocranial dysplasia |
| PMP22 | CMT |
| COMP | MED |
| DTD sulfate transporter | Diastrophic dysplasia |
| COL2A1 | SED |

### Classification

| Type | Inheritance | Defect | Course |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Malignant (infantile)** | **AR** (11q13) | **Proton pump / Cl channel** | Fatal early without **BMT** |
| **Intermediate** | AR | **Carbonic anhydrase II** / Cl channel | — |
| **Benign** | **AD** (most common) | **Cl channel** | Normal life · **type II = Albers-Schönberg** (anemia, fractures, early OA) · **most seen by orthopaedists** |

**ADO I vs II** — I: diffuse sclerosis, esp. cranial vault · **II: rugger jersey spine, bone-in-bone iliac wings, skull base**

**TO** — lists AD, AR **and X-linked recessive** forms

## 3 Presentation

### Clinical features & complications

**AR (malignant)** — **severe anemia** from infancy (marrow encroachment) → bleeding, infections · **hepatosplenomegaly** (extramedullary hematopoiesis) · macrocephaly · progressive **blindness + deafness** · dental abscess, **mandibular osteomyelitis** · death from bleeding / infection

**AD (benign)** — often asymptomatic until **low-energy (transverse) fracture** · anemia · **low back pain** (↑ spondylolysis) · early **hip OA** · normal height · **high risk of cranial nerve palsy**

**Cranial nerve palsies** — foraminal overgrowth · **optic > auditory > trigeminal > facial**

**Fractures** — lifelong **40–50%** (40–60% OB) · **LE > UE > axial** · transverse · delayed union, malunion

**Associated** — **coxa vara** (femoral neck nonunion / stress fractures) · **osteomyelitis** (poor marrow vascularity, ↓ WBC function) · carpal tunnel · Caffey disease (listed by Orthobullets) · seizures

**NOT associated** — **vertebral compression fractures**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**General** — ↑ cortical thickness · **↑ overall density** · loss of medullary canal · **bone-in-bone (endobones — pathognomonic, TO)**

**Signs** — **"rugger jersey" / sandwich spine** (dense endplate bands) · **Erlenmeyer flask** distal femur + proximal humerus · block femoral metaphysis · coxa vara · thick skull base, poor sinuses

**Not osteopetrosis** — **dripping candle wax** (melorheostosis) · Paget pelvis · bone infarct

**Labs** — AR: ↑ acid phosphatase, ± ↑ PTH, ± ↑ Ca · anemia ± thrombocytopenia · AD: normal

**Histology** — osteoclasts **lack ruffled border + clear zone** · islands of **calcified cartilage** in trabeculae · empty lacunae, plugged Haversian canals

| Rugger jersey spine | Osteopetrotic femur |
|---|---|
| ![Rugger jersey spine](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4103-xray-spine-colorado-moved.jpg) | ![Osteopetrotic femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4103-xray-femur-colorado-moved.jpg) |

### Erlenmeyer flask deformity – differential

**Definition** — constricted diaphysis + flared metaphysis (undertubulation)

**Causes** — **osteopetrosis** · **Gaucher**, Niemann-Pick · thalassemia, sickle cell · **Pyle (metaphyseal dysplasia)** · craniotubular dysplasias · **achondroplasia** · fibrous dysplasia · **Ollier** · MHE · rickets · RA · lead toxicity

**NOT** — **Langerhans cell histiocytosis** · Sprengel · melorheostosis · DEH (TO)

## 5 Management

**Medical**

| Form | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **AR malignant** | **Bone marrow transplant** (only durable cure, TO) · **high-dose calcitriol** |
| AD | **Interferon γ-1b** |
| Supportive (TO) | Erythropoietin (darbepoetin), vitamin D, corticosteroids |
| ✗ Wrong option: **alendronate** | **Inappropriate** — bisphosphonate further suppresses osteoclasts |

**Cranial nerve impingement** — neurosurgical decompression

### Fracture management

| Fracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Diaphyseal long bone, upper extremity** | **Non-op**: prolonged casting, NWB · slow healing, limited remodeling → malunion, refracture |
| **Proximal femur** | **ORIF with plate + screws** — **avoid IM devices** (can't pass reamer) · ↑ union, ↓ coxa vara |

**Technique** — **slow steady drilling, constant cooling, frequent drill-bit changes** · ↑ hardware failure

**Arthroplasty** — THA: cannulated reamers under fluoro, **short-stem uncemented** femur, small sharp acetabular reamers + screws · TKA: consider navigation

## 6 Complications

**Refracture** (brittle bone, hardware failure) · **infection** (↓ vascularity) · malunion · nonunion

## X vs Y

| | **Osteopetrosis** | **Melorheostosis** |
|---|---|---|
| X-ray | Diffuse density, bone-in-bone, rugger jersey | **Dripping candle wax** cortical hyperostosis |

## How it's asked

**"Mechanism of intermediate osteopetrosis?"** → **inactive osteoclast carbonic anhydrase**\
✗ Wrong options: Gsα · RUNX2

**"22-yr distal radius fracture, dense pelvis, rugger jersey spine — etiology?"** → **defective osteoclast function**\
✗ Wrong options: FGFR3 · 1α-hydroxylase

**"Carbonic anhydrase mutation — which X-ray?"** → **Erlenmeyer flask femur**\
✗ Wrong options: Paget · melorheostosis · infarct

**"Cause of osteopetrosis?"** → **loss-of-function of carbonic anhydrase II**\
✗ Wrong options: Gsα · FGFR3

**"Can't acidify Howship's lacuna?"** → **Albers-Schönberg disease**

**"Why is the bone thicker?"** → **↓ acidification of Howship's lacuna**

**"Fractures, CN palsies, back pain — cause?"** → **osteoclast can't create acidic Howship lacunae**

**"10-yr, seizures, anemia, dense bones — defect?"** → **carbonic anhydrase II**\
✗ Wrong options: PMP22 · COMP

**"5-yr, fractures since birth, rugger jersey — inheritance?"** → **autosomal recessive**

**"10-yr, rib fracture, HSM, deafness, caries — NOT appropriate?"** → **alendronate** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: interferon γ · calcitriol · darbepoetin

**"NOT a feature of osteopetrosis?"** → **dripping candle wax** (TO)

**"18-yr, hearing + vision loss, couldn't pass reamer — spine X-ray?"** → **rugger jersey**

**"3-yr abnormal X-rays — risk of all EXCEPT?"** → **vertebral compression fractures**

**"Erlenmeyer flask in all EXCEPT?"** → **Langerhans cell histiocytosis** (TO)

**"Erlenmeyer flask of femoral metaphysis?"** → **osteopetrosis** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Overlapping Toe

*Pediatrics · 78 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — congenital overlap of a toe from **extensor digitorum longus contracture** · rare

**Epidemiology** — **5th toe** most common · usually **bilateral** · M = F · often **familial**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain with **shoe wear**

**Exam** — toe **adducted + slightly externally rotated**, overlapping 4th · **MTP dorsiflexed** ("cock-up") · **smaller nail plate**

| Overlapping fifth toe | Butler procedure |
|---|---|
| ![Overlapping fifth toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4080-clinical-photo-moved.jpg) | ![Butler procedure](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4080-butler-procedure-slide-1-aofas.jpg) |

## 5 Management

### Surgical treatment

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| First line | **Passive stretching + buddy taping** |
| Failed, still symptomatic | **Butler procedure** |
| Alternatives | EDL tenotomy · dorsal capsulotomy · **syndactylization to 4th toe (McFarland)** |
| Skeletally mature only | **Proximal phalanx resection** (induces secondary deformity) |

**Butler** — **dorsal + plantar racquet-shaped incision**, **EDL tenotomy**, **circumferential MTP release**, toe translated plantarly · addresses chronic MTP dislocation (needs plantar release) · main problem = **recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: tenotomy alone · V-Y + dorsal capsulotomy

## How it's asked

**"9-yr, painful cock-up overlapping 5th toe with shoes?"** → **Butler procedure: racquet incision, EDL tenotomy, circumferential MTP release**\
✗ Wrong options: EDL tenotomy alone · phalangeal resection

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Patella Sleeve Fracture

*Pediatrics · 79 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — cartilage **"sleeve" pulled off the ossified patella** ± small bone fleck · unique to skeletally immature

**Epidemiology** — <1% of pediatric fractures but **>50% of pediatric patella fractures** · **boys 5:1** · **8–12 yr** (ossification nearly complete)

**Mechanism** — **indirect**: powerful **quadriceps contraction on a flexed knee** (stumble, pop) · not a direct blow · superior pole: patella pushed down → intra-articular dislocation (OB)

**Anatomy** — largest sesamoid · ossifies **3–5 yr** · lateral facet larger · cartilage up to 1 cm · quadriceps 3 layers (rectus superficial, vasti middle, intermedius deep) · **blood supply mainly inferior pole** (geniculate ring)

**Location** — **inferior pole most common** · superior pole least common

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — severe pain, can't bear weight, **painful pop**

**Exam** — hemarthrosis / tense effusion · **high-riding patella or palpable gap** = extensor mechanism disrupted · **extensor lag / can't straight-leg raise**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, tangential

**Findings** — **small bone flecks** at superior or inferior pole (easily missed) · **inferior sleeve → patella ALTA** · **superior sleeve → patella BAJA** + slight anterior tilt of superior pole

**Rule** — fragment is **much larger than it looks** (mostly cartilage)

**MRI / ultrasound** — when diagnosis unclear (soon after injury)

![Inferior patellar sleeve fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4120-inferior-patellar-sleeve-fracture.jpg)

![Superior patellar sleeve fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4120-superior-patellar-sleeve-fracture.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment - ORIF

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Nondisplaced, extensor mechanism intact** (rare) · gap <2 mm | **Cylinder cast 6 wk** |
| **>2–3 mm displacement or step-off** · **disrupted extensor mechanism** | **ORIF** |

**ORIF** — medial parapatellar approach · repair retinaculum / tendon · **transosseous sutures through bone tunnels** · **modified tension band** · suture anchors · screws · K-wires if fragment large · cylinder cast in extension **2–3 wk**

**Superior sleeve** — suture between bony fragments through **tunnels in the larger distal fragment** (fracture is distal to quadriceps insertion)\
✗ Wrong options: quadriceps tendon repair · immobiliser

**Never** — **excision of the fragment** (cartilage is large) · patellar tendon repair to tubercle · partial patellectomy · knee arthroscopy · PT alone · cast with extensor lag

## 6 Complications

**List** — patella alta · extensor lag · quadriceps atrophy · malunion · nonunion · painful hardware · worse with comminution + displacement

## How it's asked

**"10-yr fell from bike, extensor lag, inferior pole avulsion?"** → **open reduction + suture fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: plate + cerclage · patellectomy · tendon midsubstance repair

**"13-yr superior pole sleeve, can't SLR, gap on US?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: hinged brace · patellar tendon repair

**"11-yr basketball, pop, can't extend — distal pole fleck?"** → **open reduction + tension band**\
✗ Wrong options: excision · cast · tendon to tubercle

**"12-yr kicked, displaced sleeve?"** → **ORIF of patella**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial tubercle · cylinder cast

**"10-yr tripped, pop, extensor lag?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: cast in extension · arthroscopy

**"Arthrogryposis, superior pole rim avulsed, can't straighten?"** → **ORIF with sutures through patellar bone tunnels**\
✗ Wrong options: quadriceps tendon repair · cast

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pediatric Abuse

*Pediatrics · 80 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Most common presentations & epidemiology

**Burden** — **>1 million** substantiated victims/yr (US) · 15–42 per 1,000 children · >1,200 deaths/yr · **~⅓ of abused children see an orthopaedic surgeon**

**Mortality** — **2nd most common cause of death in children >1 yr** (after accidental injury) · after SIDS at 1–5 months (OB) · death usually from **head trauma**

**Age** — **90% of abuse fractures <5 yr** · **50% of fractures <1 yr** · **30% of fractures <3 yr** (Orthobullets) · 10–15% <3 yr (Kempe, TO) · most common <3 yr

**Femur** — **NAT = most common cause of femur fracture in non-ambulatory infant** · NAI up to **35% of femur fractures <3 yr**, **40–90% <1 yr** (OB)

| Type (overlap) | % |
|---|---|
| **Neglect** (✓ most common) | **78%** |
| Physical | 18% |
| Sexual | 9% (~10% TO) |
| Psychological | 8% |

| Presentation | Rank |
|---|---|
| **Skin lesions** (bruises in various stages, burns, cuts) | **Most common** (✗ Wrong options: fractures) |
| **Fractures** | **2nd** |
| **Head injury** | **Most long-term morbidity** (✗ Wrong options: burns) |

**Fracture location** — **humerus > tibia > femur** · **diaphyseal 4× more common than metaphyseal** · single transverse diaphyseal fracture classic (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: metaphyseal 4×

### Risk factors

**Child** — **first-born** · unplanned · **premature** · **disability (CP, neurocognitive syndromes, Down)** · **step-child** · age <3 yr

**Parent / social** — single parent · recent stressor (**job loss**, move) · unemployment · drugs · abused as child · low SES · no support · age <20 · Medicaid (⅔)

**NOT risk factors** — **female sex** · **no siblings**

## 3 Presentation

**Red flags** — inconsistent / no history · **delay in seeking care** · **long-bone fracture in a non-ambulatory child** (e.g. "fell running" at 6 months) · unwitnessed injury · multiple carers · bruises in various stages · **pinna bruising, cigarette burns** (highly specific)

### Fracture patterns suggestive of abuse

| Specificity | Fractures |
|---|---|
| **High** | **Classic metaphyseal lesion (corner + bucket handle)** · **posteromedial rib** · **long bone in non-ambulatory child** · **transphyseal distal humerus** (moderate per OB220897) · scapula · sternum · spinous process |
| Moderate | **Multiple fractures in various stages of healing** · vertebral body · digital · complex skull |
| Low | **Toddler's fracture** (non-displaced spiral tibia, ambulatory 9 months–3 yr) · clavicle · supracondylar humerus · SCFE |

**Classic metaphyseal lesion** — through **primary spongiosa** at metaphysis-physis junction · **torsion / traction-shear** (pull, twist, shake) · **corner fracture** = discrete metaphyseal avulsion · **bucket handle** = same seen en face · present in **40–50%** of abused infants (TO) · "almost pathognomonic" (TO) · **most suspicious femur pattern**\
✗ Wrong options: midshaft spiral/transverse

**Femur NAI vs accident** — **mid-diaphyseal most common in both** · NAI children **younger**\
✗ Wrong options: corner fracture most common in NAI

**Rib fractures** — **posteromedial** · highest specificity (OB) · 70% chance of abuse · 51% of fractures in fatal abuse · anterior rib not pathognomonic

**Transphyseal distal humerus separation** — rotatory shear · ≤3 yr · radiocapitellar line preserved (vs dislocation) · arthrogram/US/MRI if unossified · CRPP like supracondylar

**Shaken baby syndrome** — **subdural hematoma + retinal hemorrhage + posterior rib fractures** (TO) · chest ecchymosis · seizures

**No fracture is pathognomonic** — judge age, ambulation, pattern, mechanism, psychosocial picture

![Metaphyseal corner fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4001-corner-fx-xray-moved.jpg)

![Bucket handle fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4001-bh.jpg)

![Posterior rib fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4001-posterior-rib.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Skeletal survey** — **study of choice / gold standard** · AP appendicular + AP/lateral axial (skull, spine, ribs) · **mandatory <2 yr** with suspected abuse (AAP) · **repeat at 2 wk** for healing injuries\
✗ Wrong options: SPECT · CT CAP · brain/spine MRI · isolated forearm film

**Add** — lateral thoracolumbar <12 months · **head CT <1 yr** or neuro signs · ophthalmology if shaking suspected

**Bone scan** — adjunct >1 yr · ↑ sensitivity for ribs, subtle shaft fractures, periosteal elevation · **useless for metaphyseal + skull fractures** · less efficient than survey

**OI vs abuse (TO)** — **DEXA 4–6 months apart** (density rises without metabolic disease, not in OI) · fibroblast culture / collagen analysis if unclear

### Differential

**List** — accidental injury · **OI** · osteopenia of prematurity · scurvy · copper deficiency · **Menkes** · disuse osteopenia · chronic renal/liver disease · rickets

**X-ray mimics** — rickets (physeal widening, metaphyseal fraying) · wormian bones (OI, rickets; normal in 10% Caucasians, 80% Asians) · bisphosphonate zebra lines (vs Park-Harris arrest lines — persist, parallel to physis) · **Caffey disease**

### Differential – Caffey disease (infantile cortical hyperostosis)

**Features** — infant · **solid wavy periosteal new bone** along long-bone shafts (ulna, femur), **clavicles, mandible**, ribs, scapulae · **resolves spontaneously** within ~a year

**TO stems** — infant with forearm cortical hyperostosis (OI / congenital syphilis / Caffey / infantile osteomyelitis) · diffuse ulnar thickening + clavicle + femur, resolved in a year (scurvy / Ewing / Garré / Caffey) · **answers not provided by the source**

## 5 Management

### Management - skeletal survey & CPS

**Rule** — **report reasonable suspicion** → **child protective services** · **admit** for safety + multidisciplinary evaluation (pediatrics, social work, CPS, radiology) · document + photograph

**Order** — CPS/NAT work-up **before definitive fracture plan or discharge**\
✗ Wrong options: cast and send home · next-week clinic · metabolic labs first · genetic testing first · talk home safety · restrain father / police

**Fracture care** — usually splint/cast until callus (Pavlik / spica for infant femur) · surgery rarely needed · still treat appropriately (e.g. forearm: **closed reduction + cast + skeletal survey + CPS**)

**Toddler's fracture alone** — not abuse · **plus corner fracture or suspicious skin lesions** → **admit / report to CPS**

**If missed** — **30–50% repeat abuse**, **5–10% death**

### Legal duty to report

**Physicians = mandated reporters** · need only **reasonable suspicion** · **immune from civil + criminal liability in good faith** · **failure to report can be a crime**\
✗ Wrong options: prove cause · call daycare first

**Sexual abuse disclosure (minor)** → **child protective services**\
✗ Wrong options: parents · school board · athletic director · counselor

### Medical child abuse - multidisciplinary evaluation

**Munchausen by proxy (caregiver-fabricated illness)** — usually biologic mother with medical knowledge · repeated doctor shopping, unwarranted tests/surgery · normal exam + X-rays → **early multidisciplinary evaluation** (medical, psychosocial, CPS, legal)\
✗ Wrong options: CT · casting · coalition excision · another opinion

### Developmental milestones

| Milestone | Age |
|---|---|
| **Sits** | **9 months** (not sitting at 15 months → work up) |
| Walks | Up to **15 months** still normal |
| **Hand dominance** | **3rd year (~30 months)** · strong preference <2 yr → neuro deficit |
| Shoes on correct feet, buttons | 4 yr |
| Hops on one foot, heel-to-toe walk | 5 yr |

### Cast saw burns

**Reduce burns** — vacuum · **thinner cast** · **"up and down" technique** · check blade often · **avoid fiberglass** (more heat than plaster)

## How it's asked

**"2nd most common presenting sign of abuse?"** → **fractures**

**"Most common presenting sign?"** → **skin cuts or bruises**\
✗ Wrong options: fractures · head injury

**"Two most common lesions?"** → **skin lesions and fractures**

**"Correct statement on child abuse?"** → **2nd most common cause of death >1 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: >5 yr · burns most morbidity · metaphyseal 4×

**"Social risk factors — EXCEPT?"** → **child with no siblings**

**"10-month-old femur — NOT a NAT risk factor?"** → **female gender**

**"FALSE about NAT?"** → **fractures most common initial finding**

**"Most common form of maltreatment?"** → **neglect**

**"9-month-old — most common manifestation?"** → **multiple bruises in various stages of healing**

**"Metaphyseal bucket handle fracture seen in?"** → **battered baby syndrome** (TO)

**"Pathognomonic bone lesion of abuse?"** → **metaphyseal corner fracture of distal femur** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior rib · greenstick

**"Isolated spiral femur — suspect abuse when?"** → **not yet walking**

**"NOT a common abuse pattern?"** → **anterior femoral neck translation (SCFE)**

**"Highly suggestive EXCEPT?"** → **non-displaced spiral tibia in a toddler**

**"6-month-old, corner + rib fractures + physeal separation, sibling with bone disorder?"** → **non-accidental trauma**\
✗ Wrong options: OI

**"Which X-ray most concerning?"** → **corner fracture**

**"2-yr transphyseal distal humerus separation?"** → **child abuse**

**"X-ray characteristic of NAT?"** → **rib fractures**\
✗ Wrong options: rickets · wormian · Caffey

**"Femur NAI vs accidental?"** → **midshaft most common in both; NAI younger**

**"8-month-old, seizures, femur fracture, chest bruises, retinal hemorrhages?"** → **shaken baby syndrome**

**"8-month-old healing supracondylar femur fracture, multiple carers?"** → **non-accidental trauma**

**"Acute femur fracture in 5-month-old?"** → **non-accidental trauma**\
✗ Wrong options: birth trauma

**"Femur fracture most suggestive of NAT?"** → **metaphyseal corner fracture**

**"Foster parents, skin lesion — matching X-ray?"** → **metaphyseal corner lesion**\
✗ Wrong options: zebra lines · anterolateral bowing

**"Most specific isolated fracture (3-yr)?"** → **transphyseal distal humerus**

**"10-month-old femur + bruises — study?"** → **skeletal survey**

**"6-month-old corner lesions, vague history?"** → **admit + abuse work-up**

**"12-month-old physeal separation + healing femur?"** → **CPS + skeletal survey**

**"Non-ambulatory infant with diaphyseal fracture — must do?"** → **CPS consultation ± admission**

**"10-month-old distal humeral separation with callus?"** → **skeletal survey + contact authorities**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP first

**"6-month-old spiral tibia, vague parents?"** → **skeletal survey**

**"11-month-old, elbow + ankle bucket handle, 3 AM?"** → **admit to hospital**

**"9-month-old forearm fracture off changing table?"** → **reduction + cast + skeletal survey + CPS**

**"6-month-old femur, aunt says fell from highchair?"** → **immediate CPS notification**\
✗ Wrong options: Pavlik first

**"2-yr toddler's fracture + humeral corner fracture on survey?"** → **cast + admit for NAT work-up**

**"16-month-old tibia + proximal corner fracture?"** → **CPS consultation**

**"4-month-old with syndrome, femur fracture from bouncer?"** → **additional imaging + social work**

**"10-month-old Down syndrome, femur fracture, normal labs?"** → **immediate CPS notification**

**"6-month-old 'fell running after dog' — femur?"** → **additional imaging + social work**

**"3-yr toddler's fracture + pinna bruises + cigarette burn?"** → **contact state CPS**\
✗ Wrong options: call daycare

**"12-yr discloses coach touching him?"** → **inform child protective services**

**"30-month-old — milestone present?"** → **hand dominance established**

**"15-month-old not walking — worrying if not?"** → **sitting**

**"Nurse mother, repeated sprains, demands coalition surgery?"** → **early multidisciplinary evaluation**

**"Cast saw burn — NOT protective?"** → **fiberglass cast material**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pediatric Trauma Evaluation & Management

*Pediatrics · 81 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — trauma = **most common cause of death in children >1 yr** · polytrauma mortality **~20%** · falls + MVAs most common

**Morbidity** — **CNS injury = highest overall morbidity + mortality** · **spine fractures = highest among MSK injuries** · C-spine injury more common **<8 yr** (restraints don't fit)

### Anatomy & physiology vs adults

| System | Child |
|---|---|
| **Airway** | Smaller (obstructs easily) · large tongue, floppy epiglottis · **large occiput flexes neck supine** · **larynx higher + anterior (C2–C3 vs C6–C7)** |
| **Breathing** | **Hypoventilation = most common cause of arrest** · horizontal ribs · diaphragmatic · fewer type I fibres → tire fast · RR varies with age |
| **Circulation** | Relatively larger but absolutely small blood volume · lower SVR · **hypotension is LATE** (25–30% loss) · small veins → **IO access** |
| **Disability** | Open sutures/fontanelle · thin skull · **large head** → head/neck injury |
| **Exposure** | High BMR + surface area → **heat loss** · ↑ glucose need, small glycogen stores |

## 5 Management

### Resuscitation (airway, IO access, blood volume, transport)

**Transport** — **<8 yr: spine board with occipital cut-out or raised chest** (✓ modified board for suspected spine injury)\
✗ Wrong options: routine pelvic binder · routine IO · nasal airway in head injury

**ATLS** — Airway → Breathing → Circulation → Disability → Exposure · fix each before moving on

**Intubate** — **GCS <8** / can't protect airway (✗ Wrong options: GCS <12) · e.g. GCS 6 + SpO2 83% → **intubation first** (✗ Wrong options: splint ankle · CT · close laceration)

| Formula | Value |
|---|---|
| **Broselow tape** | Drug doses, equipment size, defibrillation energy |
| **ETT (uncuffed)** | **(age/4) + 4** or (age + 16)/4 |
| **Systolic BP** | **80 + (2 × age)** |
| **Chest tube** | **4 × ETT** |
| **Blood volume** | **75 mL/kg** (Orthobullets) · **75–80 mL/kg** (OB) |
| **First bolus** | **20 mL/kg NS** |
| **After 2 boluses** | **10 mL/kg PRBC** |

**Shock signs** — narrowed pulse pressure at **~15%** loss · hypotension after **25–30%** · 100 mL in 5 kg child ≈ 26.7% of volume

**IV access fails (even normotensive)** → **intraosseous line**\
✗ Wrong options: PICC · cutdown · subclavian · keep trying

**Triad of death** — **acidosis + hypothermia + coagulopathy** (inadequate resuscitation)

### Pediatric scoring systems

| PTS | Mortality |
|---|---|
| **<0** | **100%** |
| 1–4 | 40% |
| 5–8 | 7% |
| **≤8** | **Send to pediatric trauma centre** |

**Pediatric GCS <8** → ↑ mortality · **SpO2 at presentation + GCS at 72 h** predict long-term neuro recovery

![Pediatric spine board with occipital cut-out](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4124-pediatric-spine-board.jpg)

![Pediatric trauma score](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4124-pts.jpg)

### Injury patterns & outcomes (TBI, multiorgan failure)

**TBI** — **most common ATV injury** (✗ Wrong options: open fractures · pneumothorax) · **greatest long-term morbidity** (✗ Wrong options: spine · abdomen · open fracture) · pain ↑ ICP → **fracture fixation ↓ ICP** · **HO more common** → **↑ serum ALP heralds HO** → NSAID prophylaxis

**Peripheral nerve injury** — mostly with **closed fractures** · **EMG if no return by 2–3 months**

**Multiorgan failure** — **early after admission, all organs simultaneously** · adults: from 48 h, sequential starting with lungs · acute lung injury ~6× lower in children\
✗ Wrong options: 48 h · sequential

### Femur fixation in polytrauma with head injury

**Timing** — fix once **cerebral perfusion pressure stable** · early fixation eases transport, ↓ malunion/shortening, ↓ ICP

**9-yr TBI + transverse femoral shaft** → **retrograde flexible IM nails**\
✗ Wrong options: spica · 90-90 traction (spasticity) · piriformis reamed nail (AVN risk)

**Prognosis** — children recover from TBI better than adults

## How it's asked

**"8-yr fell from tree, GCS 6, SpO2 83%?"** → **intubation**

**"Blood volume per kg in a 5-yr?"** → **75–80 mL**

**"5-yr polytrauma, no peripheral IV, BP 110/70?"** → **intraosseous device**

**"Transporting 4-yr with fluctuating consciousness?"** → **modified spine board**\
✗ Wrong options: intubate if GCS <12 · routine binder

**"4-yr polytrauma — greatest long-term morbidity?"** → **traumatic brain injury**

**"Multiorgan failure in children?"** → **early, all organs simultaneously**

**"6-yr high-speed ATV — most likely injury?"** → **brain contusion**

**"9-yr TBI + transverse femur fracture?"** → **retrograde flexible IM nails**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pelvis Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 82 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **1–2%** of pediatric fractures · acetabulum 1–15% of these · avulsions almost only in **adolescents**

**Mechanism** — ring: **high energy** (MVA, pedestrian struck) · avulsion: low energy, **explosive** activity (sprint, jump, kick)

**Associated** — **CNS + abdominal visceral injury >50%** (energy needed to break elastic pelvis) · chest injury ~63%, long bones ~50%, spine ~25% · femoral head fracture/dislocation with acetabular fracture · **GU injury with Torode IV**

### Child vs adult pelvis

| Child | Reason |
|---|---|
| More **lateral compression** (adult: APC) | — |
| More **single-bone ring fractures** | Elastic bone, cartilage absorbs energy, elastic SI + symphysis, **thick periosteum** |
| "Dislocations" heal like fractures | Periosteal tube |
| **Less hemorrhage** | Small vessels vasoconstrict · pelvic volume rarely ↑ |
| Acetabulum: **transverse** > both column | Triradiate injury with less force → growth arrest |

### Anatomy

**Primary centres** (endochondral) — ilium 9 wk · ischium 16 wk · pubis 20 wk · fuse at triradiate **12 (girls) / 14 (boys)**

**Acetabulum** — enlarges by **interstitial growth of triradiate cartilage** · concavity from femoral head pressure · secondary centres appear 8–9, fuse 17–18: **os acetabuli (anterior wall)**, acetabular epiphysis (superior), ischial secondary centre (posterior wall)

| Apophysis | Appears | Fuses |
|---|---|---|
| **Iliac crest** (Risser) | 13–15 | 15–17 |
| **Ischial** | 15–17 | **19–25** |
| AIIS | 14 | 16 |

### Apophyseal avulsion fractures

| Site | Muscle | % |
|---|---|---|
| **Ischial tuberosity** | **Hamstrings + adductors** | **54%** |
| **AIIS** | **Rectus femoris** | 22% |
| **ASIS** | **Sartorius** | 19% |
| Pubic symphysis | Abdominals | 3% |
| Iliac crest | Abdominals (external oblique) | 1% |
| **Lesser trochanter** | **Iliopsoas** | — |

**Ischial avulsion** — age **13–17** as tuberosity ossifies (apophysis 2–5× weaker than tendon) · hip extension + knee flexion weakness · **X-rays negative → MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: CT (for displacement) · EMG · bone scan · steroid

**AIIS** — groin pain, snap, **pain on resisted knee extension** · **ASIS** — hockey check / eccentric sartorius

![Pelvic apophyseal avulsion sites](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3000-illustration.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — ATLS · **rectal + GU exam** (open fracture) · log-roll for ecchymosis · palpate ASIS, crests, SI, symphysis · iliac crest compression / posterior pressure pain · excess mobility = serious injury

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP pelvis** · **Judet** (45° obliques — acetabulum) · **inlet/outlet** (35° caudal/cranial — ring)

**CT** — X-rays miss **~50%** of pediatric pelvic fractures · SI tenderness, negative films with suspicion, planning, spine injury

**MRI** — occasionally for apophyseal avulsions

### Torode/Zieg classification (pediatric ring)

| Type | Injury | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Avulsion** | Protected WB if <2 cm · ORIF >2–3 cm (historically >3 cm) |
| **II** | **Iliac wing** | Protected WB if <2 cm · ORIF >2–3 cm |
| **III** | Ring, **no segmental instability** | WBAT 6 wk · ORIF if acetabulum displaced >2 mm |
| **IV** | Ring **with segmental instability** | **<2 cm → bed rest** · unstable / >2 cm → ORIF |

**Tile** — A stable · B rotationally unstable, vertically stable · C unstable both

![Torode/Zieg type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3000-pediatric-pelvic-fractures.jpg)

### Bucholz classification (acetabulum / triradiate)

| Type | Physeal | Features |
|---|---|---|
| **Shearing** | SH I / II | Blow to pubis/ischium/femur · triangular medial Thurston-Holland fragment (SH II) |
| **Crushing / impaction** | **SH V** | Hard to see · narrowed triradiate · **premature closure** · earlier = worse deformity |

## 5 Management

### Pelvic ring injury management

**Non-op** — symphysis/SI "dislocations" heal by periosteum · types I–II <2 cm: protected WB 2–4 wk → stretch/strengthen 4–8 wk → sport after 8 wk · type III WBAT 6 wk

**Stable type IV open book (symphysis 1.2 cm, SI 0.7 cm, stable)** → **bed rest + progressive mobilisation**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · binder · fixation

**Hemodynamically unstable + rotationally unstable (APC) ring** → **pelvic binder** · **ex-fix** for instability / ↑ volume · **angiography** if still bleeding\
✗ Wrong options: iliac wing · rami · avulsions

**Adolescent with displaced symphysis + SI widening** → **anterior symphyseal plate + percutaneous SI screw**\
✗ Wrong options: non-op · anterior only · ex-fix

**Operative indications** — displaced posterior SI · iliac fracture >2 cm · anterior disruption with vertical/rotational instability · older children/adolescents do better with fixation

**Acetabulum ORIF** — **displacement >2 mm** · incongruity · **intra-articular fragments** · instability (medial/posterior subluxation) · central fracture-dislocation · open · comminution not improved by traction

**Technique** — ilioinguinal / Stoppa · **physeal-sparing** · if needed **smooth pins across triradiate 4–6 wk, removed early**

### Apophyseal avulsion management

**First line** — crutches, **protected WB**, surveillance X-rays (e.g. minimally displaced lesser trochanter avulsion in hurdler)

**Differential on hip films** — SCFE → in situ screw · FAI → PT · compression femoral neck stress fracture → NWB (screw if >50% width) · **tension-sided** → cannulated screws

## 6 Complications

**Triradiate premature closure** — **<5%** · risk: **age <10 yr**, **Bucholz crush (SH V)** · thick medial wall → **shallow acetabulum, lateral subluxation**, hypoplastic hemipelvis · bar excision (may still close) · late **pelvic osteotomy (Chiari)** · follow to maturity

**Others** — LLD (vertical displacement >2 cm) · malunion (well tolerated) · nonunion (rare) · NV injury · HO · **femoral head AVN** (acetabular fracture + dislocation) · hip OA (displaced acetabular) · death (head/visceral)

**Prognosis** — complications rare · surgery more often needed after triradiate closure

## How it's asked

**"8-yr hit by car, SBP 48 — which pelvis benefits from binder?"** → **AP compression (open book) ring injury**

**"6-yr, symphysis 1.2 cm + SI 0.7 cm, stable?"** → **bed rest + progressive mobilisation**

**"14-yr, displaced symphysis + SI widening?"** → **anterior plate + percutaneous SI screw**

**"14-yr soccer snap, pain on resisted knee extension — muscle?"** → **rectus femoris (AIIS)**\
✗ Wrong options: sartorius · iliopsoas

**"14-yr gymnast, posterior thigh, negative X-rays?"** → **MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · EMG

**"16-yr hockey check, ASIS avulsion — muscle?"** → **sartorius**

**"14-yr hurdler — which injury gets crutches + repeat films?"** → **lesser trochanter avulsion**\
✗ Wrong options: SCFE · tension stress fracture

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Physeal Considerations

*Pediatrics · 83 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Pediatric bone** — **elastic** → buckle + greenstick fractures · **remodeling**: faster **in plane of joint motion** (sagittal at wrist) and at **most active physes** · intra-articular fractures still need reduction

**Most active physes** — UE: **proximal humerus, distal radius** ("away from the elbow") · LE: **distal femur, proximal tibia**, distal tibia ("at the knee")

**Skeletal age** — differs from chronologic age by **>1 yr in 26%** · maturity ~**14 girls / 16 boys**

### Laws of the physis

| Law | Statement |
|---|---|
| **Hueter–Volkmann** | ↑ **compression slows** longitudinal growth |
| **Delpech** | ↑ **tension speeds** growth |
| **Wolff** | Bone adapts to the loads placed on it |
| ✗ Wrong option: **Maxwell** | Not a physeal law (TO) |

### Physeal zones & fracture plane (hypertrophic zone)

| Zone | Function | Disease |
|---|---|---|
| **Reserve** | Stores lipid, glycogen, proteoglycan · **low O2** | **Gaucher** · Kniest · pseudoachondroplasia |
| **Proliferative** | Chondrocyte stacking → **longitudinal growth** · **most matrix production** · ↑ O2 inhibits calcification | **Achondroplasia** · gigantism |
| **Hypertrophic** — maturation, degeneration (cells ×5), **provisional calcification** | Chondrocyte death → calcium release → matrix calcifies · **type X collagen** · PTHrP / Indian hedgehog | **SCFE (non-renal)** · **rickets (provisional calcification)** · enchondroma · MPS · Schmid · **physeal fractures** |
| **Primary spongiosa** | Vascular invasion, osteoblasts on cartilage bars → woven bone | **Metaphyseal corner fracture (abuse)** |
| **Secondary spongiosa** | Internal remodeling (lamellar bone) + funnelization | **Renal SCFE** |

**Fracture plane** — physeal fractures run through the **hypertrophic zone**, specifically the **zone of provisional calcification** (weakest in tension; calcified/uncalcified transition) · applies to SH I, II and III (OB) · chronic gymnast wrist injury → same zone

**Reserve + proliferative zones** stay with epiphysis, blood supply preserved → good prognosis in SH I–II · **TO: SH III + IV involve the reserve zone** (germinal cells) → ↑ arrest risk · SH V crush of germinal layer

### Perichondral ring (Ranvier, LaCroix)

| Structure | Role |
|---|---|
| **Groove (zone) of Ranvier** | Fibrous ring in 1st year · **↑ mechanical strength** · **appositional growth** · source of **osteochondroma** |
| **Perichondral ring of LaCroix** | Dense fibrous limiting membrane · **peripheral stability** · up to **50% of shear resistance** · injured in SH VI |
| **Perichondral artery** | Major nutrition of physis |

![Physeal zones histology](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4002-histology-of-zones-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Salter-Harris classification

| Type | Path |
|---|---|
| I | Through physis (separation) |
| **II** | Physis → exits **metaphysis** · **Thurston-Holland fragment** · **most common (>50%)** · periosteum torn on convex (tension) side, intact on concave |
| III | Physis → exits **epiphysis** (intra-articular) |
| IV | Epiphysis → physis → metaphysis |
| V | **Crush** of physis · diagnosed late |

![Salter-Harris classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4002-salter.jpg)

### Physeal vs ligamentous ankle injury

**Pediatric inversion injury, normal X-ray** → **lateral ligament sprain** on MRI (more common than SH I)\
✗ Wrong options: physeal widening · bone bruise · peroneal tear

### Bone age estimation

| Method | Film / basis |
|---|---|
| **Greulich-Pyle / Tanner-Whitehouse** | **Left hand + wrist** (right more often injured; anthropology convention) (TO) |
| TW-III RUS | Distal radius, ulna, short bones · correlates with PHV |
| **Sauvegrain** | **Left elbow** · 27 points: lateral condyle, trochlea, olecranon apophysis, proximal radial epiphysis |
| Olecranon method | 6-monthly age: girls 11–13, boys 13–15 |
| **Risser** | Iliac apophysis 0–4 ossification, 5 fused · variable |
| **Peak height velocity** | Better than Risser/age · **8 cm/yr girls, 9.5 cm/yr boys** |
| **Sanders** | Phalanx, metacarpal, distal radius epiphyses · predicts scoliosis progression |

**Hand dominance** — **2–3 yr** (third year) · strong preference <2 yr → neuro deficit · persistent ambidexterity normal

## 5 Management

**Treatment** — closed reduction vs CRPP vs ORIF by pattern · **intra-articular must be anatomic**

**Displaced SH III/IV distal tibia (11-yr)** → **ORIF with fixation PARALLEL to physis** (epiphyseal or metaphyseal screw) + NWB cast · ankle physeal arrest up to **50%** → long follow-up\
✗ Wrong options: transphyseal lag screw · closed manipulation

### Growth contribution by physis

| Physis | Growth |
|---|---|
| **Distal femur** | **9–10 mm/yr** (✓ greatest LLD if arrested) |
| Proximal tibia | 6 mm/yr |
| Distal radius | 5–6 mm/yr |
| Proximal femur | 3 mm/yr |
| Distal humerus | 2–3 mm/yr |
| **Humerus** | **Proximal 80% · distal 20%** |

**12-yr boy distal femoral arrest** → 4 yr × 10 mm = **40 mm**

## 6 Complications

### Growth arrest - location, mechanism & follow-up

**Mechanism** — fracture through all zones (SH III/IV) → **vascular invasion across physis** → osteoblasts → **bony bar**\
✗ Wrong options: LaCroix proliferation · Wolff · Hueter-Volkmann

**Pattern** — complete arrest → **shortening** · partial → **angulation**

**Most common site of post-traumatic arrest** — **distal (medial) tibia** · also high: distal femur, distal ulna · rare: distal radius, proximal humerus

**Implant violation** — **<7% of physeal area unlikely to arrest** (Mäkelä: 7% arrested, 3% didn't) · retrograde nail through 5% central physis, screws above physis → **injury unlikely**

**Follow-up** — **≥2 yr** after physeal injury · multiple X-rays needed\
✗ Wrong options: one film · stop at 14/16

| Bar | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<50%** of physis + **>2 yr or 2 cm growth left** | **Bar resection + interposition** (fat / wax) |
| **>50%** | **Complete ipsilateral arrest** ± contralateral epiphysiodesis / ipsilateral lengthening |

![Angular deformity from partial arrest](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4002-deformity.jpg)

### Little League elbow outcome — Panner disease

**Panner (osteochondrosis of capitellum)** — **age 5–10** · repetitive valgus → ischemia of whole capitellar epiphysis → flattening, fragmentation, reossification (like Perthes) · **symptomatic** (pain, swelling, ↓ motion)

**Outcome** — non-op succeeds · **full motion returns** · X-ray irregularity / sclerosis often **persists**\
✗ Wrong options: anatomic restoration · incidental · spontaneous · high failure

**vs capitellar OCD** — focal lesion, **>10 yr**

## How it's asked

**"Which physeal injury involves the reserve zone?"** → **both type III and IV** (TO)

**"Law NOT pertaining to growth plate?"** → **Maxwell's law** (TO)

**"Zone affected in SH II?"** → **hypertrophic zone**

**"5-yr SH II distal femur, injury through which layer?"** → **lower hypertrophic**

**"5-yr SH III ankle → bar a year later — layer?"** → **hypertrophic zone, provisional calcification**

**"12-yr gymnast chronic wrist pain — location?"** → **zone of provisional calcification**

**"10-yr SH I distal femur — zone?"** → **hypertrophic**\
✗ Wrong options: primary spongiosa

**"11-yr FOOSH physeal fracture — zone?"** → **hypertrophic (D)**

**"Correct match?"** → **SH III: provisional calcification**\
✗ Wrong options: Gaucher-proliferative · renal SCFE-degenerative · achondroplasia-reserve

**"7-yr large Thurston-Holland — zone?"** → **hypertrophic zone (D)**

**"12-yr SH II distal tibia — region damaged?"** → **where matrix calcification occurs**\
✗ Wrong options: LaCroix · most matrix production

**"Ranvier + LaCroix — true?"** → **↑ mechanical strength of physis**

**"Distal humerus physis contributes?"** → **20%**

**"Arrest causing greatest LLD at 12?"** → **distal femur**

**"11-yr displaced SH IV ankle?"** → **ORIF with fixation parallel to physis + NWB cast**

**"Post-traumatic arrest most common at?"** → **distal tibia**

**"Mechanism of premature arrest?"** → **vascular invasion across physis**

**"Retrograde nail violated 5% of physis — counsel?"** → **physeal injury unlikely**

**"SH IV distal femur, normal at 6 months — follow-up?"** → **at least 2 yr**

**"Twisted ankle, normal X-ray — MRI?"** → **lateral ligament complex injury**

**"8-yr pitcher, Panner disease — true?"** → **full elbow motion usually returns**

**"Hand dominance by?"** → **2–3 yr**

**"Tanner-Whitehouse bone age — radiograph?"** → **left wrist** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Polydactyly of Foot

*Pediatrics · 84 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **1 in 500** (Orthobullets) · postaxial ~1 in 200 (OB) · **postaxial (lateral) most common** · African American > Caucasian · foot less common than hand

**Cause** — failure of differentiation of the **apical ectodermal ridge** in the 1st trimester

### Inheritance & associations

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant** (family history)\
✗ Wrong options: X-linked · AR · mitochondrial · spontaneous only

**Isolated postaxial with normal exam** — **normal motor milestones** · **no cardiac / renal work-up** · no systemic association

### Venn-Watson classification

| Type | Patterns |
|---|---|
| **Postaxial** (lateral) | **Y** metatarsal · **T** metatarsal · wide MT head · complete duplication |
| Central (added) | Duplicated 2nd, 3rd or 4th toe |
| **Preaxial** (medial) | Short block 1st MT · wide MT head |

![Venn-Watson classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4078-poly.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**X-ray** — MTs visible at birth · **delay films** until phalanges ossify for planning

![Postaxial polydactyly radiograph](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4078-polyrad.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Well-aligned postaxial / central | **Observation** (unless foot much wider) |
| **Malaligned toe, esp. preaxial** | **Ablate extra (usually border) digit at 9–12 months** |
| Small skin tag | Remove in newborn nursery |
| Cosmesis / shoe wear | Elective, any age per family wishes (OB) |

**Natural history** — shoe-fitting problems, angular toe deformity

## How it's asked

**"10-yr postaxial foot polydactyly, father similar, normal exam?"** → **normal milestones, no further work-up**\
✗ Wrong options: cardiac/renal work-up · spontaneous mutation

**"7-month-old postaxial polydactyly — inheritance?"** → **autosomal dominant**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Popliteal Cyst in Children

*Pediatrics · 85 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — herniated posterior knee capsule synovium between **semimembranosus** and **medial head of gastrocnemius** · common soft-tissue mass · **unlike adults, usually NOT linked to meniscal tears**

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — usually **painless**, mobile, soft · posteromedial, distal to knee crease · **most prominent with knee extended** · **transilluminates**

![Popliteal cyst](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4048-clinical-photo-miller-colorado-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — normal · **US** — cystic · **MRI** — fluid-filled simple cyst (confirms)

## 5 Management

### Treatment - observation

**Observation** — mainstay · Dinham: of 70 untreated cysts, **51 resolved spontaneously** over a mean **1 yr 8 months**

**Aspiration** — may work but **high recurrence** · **excision** only if significant discomfort or failure to resolve\
✗ Wrong options: radical excision · arthroscopic resection first · CT · bone scan

## How it's asked

**"10-yr, soft painless posteromedial mass, MRI simple cyst?"** → **observation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Posteromedial Tibial Bowing

*Pediatrics · 86 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Associated calcaneovalgus foot & aetiology

**Cause** — **intrauterine positioning** · no genetic / familial pattern\
✗ Wrong options: AR · HOXD10 (vertical talus) · Shh (anteromedial bowing/fibular hemimelia) · fracture nonunion (anterolateral)

**Site** — **middle + distal third** of tibia (distal more common) · usually unilateral · posterior bow more severe than medial · angulation 25–70° (TO)

**Associated** — **calcaneovalgus foot** (✓ most common association; together or separately) · ankle dorsiflexion contracture · distal tibial epiphyseal valgus wedging, fibular hypoplasia · shortening present at birth

**Fracture / pseudarthrosis** — **rare** (unlike anterolateral bowing)

| Tibial bowing | Association |
|---|---|
| **Posteromedial** | Packaging · **calcaneovalgus** · **LLD** |
| Anteromedial | **Fibular hemimelia** (Shh) |
| Anterolateral | **NF1, congenital pseudarthrosis** |

## 3 Presentation

**At birth** — short, bowed leg · dorsum of foot may rest on anterior shin

**Apex** — posteromedial bowing apex in **distal tibia** · calcaneovalgus apex at **ankle** (Orthobullets) / heel (TO)

**Calcaneovalgus vs vertical talus** — **plantar-flexion lateral foot X-ray**: in CVT the talar axis stays plantar to (not collinear with) the 1st MT

**TO case** — neonate, convex posteromedial bow, calf atrophy, lateral dimple, thick anterolateral cortices, normal canal · options: constriction band / CPT / posteromedial bowing / fibular hemimelia · **answer not provided by the source**

![Posteromedial bowing vs calcaneovalgus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4057-cvf-vs-postero-medial-bowing.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral tibia · posteromedial bow obvious, normal bone quality

![Posteromedial tibial bowing X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4057-xray-ap-lat-leg-courtesy-miller-moved.png)

## 5 Management

### Natural history & residual LLD

**Bowing** — **spontaneously corrects over 5–7 yr** (~80% by age 5) · **observe** · calcaneovalgus → observation + parental stretching\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy · bracing / clamshell KAFO · serial casting (no effect; casting only for severe unresolving contracture) · genetic testing

**LLD = main problem** — proportional, ↑ with growth · ∝ amount of bow

| Source | Residual LLD |
|---|---|
| Orthobullets | **Average 3–4 cm** · residual 2–5 cm |
| OB | 3–8 cm · ~4 cm · 2–5 cm |
| TO | 3–7 cm |
| Di Gennaro (OB) | 4.3 cm |

**Surgery for LLD** — **>50%** of patients (Wright 53% lengthening)

| LLD (TO) | Residual angle | Ankle | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **<2 cm** | Resolved | Normal | None |
| **2–4 cm** | Resolved | Normal | **Contralateral tibial epiphysiodesis** |
| **>4 cm** | Resolved | Normal | **Tibial lengthening** |
| >2 cm | **Residual angulation** | — | Metaphyseal lengthening + diaphyseal osteotomy |
| Any | Any | **Ankle valgus** | Supramalleolar osteotomy / medial hemiepiphysiodesis |

**Most common complication needing surgery** → **LLD** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: calcaneovalgus · bowing · pseudarthrosis fracture

**Major deformity needing correction** → **LLD** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: posterior bow · medial bow

## How it's asked

**"Newborn posteromedial bowing, normal neuro + hips?"** → **observation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: casting · early osteotomy · bracing

**"Major deformity needing correction?"** → **LLD** (TO)

**"Most common complication requiring surgery?"** → **limb length discrepancy** (TO)

**"Infant posteromedial bow — associated condition?"** → **residual LLD**\
✗ Wrong options: pseudarthrosis · knee instability · coalition

**"2-yr posteromedial bowing — complication?"** → **LLD**\
✗ Wrong options: fibular hemimelia · NF

**"Healthy newborn — next step?"** → **observe for resolution + possible LLD**\
✗ Wrong options: AFO · NF gene counselling

**"Newborn isolated posteromedial bow?"** → **observe; bow resolves, LLD remains**\
✗ Wrong options: Syme · casting

**"3-yr — counsel family about?"** → **limb-length discrepancy**\
✗ Wrong options: NF · renal

**"2-month-old posteromedial bow?"** → **observe, revisit in 4 months**\
✗ Wrong options: clamshell · casts

**"2-yr, prognosis?"** → **resolves by ~5, will likely need LLD surgery**

**"Most common associated deformity?"** → **calcaneovalgus foot** (TO)

**"3-month-old tibial deformity — foot deformity?"** → **calcaneovalgus**\
✗ Wrong options: equinovarus · MA

**"Posteromedial bow + calcaneovalgus — etiology?"** → **intrauterine positioning**

**"2-week-old bowed leg — other finding?"** → **calcaneovalgus foot**\
✗ Wrong options: café-au-lait · limb lengthening

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Proximal Femoral Focal Deficiency

*Pediatrics · 87 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — defect of the **primary ossification centre (cartilage anlage)** of proximal femur · spectrum: **absent hip · femoral neck pseudarthrosis · absent femur · short femur**

**Epidemiology** — **bilateral 15%** · mostly **sporadic** · rare AD form (dysmorphic facies)

### Genes & lab markers

**Gene** — **sonic hedgehog** (limb bud)

### Associated ACL deficiency & knee instability

**Associated** — **fibular hemimelia (50%)** · **ACL deficiency / absent cruciates** · underdeveloped tibial spines · **coxa vara** · knee flexion contractures · acetabular dysplasia · ball-in-socket ankle

**ACL deficiency** → **knee subluxation during femoral lengthening** → examine with **Lachman**\
✗ Wrong options: dial · Galeazzi · Thessaly · varus stress

**Not associated** — clubfoot · café-au-lait · webbed neck (Turner) · scapular winging (Sprengel)

**ACL deficiency most likely in** — **PFFD**\
✗ Wrong options: cleidocranial (RUNX2) · Apert (FGFR2) · achondroplasia (FGFR3) · radial head dislocation

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — severe shortening · **% shortening constant with growth** · short bulky thigh **flexed, abducted, externally rotated** · feet usually normal

![PFFD clinical appearance](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4043-clinical-photo-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Aitken classification

| Class | Femoral head | Acetabulum |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Present | Normal |
| **B** | Present | Mildly dysplastic |
| **C** | **Absent** | Severely dysplastic |
| **D** | **Absent** | **Absent** |

**Femoral head ossifies ~6 months** → defer hip decisions until then

![Femoral neck pseudarthrosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4043-ap-xrays-cervical-pseudoarthrosis-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Individualise by** — final LLD · foot · musculature · **proximal (hip) stability**

```
PFFD
├─ Hip unstable (Aitken C/D) → femoral-pelvic fusion (Brown)
└─ Hip stable (A/B or stabilised)
       ├─ LLD <20 cm, femur >50%, functional foot → lengthening ± contralateral epiphysiodesis
       ├─ Foot at level of normal knee → Van Ness rotationplasty
       └─ Foot above normal knee → Syme + knee arthrodesis → AK prosthesis
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Lengthening ± contralateral epiphysiodesis** | Predicted LLD **<20 cm** · stable hip + functional foot · **femur >50%** of other side · **head present (Aitken A/B)** · not with unaddressed coxa vara, neck pseudarthrosis, acetabular dysplasia |
| **Knee arthrodesis + foot ablation (Syme)** | Foot **proximal to opposite knee** · prosthetic knee won't sit below opposite knee · Syme at 10–14 months, knee fusion 3–4 yr (OB) |
| **Femoral-pelvic fusion (Brown)** | **Femoral head absent (Aitken C/D)** |
| **Van Ness rotationplasty** | **Foot at level of opposite knee** · ankle **>60% motion** · absent head (C/D) · **180° rotation** → ankle DF becomes knee flexion → BK prosthesis |
| **Amputation** | Femur **<50%** · keep length · **through joint** (avoid overgrowth) · prosthesis after 1 yr |
| Observation / extension prosthesis | Bilateral PFFD |

### Treatment - rotationplasty

**11-week-old, ankle will reach normal knee level** → **delayed Van Ness rotationplasty + hip stabilisation at 1 yr if head deficient**\
✗ Wrong options: immediate femoral-pelvic fusion (head status unknown) · Syme + knee fusion (too short) · immediate lengthening

## How it's asked

**"ACL most likely deficient in?"** → **PFFD**

**"PFFD — test for condition causing knee subluxation with lengthening?"** → **Lachman**

**"6-yr PFFD — other finding?"** → **knee ligamentous laxity**\
✗ Wrong options: clubfoot · café-au-lait

**"Infant PFFD, ankle at normal knee level at maturity?"** → **delayed Van Ness rotationplasty, hip stabilisation at 1 yr if needed**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Proximal Femur Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 88 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **<1%** of pediatric fractures · **boys 2.5:1** · bimodal: **<2–3 yr (NAT)** and adolescents (MVA, ATV) · **high energy 75–80%** · low energy → pathologic bone

**Associated injuries** — **30–85%** (head/face, spleen, retroperitoneal bleed, perineum, pelvis/acetabulum, hip dislocation, femur)

### Anatomy

**Proximal femoral physis** — ossifies 4–6 months (girls), 5–7 (boys) · **3 mm/yr** · **13–15% of leg length**, 30% of femur · arrest → LLD >2 cm rare (only very young)

**Trochanteric apophysis** — ossifies ~4 yr · appositional GT growth · **GT injury/arrest → coxa VALGA** · **GT overgrowth → coxa VARA**

**Superior gluteal nerve (L4–S1)** — gluteus medius + minimus

### Vascular anatomy (MFCA)

| Age | Femoral head supply |
|---|---|
| **<4 yr** (OB: <18 months) | **MFCA + LFCA + ligamentum teres** (+ metaphyseal <3 yr) |
| ~18–36 months | Ligamentum teres stops first (Trueta) |
| **≥4 yr** (dominant by 6, Trueta) | **MFCA** via **posterosuperior + posteroinferior retinacular branches** |
| 3 to 14–17 yr | Physis blocks metaphyseal vessels |
| >14–17 yr | Metaphyseal-epiphyseal anastomoses |

**AVN after fracture** → retinacular branches of **MFCA**\
✗ Wrong options: LFCA · ligamentum teres

**Piriformis-entry nail contraindicated** → damages **posterosuperior MFCA** branches → AVN · use **lateral/trochanteric** entry for IM nails

![Blood supply of the proximal femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4018-blood-supply.jpg)

### Deforming forces

**Subtrochanteric proximal fragment** — **flexion** (iliopsoas: L1–3 / femoral n.) · **abduction** (gluteus medius/minimus — **superior gluteal nerve**) · **external rotation** (short rotators — sciatic)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — severe hip pain, can't bear weight · **shortened + externally rotated** leg · <2–3 yr type I → think **NAT** (skeletal survey)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + cross-table lateral · broken trabeculae at Ward triangle = undisplaced/impacted

**Occult / stress fracture** — **MRI best** (TO) · low-signal line + T2 edema · CT alternative · **US in infants** (hemarthrosis, epiphyseal mobility)

**Differential** — Perthes · transient synovitis · hemarthrosis · infection

### AVN risk by fracture type (Delbet)

| Type | Fracture | Incidence | AVN | Nonunion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Transphyseal** (IA no dislocation / **IB with epiphyseal dislocation**) | <10% | **38–100%** (IB ~100%) | — |
| **II** | **Transcervical** (most common) | 40–50% | **28%** (0–61% OB) | 15% |
| **III** | **Cervicotrochanteric / basicervical** | 30–35% (25–35% TO) | **18%** (27% OB · 20–30% OB) | 15–20% |
| **IV** | **Intertrochanteric** (best outcome) | 10–20% (6–15% TO) | **5%** (14% OB) | 5% |

**AVN ranking** — **IB > IA > II > III > IV** · I/II/III = 15×/6×/4× type IV risk · overall ~30%

![Delbet classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4018-delbet-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment & fixation

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Nondisplaced I (no dislocation), II, III, IV **and <4 yr** | **Closed reduction + abduction spica** (rare) · OB: spica only <6 yr types III/IV |
| Displaced / >4 yr, types I–III | **CRPP** (smooth pins <4 yr · cannulated screws >4 yr) |
| Can't get anatomic closed reduction | **Open reduction + screws** (Watson-Jones) |
| **Type IV** displaced / >4 yr | **Pediatric (or adult) sliding hip screw** (lateral approach) |
| Open fracture · vessel repair · **epiphyseal / hip dislocation (IB)** · marked displacement | **Emergent ORIF + capsulotomy / aspiration** |

**Implant by age (OB)** — 0–3 yr smooth wires · 4–10 yr 4.5–6.5 mm cannulated screws · >10 yr 6.5–7.3 mm screws \
✗ Wrong options: **cephalomedullary nail** · immediate WB

**Physis** — **short of physis** <4–6 yr, most type III (less stable) · **transphyseal** >12 yr, little metaphysis, or needed for stability (**type II: transphyseal** — poor head purchase otherwise) · "easier to treat LLD than nonunion" · within 5 mm of subchondral bone, avoid anterolateral epiphysis + posterior neck

**Reduction** — gentle traction + **abduction + internal rotation** · weekly X-rays ×3 wk if casted · accept type II **<2 mm translation, <5°, no malrotation** · III/IV **<10°**

**Post-op** — spica (abduction + IR) **6–12 wk** if <4 yr or screws short of physis

### Minimising AVN - anatomic reduction

**Most important factor** — **anatomic reduction** · open reduction OK if it gets anatomy\
✗ Wrong options: timing · closed vs open · capsulotomy · transphyseal screw

**Timing** — reduction **<24 h** may ↓ AVN (Orthobullets) · surgery **<48 h** ↓ AVN (OB4584) · delay to 24 h doesn't ↑ complications; literature conflicting (OB) — fix with rested experienced team

**Capsulotomy** — favoured by some, unproven · **skeletal traction ↑ intracapsular pressure** (avoid)

## 6 Complications

**AVN** — **most common** · kinked/torn vessels, intracapsular tamponade · risk: **type (I highest)**, displacement, **age (×1.14 per year; >10 yr)**, delay >24 h, poor reduction · presents **2–12 months** (groin/knee pain) · Ratliff I whole head, II partial, III neck · **core decompression**, vascularised fibula · annual X-rays to maturity (≥2 yr in older teens)

**Coxa vara** (NSA <120°) — **2nd most common** · non-op treatment, types I–III (**25% type III**; ~15% OB) · 0–3 yr remodels if NSA >110° · **GT apophyseal arrest** (mild, <6–8 yr) · **valgus osteotomy** (nonunion, Trendelenburg, FAI, older)

**Coxa valga** — type IV in young child → premature GT apophysis closure

**Nonunion** — non-op II/III, occult infection, malreduction → **ORIF** (spica if young) ± graft

**Physeal arrest** — implants across physis, AVN (Ratliff II/III) · LLD: lift <2 cm, **contralateral distal femur ± proximal tibia epiphysiodesis 2–5 cm**

**Chondrolysis** — rare, with AVN or hardware penetration · TO: "**not a sequel**" in its MCQ (✓ answer) despite listing it as rare

**Order (TO)** — **osteonecrosis > coxa vara > physeal arrest/LLD > nonunion > infection > chondrolysis**

**Poor function** — head trauma · amputation · nerve damage · AVN

## How it's asked

**"Pediatric hip fracture with maximum AVN?"** → **type I (transphyseal)** (TO)

**"8-yr basicervical fracture fixed — most common complication?"** → **AVN**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrolysis · LLD

**"14-yr displaced transcervical fixed — risk?"** → **AVN**\
✗ Wrong options: coxa vara

**"Order AVN risk IA, IB, II, III, IV?"** → **IB > IA > II > III > IV**

**"Highest osteonecrosis incidence?"** → **13-yr displaced transcervical femoral neck**\
✗ Wrong options: Garden I · Jones

**"4-yr basicervical — AVN risk?"** → **25%**

**"10-yr sled vs tree, transphyseal without dislocation — complication?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Delbet II — most likely complication?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"13-yr ATV type III — age raises risk of?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"13-yr femoral neck — true?"** → **closed reduction + transphyseal cannulated screws acceptable**\
✗ Wrong options: cephalomedullary nail · capsulotomy contraindicated

**"14-yr Delbet II?"** → **transphyseal cancellous screws**\
✗ Wrong options: physis-sparing · DHS · spica

**"11-yr, non-anatomic closed reduction?"** → **open reduction + screws**

**"Femoral epiphysis supply <18 months?"** → **MFCA + LFCA + ligamentum teres**

**"Juvenile intertroch/subtroch — preserve which artery?"** → **MFCA**

**"11-yr Delbet IB → AVN — vessel injured?"** → **MFCA**

**"Why no piriformis nail in a child?"** → **osteonecrosis from posterosuperior MFCA damage**

**"Most important in minimising AVN (type III)?"** → **anatomic reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: fixation within 2 h · traction · capsulotomy

**"Fixed at 28 h, anatomic — biggest AVN factor?"** → **anatomic reduction**

**"Subtrochanteric proximal fragment abduction — nerve?"** → **superior gluteal**

**"NOT a sequel of pediatric neck fracture?"** → **chondrolysis** (TO)

**"Best test for occult pediatric neck fracture?"** → **MRI** (TO)

# Proximal Humerus Fracture - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 89 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — <5% of pediatric fractures · **peak age 15**

**Pattern by age** — **<5 yr: SH I** · **5–12 yr: metaphyseal** · **>12 yr: SH II**

**Deforming forces** — epiphysis **abducted + externally rotated** (rotator cuff) · shaft **anterior, adducted, shortened** (pectoralis major, deltoid)

### Humeral growth contribution

**Proximal : distal humerus = 80 : 20** (highest ratio; femur 30 : 70) · proximal physis ~7 mm/yr → **huge remodeling**

**Ossification** — epiphysis 6 months · greater tuberosity 1–3 yr · lesser tuberosity 4–5 yr · closes 14–17 (girls), 16–18 (boys)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — shoulder pain, deformity, ecchymosis · check neck, SC joint, elbow · brachial plexus + vascular exam

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, **axillary / scapular Y** · ± contralateral · assess max angulation · **US in newborn** (unossified)

**Differential** — **Little Leaguer's shoulder** (throwers: physeal widening, metaphyseal change — not a fracture) · pathologic fracture (**UBC, ABC**)

![Little Leaguer's shoulder — physeal widening](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4004-screen-shot-2012-07-24-at-8-47-45-am.jpg)

### Neer-Horowitz classification

| Type | Displacement |
|---|---|
| I | **<5 mm** |
| II | **<⅓** shaft width |
| III | **⅓–⅔** |
| IV | **>⅔** |

![Neer-Horowitz type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4004-type-4.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment by age & displacement

| Age | Acceptable alignment (non-op) |
|---|---|
| **<10 yr** | **Any angulation** |
| **10–12 yr** | Up to **60–75°** |
| **>12 yr** | Up to **45°** or **⅔ displacement** |

**Rockwood (OB)** — reduce ± fix if **>50% displaced in >11 yr**

**Non-op** — sling ± swathe, immobiliser, coaptation splint · 7-yr Neer-Horowitz IV → **sling + PT** · 9-yr displaced → **sling + follow-up X-rays**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · ORIF (higher complications)

**Older adolescent (≥15, <1 yr growth) Neer-Horowitz III/IV** → **closed reduction + percutaneous pinning** · 13-yr marked varus SH II → **closed/open reduction + pins** (varus = lost abduction)\
✗ Wrong options: shoulder immobiliser · hanging arm cast · spica (redisplaces) · hemiarthroplasty

**Outcome by age** — older patients with III/IV treated non-op → **↑ limitations in daily activities** vs younger · long-term (Lähdeoja, 13 yr) no impairment vs controls, op = non-op\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · more displacement

**Reduction** — traction + **90° abduction + ER**

**Fixation** — 2–3 lateral threaded pins (avoid axillary n. laterally, musculocutaneous anteriorly) · retrograde elastic nails · cannulated screws in older teens

**Open reduction** — irreducible from **interposed long head of biceps (most common)**, capsule, periosteum, deltoid · **open fracture** · **vascular injury** · intra-articular displacement · deltopectoral approach

## 6 Complications

**Loss of reduction** — unstable fracture reduced without pins

**Axillary nerve** — <1% from injury (neurapraxia, **medially displaced shaft**) · ↑ with pins → lateral pin below nerve (~5 cm below acromion in adults; = 2× humeral head height)

**Others** — varus malunion → impingement · shortening (<3 cm tolerated <12 yr) · growth arrest rare · hypertrophic scar · **pin-site infection 5–22%**

**Prognosis** — excellent (remodeling + shoulder ROM)

## How it's asked

**"9-yr skateboard, displaced proximal humerus?"** → **sling + follow-up X-rays**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · ORIF

**"16-yr, <1 yr growth — which needs CRPP?"** → **Neer-Horowitz III physeal fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: type I · 10° metaphyseal

**"13-yr hockey SH II, marked varus — best motion?"** → **closed or open reduction + percutaneous pins**\
✗ Wrong options: spica

**"15-yr displaced proximal humerus?"** → **closed reduction + percutaneous pins**\
✗ Wrong options: hanging cast · hemiarthroplasty

**"7-yr Neer-Horowitz IV?"** → **sling, graduated PT, monitoring**

**"12-yr gymnast, non-op — vs younger child?"** → **↑ risk of activity limitations**

**"Proximal : distal humeral growth?"** → **80 : 20**
