# Proximal Tibia Epiphyseal Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 90 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — <1% of pediatric fractures · **12–14 yr** · high energy · varus/valgus or **hyperextension**

**Associated** — part of **type III tibial tubercle fracture** · **popliteal artery injury (5%)** — **hyperextension** · peroneal nerve (5%) · **ligament injury up to 40% in SH III/IV** · **compartment syndrome 3–4%**

### Anatomy

**Physis** — proximal tibia **6 mm/yr** · knee = 15 of 23 mm/yr leg growth · closes **posterior → anterior**, **medial → lateral**, **posteromedial → anterolateral**

**Buttresses** — **superficial MCL inserts below physis** (medial buttress) · **LCL to fibula** (lateral buttress) · **patellar tendon** (resists posterior displacement) → physeal fractures are rare

**Popliteal artery** — **tethered** to posterior proximal tibia by septa · **trifurcation at physeal level** → injury with displacement · geniculate arteries around popliteus

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — can't bear weight · physeal tenderness · step-off · varus/valgus instability · **thorough neurovascular exam**

**Normal X-ray + exquisite physeal tenderness after high-energy hit** → may have displaced + reduced → **splint, admit, frequent neurovascular / compartment checks** (treat like knee dislocation) · MRI/CT to define\
✗ Wrong options: cylinder cast + discharge · arthroscopy · prophylactic fasciotomy

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP, lateral ± obliques · stress views risk physis

**CT** — displacement · **best for SH III / IV**

### Salter-Harris classification

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| **I** | Mean age 12 · usually displaced (>50%) |
| **II** | Mean age 14 · usually displaced (>67%) · **medial gapping + lateral Thurston-Holland + proximal fibula fracture** |
| III | Exits epiphysis · usually **tibial tubercle** fractures |
| IV | Physis + metaphysis + epiphysis |

![Proximal tibial SH II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4024-sh-2.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Fracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Non-displaced (<2 mm), stable SH I/II | **Long leg cast, slight flexion, 6 wk** · redisplacement common |
| **Unstable SH I/II** · redisplaced | **CRPP** |
| **Irreducible** (periosteal flap) · **displaced >2 mm SH III/IV** · **vascular injury** | **ORIF** |

**Fixation** — **crossed smooth pins**: **transphyseal** for SH I/II with small Thurston-Holland · **extraphyseal** for SH III/IV · **cannulated screws parallel to physis** for SH II with large metaphyseal fragment or III/IV · cast in slight flexion 4–6 wk

**Approach** — anterior midline (patella → tubercle) · **medial approach if vascular injury**

![ORIF proximal tibial physeal fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4024-orif.jpg)

### Popliteal artery injury & vascular monitoring

**Hyperextension physeal fracture** → **popliteal artery** most at risk\
✗ Wrong options: tibial n. · peroneal n. · PCL

## 6 Complications

**Growth disturbance 25%** (LLD, angulation; ↑ open fractures) · loss of reduction · compartment syndrome · ligament instability

## How it's asked

**"9-yr hyperextension proximal tibial physeal fracture — at risk?"** → **popliteal artery**

**"11-yr sled hit, normal X-rays, tender physis, NV intact?"** → **splint, admit, frequent neurovascular checks**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Proximal Tibia Metaphyseal Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 91 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **3–6 yr** (2–10 yr OB) · "Cozen fracture"

**Mechanism** — low-energy **valgus** force → **incomplete (greenstick/torus)** medial fracture · torsion · classic: **down a slide on an adult's lap**, leg extended · fibula fracture = higher energy, less stable

**Anatomy** — posterior tibial artery → nutrient (inner ⅔) + periosteal vessels

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — pain, refuses to bear weight · little swelling / deformity · check compartments

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**X-ray** — AP + lateral · complete vs incomplete · displacement · usually **valgus** · fibula fracture

![Proximal tibial metaphyseal fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4025-proximal-tibia-fracture-cropped.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Initial treatment & counselling

| Fracture | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Nondisplaced | **Long leg cast in EXTENSION with VARUS mold** (slight overcorrection) 4–6 wk · WB at 2–3 wk |
| Displaced | **Reduction under sedation** (complete the greenstick) → same cast, three-point varus mold |
| Irreducible (rare) | **Limited medial open reduction** — remove interposed **periosteum, pes tendons, MCL** ± crossed K-wires |

**Counsel before treatment** — valgus may develop despite perfect care but **often resolves spontaneously**\
✗ Wrong options: knee immobilizer · prophylactic surgery · "always needs surgery"

## 6 Complications

### Post-traumatic valgus (Cozen) - natural history & observation

**Cozen phenomenon** — **late valgus** · etiology unknown (medial physeal overgrowth / stimulation, lateral tether, pes anserinus loss) · **occurs regardless of treatment** · not physeal arrest

| Feature | Value |
|---|---|
| Incidence | **50–90%** (14–100% OB) |
| Appears | **5–15 months** |
| Maximum | **12–18 months**, average **18°** |
| Resolves | By **~3 yr**, residual ~6° (can leave S-shaped tibia) |
| Risk | Incomplete reduction · physeal injury · infolded periosteum · pes injury |

**Treatment** — **observation 12–24 months** · surgery only **>15–20° near skeletal maturity** → **medial proximal tibial hemiepiphysiodesis** or varus proximal tibia/fibula osteotomy\
✗ Wrong options: KAFO · hemiepiphysiodesis early · osteotomy · ring fixator · MRI · bar resection

**LLD** — affected tibia **longer** (~9 mm) · **doesn't resolve** · usually no treatment

![Cozen valgus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4025-valgus-xr.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"3-yr, 6 months after metaphyseal fracture, valgus?"** → **follow-up X-ray in 6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: hemiepiphysiodesis · osteotomy · KAFO

**"Which can be observed alone?"** → **5-yr with 15° valgus 8 months after fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: Blount MDA 18° · SCFE · AIS 80°

**"5-yr trampoline proximal tibia — counsel about?"** → **valgus**

**"6-yr, 15° valgus 1 yr after healed fracture?"** → **continued observation**

**"4-yr knock-knee 9 months after fracture?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: stapling · bar resection

**"5-yr proximal tibia — deformity + treatment?"** → **valgus; observation**

**"Deformity within a year — initial treatment?"** → **observation**

**"13 months post-injury, leg looks funny?"** → **maximum ~18 months, resolves over ~3 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: shorter leg · short immobilisation

**"Valgus 12 months after perfectly reduced injury?"** → **late valgus from proximal tibial metaphyseal fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin D · physiologic bowing

**"4-yr proximal tibia fracture — tell parents?"** → **reduce + long leg cast; deformity may develop but often resolves**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Psoas Abscess - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 92 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **more common in the tropics** · primary form favours children

**Organism** — ***S. aureus*** most common

**Spread to hip** — via **psoas bursa** (communicates with hip joint in **15%**) · or directly between iliofemoral + iliopubic ligaments → septic arthritis

**Associated** — septic hip · **tuberculous spondylitis** · bowel source (secondary)

## 3 Presentation

**Mimics septic hip** — **fever, hip pain**, limp / refuses weight

**Exam** — **hip held in flexion** · tender iliac fossa mass · **psoas sign**: pain with **extension + internal rotation**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + frog pelvis to exclude septic hip · enlarged / blurred psoas shadow

**Ultrasound** — **study of choice** (imaging section) · summary says MRI generally required — read the options

**MRI** — early changes · hard to get quickly

**CT with contrast** — mass with **ring enhancement** · may be negative early · radiation

**Bone scan** — helpful

![Psoas abscess on ultrasound](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12779-psoas-abscess.jpg)

![Psoas abscess on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12779-psoas-mri.jpg)

## 5 Management

**First line** — **percutaneous US/CT-guided drainage + IV antibiotics**

**Open (extraperitoneal) drainage** — **secondary abscess** (e.g. bowel) to address source

## 6 Complications

**Septic arthritis** of hip · **sepsis**

## X vs Y

| | **Psoas abscess** | **Septic hip** |
|---|---|---|
| Hip X-ray | No effusion widening | Effusion / widening |
| Exam | Psoas sign, iliac fossa mass | Pain with all motion |
| Treatment | Percutaneous drainage | Arthrotomy / I&D |

## How it's asked

**"17-month-old, fever, hip flexed, pain on extension + IR, CT iliopsoas mass?"** → **psoas abscess**\
✗ Wrong options: septic arthritis · sacroiliitis · transient synovitis

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radial Head and Neck Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 93 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **5–10% of pediatric elbow fractures** · 1% of all · 3rd most common elbow fracture (OB) · median age **9–10** · M = F

**Site** — **90% physis or metaphysis (neck)** · ~50% metaphyseal, 50% SH I/II · articular head fracture only 4–7% with open physes (52–96% after closure)

**Mechanism** — FOOSH with **extension + valgus** · elbow dislocation

**Associated (30–50%)** — **elbow dislocation** · **medial epicondyle** · olecranon · condyles · **forearm compartment syndrome**

**Anatomy** — radial head ossifies **3–5 yr** (may be bipartite) · fuses 16–18 · **annular ligament covers epiphysis + physis** → stress riser at metaphysis → **neck fractures** · thick epiphyseal cartilage resists articular extension · head blood supply mainly **metaphyseal**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — lateral swelling · pain with **pronation/supination** · may refer to wrist · **watch for forearm compartment syndrome** (rising forearm pain overnight)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral · **radiocapitellar (Greenspan) view**: elbow 90°, thumb up, beam 45° proximal

![Greenspan (radiocapitellar) view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4011-shows-the-technique-to-obtain-a-greenspan-view.jpg)

**Lines** — radiocapitellar line (unreliable in young, esp. AP) · **lateral humeral line** (tangent to lateral condyle, lies lateral to radial neck) — more reliable in young children

**Pitfalls** — nondisplaced hard to see · **radial head flipped 180°** · posterior fat pad always pathologic · **neck partly extra-articular → effusion / fat pad may be absent**

**CT** — comminuted head in older child · **MRI** — rare (unossified elbow, anterior head compression with ligament injury)

### Classification

| O'Brien | Angulation |
|---|---|
| I | <30° |
| II | 30–60° |
| III | >60° |

| Judet | Angulation |
|---|---|
| I | Undisplaced |
| II | <30° |
| III | 30–60° |
| IVa | 60–80° |
| IVb | >80° |

**Chambers** — group 1 primary radial head displacement (most common; valgus: SH I/II, SH III/IV, metaphyseal; dislocation: reduction / dislocation injury) · group 2 radial neck displacement (**Monteggia variant**) · group 3 stress (**OCD**)

![Judet classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4011-illustration-showing-judet-classification-of-radial-neck-fra.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Pediatric radial neck fracture
├─ <30° and <3 mm translation → long arm cast/splint, no reduction (7–21 days, then ROM)
├─ >30° → closed reduction (Patterson, Israeli, Neher-Torch, Esmarch)
│      (stable once reduced — fixation usually not needed)
├─ Residual >30° / >3–4 mm / <45° pro-supination → percutaneous reduction (K-wire joystick, Metaizeau) ± pin
└─ Can't get <45° → open reduction (lateral approach, forearm pronated)
```

### Treatment by angulation (closed, percutaneous, open)

**Closed reduction** — 45° or 35° angulation → **attempt closed reduction** · >60° unlikely to close-reduce (OB) · some tolerate up to 60° (Radomisli) · >50° often needs fixation (Nicholson & Skaggs)\
✗ Wrong options: cast without reduction · percutaneous first · open

**Techniques** — **Patterson**: elbow extended, traction, supination, **varus** + direct radial head pressure · **Israeli (Kaufman)**: elbow 90°, **pronate** the supinated forearm with thumb on head · **Neher-Torch**: extension, supination, traction, varus; assistant pushes shaft lateral, surgeon pushes head medial · **Esmarch** wrap → spontaneous reduction

![Patterson maneuver](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4011-shown-is-an-illustration-of-the-patterson-maneuver-used-in-r.jpg)

**Percutaneous** — **K-wire joystick** (push posterolateral / lever) · **Metaizeau**: retrograde flexible nail across fracture, rotate to reduce · hardware out at ~8 wk · after failed ED reduction with >30° (e.g. 62°) → **percutaneous reduction ± pin / ESIN**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 6 wk · CT · ORIF first · plate · epiphysiodesis

**Open** — widely displaced head fracture with radial subluxation → **ORIF** · **lateral approach**, **pronate forearm to protect PIN** · fix only if unstable · no **transcapitellar pins**

**Outcome** — better if younger, less angulation, isolated neck · open reduction → more stiffness, **AVN, synostosis**

## 6 Complications

### Complications & prognosis

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **↓ ROM** | **Most common (10–31%)** · pronation > supination |
| Radial head overgrowth | 20–40% · rarely matters |
| **Osteonecrosis** | 10–20% · **up to 70% after open reduction** |
| **PIN palsy** | ~15% after surgery |
| Physeal arrest | → cubitus valgus |
| **Synostosis** | **Most serious** · open reduction, extensive dissection, delay |
| Malunion (intra-articular) | Radiocapitellar instability, pain, arthritis |
| **Forearm compartment syndrome** | Increasing pain + swelling overnight |

**Worse outcome** — **age >10** · intra-articular (50% complications, 25% revision vs 0% extra-articular)\
✗ Wrong options: BMI · dominance · sex

### PIN injury

**PIN** — branch of radial nerve wrapping radial neck · **loss of thumb IP extension (EPL)**, finger extension incl. **small finger (EDM)**, partial wrist extension · supplies posterior compartment (except anconeus) + ECRB · **pins posterolateral with arm in maximal pronation**

## How it's asked

**"10-yr, 45° physeal radial neck — first step?"** → **attempt closed reduction**

**"12-yr, >30° after ED reduction?"** → **further reduction + percutaneous ESIN/pin**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 6 wk · CT · ORIF

**"10-yr, 35° within 2 hours?"** → **closed reduction in ER**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · open

**"9-yr, 62° residual after sedated reduction?"** → **percutaneous reduction ± pin**

**"8-yr displaced radial head fracture + anterior subluxation after reduction attempt?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: annular ligament reconstruction · repeat closed

**"6-yr radial neck fracture, increasing forearm pain overnight?"** → **forearm compartment syndrome**

**"Factor for poor surgical outcome?"** → **age >10 yr**

**"7-yr after percutaneous pinning, can't extend thumb IP — nerve origin?"** → **radial nerve (PIN)**\
✗ Wrong options: median · ulnar

**"14-yr open reduction, nerve palsy — deficit?"** → **small finger extension**\
✗ Wrong options: thumb IP flexion (AIN) · opposition

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rett Syndrome

*Pediatrics · 94 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **1 in 10,000** · **girls** · males die before term

### Genetics - X-linked dominant MECP2

**Inheritance** — **X-linked dominant**\
✗ Wrong options: AR · AD · mitochondrial · XLR

**Gene** — **MECP2** (methyl-CpG-binding protein 2, DNA methylation) · **95% de novo** · on paternal X · affects grey matter

**Associated** — developmental delay · **seizures (80%)** · screaming, loss of speech · constipation · spasticity + contractures · ataxia · hypotonia · **bruxism** · chorea-like hand movements · cold blue extremities · **coxa vara**

## 3 Presentation

**Course** — normal to **6–18 months** → rapid regression to age 3 → plateau to 10 → **half can't walk after 10** · **first sign: apraxia (loss of purposeful hand use)**

**Exam** — ↓ head circumference (from 5–6 months) · abnormal gait, breathing · dementia-like slowing · **diagnosis** clinical + developmental history (genetic testing may miss some)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Scoliosis** — **C-shaped (long) curve** · by **~age 10** · **>50%**

| C-shaped curve in Rett syndrome | Rett syndrome |
|---|---|
| ![C-shaped curve in Rett syndrome](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4107-rett-xray.jpg) | ![Rett syndrome](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4107-rett.jpg) |

## 5 Management

**Non-op** — supportive (feeding, hygiene, seizures) · **PT to prevent hand contractures**

**Scoliosis** — **bracing ineffective** · **PSF** when thoracic curve hurts sitting/balance · **include entire scoliotic + kyphotic curve**

## How it's asked

**"14-yr girl, seizures, delay, C-shaped curve, MECP2 — inheritance?"** → **X-linked dominant**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sacral Agenesis

*Pediatrics · 95 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Clinical features & maternal diabetes

**Definition** — partial/complete absence of sacrum + lower lumbar spine · part of **caudal regression syndrome**

**Incidence** — 1–2.5 per 100,000

**Risk factor** — **maternal diabetes** (poor glucose control early pregnancy)\
✗ Wrong options: folate deficiency (myelodysplasia) · alcohol · drugs

**Neurology** — **motor deficit matches the level** · **protective sensation usually intact** (sensory doesn't match level) → **fewer decubitus ulcers**\
✗ Wrong options: like myelodysplasia

**Progression** — neurologic deficit present at birth + static (OB6072) · OB2199: neuro + MSK manifestations may be progressive — read the options

**Associated** — **imperforate anus** / GI · GU · cardiac · LE deformities · **progressive kyphosis**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — prominent last vertebra (T12) · **buttock dimpling** · **"sitting Buddha" posture** · **popliteal webbing**, extended or flexed knees, flexed hips, **rigid equinovarus** feet · motion at spine-pelvis junction

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Renshaw classification

| Type | Defect |
|---|---|
| I | Partial/total **unilateral** sacral agenesis |
| II | Partial sacrum, **bilaterally symmetric** defect |
| III | **Ilium articulates with sides of lowest vertebra** |
| IV | Lowest vertebral endplate **rests above fused ilia** / iliac amphiarthrosis |

![Renshaw type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4042-t4.jpg)

![Sitting Buddha posture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4042-sit.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Type | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **I–II** | **PT** · most become community ambulators · treat foot/knee deformity |
| **III–IV** with progressive kyphosis/scoliosis | **Spinopelvic stabilisation (fusion)** → **sitting balance + frees hands** (✗ Wrong options: goal of ambulation) |
| Non-functional limbs | Amputation → better mobility |

**Untreated complete absence** → progressive spinopelvic kyphosis → **must use hands to sit**\
✗ Wrong options: progressive paralysis · hip dislocation

## How it's asked

**"6-yr, colorectal surgery at birth, sits only with hands — true?"** → **motor deficits match level, sensory deficits don't**\
✗ Wrong options: protective sensation absent · fusion for ambulation · folate

**"2-yr complete sacral + lower lumbar absence, no stabilisation — outcome?"** → **can't sit without hand support**

**"Newborn, knee webbing, rigid clubfeet, Buddha posture, absent lumbar spine — maternal condition?"** → **diabetes mellitus**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sever's Disease

*Pediatrics · 96 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **calcaneal apophysitis** · **most common cause of heel pain in growing children** (TO) · traction apophysitis + repetitive microtrauma (like Osgood-Schlatter) · ± para-apophyseal stress fracture (OB)

**Who** — running/jumping athletes (soccer, basketball, volleyball) · **just before / during peak growth** · ages **9–12** · boys ~12, girls ~11 · **bilateral ~60%** (TO)

**Forces** — heel-strike impact + opposing pull of **Achilles (gastrocsoleus)** and plantar fascia

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis

**Symptoms** — posterior heel pain with activity / impact, relieved by rest · ± warmth, erythema, swelling

**Exam** — **tight Achilles** (↓ dorsiflexion) · **positive squeeze test** (medial-lateral calcaneal compression) · apophyseal tenderness · pain on triceps surae stretch · difficulty single-leg heel raise · Thompson negative

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — diagnosis is **clinical** · **sclerosis** in both affected and normal · **fragmentation** more frequent in Sever · rules out osteomyelitis, cysts

![Sever's disease — sclerotic fragmented apophysis](../../MCQ/attachments/q3898-b-sever-disease-lateral-1.jpg)

**MRI** — localises apophyseal inflammation · excludes stress fracture, lytic lesion, osteomyelitis (usually unnecessary)

**Bone scan** — ↑ apophyseal uptake, not helpful

**Incidental calcaneal cyst** — treat only if full width or ⅓ of calcaneal length

![Calcaneal apophysis fragmentation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4074-sever.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment - activity modification

**Non-op** — **activity modification** · **Achilles stretching** (↓ recurrence) · ice · **heel cups/pads** · NSAIDs · **short leg cast** if persistent

**Never** — **surgery** · custom orthotics not indicated · curettage of incidental cyst\
✗ Wrong options: calcaneal epiphysiodesis · Achilles lengthening

**Prognosis** — **self-limiting**, resolves with apophyseal closure · recurrence common

## X vs Y

| Heel pain | Clue |
|---|---|
| **Sever** | Squeeze test +, apophysis, 9–12 yr athlete |
| Achilles rupture | **Thompson +** |
| Os trigonum | Posterior impingement, ballet |
| Gastrocnemius strain | Proximal myotendinous junction |
| Plantar fasciitis | Anterior heel pad, no squeeze pain |
| Baxter nerve | Arch pain like plantar fasciitis |
| Haglund | Posterolateral prominence, women 15–35 in heels |
| Kohler | Dorsomedial midfoot, navicular |
| Coalition | Recurrent ankle sprains |

## How it's asked

**"9-yr player, activity heel pain relieved by rest?"** → **Sever's disease** (TO)

**"12-yr soccer, warm tender calcaneal tuberosity, imaging?"** → **Sever's disease**\
✗ Wrong options: stress fracture · osteomyelitis · Kohler

**"12-yr, DF 5°, squeeze +, Thompson −?"** → **calcaneal apophysitis**\
✗ Wrong options: Achilles rupture · os trigonum

**"11-yr volleyball, squeeze +, etiology?"** → **Achilles traction apophysitis**\
✗ Wrong options: Baxter nerve · plantar fascia

**"14-yr peak growth, red swollen heel, squeeze +?"** → **Sever's disease**\
✗ Wrong options: Haglund · retrocalcaneal bursitis

**"11-yr basketball heel pain — image?"** → **fragmented calcaneal apophysis**\
✗ Wrong options: Kohler · Iselin · coalition

**"10-yr soccer, tight Achilles, 2-cm anterior calcaneal cyst?"** → **observation with reduced activity**\
✗ Wrong options: curettage · bone scan

**"10-yr year-round soccer, heel pain every step?"** → **activity modification**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · orthotics · epiphysiodesis

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# SI Joint Infection - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 97 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — rare · **1–2% of septic arthritis** · children >10 yr more than adults

**Adult risk factors** — IV drug use · immunosuppression · pregnancy · trauma · endocarditis · sepsis · TB

### Causative organism

| Organism | Note |
|---|---|
| ***S. aureus*** | **Most common** (✗ Wrong options: GBS (neonatal osteomyelitis) · H. influenzae (vaccine)) |
| *M. tuberculosis* | Skeletal TB = 3–5% of TB; ~10% of that at SIJ |
| *Salmonella* | Sickle cell disease |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — progressive **low back + buttock pain** · **can't bear weight** on that side · **fever**

**Exam** — pain with **iliac wing compression** + SI palpation · **normal hip ROM** · **FABER strongly positive**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP/lateral pelvis ± inlet/outlet · late: destruction, fusion, periarticular osteopenia, widening, cavitation

**MRI** — **most sensitive** (> CT) · acute: fluid/gas, subchondral edema, enhancement · chronic: marrow reconversion, pannus, erosion, sclerosis, ankylosis

**Labs** — ↑ WBC · **ESR >50** · **CRP >70** · **blood cultures before antibiotics** (often guide treatment; SIJ aspiration usually unnecessary)

![SI joint infection on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4033-joint-infection.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **First line** | **Organism-targeted IV antibiotics** (cover S. aureus if cultures negative) |
| Abscess · failed antibiotics · sequestrum | **Debridement** (rare in children) |
| Chronic · sequestrum with joint destruction · recurrent | **Debridement + SI arthrodesis** (longer, more complications) |

![SI joint arthrodesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4033-si-1.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**List** — chronic pain · recurrence · wound healing delay · hardware problems · sepsis

## How it's asked

**"Most common organism in acute pediatric SI septic arthritis?"** → **Staphylococcus aureus**\
✗ Wrong options: GBS · TB · Salmonella

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Slipped Capital Femoral Epiphysis (SCFE)

*Pediatrics · 98 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **most common adolescent hip disorder** · 10 per 100,000

**Who** — **obese** (single greatest risk) · **males** (2:1.4 Orthobullets; ~2× TO) · **African American**, Pacific Islander, Latino · rapid growth **10–16 yr** · boys **12–13.4**, girls **11.2–12.2** · **left hip** more common (2× TO)

**Bilateral** — 17–50%, average **25%** (Orthobullets) · 25–40% (TO) · 20–30% at presentation (TO) · second slip usually within **12–18 months**

**Risk factors** — obesity · **acetabular + femoral retroversion** (↓ anteversion, ↓ neck-shaft angle) · prior **radiation** · **↑ leptin (4.9× odds, independent of obesity)** · Down syndrome · open triradiate

### Pathophysiology

**Slip** — **metaphysis moves ANTERIOR + externally rotates** · epiphysis stays in acetabulum, **posterior/inferior** to neck

**Plane** — through the **hypertrophic zone** (✓) · renal SCFE through **secondary spongiosa**

**Why adolescents** — **perichondral ring thins** · mammillary processes unlock · physis still vertical (160° birth → 125° maturity) → shear · epiphyseal tubercle pivot

**vs SH I fracture** — antecedent epiphysiolysis, slower, periosteum intact (chronic) · type I transepiphyseal fracture = high energy, young child, M = F (TO)

### Histopathology

**Chronic SCFE histology** — **granulation tissue between columns in hypertrophic zone** · ↓ perichondral ring thickness · ↓ physeal undulations · abnormal proteoglycan accumulation · ↑ hypertrophic zone height\
✗ Wrong options: vascular invasion of proliferative zone · thick ring

### Endocrine & metabolic associations

**Endocrine** — **hypothyroidism (most common non-idiopathic; ↑ TSH)** · **renal osteodystrophy** (↑ BUN, creatinine) · **GH deficiency** · **panhypopituitarism** (pituitary adenoma) · Loder: hypothyroidism 40%, GH deficiency 25%, all hypothyroid → bilateral

**Endocrine work-up if** — **<10 yr** · **weight <50th percentile** · prepubertal / short · labs: **TSH, free T4, BUN, creatinine**

**Atypical SCFE** — <10 or >16 yr (TO) · renal failure, radiation, hypogonadism, Down

### Blood supply

**Lateral epiphyseal artery (MFCA)** = main supply · LFCA anterior ring · ligamentum teres insignificant · **posterosuperior** hardware endangers vessels

## 3 Presentation

### Presentation & diagnosis (knee pain, hip films)

**Pain** — hip 52%, **thigh 35%**, groin 14% · **knee pain 15–50%** (23% Matava; obturator nerve referral) · hip pain still more common than knee\
✗ Wrong options: "knee more often than hip"

**Delay in diagnosis** — **knee pain** · stable slip · Medicaid · severity · 88% of unstable slips had ~42 days of prior symptoms

**Exam** — antalgic / Trendelenburg / out-toeing gait · **obligatory ER with hip flexion (Drehmann sign)** · ↓ **IR**, abduction, flexion · sits with leg crossed · tender Scarpa triangle (TO)

**Obese teen with knee pain + normal knee X-rays** → **examine hip + AP pelvis/frog-lateral**\
✗ Wrong options: knee MRI · arthroscopy · labs · PT

**Unmanipulated severe SCFE on AP** → leg lies in **external rotation**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP + frog-lateral of both hips** · lateral best for subtle slip · **unstable → cross-table lateral** (not frog)

**Klein line** — along superior neck · normally cuts lateral epiphysis · SCFE: intersects less / not at all · ≥2 mm asymmetry improves sensitivity 40% → ~80% (TO)

**Other signs** — **metaphyseal blanch sign of Steel** (double density, AP) · **S sign** broken on frog · **epiphysiolysis** (early widening)

**MRI** — **pre-slip**: physeal widening + **metaphyseal edema** (T1 ↓, T2 ↑) · CT: physeal closure, osteotomy planning

![Klein line](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4040-kleins-line-moved.jpg)

### Loder classification (stability)

| Type | Definition | AVN |
|---|---|---|
| **Stable** (85–95%) | Can walk ± crutches | **<10%** (~0%) |
| **Unstable** (5–15%) | **Can't walk even with crutches** | **24–47%** (Orthobullets) · 47–50% · 25–50% · 15–50% · up to 60% (OB) · 2–5× stable |

**Temporal** — acute <3 wk · chronic >3 wk · acute-on-chronic (no prognostic value)

### Slip severity measurement

**Southwick (epiphyseal-shaft) angle** — **severity** · frog lateral (± AP) · affected minus normal · bilateral reference **145° AP / 10° lateral** (Orthobullets) · 12° (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: presence · AVN prognosis · chronicity

| Severity | Orthobullets | OB / TO |
|---|---|---|
| Mild | **<30°** | <30° |
| Moderate | **30–50°** | 30–60° |
| Severe | **>50°** | >60° |

**% displacement grade** — I 0–33% · II 34–50% · III >50%

![Southwick angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4040-southwick-angle.jpg)

**Differential** — septic hip, transient synovitis, osteomyelitis, Perthes, DDH, trauma (adductor strain, AIIS avulsion) · breaststroke swimmer, open physes, normal head-neck → **external rotator (obturator externus) tear**

## 5 Management

```
SCFE
├─ Stable → single central screw in situ (no reduction), WB after
├─ Unstable → urgent bed rest → in situ fixation ± 2nd screw, ± decompression/capsulotomy, TDWB
├─ High-risk contralateral → prophylactic pinning
└─ Residual deformity → osteochondroplasty (<30°) / Imhauser osteotomy (>30–45°)
```

### In situ fixation technique

**Rule** — **percutaneous in situ fixation for stable AND unstable** · **no forceful reduction** (↑ AVN) · serendipitous positional reduction OK

**Screw** — **single cannulated screw** usually (↓ AVN vs multiple) · **2 screws more stable** (some for unstable) · not inverted triangle (adult neck fracture, ↑ penetration + AVN)

**Placement** — **start on ANTERIOR femoral neck** → **perpendicular to physis** → **centre-centre** of epiphysis on AP + lateral · **not medial to intertrochanteric line** (screw head impinges, labral tear) · severe slip → more oblique from intertrochanteric region

**Depth** — **≥5 threads across physis** (<5 → 41% progression vs 0%) · tip ~5 mm from subchondral bone · partially threaded: 40–60% of threads in epiphysis strongest · **approach-withdraw** fluoroscopy to exclude penetration

**Post-op** — stable WB · unstable TDWB · young child: short-thread screw not crossing physis (TO)

**Stable mild slip in 13-yr** → **single-screw in situ fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: spica (chondrolysis) · ORIF · NWB · closed reduction

**Stable SCFE seen only on MRI** → **in situ fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · labral repair

![In situ screw fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4040-scfe4-pinning.jpg)

### Unstable SCFE & AVN

**Unstable slip** → **strict bed rest + urgent surgery** (within hours; 8 h OB, 24 h OB) on affected hip\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · elective · NSAIDs trial

**AVN risk factors** — **instability (greatest)** · initial displacement · **forceful / over-reduction**, reduction of chronic slip · **posterosuperior (superolateral) hardware** · multiple pins · femoral neck osteotomy\
✗ Wrong options: age, sex, obesity · single screw · in situ without reduction

**Capsulotomy / decompression** — unstable ICP ~2× normal · may ↓ AVN (low evidence) · TO advocates routine capsulotomy in acute unstable · **ICP probe monophasic waveform** → **decompress hip, then capsulotomy if still monophasic**

**Modified Dunn (surgical dislocation, capital realignment)** — unstable + severe · posterior retinacular flap carries blood supply · remove posterior callus · AVN **~26%** vs 24% in situ · complications 37% · steep learning curve

**Most dreaded complication** — **AVN** (TO); MFCA injury

![Modified Dunn](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4040-illustration-of-modified-dunn.jpg)

### Contralateral prophylactic pinning

| Indication | NOT an indication |
|---|---|
| **Age <10** (girls <10, boys <12–12.5) | Unstable presenting slip |
| **Open triradiate cartilage** | Chronicity / duration of symptoms |
| **Endocrinopathy** (hypothyroid, renal, GH, pituitary adenoma) | Height >95th percentile |
| **Obesity** (BMI >95th) / obese males | African American ethnicity |
| **Posterior sloping angle >12° (TO) / >14.5° (OB)** | Elite athlete · DI · weight 40th percentile |
| **Modified Oxford score 16–18** (~98% risk; 24 = 0%) | Closed triradiate |
| Weight <10th or >90th percentile · unreliable follow-up | Continued slipping after fixation (TO) |

**Contralateral slip** — **most common complication after unilateral fixation** · 20–80% · up to 36% high-risk · ↓ with weight loss · **more common in YOUNGER children**\
✗ Wrong options: older (TO false statement)

**Prophylactic pinning risk** — 2–6% complications

**Both hips involved / silent grade 1 on other side / pre-slip on MRI** → **pin both**

### Chronic deformity, osteotomy & natural history

**Residual deformity** — ↑ α-angle, **pistol-grip**, cam FAI · sequelae: **shortening, ↓ flexion, ↓ IR**, out-toeing

| Deformity | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Mild–moderate (<30°) cam FAI | **Osteochondroplasty** (arthroscopy, limited anterior arthrotomy, surgical dislocation) |
| Severe (>30–45°), painful, no OA/AVN | **Imhauser intertrochanteric osteotomy** — **flexion + internal rotation + valgus** (most used; AVN 2–7%) |
| | Southwick subtrochanteric osteotomy |
| Greatest correction | **Femoral neck (subcapital) cuneiform osteotomy** — AVN 37%, OA 37% · sole indication **Southwick >60°** (TO) |

**Natural history** — most well tolerated · **~5% THA at 20 yr** · realignment not proven to prevent OA · closed reduction worse\
✗ Wrong options: 25%

![Imhauser osteotomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4040-osteotomy.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Complications - chondrolysis, impingement, slip progression

**Chondrolysis** — 0–2% · joint space **<3 mm** (normal 4–6) + ↓ ROM (TO) · **pin penetration, ANTEROSUPERIOR quadrant** · **spica cast** · CT best for penetration\
✗ Wrong options: grade of slip · no reduction

**Labral tear / impingement** — **anterior 2nd screw medial to IT line** (in-out-in)

**Slip progression** — 1–2% with single screw · prevented by **two (fully threaded) screws**, ≥5 threads, WB restriction\
✗ Wrong options: spica · bone graft epiphysiodesis

**Other** — AVN (op-related 4–6%) · infection 0–2% · chronic pain 5–10% · knee pain 5–10% · LLD · OA · femoral neck fracture (extra drill holes)

## X vs Y

| | **SCFE** | **Delbet I (transepiphyseal) fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Energy | Minor / none | High |
| Age | 10–16 | Young child |
| Sex | M > F, obese | M = F |
| Course | Insidious ± acute-on-chronic | Acute |

## How it's asked

**"11-yr 70 kg, Scarpa tenderness, obligatory ER?"** → **SCFE** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Perthes · type I fracture

**"SCFE — all true EXCEPT?"** → **knee pain more often than hip pain**

**"14-yr overweight, knee pain, normal knee films?"** → **AP pelvis + frog-lateral**\
✗ Wrong options: knee MRI

**"12-yr knee pain 6 months, growth chart obese?"** → **hip + pelvis X-rays**

**"Severe SCFE, unmanipulated AP — hip position?"** → **external rotation**

**"12-yr limping, knee pain, normal knee?"** → **examine the hip**

**"Presentation that delays diagnosis?"** → **knee pain**

**"11-yr, BMI 95th, knee pain, limp?"** → **X-ray of left hip**

**"Southwick angle associated with?"** → **SCFE** (TO)

**"Southwick angle purpose?"** → **severity of slip**

**"Method for radiographic severity?"** → **Southwick angle image**\
✗ Wrong options: Klein · Shenton · CE angle

**"Hypothyroidism associated with?"** → **SCFE**

**"10-yr hypothyroid girl after unilateral pinning — likely problem?"** → **contralateral SCFE**

**"11-yr hypothyroid obese girl, unable to walk?"** → **in situ pinning of both hips**

**"12-yr, TSH 11, low T4, SCFE?"** → **pin both hips**\
✗ Wrong options: endocrine referral first

**"8-yr hypothyroid, stable slip + widened other physis?"** → **in situ pinning both hips**

**"Pituitary adenoma + unstable slip — indication for prophylaxis?"** → **endocrine disorder**\
✗ Wrong options: unstable presentation · age 13

**"Indication for same-admission contralateral fixation?"** → **renal osteodystrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: chronicity · height

**"11-yr BMI 32, knee pain, out-toeing — true?"** → **bilateral treatment with endocrinopathy**

**"Elevated leptin — X-ray?"** → **SCFE**

**"SCFE — all EXCEPT?"** → **contralateral slips more common in older children** (TO)

**"NOT predictive of contralateral slip?"** → **continued slipping after fixation** (TO)

**"Relative indications EXCEPT?"** → **African American ethnicity**

**"9-yr unstable slip — NOT a contralateral risk?"** → **height >95th percentile**

**"10-yr hypothyroid obese — prophylaxis EXCEPT?"** → **symptoms for one week**

**"12-yr 95th percentile, bilateral stable slips?"** → **in situ pinning of both hips**

**"14-yr, prior left pinning, intermittent right pain, MRI physeal edema?"** → **in situ fixation of right hip**

**"12-yr left SCFE + silent grade 1 right, open triradiate?"** → **pin both hips**

**"9-yr BMI 42, grade 1 left slip?"** → **percutaneous screws both hips**

**"Factor favouring contralateral pinning?"** → **age <10**\
✗ Wrong options: Oxford 24 · closed triradiate

**"↓ AVN in unstable SCFE?"** → **in situ single screw, no reduction**

**"13-yr acute-on-chronic, no endocrine findings?"** → **single in situ screw left hip only**

**"Stable SCFE on MRI, X-rays read normal?"** → **in situ screw fixation**

**"Correct screw start point?"** → **anterior femoral neck**

**"13-yr mild stable SCFE?"** → **single screw in situ**

**"12-yr 4 months thigh pain, stable?"** → **in situ pinning**\
✗ Wrong options: reduction · surgical dislocation

**"Most dreaded complication of SCFE surgery?"** → **AVN** (TO)

**"8-yr — vessel supplying most of head?"** → **MFCA**

**"Risk factor for post-op AVN?"** → **hardware in posterosuperior neck**\
✗ Wrong options: <5 threads (progression)

**"8 months post-op complication?"** → **AVN**

**"Most likely to cause AVN?"** → **unstable SCFE**\
✗ Wrong options: sex · obesity

**"14-yr can't bear weight even with crutches?"** → **bed rest, urgent surgery on left hip only**

**"Closed reduction + pinning → head collapse — vessel?"** → **MFCA**

**"ICP probe monophasic waveform?"** → **decompress hip ± capsulotomy, reassess**

**"Osteonecrosis 6 months after severe SCFE — association?"** → **inability to bear weight pre-op**

**"Labral tear after fixation — technique?"** → **anterior two-screw fixation**

**"Slip progression after fixation — prevent with?"** → **two fully threaded screws**

**"Chondrolysis 4 yr after pinning — cause?"** → **pin tip in anterosuperior quadrant**

**"Sole indication for subcapital osteotomy?"** → **Southwick >60°** (TO)

**"Long-term limb + rotation after unstable SCFE?"** → **shortening, ↓ flexion, ↓ IR**

**"Missed SCFE, ER deformity, FAI pain?"** → **Imhauser osteotomy: flexion, IR, valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: Pauwels · PAO

**"Natural history after pinning?"** → **mostly well tolerated, ~5% THA at 20 yr**

**"Chronic SCFE histology?"** → **granulation tissue between hypertrophic columns**

**"Zone involved (no renal disease)?"** → **hypertrophic zone**

**"15-yr breaststroke swimmer, hip pain, MRI?"** → **external rotator muscle tear**\
✗ Wrong options: SCFE · stress fracture

# Spinal Muscular Atrophy

*Pediatrics · 99 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **most common genetic cause of death in childhood** · 1 in 10,000

**Pathology** — progressive loss of **α-motor neurons in the anterior horn** (✓ SMN protein deficiency) · weakness **proximal > distal**, **LE > UE**, symmetric

### Genetics & pathophysiology (SMN1, SMN2)

**Inheritance** — **autosomal recessive**

**Gene** — **homozygous telomeric deletion of SMN1**, chromosome 5 · in **90%** (Orthobullets) · **95–98%** (OB) · SMN protein: RNA metabolism, apoptosis

**Severity** — all lack SMN1 protein · **number of functional SMN2 copies sets severity** (✓ SMN2 correlates with severity) · type I has 1 SMN2 copy

| Gene (distractors) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **SMN1** | **SMA** |
| Dystrophin (↑ CPK, DTRs present, calf pseudohypertrophy) | Duchenne |
| PMP22 duplication | CMT (70–80%) |
| Androgen receptor CAG repeat | Spinobulbar (Kennedy) |
| Frataxin GAA repeat | Friedreich ataxia |
| β-galactosidase | Morquio B |
| COMP / COL1A1 | Pseudoachondroplasia / OI |
| Sphingolipid (glucocerebroside) | Gaucher |
| Fetal ACh receptor, non-progressive contractures | Arthrogryposis |

**One SMN1 deletion → any of the 3 types** (✓ "any of the above")

### Types

| Type | Name | Onset | Function | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Acute Werdnig-Hoffmann** | **<6 months** | Floppy, poor head control, feeding difficulty, **tongue fasciculations**, absent DTRs | **Death by ~2 yr** |
| **II** | Chronic Werdnig-Hoffmann | **6–12 months** | **Sits but can't walk** · LE worse | To **5th decade** |
| **III** | **Kugelberg-Welander** | **2–15 yr** | Walks as child, wheelchair as adult · proximal weakness | **Normal** life expectancy ± respiratory support |

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **absent DTRs** · **fasciculations** · hypotonia · normal CPK + aldolase\
✗ Wrong options: Duchenne (present)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **DNA testing** (positive test usually avoids biopsy) · muscle biopsy · prenatal possible

**X-ray** — scoliosis series · pelvis

## 5 Management

### Medical therapy - nusinersen

**Nusinersen** — **antisense oligonucleotide** · alters **SMN2 splicing to include exon 7** → full-length SMN · **intrathecal** (doesn't cross BBB) · FDA-approved

**Others** — **risdiplam** (same mechanism, **oral** small molecule) · **Zolgensma** (onasemnogene, **IV gene replacement of SMN1**) · albuterol (adjunct) \
✗ Wrong options: prednisone (Duchenne)

### Scoliosis management

**Curve** — **almost universal** (100% type II) · from **age 2–3** · **long C-shaped thoracolumbar**, **very flexible** · progresses despite brace · curve size correlates with ambulatory status

**Bracing** — may **delay, not prevent** surgery (<10 yr)

**Surgery** — **PSF to pelvis** for progressive curve (>30–40°, OB; >50–60° with sitting/pulmonary decline, OB) · goals: **sitting balance, free the hands, slow pulmonary decline** · **release hip / LE contractures first** for seating · leave a **lower lumbar laminectomy window unfused** for intrathecal nusinersen

**Anterior + posterior** — only **curves >100°** or **very young (crankshaft)** · avoid with poor lungs · **typically NOT needed** · growing rods only if young\
✗ Wrong options: anterior alone (secondary progression)

**Post-op** — **temporary ↓ upper-limb function** (e.g. ↓ self-propelling wheelchair)\
✗ Wrong options: better strength · walking · contracture improvement

![SMA scoliosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4091-42-moved.jpg)

### Hip subluxation - observation

**Hip instability** — subluxation/dislocation in **62% of type II**, less in III · usually **painless** · open reduction redislocates

**Treatment** — **observation (leave dislocated)** = standard\
✗ Wrong options: adductor/psoas release · varus osteotomy · PAO · spica · abduction brace

**Contractures** — hip + knee (± ankle equinus in non-walkers) · PT · release controversial (rarely helps non-walkers, recurs)

![Anterior horn cell loss](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4091-sma.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **SMA** | **Duchenne** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Anterior horn cell | Muscle (dystrophin) |
| DTRs | **Absent** | Present |
| CPK | Normal | **Very high** |
| Fasciculations | Yes (tongue) | No |
| Inheritance | AR | X-linked recessive |

## How it's asked

**"Homozygous SMN1 deletion results in?"** → **any of types I–III**

**"3-month-old floppy, absent DTRs, tongue fasciculations — pathology + prognosis?"** → **anterior horn cell loss, unlikely to live past 2**\
✗ Wrong options: dystrophin · CP · CMT

**"Newborn tongue fasciculations, hypotonia, no DTRs?"** → **SMN1 mutation**

**"12-month-old sits not walks, normal CPK?"** → **SMN-1 gene**\
✗ Wrong options: dystrophin · PMP22 · frataxin

**"SMN protein deficiency leads to?"** → **anterior horn cell loss, progressive weakness**

**"Gene correlating with severity?"** → **SMN2**

**"Gene implicated in SMA?"** → **SMN-I**

**"Intrathecal SMN2 splicing modifier?"** → **nusinersen**\
✗ Wrong options: risdiplam (oral) · Zolgensma (IV gene therapy)

**"13-yr non-ambulatory, flexible 68° curve progressing in brace?"** → **posterior spinal fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: AP fusion · growing rods · hip reduction

**"Expected change after PSF?"** → **diminished ability to self-propel wheelchair**

**"SMA scoliosis — INCORRECT?"** → **anterior release + posterior fusion typically required**

**"12-yr type 3, 60° curve, night ventilation?"** → **posterior spinal fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: AP · anterior alone

**"7-yr type II, painless hip popping, subluxation?"** → **observation**

**"11-yr non-ambulatory, painless full ROM, dislocated hip?"** → **observation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Spondyloepiphyseal Dysplasia (SED)

*Pediatrics · 100 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — abnormal **type II collagen** → vertebrae + epiphyses involved · rare

### Genes & lab markers

**Gene** — **COL2A1**, chromosome **12**

| Form | Inheritance | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| **SED congenita** | **Autosomal dominant** | More severe · LE angular deformity · myelopathy from AAI |
| **SED tarda** | **X-linked recessive** | Milder · no LE angular deformity · nephrotic syndrome |
| — | Random mutation in **50%** | — |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **cervical myelopathy** (AAI) · respiratory insufficiency (thoracic dysplasia) · **myopia / retinal detachment** · hip pain (coxa vara) · ↓ walking distance

**Exam** — **short stature** (short trunk) · **flat facies** · kyphoscoliosis · ↑ lumbar lordosis · **coxa vara** · genu valgum · waddling gait, ↓ hip ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**C-spine** — AP, lateral, **open-mouth** + **flexion–extension** · **upper cervical instability** · **odontoid hypoplasia / os odontoideum**

**Thoracolumbar** — **platyspondyly** · incomplete ossification fusion · endplate irregularity, narrow discs · kyphoscoliosis · lordosis

**Hips** — horizontal acetabular roofs · delayed pubic + femoral head ossification · wide Y cartilage · **coxa vara**

**MRI** — instability / myelopathy → cord signal

![Platyspondyly in SED](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4096-sed-spine.jpg)

![SED with cervical instability](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4096-sed-with-cervical-instability.jpg)

### SED vs MED

| | **SED** | **MED** |
|---|---|---|
| **Spine** | **Involved** (✓ distinguishing) | Normal |
| Gene | COL2A1 (type II collagen) | COMP / type IX collagen |
| Shared | Irregular epiphyses, genu valgum, early hip OA, short stature | Same |
| Neck | **AAI, myelopathy** | — |

**Differential** — achondroplasia · diastrophic dysplasia · MED

## 5 Management

**Non-op (all)** — multidisciplinary: PT, OT, **yearly eye exam**, pulmonology, bracing mild scoliosis

### Operative criteria

| Problem | Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|---|
| **Atlantoaxial instability** | **≥8 mm** or **myelopathy** | **Posterior atlantoaxial fusion** |
| Scoliosis | **>50°** | Growing rods (young) / PSF (older) |
| **Coxa vara** | **NSA <100°**, progressive, symptomatic OA | **Valgus (+ extension) intertrochanteric osteotomy** |

### Atlantoaxial instability & myelopathy

**SEDC child, new unsteadiness, hyperreflexia, Hoffmann, ↓ dexterity** → **cervical myelopathy from AAI**\
✗ Wrong options: scoliosis · tethered cord · coxa vara gait

## 6 Complications

**List** — cervical instability · spinal deformity · eye problems · hip deformity · early OA

## How it's asked

**"10-yr SEDC, new myelopathic signs?"** → **cervical myelopathy due to atlantoaxial instability**

**"Feature distinguishing SED from MED?"** → **spine involvement**\
✗ Wrong options: irregular epiphyses · genu valgum · short stature

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sprengel's Deformity

*Pediatrics · 101 / 112*

## 1 Basics

### Diagnosis & associations (Klippel-Feil)

**Definition** — **congenital elevation of a small, hypoplastic scapula** from **failure of caudal migration** · **most common congenital shoulder anomaly**

**Demographics** — **M:F 1:3** (Orthobullets) · equal (TO) · **left** more common (TO) · **bilateral 10–30%** (10% TO)

**Cause** — interrupted **embryonic subclavian supply** (subclavian, internal thoracic or suprascapular) · Poland = subclavian interruption proximal to internal thoracic, distal to vertebral

**Omovertebral connection** — cartilage/fibrous/bony bar from superomedial scapula to **lower cervical spine** · **30–50%** (Orthobullets) · ~30% (OB) · ~½ (TO) · limits abduction to ~90°

**Associations** — **Klippel-Feil** (most common associated finding: **cervical spine anomalies**) · congenital scoliosis · fused/cervical ribs · torticollis · diastematomyelia · myelomeningocele · kidney disease · UE anomalies · iniencephaly (TO)

| Overlap | Rate |
|---|---|
| Klippel-Feil patients with Sprengel | ~⅓ (Orthobullets) · 35% (OB) · 20% (TO) |

### Klippel-Feil (TO)

**Triad (~50%)** — short neck · low posterior hairline · ↓ neck motion · fusion **C2–C3 most common (71%)** · gene chromosome 8 · fetal alcohol → 50% fusions

**Associated** — **scoliosis 60%** (most common orthopaedic) · renal anomaly · VSD · deafness · **synkinesis** · respiratory · occipitalization, hemivertebrae, basilar impression · symptomatic → posterior C3–C7 fusion ± decompression

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — scapula **high-riding, medially rotated** (inferior pole medial) · small equilateral-triangle shape, loss of long medial border · **abduction most limited** + forward flexion · short, broad, webbed-looking neck (Turner-like) · often referred for "scoliosis"\
✗ Wrong options: adduction · rotation

**Differential** — FSHD (no omovertebral bone) · Turner · torticollis

![Sprengel deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4038-clinical-photo-colorado-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification & surgery (Cavendish, Woodward)

**Cavendish** — 4-grade **cosmetic** classification of **congenital elevation of scapula**\
✗ Wrong options: torticollis · radial head dislocation · clavicle pseudarthrosis

| Rigault (TO) | Superomedial angle |
|---|---|
| 1 | Below T2, above T4 transverse process |
| 2 | Between C5 and T2 |
| 3 | Above C5 |

**Pre-op imaging** — **MRI or CT for omovertebral bar**

![Omovertebral connection](../../MCQ/attachments/q5231-illb-sprengel-s-drawing.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

**Observe** — no significant cosmetic or functional problem

**Operate** — severe cosmesis or **abduction <110–120°** · ideal **age 3–8** (soon after 3) · **>8 yr → brachial plexus risk**\
✗ Wrong options: defer until after 8 (TO false statement) · wait for maturity

| Procedure | Essence |
|---|---|
| **Woodward** | Detach + reattach medial parascapular muscles (**trapezius + rhomboid origins shifted distally at spinous processes**) → scapula descends, rotates to abduction · modified: + superomedial border resection |
| **Schrock / Green** | Extraperiosteal detachment of muscles at scapular insertion, scapula pulled down with traction, reinserted |
| **Clavicle osteotomy / morcellation** | **Severe deformity**, before scapular descent → protect **brachial plexus** (2 cm midclavicular if >8 yr, 1 cm younger) (✗ Wrong options: mild deformity) |
| Bony resection | Extraperiosteal superomedial prominence (cosmesis) |
| Mears | Most abduction gain (TO) |

**Outcome** — **abduction +40–50°** (Orthobullets) · ~40° (TO) · +38° (Farsetti)

**Complication** — **brachial plexus palsy = most severe and most common** (TO) · SSEP monitoring

## How it's asked

**"4-yr Klippel-Feil, elevated left scapula — most limited motion?"** → **abduction**

**"Failure of scapular caudal migration?"** → **Sprengel deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: Poland

**"5-yr elevated, medially rotated scapula — commonest imaging association?"** → **cervical spine abnormalities**\
✗ Wrong options: absent clavicle · horseshoe kidney

**"6-yr bilateral, omovertebral bone on CT, can't raise arms?"** → **Sprengel deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: FSHD · Turner

**"Woodward procedure used for?"** → **Sprengel shoulder** (TO)

**"6-yr, abduction 100°, surgery planned — NOT true?"** → **defer 2 yr because plexus palsy is rare after 8** (TO)

**"Cavendish classification for?"** → **congenital elevation of scapula** (TO)

**"True about Sprengel?"** → **brachial plexus palsy is the most severe and common surgical complication** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: delay to maturity · morcellation for mild · right more common

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Supracondylar Fracture - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 102 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — most common pediatric elbow fracture (50–80%) · age **5–7** · **M = F** · FOOSH

**Type** — **extension 95–98%** (98% TO) · **flexion <5%** (2–4%) — fall on olecranon

**Associated** — **ipsilateral distal radius / forearm 5–10%** (TO) · vascular compromise **5–17%** · nerve injury 10–20%

### Elbow ossification order

| Centre | Appears (yr) | Fuses (yr) |
|---|---|---|
| Capitellum | 1 | 12–14 |
| Radial head | 3 | 14–16 |
| **Medial (internal) epicondyle** | 5 (6 in Orthobullets text) | **16–18 — LAST to fuse** (16–19 OB) |
| Trochlea | 7 | 12–14 |
| Olecranon | 9 | 15–17 |
| **Lateral (external) epicondyle** | **11 — last to appear** | 12–14 |

## 3 Presentation

### Nerve injuries (AIN, radial, ulnar)

| Pattern | Nerve | Deficit |
|---|---|---|
| **Extension (overall most common)** | **AIN** (median branch) | **Can't flex thumb IP (FPL) + index DIP (FDP)** · no "OK" sign · PQ |
| Extension, **posteromedial** displacement | **Radial** (2nd most common) | Wrist drop, no thumb/MCP extension (PIN: EPL, ECU) · dorsal hand numbness |
| Extension, **posterolateral** displacement | **Median** nerve, **brachial artery** | Volar index sensory loss |
| **Flexion** | **Ulnar** (C8–T1) | **Intrinsics**: can't cross fingers / abduct index (FDI) · small-finger numbness |
| **Medial pin** | **Ulnar** (iatrogenic) | Can't cross fingers |

**Rates** — extension neurapraxia 11% · flexion 17% · pre-op deficit ~20% in displaced extension (OB)

**Management** — examine **before** any reduction · **observe** · recovery 6–12 wk, complete 3–6 months (2–3 months OB)\
✗ Wrong options: EMG · exploration · neurolysis · nerve repair

**Recovery (TO)** — radial 100% · median 88% · **ulnar only 25% fully recover**\
✗ Wrong options: "majority recover in 3 months" (TO false statement)

**Obese children** — more pre- and post-op nerve palsy (TO)

### Vascular exam

**Assess** — pulse (palpation, Doppler) + **perfusion** (warm, pink vs **cold, pale, CRT >2 s**) · **decision to explore is based on perfusion, not pulse** · radial pulse absent at presentation 7–12%

**Brachialis sign** — antecubital ecchymosis, **dimpling/puckering**, palpable bone → proximal fragment buttonholed through brachialis → more arterial injury, swelling, harder reduction

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification & radiographic assessment

| Gartland | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Nondisplaced** (beware medial comminution → varus) | Long arm cast **3–4 wk**, X-ray at 1 wk |
| **II** | Displaced in 1 plane, **posterior cortex/hinge intact** (IIA no rotation, IIB rotation) | **CRPP** (US standard) · cast only if AHL hits capitellum, little swelling, no medial comminution |
| **III** | Displaced in 2–3 planes, no cortical contact | **CRPP** ± open |
| **IV** | **Periosteum completely disrupted** — unstable in flexion AND extension (found under EUA) | CRPP ± open |
| **Medial comminution** | Medial column collapse, ↓ Baumann | **CRPP** (valgus force) — else **cubitus varus** |
| **Flexion type** | Distal fragment **anterior** | **CRPP**, **more often open** |

**Fat pads** — **posterior fat pad** = occult fracture · **anterior "sail" sign** (TO) = effusion · most common cause: supracondylar (child), radial head (adult)

**Anterior humeral line** — through middle ⅓ of capitellum **>5 yr** · **<5 yr: anterior and middle ⅓ about equally**, posterior ⅓ in a few normal kids · capitellum visible from ~1 yr · extension type → capitellum **posterior** to line · compare other side\
✗ Wrong options: always fracture

**Baumann angle** — humeral shaft vs lateral condylar physis on AP · **70–75°** · deviation **>5–10°** unacceptable

**Angiography** — not indicated (lesion is at fracture)

![Gartland classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4007-gartland.jpg)

![Anterior humeral line](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4007-anterior-humeral-line.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Treatment indications - CRPP

```
Supracondylar fracture — timing by neurovascular status
├─ Pulseless + POORLY perfused (cold, pale) → EMERGENT CRPP (hours, override NPO)
├─ Pulseless + well perfused, sensory deficit, swelling, brachialis sign, open, floating elbow → URGENT (same day, 6–8 h)
└─ Warm, perfused, neuro intact (± isolated AIN) → next day (splint 30–40°, elevate)
```

**Operative** — **Gartland II, III, IV** · **flexion type** · **medial comminution** · displaced type II with medial impaction (3-yr) → **CRPP**\
✗ Wrong options: cast (varus) · hyperflexion cast (compartment syndrome)

**Floating elbow** (supracondylar + forearm / distal radius) → **pin BOTH** (✗ Wrong options: casting either (compartment risk up to **33%**)) · watch for compartment syndrome · ↑ nerve injury (✗ Wrong options: ↑ vascular · ↑ open)

**Isolated AIN palsy** → **not** a reason for overnight surgery

### Pin configuration & iatrogenic ulnar nerve injury

**Reduction (extension type)** — traction, correct coronal/rotation, **hyperflexion** · posteromedial → **pronate** · posterolateral → **supinate** (try opposite if fails)

| Construct | Use |
|---|---|
| **2 lateral pins** | Type II · test stability |
| **3 lateral pins** | **Type III / IV, comminution (medial column)** · = crossed-pin stability · **construct of choice** (AAOS) |
| **Crossed (medial + lateral)** | **Strongest in torsion** (experimental) · **ulnar nerve injury 3–8%** |

**AAOS** — **2–3 lateral pins**, avoid medial pin (weak recommendation) · **no Level 1 evidence that lateral is superior** (RCT: equal outcomes) — the INCORRECT statement · lateral = fewer ulnar injuries, equal radiographic + ROM outcomes

**Pin tips** — **maximise separation at fracture**, engage both columns, cross **proximal** to fracture · 3rd lateral pin ↑ stiffness with medial comminution or residual IR

**Elbow position for pinning** — **flexion** for extension type · **extension for flexion type**

**Medial pin safety** — place with **elbow extended** (ulnar nerve subluxes anteriorly in flexion) · small incision · anterior-to-posterior sagittal angle ↑ injury

**Post-op ulnar palsy after crossed pins** → **observe + avoid hyperflexion >90°** (TO) · resolves 1–7 months\
✗ Wrong options: remove medial pin now · exploration/transposition

**Open reduction** — failed closed · **open fracture** · irreducible with gap (entrapped vessel/nerve) · **flexion type more often** · **coronal displacement >7 mm** predicts it · approach **anterior** (pulseless / median injury) or medial/lateral where periosteum torn · **never posterior** (AVN)

![Lateral pin construct](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4007-supracondylar-courtesy-wheeless-moved.jpg)

### Pulseless hand & vascular management

| Situation | Action |
|---|---|
| **Pulseless + cold/pale** at presentation | **Emergent CRPP** → reassess (✗ Wrong options: angiogram · splint and wait · explore first) |
| **Pink pulseless** (warm, brisk CRT ± Doppler) | **Urgent CRPP** · after: if still pink (± triphasic Doppler) → **long arm splint + admit 48 h observation** · pulse returns **24–48 h** in most |
| **Pulseless + still cold/pale after CRPP** | **Explore antecubital fossa** (✗ Wrong options: observe · re-pin · fasciotomy) |
| **Perfused hand becomes pale/pulseless after CRPP** | **Remove pins, re-displace, reassess** → still bad → **open exploration** (✗ Wrong options: medial pin · CT angiogram · warm and wait) |
| **Irreducible + anterior gap + poor perfusion** | **Vascular exploration** (entrapped artery) |

**Pulseless + poorly perfused after CRPP** → brachial artery injury up to 82%

![Gartland III with vascular risk](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4007-g3.jpg)

### Postoperative care (pin removal, PT, pin infection)

**Pins** — remove at **3 (–4) wk** in clinic, stop immobilisation\
✗ Wrong options: 3 more wk cast

**ROM** — ~**94% by 6 months** · **no routine PT** (no functional or motion benefit)

**Pin infection** — 1–2.4% · **only proven risk: age <4.5 yr** · oral antibiotics\
✗ Wrong options: delay >24 h · no antibiotics · late follow-up; daily pin care ↑ infection

## 6 Complications

### Compartment syndrome

**Rate** — 0.1–0.3% (TO) · <1% · ↑ with **floating elbow**, splint >90°, type III, vascular injury, delay

**Signs in children** — **3 A's: anxiety, agitation, ↑ analgesic need** · rising narcotic need overnight → **emergent surgery ± fasciotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: rely on 6 P's

**Volkmann ischemic contracture** — deep volar compartment · **casting in >90° flexion**

### Cubitus varus

**Cubitus varus (gunstock)** — **most common complication** (OB) · Orthobullets lists **pin migration (~2%)** as most common · **malreduction / malunion** (medial collapse, IR, extension) — **NOT growth arrest**\
✗ Wrong options: medial physeal arrest · lateral overgrowth

**Effects** — mainly **cosmetic**, function normal · posterolateral rotatory instability · forearm IR deficit · **tardy ulnar palsy** (anterior nerve subluxation most common, then FCU bands)

**Osteotomy** — for **cosmesis** · **lateral closing wedge → lateral prominence** · reverse V, step-cut, dome, medial opening with medialisation avoid it

**Cubitus valgus** — malunion → tardy ulnar palsy · **recurvatum** — non-op type II/III

**Fishtail deformity (TO)** — **lateral trochlear ossification centre fails/resorbs** (AVN of posterior vessels to **lateral** trochlea, "type A") → inverted-V distal humerus · hard to see before **~9 yr** · after supracondylar (most), lateral/medial condyle, SH I · (✗ Wrong options: "medial trochlea blood supply") · **Hegemann** = trochlear osteochondrosis\
✗ Wrong options: epicondylar

## X vs Y

| | **Extension type** | **Flexion type** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | 95–98% | <5% |
| Distal fragment | Posterior | **Anterior** |
| Nerve | **AIN** (then radial) | **Ulnar** |
| Pin elbow position | Flexion | **Extension** |
| Open reduction | Less | **More** |

## How it's asked

**"Arrow on anterior elbow fat pad?"** → **sail sign** (TO)

**"6-yr, displaced, posterior cortex intact?"** → **Gartland II**

**"FOOSH injury needing surgery?"** → **Gartland II supracondylar**\
✗ Wrong options: angulated BBFF · proximal humerus <12

**"Fully displaced, no cortical contact?"** → **Gartland III**

**"22-month-old normal AHL position?"** → **anterior ⅓ more often than posterior ⅓**

**"Supracondylar — all EXCEPT?"** → **ulnar injuries in flexion type mostly recover in 3 months** (TO)

**"Flexion type — motor deficit of commonest nerve?"** → **hand intrinsic weakness**

**"Commonest nerve innervates all EXCEPT?"** → **EPL**

**"NOT an urgent/emergent indication?"** → **can't flex thumb IP (isolated AIN)**

**"Commonest nerve controls?"** → **thumb IP flexor**

**"Small-finger numbness — which X-ray?"** → **flexion-type supracondylar**

**"Can't flex thumb IP, pulses present?"** → **AIN**

**"5-yr, no thumb IP / index DIP flexion — nerve management?"** → **observation**

**"8-yr 100% displaced, no thumb IP / index DIP flexion?"** → **AIN palsy**\
✗ Wrong options: Volkmann

**"No wrist/thumb extension — initial management?"** → **CRPP, expect nerve recovery**\
✗ Wrong options: explore radial nerve

**"Posteromedial displacement, dorsal hand numbness — muscles?"** → **EPL + ECU**

**"Flexion type — exam finding?"** → **can't abduct index finger**

**"Flexion type — nerve + management?"** → **ulnar; observation**

**"Extension type — nerve + management?"** → **AIN; observation**

**"Most common deficit muscle?"** → **FPL**\
✗ Wrong options: interossei · EPL

**"Puckering + pink hand + deficit?"** → **thumb flexion**

**"Flexion type commonest nerve roots?"** → **C8–T1**

**"Partial ulnar palsy after crossed pins?"** → **observe, avoid hyperflexion >90°** (TO)

**"Crossed vs lateral pins — disadvantage?"** → **ulnar nerve injury risk**

**"Advantage of crossed pins?"** → **greater experimental biomechanical stability**

**"6-yr type III, NV intact — AAOS?"** → **closed reduction + 2–3 lateral pins**\
✗ Wrong options: crossed

**"7-yr type III, OK/thumbs up/cross fingers intact?"** → **CRPP with lateral pins**

**"Type III + medial comminution — construct?"** → **3 lateral pins**

**"4-yr Gartland III — preferred pins?"** → **3 lateral**

**"INCORRECT about lateral pinning?"** → **Level 1 evidence of superiority**

**"Medial pin (A) palsy sign?"** → **can't cross fingers**

**"Adding 3rd lateral pin?"** → **↑ stiffness with medial comminution**

**"Pin with elbow EXTENDED for?"** → **fracture displaced anteriorly (flexion type)**

**"22-yr dorsoulnar numbness after varus malunion?"** → **anterior ulnar nerve subluxation**

**"10-yr pulseless cold hand?"** → **emergent CRPP**\
✗ Wrong options: angiogram · exploration

**"Pink pulseless after CRPP — outcome?"** → **pulse returns in 24–48 h**

**"Pink, triphasic Doppler, no palpable pulse after CRPP?"** → **keep pins, long arm immobilisation, observe**

**"Cool pale hand after anatomic CRPP (pulseless at start)?"** → **explore antecubital fossa**

**"Lost pulse after CRPP with fracture gap?"** → **remove pins + displace fracture**

**"3-yr pink pulseless → pink with Doppler after CRPP?"** → **splint + admit for observation**

**"10-yr type III, perfused, no pulse?"** → **closed reduction + pinning**

**"7-yr pulseless after ED realignment?"** → **CRPP in OR, reassess**\
✗ Wrong options: angiogram

**"3-yr pale hand, diminished pulses?"** → **CRPP**

**"8-yr floating elbow, pink pulseless?"** → **compartment monitoring + urgent surgery**

**"Pulse lost after CRPP of perfused hand?"** → **remove pins, re-displace, reassess**

**"6-yr pale pulseless at end of call?"** → **emergent CRPP tonight**\
✗ Wrong options: vascular surgery first

**"5-yr cold pulseless at 9 pm — timing?"** → **emergent, as soon as OR allows**

**"Became pale after anatomic CRPP?"** → **remove pins, reassess**

**"Exploration indicated when?"** → **irreducible, anterior gap, poorly perfused**

**"Still cold after pin removal?"** → **open exploration + vascular**

**"7-yr supracondylar + 25° distal radius?"** → **CRPP of both fractures**

**"3-yr type II with medial impaction?"** → **CRPP**

**"7-yr displaced, AHL misses capitellum?"** → **CRPP**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · plate

**"7-yr type II, antecubital ecchymosis, pink hand?"** → **CRPP and discharge**\
✗ Wrong options: arteriography

**"Gymnast — supracondylar + BBFF — ↑ complication?"** → **compartment syndrome**

**"Floating elbow overnight — prompts emergent surgery?"** → **↑ anxiety + narcotic need**

**"Fishtail deformity seen in?"** → **old supracondylar humerus fractures** (TO)

**"Fishtail — all EXCEPT?"** → **disturbed medial trochlear blood supply** (TO)

**"Correcting mild gunstock — result?"** → **improved cosmesis**

**"Etiology of cubitus varus?"** → **malreduction**\
✗ Wrong options: medial physeal arrest

**"8-yr varus after splint — cause?"** → **malreduction → malunion**

**"Osteotomy causing lateral prominence?"** → **lateral closing wedge**

**"Healed in varus, untreated?"** → **poor appearance, normal function**\
✗ Wrong options: PMRI · tardy AIN

**"Routine hardware removal at 3–4 wk?"** → **supracondylar CRPP pins**

**"ROM >90% by?"** → **6 months**

**"3 wk post-CRPP, clean pins?"** → **remove all pins, stop immobilisation**

**"When is PT indicated?"** → **no routine PT**

**"Twins, one gets PT?"** → **no difference**

**"Pin infection — highest risk factor?"** → **age <4.5 yr**

**"Flexion type vs extension — counsel?"** → **higher odds of open reduction**

**"7 mm coronal displacement ↑ risk of?"** → **open reduction**

**"Last elbow apophysis to fuse?"** → **medial (internal) epicondyle**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Syndactyly of the Toes

*Pediatrics · 103 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **1 in 2,000** · most often **2nd–3rd toes**

**Cause** — incomplete / absent **apoptosis** between digits

**Inheritance** — mostly **autosomal dominant**

### Syndromic associations

**Associated** — familial syndactyly · **Down** · **Klippel-Feil** · **Smith-Lemli-Opitz**

**Smith-Lemli-Opitz** — **AR** · **7-dehydrocholesterol reductase** deficiency (cholesterol synthesis) · **microcephaly, cleft palate, cardiac defect (ASD)**, postaxial polydactyly, **2–3 toe syndactyly**\
✗ Wrong options: Beckwith-Wiedemann (hemihypertrophy, macroglossia) · Prader-Willi (chr 15, obesity) · FSHD · Rett

| Type | Fusion |
|---|---|
| **Simple** | Soft tissue only |
| **Complex** | Bony fusion |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — painless, cosmetic · no motor delay

**X-ray** — AP foot

| Toe syndactyly | After release |
|---|---|
| ![Toe syndactyly](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4077-preoperative-image-of-syndactyly.jpg) | ![After release](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4077-webbed-toes-after.jpg) |

## 5 Management

| Type | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Simple** | **Observation** (cosmetic) · surgery risks contracture, painful scar · no toe spacers |
| **Complex** | Digit release |

## How it's asked

**"Microcephaly, cleft palate, ASD + 2–3 toe syndactyly?"** → **Smith-Lemli-Opitz syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: Beckwith-Wiedemann · Prader-Willi · Rett

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tarsal Coalition

*Pediatrics · 104 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Prevalence** — **1–2%** (symptomatic ~1%) · **~75% asymptomatic** · bilateral ~50% · 2 coalitions in 10–20% (OB) · extra coalition on CT ~5% (Orthobullets)

### Embryology - failure of segmentation

**Cause** — **failure of mesenchymal differentiation + segmentation** (✓ both CN and TC) · bridge fibrous → cartilage → bone\
✗ Wrong options: failure of formation · ischemic necrosis · microtrauma · peroneal hypertonicity

| Interface | Name |
|---|---|
| Fibrous | **Syndesmosis** |
| Cartilaginous | **Synchondrosis** |
| Bony | **Synostosis** |

**Acquired** (less common) — trauma, degeneration, infection

### Genes & lab markers

**Non-syndromic** — **autosomal dominant** (sibling history)

**Syndromic** — **fibular hemimelia** · carpal coalition · **FGFR1/2/3 craniosynostosis** (**Apert**, Pfeiffer, Crouzon, Jackson-Weiss, Muenke) · TN coalition: Apert, Nievergelt (TO)

| Site | Incidence | Symptom age | Interface |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Calcaneonavicular (CN)** | **Most common** (Orthobullets) · ~45% (TO) | **8–12 yr** (10–14 OB) | Synchondrosis / syndesmosis |
| **Talocalcaneal (TC), middle facet** | ~45% (TO) | **12–15 yr** (12–16 TO) | Synchondrosis → **synostosis** |
| Talonavicular | 1–2% | Infancy | Synostosis |
| Calcaneocuboid · naviculocuneiform | <1% | Variable / adult | — |

**Symptoms start when the bar ossifies** (CN earlier than TC)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanics** — subtalar joint normally internally rotates ~10° in stance → blocked → **flat arch, forefoot abduction, hindfoot valgus, peroneal spasm** ("**peroneal spastic flatfoot**" — coalition = leading cause)

**Pain sources** — ossification of bar · microfracture at interface · chondral damage · stress on other joints

## 3 Presentation

### Presentation & diagnosis (imaging, anteater sign)

**History** — **recurrent ankle sprains** · activity pain · calf pain (peroneal spasm) · uneven ground

**Pain site** — **sinus tarsi / below fibula → CN** · **distal to medial malleolus / medial foot → TC**

**Exam** — **rigid pes planus** · hindfoot valgus that **doesn't swing into varus on toe-rise** · arch doesn't reconstitute · **↓ subtalar motion** · heel cord contracture · reverse Coleman block for subtalar rigidity

**Deformity association** — **rigid pes planus**\
✗ Wrong options: cavus · rocker-bottom · varus

**Ball-and-socket ankle** — from large **neonatal TC coalition** (develops by ~5 yr)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP · standing lateral · **45° internal oblique (best for CN)** · Harris axial heel

| Coalition | Sign |
|---|---|
| **CN** | **"Anteater (nose)" sign** — elongated anterior calcaneal process (lateral/oblique) · **"comma" sign** (TO) · ± talar beaking |
| **TC** | **"C-sign"** — talar dome to posteroinferior sustentaculum (lateral) · **talar (talonavicular) beaking** — dorsal talar lip remodeling from altered motion, not OA · irregular middle facet on Harris · **dysmorphic, rounded sustentaculum** (Oestreich) |

**CT** — **pre-op**: size, location (TC % of posterior facet on coronal), **rule out 2nd coalition**, arthritis · gold standard in adults

**MRI** — **fibrous / cartilaginous coalition** (normal X-rays + suspicion) · STIR for inflammation

**Normal X-rays, rigid valgus heel, sinus tarsi pain, repeated sprains** → **suspect coalition → MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: Sever · Kohler · Iselin · flexible flatfoot surgery

![Anteater sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4068-anteater.jpg)

![C-sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4068-c-sign.jpg)

![45° oblique view of CN coalition](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4068-45-degree-oblique.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic tarsal coalition
├─ First → short-leg walking cast 4–6 wk + NSAIDs (then orthosis / UCBL)
├─ Recurs — CN → resection + EDB or fat interposition
├─ Recurs — TC <50% → resection + fat / split FHL interposition ± deformity correction
├─ TC >50%, no arthritis → flatfoot reconstruction (OB) / subtalar fusion (Orthobullets)
└─ Failed resection / diffuse arthritis → triple arthrodesis
```

### Initial nonoperative treatment - cast immobilisation

**First line (every first presentation)** — **below-knee walking cast 4–6 wk** (6 wk Orthobullets · 4 wk OB) ± NSAIDs · up to **30% pain-free** (Orthobullets) · very high short-term relief (OB) · inserts may hurt rigid feet · **UCBL** after\
✗ Wrong options: resection first · arthrodesis · CT/MRI first if diagnosis clear · sinus tarsi injection

**Pain recurs after good cast** → **surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat cast · NWB cast · orthotic · more PT · quit sport

### Calcaneonavicular coalition resection with interposition

**Procedure** — **resect bar + interpose EDB muscle or fat** (or bone wax) · resection alone ↑ recurrence · **lateral oblique incision over sinus tarsi between EXTENSOR and PERONEAL tendons** · protect superficial peroneal + sural nerves · ~1 cm gap · NWB cast 3–4 wk

**Add** — calcaneal osteotomy / lengthening for marked valgus · heel cord lengthening if DF < neutral · mild flatfoot → no osteotomy

**Outcome** — **80–85% pain relief** · **full return to sport (87%, Mubarak)** · **↑ subtalar motion (74%)**, ↑ plantar flexion (82%) · repeat resection ~5%\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar fusion · arthroereisis · manipulation

**Contraindications** — advanced degeneration · multiple coalitions → arthrodesis

### Talocalcaneal coalition - resection vs arthrodesis/reconstruction

### Operative criteria

| TC situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **≤50%** of subtalar width (coronal CT), acceptable alignment, no OA | **Resection + fat / split FHL interposition** ± valgus correction |
| **>50%**, joints healthy | **Bony flatfoot reconstruction WITHOUT resection** (OB220914) · **subtalar arthrodesis** (Orthobullets, OB3016) — read the options |
| >50% + **heel valgus >16°** | Poor results with resection (Wilde) |
| **Failed resection**, >50%, or **diffuse TN/CC arthritis** | **Triple arthrodesis** |

**TC approach** — **medial**, curved incision over **sustentaculum**, between **FDL and neurovascular bundle** (Orthobullets) · between FDL and FHL (OB) · find normal anterior + posterior facets, resect with burr, check inversion/eversion · NWB cast 3 wk

**Subtalar fusion** — avoid in young (10–12 yr) — limits foot growth (OB)

**Poor outcomes** — resecting **>50%** · **uncorrected hindfoot valgus** · degenerative change

**Adults (TO)** — usually ossified + secondary arthritis → CT · accommodative orthosis, AFO, injections · resection only if small bar and normal joints · otherwise subtalar / double / triple arthrodesis ± gastrocnemius recession

## 6 Complications

**List** — incomplete resection · **recurrence** · residual pain / stiffness (malalignment, arthritis, **missed 2nd coalition** → pre-op CT)

## X vs Y

| | **CN coalition** | **TC coalition** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | 8–12 | 12–15 |
| Pain | Sinus tarsi, lateral | Below medial malleolus |
| X-ray | **Anteater**, 45° oblique | **C-sign**, talar beak, Harris view |
| Interposition | **EDB** / fat | Fat / **split FHL** |
| Approach | Lateral (extensor/peroneal interval) | Medial (sustentaculum) |

## How it's asked

**"Identify the pathology (anteater sign)?"** → **calcaneonavicular coalition** (TO)

**"18-yr rigid flatfoot — most common site, age, interface?"** → **TC middle facet; 12–16 yr; synchondrosis or synostosis** (TO)

**"Lateral sign of TC coalition kinematics (dorsal TN lip)?"** → **talonavicular beaking** (TO)

**"14-yr bilateral sinus tarsi pain, heel stays valgus on rise?"** → **recurrent ankle sprains**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT insufficiency · supination in swing

**"13-yr ↓ subtalar motion, C-sign + coronal CT?"** → **rigid pes planus**

**"11-yr recurrent sprains, valgus heel, normal X-rays?"** → **tarsal coalition; MRI**

**"13-yr ↓ subtalar motion, talar beak, C-sign, MRI fibrous bar?"** → **talocalcaneal coalition**\
✗ Wrong options: osteochondroma

**"57-yr ball-and-socket ankle?"** → **significant neonatal talocalcaneal coalition**

**"12-yr sinus tarsi pain, brother had foot problem?"** → **CN coalition; autosomal dominant**

**"15-yr 'anteater sign' — what's resected?"** → **anomaly between calcaneus and navicular**\
✗ Wrong options: os trigonum · accessory navicular

**"Primary embryological failure?"** → **failure of mesenchymal differentiation + segmentation** (TO)

**"CN vs TC pathology?"** → **both failure of segmentation**
