**"14-yr pain below medial malleolus, C-sign — cause?"** → **failure of mesenchymal segmentation**\
✗ Wrong options: glycine substitution

**"10-yr 4 wk foot pain, CN coalition?"** → **4 wk short leg cast**\
✗ Wrong options: excision · arthrodesis

**"13-yr first visit, sinus tarsi pain, CN coalition?"** → **cast immobilization**

**"23-yr medial pain, C-sign?"** → **immobilization**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · MRI · injection

**"16-yr 1-yr sprains, CN bar?"** → **activity modification ± immobilization**

**"16-yr anteater sign, first visit?"** → **calcaneus–navicular; short immobilization**

**"13-yr first evaluation, CN on oblique?"** → **cast or boot 6 wk**

**"24-yr cartilaginous CN, <⅓ joint, no arthritis?"** → **resection + EDB or fat** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroereisis · MDCO + FDL

**"14-yr failed bracing/therapy, CN?"** → **resection + EDB interposition**

**"10-yr failed 5-wk cast, isolated CN, neutral heel?"** → **resection + EDB**\
✗ Wrong options: sustentaculum excision · triple

**"16-yr after CN resection — likely?"** → **full return to sport**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ subtalar motion · repeat surgery

**"11-yr failed 6-wk cast, CT clear?"** → **resection with fat or muscle**

**"12-yr softball, pain back after cast?"** → **excision of coalition**

**"15-yr TC <50%, fibrocartilaginous, relief only with cast?"** → **surgical resection**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat cast · fusion

**"15-yr CN, failed non-op — best for sport?"** → **bar resection with tissue interposition**

**"11-yr gymnast, failed cast + orthotics?"** → **surgical treatment**

**"13-yr failed WB cast, PT, orthosis — CN?"** → **resection CN + fat/muscle**\
✗ Wrong options: Broström

**"Interval for CN resection?"** → **between extensor and peroneal tendons**

**"14-yr failed TC resection, near-complete subtalar involvement?"** → **triple arthrodesis**

**"11-yr — isolated TC resection best when?"** → **40% width, no ↑ valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: 80% · 25° valgus

**"Appropriate treatment for indication?"** → **TC <50% → resection + graft**

**"18-yr TC 70% posterior facet, healthy joints?"** → **bony flatfoot reconstruction without resection**\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar fusion · triple

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tibial Deficiency

*Pediatrics · 105 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — congenital **longitudinal deficiency of the tibia** (tibial hemimelia), partial → complete absence

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant** → genetic counseling

**Associated MSK anomalies (75%)** — **ectrodactyly** · **preaxial polydactyly** · **ulnar aplasia**

## 3 Presentation

**Deformity** — short limb · **anterolateral tibial bowing** · prominent fibular head

**Knee** — usually flexed, **flexion contracture** · check **active knee extension** (quadriceps) · stability in all planes → **treatment based mainly on knee stability**

**Foot** — **rigid equinovarus + supination** · sole faces perineum

![Tibial hemimelia, clinical](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4058-tib-hemimelia-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + lateral tib/fib → tibial deficiency · early films: small, poorly ossified **distal femoral epiphysis**

### Jones classification

| Type | Findings |
|---|---|
| **1a** | **No proximal tibia** on X-ray · **extensor mechanism absent** · hypoplastic distal femoral epiphysis |
| **1b** | Proximal tibia ossifies later, extensor mechanism works · distal femoral epiphysis normal |
| **2** | Proximal tibia present at birth, short tibia |
| **3** | Diaphysis + distal tibia present, **proximal tibia absent** |
| **4** | Short tibia, fibula migrated proximally, **distal tib-fib diastasis** |

**Alternative** — Kalamchi and Dawe (types 1–3)

![Jones classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4058-jones-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Finding | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Complete tibial absence** · **no active knee extension** (most cases) | **Knee disarticulation + prosthesis** |
| Proximal tibia present, **intact extensor mechanism**, minimal flexion contracture | **Tibiofibular synostosis + modified Syme** |
| **Ankle diastasis** | **Syme / Boyd amputation** |
| Brown (fibula centralized under femur) | **No longer recommended** — high failure |

### Syme amputation prognosis

**Good below-knee result** — **full functional quadriceps** + **no knee flexion contracture** · otherwise knee disarticulation\
✗ Wrong options: 35° flexion contracture · absent synostosis · bilateral · family history

## How it's asked

**"13-mo tibial hemimelia — best predictor of good Syme outcome?"** → **full functional quadriceps strength**\
✗ Wrong options: 35° flexion contracture · absent tibiofibular synostosis · bilateral

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tibial Eminence Fracture

*Pediatrics · 106 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — intra-articular **avulsion of the bony ACL attachment** (tibial spine) · **ages 8–14** · 2–5% of pediatric knee injuries with effusion

**Why bone fails** — eminence **incompletely ossified** → weaker than ACL · fails through **deep cancellous bone** · may extend into plateau (medial most common)

**Anatomy** — non-articular area between plateaus · ACL → **medial spine** (lateral aspect) · insertion 9 mm behind intermeniscal ligament, beside anterior horns · **PCL doesn't attach to spines**

**Associated injuries (~40%)** — meniscal · collateral ligament · capsular · osteochondral · MRI: 15–37% intra-articular pathology

### Adult equivalent - ACL avulsion

**Equivalent in skeletally mature** — **ACL tear** · with fracture → Segond (lateral capsule)\
✗ Wrong options: PCL tear · patellar tendon rupture · lateral meniscus tear

**Adult tibial ACL avulsion** — bony fragment on MRI + arthroscopy\
✗ Wrong options: midsubstance ACL tear · PCL avulsion · bucket-handle

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — rapid deceleration, **hyperextension / rotation** (sport) · fall from bike (hyperextension) · same mechanism as adult ACL tear

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — **immediate effusion (hemarthrosis)** · knee held **flexed** · can't bear weight · +anterior drawer / Lachman · motion block after analgesia → meniscus or entrapped fragment

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP · **lateral (best for displacement)** · intercondylar notch · oblique (plateau extension)

**CT** — pre-op planning · displacement unclear on X-ray

**MRI** — ligament / meniscal injury (better than CT) · not routine once X-ray confirms\
✗ Wrong options: MRI as next step

### Classification & closed reduction in extension

| Modified Meyers & McKeever | Description |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Nondisplaced (<3 mm)** |
| **II** | **Anterior displacement, intact posterior hinge** |
| **III** | **Completely displaced** |
| III+ | Type III + rotation |
| **IV** | Completely displaced, rotated, comminuted |

**OB variant** — IIIa comminution · IIIb rotation

![Meyers and McKeever classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q2253-illa-meyers-and-mckeever-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Tibial eminence fracture
├─ Type I → long-leg cast in extension
├─ Type II → aspirate + closed reduction in extension
│      ├─ reduces → long-leg cast in extension, close X-ray follow-up
│      └─ doesn't reduce → arthroscopic / open reduction + fixation
└─ Type III → arthroscopic / open reduction + fixation
```

**Closed reduction** — aspirate tense hemarthrosis + lidocaine · **full extension / hyperextension** · cast at **0°** (or 10–20° flexion — debated) · **3–4 wk** (Orthobullets) · 4–6 wk then hinged brace (OB) · lateral X-ray to confirm + serial films · CT/MRI if unclear · **<5 mm displacement → closed** (OB)

**Traps** — cast in **extension** (✗ Wrong options: 90° flexion) · reduce before immobilizing (✗ Wrong options: knee immobilizer + NWB) · 1–2 wk cast too short\
✗ Wrong options: immediate WB · ACL reconstruction · fragment excision (ACL attached)

### Meniscal entrapment blocking reduction

**Block to reduction** — **anterior horn of medial meniscus (most common)** > intermeniscal ligament > anterior horn lateral meniscus\
✗ Wrong options: ACL · PCL · fat pad

**Kocher (80 cases)** — medial meniscus 36 · intermeniscal 6 · lateral meniscus 1 · entrapment in **26% type II**, **65% type III** · meniscal tear 3.8%

**Lowe** — lateral meniscus + ACL pull fragment in opposite directions (not entrapment)

**Associated with type II** — **meniscal entrapment**\
✗ Wrong options: compartment syndrome (tibial tubercle fracture) · patellar dislocation · growth arrest

### Operative fixation

### Operative criteria

**Indications** — **type III** · **irreducible type II** · block to extension · failed closed reduction · late displacement

**Treatment** — **arthroscopic (or open) reduction + internal fixation** of eminence · anatomic reduction\
✗ Wrong options: ACL reconstruction · tibial tubercle ORIF

| | **Suture** | **Screw** |
|---|---|---|
| Pros | Minimal physeal damage · growth breaks non-absorbable sutures · **biomechanically superior** in type II (OB) | Easier · earlier motion |
| Cons | Technically demanding | Needs large fragment · impingement · iatrogenic comminution · physeal damage · **remove only if malpositioned** |

**Post-op** — cast in extension 7–10 days (variable) → early controlled ROM

![Screw fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4022-fixation-with-screw.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Outcomes (laxity, arthrofibrosis)

**Loss of motion** — very common, esp. **extension** (fragment impinges in notch)

**Arthrofibrosis** — **more common after surgery** · adolescents / adults → counsel stiffness · **concomitant ACL injury = independent risk factor for return to OR for MUA**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · physeal closure · compartment syndrome

**ACL laxity** — **38–100%**, more after surgery · **common but rarely symptomatic** (even after anatomic fixation — ligament stretched at injury) · ACL reconstruction later 15–25%

**Growth arrest** — iatrogenic at surgery

**Prognosis** — good · **85% return to prior sport**

## How it's asked

**"Which may be treated with closed reduction in extension?"** → **McKeever type II**\
✗ Wrong options: SH II distal femur · type III · SH IV proximal tibia · tibial tubercle type IV

**"12-yr type II, tense effusion — next?"** → **closed reduction; cast if successful, surgery if not**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · immobilizer

**"7-yr successful closed reduction, +Lachman — next?"** → **immobilization in near full extension + frequent monitoring**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic fixation · ACL reconstruction

**"14-yr gymnast, moderately displaced eminence?"** → **long-leg cast after successful fluoroscopic closed reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: 1–2 wk cast · ACL reconstruction

**"9-yr bike fall — type and next step?"** → **type II; closed reduction + long-leg cast in extension**\
✗ Wrong options: 90° flexion

**"Failed reduction — structure entrapped under fragment?"** → **anterior horn of medial meniscus**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral meniscus · ACL · fat pad

**"More likely with type II tibial spine fracture?"** → **meniscal entrapment**\
✗ Wrong options: compartment syndrome · growth arrest

**"14-yr obese, surgery planned — interposed structure?"** → **medial meniscus**\
✗ Wrong options: intermeniscal ligament

**"12-yr soccer, failed ER reduction — block?"** → **medial meniscus**

**"12-yr failed closed reduction?"** → **arthroscopic reduction + fixation of tibial eminence**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL reconstruction

**"12-yr displaced eminence, Lachman lax?"** → **anatomic reduction + internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: casting · ACL reconstruction

**"Adult equivalent of tibial spine fracture?"** → **ACL tear**

**"22-yr wakeboard, bony fragment on MRI?"** → **tibial avulsion of ACL**\
✗ Wrong options: midsubstance ACL

**"19-yr arthroscopic suture fixation — likely complication?"** → **arthrofibrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: hardware removal · SONK

**"ACL laxity after eminence fracture?"** → **common, infrequently symptomatic**

**"Concomitant ACL injury + all-epiphyseal reconstruction — outcome?"** → **return to OR for MUA**\
✗ Wrong options: physeal closure · nonunion

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tibial Shaft Fractures - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 107 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **3rd most common pediatric long-bone fracture** · **15% of pediatric fractures** · boys > girls · **mean age 8 yr** · **39% mid-diaphysis** · fibula fractured in 30%

**NAT** — **2nd most common bone fractured in non-accidental trauma** · suspect abuse in non-ambulatory child

**Anatomy** — triangular, apex anterior · anteromedial border subcutaneous · distal flare mostly cancellous · **posterior tibial a.** → nutrient + periosteal vessels · **anterior tibial a.** at risk through interosseous membrane · fibula carries 6–17% of load

## 2 Mechanism

**Adolescent** — **pedestrian vs vehicle (50%)** · direct blow

**Toddler** — low-energy twist / fall → spiral or oblique ("toddler's fracture")

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — pain, bruising · **refuses to bear weight** · tenderness · **pain on ankle dorsiflexion** · always think **compartment syndrome**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **AP + lateral tib/fib** · **image ipsilateral knee + ankle** · ± contralateral · toddler's fracture may be **normal initially** → repeat at **2 wk** for callus / periosteal reaction

**CT** — physeal / intra-articular extension (distal third) · pathologic lesion

**MRI** — pathologic / stress fracture · occult fracture

**Bone scan** — occult fracture

| Pattern | Description |
|---|---|
| Incomplete | **Greenstick** of tibia ± fibula |
| Complete | Tibia ± fibula fracture or plastic deformation |
| **Toddler's fracture** | **Nondisplaced spiral / oblique tibia, intact fibula, age <2.5 yr** |

### Toddler's fracture

**Typical** — afebrile · normal WBC / ESR / CRP · distal tibial tenderness · X-rays often negative · usually unwitnessed

**Treatment** — **long leg cast** · heals **3–4 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · aspiration · vitamin D / metabolic labs · child services · chromosomal analysis

**Not** — osteomyelitis / subperiosteal abscess (afebrile, normal labs) · NOF · NAT (age + story fit)

![Toddler's fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4026-tibial.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

**Acceptable alignment** — translation **50%** (Orthobullets, OB: <50% = >50% cortical overlap) · shortening **<1 cm** · angulation **<5–10°** sagittal + coronal (Orthobullets) · **<10°** (OB210163) · **<5°** (OB218688) — read the options

| Method | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Long leg cast** | Toddler's · greenstick |
| **Closed reduction + long leg cast** | **Most traumatic fractures** with acceptable reduction |
| **External fixator** | Extensive soft-tissue injury · length unstable · polytrauma |
| **Flexible IM nails** | Open / closed in skeletally immature · multiple long bones · **floating knee** |
| Percutaneous pins | Noncomminuted unstable oblique (± cast) |
| **Rigid IM nail** | **At / near skeletal maturity** (✗ Wrong options: open physes) |
| Plate | Physeal / articular extension · length unstable · nonunion / malunion |

**Reasons to operate (Ho)** — open fracture · obesity · compartment concern · segmental · polytrauma · floating knee · failed non-op

### Treatment - casting vs flexible nailing

**Casting technique** — sedation / GA · cast **to groin, knee flexed 30°**, molded · ± bivalve · loss of reduction → **cast wedging**

**Early weight bearing** — WB at **1 wk** in long leg cast (knee ~10°) is **safe and may speed healing** (Jenkins: +0.3 days healing per day delayed)\
✗ Wrong options: NWB 4–6 wk · short leg cast · flexible nails first

**Distal tibia fractures** — **high secondary displacement** → **follow-up within 1–2 wk** · most non-op\
✗ Wrong options: >1 month

**Casting vs flexible IMN** — only real difference: **casting = prolonged immobilization** (>3 months) · **compartment syndrome more with IMN** (up to 20%) · union, malunion, LLD similar

**Elastic nails** — **period of casting / splint + NWB** after (nails don't control rotation, bending) · **no 50-kg contraindication** (unlike femur) · open fractures OK\
✗ Wrong options: soft dressing + WBAT · added ex-fix

| Complication | Rate |
|---|---|
| Ex-fix nonunion | ~2% · **refracture** · pin tract infection |
| Flexible nail nonunion | ~10% |

![Long leg cast after reduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4026-pediatric-tibial-shaft-fracture-s-p-casting.jpg)

### Valgus deformity

| Fibula | Drift |
|---|---|
| **Intact** | **Varus** |
| **Fractured** (both bones) | **Valgus + recurvatum (procurvatum, OB)** |

**Why valgus** — lateral compartment pull + pes anserinus · **gentle varus mold** (too much → varus malunion) · residual → wedging

![Tib-fib fracture drifts into valgus](../../MCQ/attachments/q218660-illa-associated-fibula-fx.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Compartment syndrome

**Compartment syndrome** — less common than adults · open + closed · signs in kids: **3 As — analgesia ↑, anxiety, agitation** · firm leg with pulses → **4-compartment fasciotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: more narcotics · nerve block · PCA · observe

**LLD** — age <10 · comminution → overgrowth · pins → growth arrest, **recurvatum from tibial tubercle arrest**

**Angular deformity** — complex · **valgus + apex posterior** · physeal extension · osteotomy for rotational malunion / symptomatic

**Nonunion** — **25% of open fractures** · ↑ age, wound severity · hypertrophic → graft + rigid fixation

**Healing** — **3–4 wk toddler's** · **6–8 wk** others

## How it's asked

**"23-mo refuses to bear weight, normal labs, X-rays negative?"** → **long leg cast**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI pelvis · knee aspiration · vitamin D

**"3-yr won't bear weight, afebrile, normal CRP/ESR — diagnosis?"** → **nondisplaced oblique / spiral tibia, intact fibula**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis · NAT · NOF

**"2-yr 11-mo fall, spiral distal tibia?"** → **long leg cast**\
✗ Wrong options: child services · MRI

**"Casting vs flexible IMN — casting has more?"** → **prolonged immobilization**\
✗ Wrong options: compartment syndrome · nonunion · LLD

**"Titanium elastic nailing — TRUE?"** → **may need a period of casting**\
✗ Wrong options: higher union · >50 kg contraindicated · avoid in open

**"13-yr ATV, elastic nails — post-op?"** → **NWB in splint or cast**\
✗ Wrong options: soft bandage + WBAT

**"7-yr tree fall — maximize healing?"** → **closed reduction + long leg cast, WB at 1 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: NWB 4–6 wk · short leg cast · nailing

**"11-yr distal tibia, long leg cast — TRUE?"** → **follow-up within 1–2 wk (secondary displacement)**\
✗ Wrong options: most need surgery · rigid nail

**"9-yr tib + fib fracture in cast — likely deformity?"** → **valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: varus · procurvatum

**"3-yr, 30 h after fracture, firm leg, pulses present, pain despite morphine?"** → **4-compartment fasciotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: nerve block · PCA · observe

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tibial Tubercle Fracture

*Pediatrics · 108 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — <1% of pediatric fractures · **males >> females** · **12–15 yr** (near skeletal maturity) · basketball, football, sprinting, high jump · growth hormone weakens physis

**Ossification** — **2 centers**: primary (proximal tibial physis) + secondary (tubercle apophysis, patellar tendon insertion)

**Physeal closure** — **posterior → anterior, proximal → distal** · **tubercle last to fuse** → vulnerable in older adolescents

**Vessel** — **recurrent anterior tibial artery** at **lateral border of tubercle** → lacerated → retracts under fascia → **anterior compartment syndrome**

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **knee flexion + quadriceps contraction** · concentric quad contraction at **jump take-off** · eccentric quad contraction in forced knee flexion\
✗ Wrong options: hamstring · gastrocnemius · direct blow

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — sudden pain at jump / sprint start · can't walk · effusion / hemarthrosis (type III) · tubercle tenderness · **extensor lag / can't straight-leg raise** (II–III; retinaculum may allow some extension) · **patella alta** · palpable defect · watch **anterior compartment**

**Associated** — **compartment syndrome** · **meniscal tears with type III** · cruciate / collateral / patellar injuries uncommon (OB7708, OB210101) · patellar / quadriceps tendon avulsion, ligament tears to assess (OB1487)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + lateral · **internal rotation view** (tubercle in profile) · contralateral comparison (younger) · widening / hinging of apophysis · **patella alta** · anterior swelling only → type V sleeve

**CT** — intra-articular / posterior extension · arteriogram if popliteal concern\
✗ Wrong options: delay fasciotomy

**MRI** — not routine · nondisplaced type II extension or type V

### Ogden classification (modified Watson-Jones)

| Type | Fracture |
|---|---|
| **I** | Secondary ossification center near patellar tendon insertion |
| **II** | Propagates proximally **between primary and secondary centers** |
| **III** (most common) | **Coronal**, extends posteriorly **across primary center → intra-articular** |
| **IV** | **Through entire proximal tibial physis** |
| **V** | **Periosteal sleeve avulsion** of extensor mechanism from secondary center |
| Modifier | **A** nondisplaced · **B** displaced |

![Ogden classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q8875-illb-ogden-class.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Type I** / displacement **<2 mm** / acceptable after closed reduction | **Long leg cast in extension 6 wk** |
| **Displaced, types II–IV** | **ORIF** ± arthrotomy / arthroscopy (joint surface, meniscus) |
| **Type V** | **Soft-tissue (periosteal) repair** |
| Compartment syndrome | **Emergent fasciotomy + ORIF** (± prophylactic fasciotomy) |

### Treatment - ORIF

**ORIF** — midline incision · **remove interposed periosteum** (blocks closed reduction) · anatomic reduction · **4.0 cancellous partially threaded screws** (or headless compression) · smooth K-wires if >3 yr from maturity · cannulated screws **better than CRPP** (OB) · NWB long leg cast / brace **4–6 wk** · sport **≥3 months**

**Arthrotomy / arthroscopy** — intra-articular (type III) → visualize joint, evacuate hematoma, meniscus repair

**Soft-tissue repair (V)** — heavy suture of periosteum to secondary center · cast **8–10 wk**

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: long leg cast for displaced · patellar tendon bearing cast · CRPP · ligament reconstruction · spanning ex-fix (temporizing only) · patellar tendon repair (tendon intact, deforming) · fragment excision · MRI / CT if X-ray clear

![Screw fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4023-intra-operative-fluoro-showing-reduction-using-partially-thr.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Compartment syndrome - anterior tibial recurrent artery

**Rate** — **4%** (Orthobullets, OB) · 4–5% (OB) · up to **10%** (OB) — read the options · high with **type III**

**Vessel** — **anterior tibial recurrent artery**\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal · posterior tibial · middle geniculate (ACL) · medial / lateral inferior geniculate · popliteal (knee dislocation)

**Signs** — rising analgesic need, anxiety, distress, swelling overnight → **emergent fasciotomies + ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · vascular exploration · ORIF alone

![Recurrent branch of anterior tibial artery](../../MCQ/attachments/q857-illa-recurrent-branch-of-anterior-tibial.jpg)

**Other** — stiffness · refracture

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Bursitis** | **Most common after surgery** · screw prominence → removal |
| **Recurvatum** | Anterior arrest, posterior growth → ↓ slope · **more common than LLD** (LLD low — age) · unusual (near maturity) |
| Popliteal artery injury | Over distal metaphyseal fragment (physeal types) |

## How it's asked

**"15-yr ski jump, type III tubercle — watch for?"** → **compartment syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL disruption · popliteal artery · patella baja

**"Compartment syndrome after tubercle fracture — artery?"** → **anterior tibial recurrent**\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal · middle geniculate · saphenous

**"15-yr ramp jump, intra-articular tubercle, ↑ leg pain in 3 h?"** → **anterior tibial recurrent artery**\
✗ Wrong options: inferior geniculates

**"220-lb basketball, tubercle fracture — complication?"** → **compartment syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: LLD · varus · procurvatum

**"Displaced type III, noncontact — more likely?"** → **compartment syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL + MCL · patella dislocation · peroneal palsy

**"13-yr ↑ analgesia — vessel in figure?"** → **recurrent anterior tibial artery (lateral border of tubercle)**

**"13-yr admitted for morning ORIF, ↑ narcotics, swollen leg?"** → **emergent fasciotomies + ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF only · observe · vascular exploration

**"12-yr basketball, anterior tibial pain at 6 h — artery?"** → **recurrent branch of anterior tibial artery**\
✗ Wrong options: popliteal

**"14-yr volleyball, type III, anxious overnight?"** → **recurrent anterior tibial artery**

**"14-yr can't straight-leg raise, displaced tubercle?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: long leg cast · PTB cast · CRPP

**"13-yr displaced intra-articular tubercle?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · cast · fasciotomy

**"10-yr cheerleader, palpable defect, patella elevated, ligament retracted >2 cm?"** → **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · CT · excision · cast

**"14-yr CT shows articular extension — definitive?"** → **ORIF** (± arthrotomy)\
✗ Wrong options: spanning ex-fix · percutaneous screws · tendon repair

**"17-yr can't SLR, screw ORIF — which image?"** → **type III tibial tubercle fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: quadriceps rupture · Osgood-Schlatter · ACL tear

**"Mechanism of tubercle avulsion?"** → **knee flexion + quadriceps contraction**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tillaux Fractures

*Pediatrics · 109 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **Salter-Harris III** of the **anterolateral distal tibial epiphysis** · **transitional** fracture (physis closing) · entirely epiphyseal

**Epidemiology** — **3–5% of pediatric ankle fractures** · **more common in girls** · **12–14 yr** (12–15 OB) · within ~1 yr of complete distal tibial closure (18-month transitional window) · **older than triplane** (triplane ~10–12, OB)

**Associated** — distal fibula fracture (SH I/II) · ipsilateral tibial shaft fracture

### Distal tibial physeal closure pattern

**Order** — **central → anteromedial → posteromedial → lateral** (Orthobullets, TO, OB) · central → posterior → medial → anterolateral (OB3880) · central → medial → lateral (OB) · central → anteromedial → posterolateral (TO17124838)

**Last to fuse** — **anterolateral** (TO) → open lateral physis avulsed by AITFL\
✗ Wrong options: anteromedial · posteromedial · posterolateral

**Distal tibial physis** — 35–40% of tibial growth · 15–20% of lower-limb growth · 3–4 mm/yr · growth until **14 girls / 16 boys**

![Pattern of distal tibial physeal closure](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4028-pattern-of-distal-tibia-physeal-closure.jpg)

### Anatomy - AITFL avulsion

**Ligament** — **anterior inferior tibiofibular ligament (AITFL)** · Chaput tubercle (anterolateral tibial epiphysis) → **Wagstaffe tubercle** (anterior distal fibula) · part of **syndesmosis** (+ PITFL)\
✗ Wrong options: PITFL · ATFL (ankle sprain) · PTFL · CFL · deltoid

**Same ligament injured in** — **high ankle sprain** (syndesmosis)\
✗ Wrong options: low ankle sprain (ATFL) · Weber A (below syndesmosis) · Gosselin (V-shaped plafond fracture) · subtalar dislocation

**Axial CT** — displaced fragment = **anterolateral region (AITFL)**\
✗ Wrong options: posterolateral · medial malleolus

![AITFL on the Tillaux fragment](../../MCQ/attachments/q488-illc-graphic-11-small.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **supination / eversion – external rotation** → AITFL tension → anterolateral avulsion\
✗ Wrong options: pronation/eversion–ER (SH II lateral tibia + suprasyndesmotic fibula) · supination–plantarflexion (posterior tibia) · axial compression (SH V) · supination–inversion (ankle sprain)

**vs triplane** — **no coronal fracture in posterior metaphysis** = Tillaux

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — pain · can't bear weight · slight swelling · **focal anterolateral joint-line tenderness** · deformity rare (fibula blocks displacement)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP · lateral · **mortise = best view** · SH III anterolateral epiphysis

**CT** — fracture pattern · **articular displacement** · intramalleolar / medial variants · **after closed reduction → CT for residual step-off**\
✗ Wrong options: discharge without CT

![Tillaux on mortise view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4028-xray-ankle-mortise.jpg)

![Axial CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4028-ct-ankle-axia-shows-tillaux.jpg)

## 5 Management

### CT & surgical indications (>2 mm step-off)

### Operative criteria

| After closed reduction | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<2 mm** displacement (rare) | **Long leg cast** 3–4 wk → short leg cast / boot 2–4 wk |
| **>2 mm** residual step-off | **CRPP or ORIF** |

**Not indications** — age, sex, any step-off <2 mm\
✗ Wrong options: age >12 · male · any step-off

**Closed reduction** — **internal rotation** of foot (or dorsiflex pronated foot then IR) · long leg cast controls **rotation** · early X-rays

**CRPP** — K-wire joystick · fluoro / arthrogram · K-wire or cannulated screw · **crossing physis OK** (little growth left) · good outcome if residual **<2.5 mm**

**ORIF** — **anterolateral approach** · reduce with **internal rotation** · intraepiphyseal or **transphyseal** wires / screws · long leg cast 3–4 wk → short leg walking cast 2 wk

**Post-reduction care** — low compartment risk · long leg > short leg cast\
✗ Wrong options: admit + splint

## 6 Complications

**Growth arrest** — **rare** (physis already closing) · ↓ with anatomic reduction

**Early arthritis** — ↑ with articular displacement · degenerative change as early as 4 yr

## X vs Y

| | **Tillaux** | **Triplane** |
|---|---|---|
| Salter-Harris | **III** | **IV** (as described by OB) |
| Age | **Older**, nearer maturity | Younger (~10–12, OB) |
| Posterior metaphyseal coronal fracture | **Absent** | Present |

## How it's asked

**"Juvenile Tillaux — avulsed by?"** → **anterior inferior tibiofibular ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: PITFL · ATFL · CFL

**"Axial CT — Tillaux fragment region + ligament?"** → **anterolateral region; AITFL**\
✗ Wrong options: posterolateral · deltoid

**"Ligament displacing Tillaux fragment?"** → **anterior tibiofibular**\
✗ Wrong options: deltoid · calcaneofibular

**"14-yr girl, displaced fragment — attached ligament?"** → **AITFL**\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL · long plantar

**"14-yr eversion injury — ligament in figure?"** → **AITFL**\
✗ Wrong options: PITFL · PTFL · CFL

**"Tillaux — same ligament involved in?"** → **high ankle sprain**\
✗ Wrong options: low ankle sprain · Weber A · Gosselin

**"13-yr SH III anterolateral distal tibia — mechanism?"** → **supination/eversion–external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: pronation/eversion–ER · axial compression

**"12-yr after closed reduction — next to optimize outcome?"** → **CT scan of ankle**\
✗ Wrong options: discharge in cast · pinning before CT

**"Strongest indication for fixation after reduction?"** → **articular step-off >2 mm**\
✗ Wrong options: age · male sex · any step-off

**"Last part of distal tibial physis to fuse?"** → **anterolateral** (TO)

**"Correct pattern of distal tibial physeal closure?"** → **central → anteromedial → posteromedial → lateral** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Transient Synovitis of Hip

*Pediatrics · 110 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — nonspecific inflammation + hypertrophy of synovium · **most common cause of pediatric hip pain** · diagnosis of exclusion vs **septic arthritis**

**Epidemiology** — **3% of children 3–10 yr** · peak **4–8 yr** · **M:F 2:1** · hip most common joint · **recurrence up to 20%**

### Diagnosis & preceding viral illness

**Cause** — largely unknown · **viral upper respiratory infection** (most cited) · bacterial (**poststreptococcal toxic synovitis**) · trauma · ↑ interferon · allergy

**Preceded by** — **infectious respiratory illness**\
✗ Wrong options: hematogenous bacterial inoculation (septic arthritis) · hypothyroidism (SCFE) · secondhand smoke (Perthes) · LLD (painless limp)

### Genes & lab markers

| Lab | Transient synovitis |
|---|---|
| WBC | Normal / slightly ↑ |
| **CRP** | **<20 mg/L** — **most important to RULE OUT septic arthritis** · CRP >20 = independent risk factor for sepsis |
| ESR | Usually <20 mm/h (can be ↑ — 35 with normal CRP + rapid improvement still synovitis) |
| Synovial WBC | Near normal · **>50,000 → treat as septic** |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — recent URI or trauma · mild / no fever · acute or **insidious groin / thigh pain** · refuses to bear weight → **better later in day** (limps by evening) · muscle spasm

**Exam** — hip in **flexion, abduction, external rotation** (lowest intracapsular pressure) · not toxic · **↓ internal rotation = most sensitive** ROM sign · painless arc → synovitis > sepsis · log-roll guarding · spine + knee non-tender · toe-walking / cavus / claw toes → neurological limp

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + frog-leg / lateral · ± spine · **usually normal**

**US** — effusion (accurate) · synovial thickening · **can't separate synovitis from sepsis**, but **no effusion → infection unlikely**

**MRI** — myositis / osteomyelitis suspicion · markedly ↑ labs

**Aspiration** — if septic suspicion (clinical / Kocher criteria)

**Differential** — septic hip · osteomyelitis (point tenderness, swelling, ↑ markers) · **SCFE** (10–16 yr, visible on AP) · **Perthes** (visible on X-ray) · leukemia (normal CBC rules out) · JIA (rarely hip) · fracture (no trauma)

![Hip effusion on ultrasound](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4030-ultrasound-left-hip.jpg)

![Normal pediatric pelvis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4030-normal-radiograph-of-a-pediatric-pelvis.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Low septic suspicion** — **IV / PO NSAIDs + close observation 24 h** · minimize walking · ± traction · **improves on NSAIDs → synovitis**

**Course** — **marked improvement 24–48 h** · resolves **<1 wk** (Orthobullets treatment) · 1–2 wk (prognosis)

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** — **~20%**

**Perthes** — 0–10% (controversial; most series no sequelae)

## X vs Y

| | **Transient synovitis** | **Septic arthritis** |
|---|---|---|
| Fever | Mild / none | Higher |
| CRP | **<20 mg/L** | >20 |
| Course | Rapid improvement on NSAIDs | Doesn't improve |
| Synovial WBC | Near normal | >50,000 |

## How it's asked

**"8-yr hip pain, CRP 1.8, ESR 35, walking after 24 h ketorolac?"** → **transient synovitis**\
✗ Wrong options: septic arthritis · leukemia · JIA

**"5-yr recent URI, CRP 9, ESR 20, limited IR?"** → **transient synovitis**\
✗ Wrong options: septic · SCFE · Perthes

**"5-yr limp, normal labs — what preceded?"** → **infectious respiratory illness**\
✗ Wrong options: bacterial inoculation · hypothyroidism · secondhand smoke

**"2-yr improving, afebrile, normal CRP, US effusion?"** → **toxic synovitis**\
✗ Wrong options: septic · osteomyelitis · leukemia

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Traumatic Hip Dislocation - Pediatric

*Pediatrics · 111 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — **80% posterior** · **more common than hip fracture** in children

**Mechanism** — **<10 yr: low-energy** sport / trip and fall (lax ligaments, cartilaginous acetabulum; <6 yr minimal force, OB) · **>10 yr: high-energy** (MVA)

**Associated** — acetabular fractures **less common than adults** (posterior wall most common) · femoral head / neck / proximal femoral physis fracture · missed ipsilateral femoral shaft fracture (OB) · **AVN ↑ if not reduced within 6 h**

## 3 Presentation

| Direction | Limb position |
|---|---|
| **Posterior** (most common) | **Slight flexion, adduction, internal rotation** · LLD (large posterior wall fragment → short without malalignment) |
| Anterior | Slight flexion, **abduction, external rotation** |
| Inferior | External rotation + extension |

**Nerves** — **sciatic or gluteal** palsy (rare) · usually resolves after reduction

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **AP pelvis** (most diagnosed) · **lateral** (anterior vs posterior; check femoral neck before reduction) · **post-reduction films → joint incongruity / nonconcentric reduction**

**MRI** — **study of choice** for abnormal post-reduction films (joint widening) · less radiation · **best for interposed soft tissue, entrapped labrum / capsule**

**CT** — **2nd choice** · **osteochondral fragments** (older children) · soft-tissue interposition hard to see

![Nonconcentric reduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4017-nonconcentric.jpg)

![Entrapped labrum — subtle asymmetry](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4017-an-entrapped-labrum-can-produce-subtle-asymmetry-as-seen-in-.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Pediatric hip dislocation
├─ First → urgent closed reduction under GA / sedation, within 6 h (85% succeed)
├─ Post-reduction films normal → spica / abduction splint (<10 yr) or protected WB (older)
└─ Nonconcentric / fragment / irreducible → MRI or CT, then open reduction
       └─ Approach → in direction of dislocation (usually posterior)
```

**Closed reduction** — **adequate anesthesia mandatory** (avoid displacing unrecognized **proximal femoral epiphyseal fracture**) · **under fluoroscopy** (epiphyseolysis risk) · traction in flexion + gentle rotation · test stability before waking · post-reduction imaging

**Aftercare** — spica / bed rest with abduction splint **4 wk** if <10 yr · brace + **6–12 wk** protected WB in older

### Operative criteria

| Open reduction indications |
|---|
| **Nonconcentric reduction** |
| **Intra-articular fragment** |
| Unstable acetabular rim fracture · femoral head / neck fracture |
| **Irreducible closed** |

### Non-concentric reduction - arthrotomy

**Persistent incongruity** — soft-tissue interposition (**torn labrum + lateral acetabular apophysis**) · osteochondral fragment → **hip arthrotomy via POSTERIOR approach** (posterior structures already torn)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior approach · repeat closed reduction · traction 1 month · WBAT

## 6 Complications

### AVN with delayed reduction

**AVN** — **3–15%** (Orthobullets) · 0–43% (OB) · ↓ under age 5 · less than adults (without neck fracture) · **only complication linked to delay in reduction** · >24 h → 66% (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: coxa magna · redislocation · chondrolysis · HO (not time-dependent)

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Coxa magna** | **20%**, radiographic, no functional limit |
| Redislocation | Rare · prolonged immobilization → capsulorrhaphy if recurrent |
| Nerve | Sciatic / gluteal, usually resolves |
| Other (OB) | Femoral head deformity · premature physeal fusion · degenerative arthritis · chondrolysis |

**Prognosis** — good when treated promptly · most return to high-demand activity

## How it's asked

**"10-yr post-reduction CT — joint widened, fragment?"** → **hip arthrotomy via posterior approach**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior approach · repeat closed reduction · traction

**"3-yr MVC hip dislocation — delayed concentric reduction ↑ risk of?"** → **avascular necrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: coxa magna · recurrent dislocation · chondrolysis · HO

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Triplane Fractures

*Pediatrics · 112 / 112*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — complex **Salter-Harris IV** pattern in **3 planes** · **transitional** fracture (physis closing) · 2, 3 or 4 parts

**Epidemiology** — **5–15% of pediatric ankle fractures** · **males** · ages 10–17 · **mean 13 yr** · **younger than Tillaux**

**Physeal closure** — central → anteromedial → posteromedial → lateral (Orthobullets) · central → medial → lateral (OB) · 18-month transitional period

**Associated** — **fibula fracture 50%** (spiral, above physis · unstable after tibial fixation → **fix fibula**) · ipsilateral tibial shaft fracture

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism

| Pattern | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Lateral triplane** | **Supination–external rotation** (like Tillaux) |
| Medial triplane | **Adduction** |

**Asked** — **external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: internal rotation · supination–adduction · pronation–abduction · axial load

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — pain · can't bear weight · swelling · focal tenderness · deformity

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Radiographic fracture pattern

| Plane | Structure | Seen on |
|---|---|---|
| **Sagittal** | **Epiphysis** (lateral) | **AP X-ray → SH III** |
| Axial (transverse) | Physis | — |
| **Coronal** | **Posterior metaphysis** | **Lateral X-ray → SH II** |

**Overall** — SH IV pattern, but **no true SH IV component** on either view\
✗ Wrong options: SH II on AP · SH III on lateral

**von Laer** — SH III epiphyseal component on AP depends only on **physeal maturity**, not mechanism · biplane vs triplane type I (metaphyseal line ends in physis) vs type II (into epiphysis + joint)

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP · lateral · **mortise = best for displacement**

**CT** — usually **required** (pattern + articular congruity) · after closed reduction · all 3 planes · **"Mercedes-Benz sign"** · 4-part only seen on CT

![Triplane on AP and lateral](../../MCQ/attachments/q212972-illb-lateral-diagram.jpg)

![Mercedes-Benz sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4029-mercedes-benz-sign.jpg)

### Classification by parts

| Parts | Fragments |
|---|---|
| **2-part** | (1) anterolateral + posterior epiphysis with posterior metaphyseal fragment · (2) anteromedial epiphysis with rest of tibia |
| **3-part** | (1) anterolateral epiphysis · (2) posterior epiphysis + posterior metaphyseal fragment · (3) anteromedial epiphysis + rest of tibia |
| **4-part** | Comminuted · **CT only** separates from 3-part |

### Classification by pattern

| Pattern | Epiphysis | Metaphysis | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lateral** | **Sagittal** | **Coronal** | **Most common** · looks like Tillaux on AP → SH II on lateral distinguishes |
| Medial | Coronal | Sagittal | — |
| Intramalleolar | I — intra-articular, weight-bearing · II — intra-articular, non-weight-bearing · III — extra-articular | — | — |

![3-part triplane](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4029-3-part.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Surgical indications & articular gap outcomes

### Operative criteria

| Displacement | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<2 mm** | **Closed reduction + long leg cast 3–4 wk** → short leg cast / boot 2–4 wk · ideal for **2-part** |
| **>2 mm** (Orthobullets) · >2.5 mm (OB220849) | **CRPP or ORIF** |

**Closed reduction** — **reduce fibula first** · lateral → **internal rotation** · medial → **eversion** (closed) / external rotation (operative) · post-reduction CT

**ORIF** — anterolateral approach (lateral) · anteromedial (medial) · arthroscopic assist described · K-wires / cannulated screws · **epiphyseal screws parallel to physis** · metaphyseal screw if large · long leg cast 3–4 wk → short leg walking cast 2 wk · hardware irritation, transient neuropathy

**Why operate** — **restore articular congruity → ↓ posttraumatic arthritis** (near maturity)\
✗ Wrong options: avoid growth arrest · osteonecrosis · rotation deformity · tarsal tunnel

**Residual gap** — **>2.5 mm → worse functional outcome**\
✗ Wrong options: observation fine to 5 mm · bracing / WB protocol matters more · arthritis risk negligible · physeal closure dominates

## 6 Complications

**Growth arrest** — **7–21%** · ↑ **pronation–abduction** vs SER · ↑ residual displacement · usually insignificant · watch if **>2 yr growth left**

**Ankle pain / degeneration** — ↑ with **step-off >2 mm**

## X vs Y

| | **Triplane** | **Tillaux** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **Younger** (mean 13) | Older |
| Sex | Males | Girls (Orthobullets) |
| Incidence (peds ankle) | 5–15% | 3–5% |
| Lateral X-ray | **Coronal posterior metaphyseal fracture (SH II)** | None — entirely epiphyseal |
| Surgery | >2 mm | >2 mm |

## How it's asked

**"15-yr skateboarder, triplane — mechanism?"** → **external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: supination–adduction · axial load · pronation–abduction

**"Most common triplane — fracture lines and views?"** → **sagittal epiphyseal on AP + coronal metaphyseal on lateral**

**"Most common triplane — TRUE?"** → **SH III component seen on AP**\
✗ Wrong options: SH II on AP · SH IV on either view

**"14-yr failed closed reduction — main reason for surgery?"** → **↓ posttraumatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: avoid growth arrest · osteonecrosis

**"13-yr post-op CT 3 mm gap — counsel?"** → **residual gap >2.5 mm → worse function**\
✗ Wrong options: observe to 5 mm · arthritis negligible
